# Knowledge Base Catalog

Evergreen documentation for Pyrycode. Updated as things change, not appended to.

Search with QMD using its current tool schema. Start with [the short index](INDEX.md).

## Decisions

| # | File | Decision |
|---|------|----------|
| 001 | [001-go-language.md](decisions/001-go-language.md) | Go as the implementation language |
| 002 | [002-pty-supervisor.md](decisions/002-pty-supervisor.md) | PTY-level wrapping over alternatives |
| 003 | [003-session-addressable-runtime.md](decisions/003-session-addressable-runtime.md) | `internal/sessions` Pool wraps the supervisor for additive Phase 1.1+ |
| 004 | [004-fsnotify-for-rotation-detection.md](decisions/004-fsnotify-for-rotation-detection.md) | `fsnotify` for live `/clear` detection (over polling or raw inotify+kqueue) |
| 005 | [005-idle-eviction-state-machine.md](decisions/005-idle-eviction-state-machine.md) | Per-session two-state machine + explicit `Activate` for idle eviction / lazy respawn |
| 006 | [006-concurrent-active-cap-lru.md](decisions/006-concurrent-active-cap-lru.md) | `Config.ActiveCap` + LRU victim selection at `Pool.Activate`; force-eviction `Session.Evict` primitive |
| 007 | [007-bridge-iteration-boundaries.md](decisions/007-bridge-iteration-boundaries.md) | `Bridge` input path moves from `io.Pipe` to `chan []byte` + per-iteration cancel (`BeginIteration` / `EndIteration`) so the input pump terminates per `runOnce` iteration instead of leaking and racing the next one for typed bytes during a restart |
| 008 | [008-bridge-resize-seam.md](decisions/008-bridge-resize-seam.md) | `Bridge.Resize(rows, cols)` + `SetPTY(*os.File)` is the supervisor-side resize seam (over a method on `*Supervisor` or a `Run`-wired callback); reuses the `Session → Bridge` chain and `BeginIteration`/`EndIteration` hook points |
| 009 | [009-resize-wire-shape.md](decisions/009-resize-wire-shape.md) | Live SIGWINCH propagation rides a side-channel `VerbResize` on a fresh control connection (over a framed escape inside the attach byte stream); raw byte purity preserved, AC#2 satisfied structurally |
| 010 | [010-sessions-cli-sub-router.md](decisions/010-sessions-cli-sub-router.md) | `pyry sessions <verb>` dispatches via a `runSessions` sub-router that peels global pyry flags via `parseClientFlags` then dispatches on the first positional; each sub-verb owns its own `flag.NewFlagSet`; `sessionsVerbList` constant over a derived list |
| 011 | [011-cli-prefix-resolution.md](decisions/011-cli-prefix-resolution.md) | UUID-or-prefix resolution for `sessions.*` CLI verbs is client-side via `control.SessionsList` (mirroring `Pool.ResolveID`'s exact-then-prefix order) over extending the wire with a prefix-aware `sessions.rm` or a dedicated `sessions.resolve` verb |
| 012 | [012-attach-stdio-flag-vs-verb.md](decisions/012-attach-stdio-flag-vs-verb.md) | `pyry attach --stdio` is a flag on the existing verb (over a separate `attach-stdio` verb or auto-detect via `term.IsTerminal`); reuses `VerbAttach` with `Cols=0/Rows=0` riding `omitempty` off the wire — zero server change |
| 013 | [013-evict-activate-persist-ordering.md](decisions/013-evict-activate-persist-ordering.md) | `transitionTo` closes the per-direction wake channel AFTER `pool.persist` returns (over moving persist into `lcMu` — deadlocks via `saveLocked` — or serialising transitions through a goroutine — overkill since `Run` is the sole caller); `Activate`/`Evict` drop their already-in-target-state early-return and always wait on the channel so disk and memory agree at every return point |
| 014 | [014-get-or-create-take-or-create.md](decisions/014-get-or-create-take-or-create.md) | `Pool.GetOrCreate` is take-or-create rather than insert-or-error (no `CreateWithID`); validates UUIDv4 shape at the Pool boundary via `ValidID`; holds `p.mu` across register + persist + skip-set + `g.Go` so concurrent same-UUID callers cannot observe a registered entry before its lifecycle goroutine is parked on `activateCh` |
| 015 | [015-update-restart-probe-inline.md](decisions/015-update-restart-probe-inline.md) | `pyry update` daemon-restart probe (`os.Stat` of launchd plist / systemd user unit) lives inline in `cmd/pyry/update.go`, keeping `internal/update/restart.go` a pure decision function with no filesystem calls; restart executor is an injectable function-typed seam on `updateOptions` (mirrors `replace` / `executablePath` pattern) so the integration test asserts argv without exec'ing real `launchctl` / `systemctl` |
| 016 | [016-bootstrap-ignores-persisted-lifecycle-state.md](decisions/016-bootstrap-ignores-persisted-lifecycle-state.md) | Bootstrap session ignores persisted `lifecycle_state` on warm-start: `Pool.New`'s `pickBootstrap` arm forces `lcState = stateActive` regardless of disk because nothing under non-TTY stdin (launchd / systemd / piped wrapper) calls `Pool.Activate` on the bootstrap, so a persisted `evicted` parks `runEvicted` on `activateCh` forever (#202). Non-bootstrap sessions retain persisted state — lazy respawn on attach is correct there |
| 017 | [017-install-script-smoke-check.md](decisions/017-install-script-smoke-check.md) | `install.sh` post-install smoke check is bash + sentinel-grep against `Uptime: 2562047h47m16.854775807s` (over a Go subcommand or daemon-side watchdog); gates on currently-running default-named services only (`launchctl print | grep 'state = running'` / `systemctl --user is-active --quiet pyry`) so deliberately-disabled units aren't auto-started; four exit codes (0 healthy / 2 sentinel / 3 dial-fail / 4 restart-fail) preserve the diagnostic distinction AC #4 names; no manual sleep before the probe — `pyry status`'s built-in dial retry (#199) + 5s deadline IS the grace period |
| 018 | [018-config-overlay-decode.md](decisions/018-config-overlay-decode.md) | `internal/config.Load` initializes `cfg := DefaultConfig()` before `json.Unmarshal(data, &cfg)` so absent JSON fields keep their default values (over a two-pass `map[string]json.RawMessage` merge or pointer-fields distinguishing absent-vs-empty); malformed JSON wraps and surfaces (no silent fallback); error path returns `Config{}` not `DefaultConfig()` so callers that ignore `err` break loudly |
| 019 | [019-identity-loader-co-located.md](decisions/019-identity-loader-co-located.md) | `LoadOrCreate` lives in `internal/identity/store.go` (relaxed package doc-comment) over a sibling `internal/identitystore`; loader has no API surface beyond the type, future identifier-loaders (e.g. `devices.json`) compose by adding files rather than packages |
| 020 | [020-devices-registry-snapshot-then-write.md](decisions/020-devices-registry-snapshot-then-write.md) | `internal/devices.Registry.Save` snapshots the slice under `mu`, releases, then sorts + atomic-writes outside the lock (over `internal/sessions`'s hold-across-I/O); auth-path readers (`List`, `FindByTokenHash`, called per WS handshake) stay unblocked while pairing writes; `Device`'s all-value-type fields make shallow snapshot strictly safe |
| 021 | [021-pair-cli-order-of-operations.md](decisions/021-pair-cli-order-of-operations.md) | `pyry pair` runs all loads (config / devices / server-id) + relay resolution before `crypto/rand`, and `Registry.Save` before `pair.Render`; converts a silent-at-pair / fatal-at-connect failure (Render before Save) into a loud-at-pair / recoverable failure, and contracts the plaintext-token's lifetime to the shortest closed interval consistent with rendering it |
| 022 | [022-conversations-update-callback-under-lock.md](decisions/022-conversations-update-callback-under-lock.md) | `internal/conversations.Registry.Update` runs the caller's `func(*Conversation)` callback while holding the registry mutex (over snapshot-mutate-swap or CAS-loop alternatives); pointer-to-slice-element mutation pattern, no-callback-back-into-registry contract documented; AC-bound `bool`-only return — post-mutation snapshot read happens inside `fn` if needed |
| 023 | [023-activate-waits-pty-readiness.md](decisions/023-activate-waits-pty-readiness.md) | `Session.Activate` strengthened to wait for supervisor PTY readiness (`Supervisor.WaitForPTY` backed by a freshening `ptmxReadyCh`) at the tail, over making `WriteUserTurn` wait internally (would break the `TurnWriter` shape) or restructuring `transitionTo` to defer the `activeCh` close (would entangle the `#202` bootstrap warm-start invariant); closes the ~hundreds-of-ms gap between `transitionTo` closing `activeCh` and `runOnce` binding the new PTY master, eliminating the silent-drop-on-`ptmx == nil` failure mode the relay-routed `send_message` path hit on idle-evicted bootstraps (#396) |
| 024 | [024-noise-ik-mobile-e2e.md](decisions/024-noise-ik-mobile-e2e.md) | Mobile wire protocol v2 adopts Noise_IK over `Noise_IK_25519_ChaChaPoly_BLAKE2s` for E2E encryption phone↔binary through the stateless relay (#430); AEAD-seals the entire inner mobile-protocol envelope (relay sees only `{conn_id, frame}` outer + opaque ciphertext); per-binary static keypair at `~/.pyry/<daemon-name>/static_key.json` (`0600` enforced at startup, parent dir `0700`, `<daemon-name>` allowlist-canonicalised, `O_NOFOLLOW` on read) shared across paired phones, per-phone device-static keypair in Android Keystore; 1-hour time-based re-key + explicit `rekey_request` envelope (re-key `noise_init` early-data is empty — WireGuard/Tailscale precedent); hard cutover from v1 (no soft fallback, pre-flight `pyry pair list`-empty gate filed as #436) — v1's reserved `payload_encrypted` flag becomes structurally redundant and is removed not renamed; `flynn/noise` for Go (pinned in #431), three Kotlin options surfaced for mobile team to pick |
| 025 | [025-mobile-remote-head-interactive-session.md](decisions/025-mobile-remote-head-interactive-session.md) | Mobile is a full **remote head** for an interactive Claude Code session, not a chat client — drive model, wire versioning, and remote-permission model accepted (2026-06-06/07), implementation phased across epic #600; extends ADR 024, does not change the transport or `noise_msg` frame |
| 026 | [026-embedded-acp-pool-exact-one-claude.md](decisions/026-embedded-acp-pool-exact-one-claude.md) | Embedded ACP pool runs **exactly one interactive claude per ACP session** — `Config.BootstrapEvicted` suppresses the eager bootstrap claude (over adopting `Pool.Default()`; divergence 6) and `Pool.Ready()` gates the unrecoverable first-`Create → ErrPoolNotRunning` startup race; service-mode `Bridge` keeps claude off the JSON-RPC stdout (#761); refines ADR 016, sibling to ADR 023 |
| 027 | [027-acp-mapping.md](decisions/027-acp-mapping.md) | Ports the canonical **internal-turn-event → ACP mapping** out of the personal vault into an in-repo ADR sibling to ADR 025 (#745) — the outbound/inbound mapping tables (each row flagged **built** / planned-deferred / other-surface against live `internal/turnevent`), the **seven numbered divergences** (ADR 026's "divergence 6" reference now resolves here — 027 is that list's home; divergence 7, #1401, is the three `pyry/background_task_*` extension discriminants), the self-contained ACP taxonomy reference, the hard cost invariant (interactive `claude`, never `claude -p`/Agent SDK), and three open items recorded as epic #600 decisions not blockers; the implementation contract T2–T10 build to, superseding the vault. Rationale: ACP is one thin adapter (like the mobile wire) over the neutral daemon-owned model, containing Zed-cadence spec churn to the adapter |
| 028 | [028-ed25519-checksums-signature.md](decisions/028-ed25519-checksums-signature.md) | `pyry update` verifies a detached **raw 64-byte Ed25519 signature** over `checksums.txt` (published as `checksums.txt.sig`) against a public key **compiled into the binary** (`releaseSigningPublicKeyHex`), gating *before* any digest is parsed — so a compromised release or stolen publishing token can't install attacker-chosen bytes (#776). Raw signature over the minisign envelope (we own both ends; no algorithm field ⇒ **no downgrade vector**; minisign's default `ED` mode would force a BLAKE2b prehash dep); sign the checksums *manifest* not the tarball (transitively binds every listed asset); `crypto/ed25519.Verify` is pure stdlib (zero new deps); `openssl pkeyutl -sign -rawin` in CI emits the byte-identical raw sig `ed25519.Sign` produces (deterministic RFC 8032), so Go fixtures signed with `ed25519.Sign` exercise the exact production verify path. Fail-closed is structural (unconditional sig-fetch + verify between checksums-fetch and parse; 404 aborts like a missing tarball, no unsigned fallback); private key = `PYRY_RELEASE_SIGNING_KEY` Actions secret, distinct from + tighter than the publishing token |
| 029 | [029-devices-registry-reload-at-handshake.md](decisions/029-devices-registry-reload-at-handshake.md) | The daemon reloads `devices.json` at each v2 handshake (`(*Registry).Reload`) so a device paired via `pyry pair` after startup authenticates without a restart (#782) — mechanism (a) read-through reload, self-contained in the daemon, over (b) signalling a running daemon (which would couple daemon-independent pairing to a running service); reconcile is a **read-modify-write keyed on `TokenHash`** (keep-in-memory-survivor for the daemon-only `LastSeenAt`/push-registration fields; adopt disk-only = newly paired; drop memory-only = revoked), **not a blind swap** — membership == disk's exact set so the accept set is never widened, malformed/unreadable disk fails closed (error, in-memory set unchanged); applied at **both** the read (handshake, before `Validate`) **and** write (`register_push_token`, before `Save`) sites because reload-at-read alone trades the restart bug for a data-loss bug (a stale-memory `Save` erases a device `pyry pair` just added); new reload-fail log sites carry `path`+static reason only, never the wrapped `err` (a corrupt file's `json.Unmarshal` error can echo a `token_hash`); sibling to [ADR 020](decisions/020-devices-registry-snapshot-then-write.md) (same lock discipline: I/O off `r.mu`, reconcile-and-assign under it); **corrected 2026-08-19 (#1530)** — the ADR's "next reload reconciles it" consequence and its flock-rejection rationale were wrong: `Reload` reconciles memory *from* disk, so a `Save` that races out a `pyry pair` write erases it from disk permanently, and the window is wide (not negligible) on `pyry pair`'s side; #1530 ships `internal/devices.WithLock`, the primitive this ADR had rejected, though no caller is rewired to use it yet |
| 030 | [030-plain-bool-failsafe-persisted-flag.md](decisions/030-plain-bool-failsafe-persisted-flag.md) | Persisted `YOLO bool` needs no `*bool`/custom decoder — Go's zero value + the registry's existing whole-parse-strict `json.Unmarshal` are already the entire fail-safe argument (#833) |
| 031 | [031-settings-restart-fire-and-forget.md](decisions/031-settings-restart-fire-and-forget.md) | Live-restart on a settings change (`Supervisor.Restart`) is fire-and-forget and non-blocking, modeled as an induced child-exit on the existing crash-recovery loop rather than a synchronous wait or a dedicated supervisor-rebuild path (#842) |
| 032 | [032-bootstrap-resume-per-spawn-existence-probe.md](decisions/032-bootstrap-resume-per-spawn-existence-probe.md) | Bootstrap **resumes** (never rotates) its pinned id when claude 2.1.199 refuses `--session-id` for a transcript that already exists (a hard daemon restart survives it) — `ResolveSessionID` widened to `func() (id, resume bool)`, decided **per spawn** by a by-id `transcript.StatByID` existence probe (no dir scan), over a `firstRun` heuristic or a fresh-id rotate; preserves the pinned bootstrap's idle conversation across restart; #1165's N-fast-crash rotate net stays independent, different-fabric backstop for the residual unresumable-transcript case (#1164) |
| 033 | [033-supervisor-self-heal-dedicated-rotation-no-skip-set.md](decisions/033-supervisor-self-heal-dedicated-rotation-no-skip-set.md) | Supervisor's N-fast-crash self-heal (#1165) mints via a dedicated `Pool.RotateBootstrapForSelfHeal`, not `RotateForNewSession` — shares the `rekeyLocked`/`saveLocked` seam but deliberately skips the allocated-UUID skip-set entry (the rekey-before-spawn ordering already makes the rotation watcher's `ref.ID==stem` guard a no-op, cold-start parity) and fires no `ReasonClear` client transition (a self-heal isn't a user `/clear`) |
| 034 | [034-two-phase-eviction-commit-signal-before-teardown.md](decisions/034-two-phase-eviction-commit-signal-before-teardown.md) | Eviction now commits `stateEvicted` (signal + flip via `beginEvict`) BEFORE child teardown and persists+closes (`endEvict`) after — fixes a racing `Activate` (e.g. a `send_message`) no-op'ing against a still-`stateActive` session mid-teardown and silently dropping the delivery (#1186); partially supersedes [013](decisions/013-evict-activate-persist-ordering.md) for the evicted direction only |
| 035 | [035-lazy-revive-not-registry-rehydrate.md](decisions/035-lazy-revive-not-registry-rehydrate.md) | A conversation bound to a minted session revives lazily at first post-restart touch (`sessionRouter.resolve` catching `Pool.Lookup`'s `ErrSessionNotFound` → new non-spawning `Pool.Revive`, sharing `GetOrCreateIn`'s register/persist/schedule core via extracted `materialise`) sourced from the **conversations** registry's `Cwd`, re-validated through the same `resolveSpawnDir` the mint path uses — over rehydrating `sessions.json` at `Pool.New`, which would need a new `registryEntry` cwd field since a minted session's spawn dir is not persisted on the sessions side (#1487); revived sessions carry zero `SessionSettings`, so a phone-persisted YOLO does not survive the restart (extends [ADR 030](decisions/030-plain-bool-failsafe-persisted-flag.md)'s fail-safe rationale) |
| 036 | [036-aggregate-cap-product-is-not-a-ceiling.md](decisions/036-aggregate-cap-product-is-not-a-ceiling.md) | An aggregate cardinality cap's product (`maxTaskRosterEntries`' 8192 bytes, noticed to land on half of `maxUnrecognizedRaw`) is a result of that variant's own doctrine, not a ceiling the next aggregate variant must inherit — `maxModelListEntries` had treated it as one until #1821's third size dimension made the inherited number impossible to satisfy (9600 > 8192); each variant now re-derives its own fraction of `maxUnrecognizedRaw` (roster 1/2, model list 5/8) |
| 037 | [037-capability-strings-not-version-numbers.md](decisions/037-capability-strings-not-version-numbers.md) | A user-facing, cross-repo v2 wire feature adds its own capability string to `supportedV2Capabilities` (e.g. `CapabilityQuestion`) rather than the daemon exposing an orderable version number — the intersection-based negotiation makes a client's advertised string a positive, checkable stale-daemon signal; the string is detection only and grants no access by itself (#2020, motivated by pyrycode-desktop#928 losing a day to an undetectable stale daemon) |

## Features

- [Development verification](features/development-verification.md): shared source-reading, protocol, capture and test-evidence practices.

A package overview is capped at 50000 bytes. Past that it is split into
sibling documents named `<package>-<section>.md`, and the overview keeps its
own path and becomes a map linking to them. Only the overview is listed here;
open it for the section documents. The cap exists because search cuts a
document into roughly 900-token chunks and can only prefer a heading boundary
when one falls near the cut. Sections much larger than a chunk get cut at
paragraph breaks instead, and the document stops being retrievable.

| File | Topic |
|------|-------|
| [session-transcript-and-resume-probe.md](features/session-transcript-and-resume-probe.md) | #1655 live measurement (claude 2.1.220): a `--session-id <id>` launch that runs no turn leaves no `<id>.jsonl` on disk, both while the child is alive and after a graceful `SIGTERM` exit (**HOLDS**) — the premise [ADR 032](decisions/032-bootstrap-resume-per-spawn-existence-probe.md) and #1630's respawn-time existence probe rest on; #1630 consumes the after-exit reading. #1656 extends it: `--resume <id>` against that absent transcript exits non-zero while the identical `--resume` against an id whose transcript exists does not (**HOLDS**), confirming the mechanism the suspected respawn crash-loop needs. |
| [sessions-package.md](features/sessions-package.md) | `internal/sessions` — `SessionID`, `Session`, `Pool`; supervisor wrapper for multi-session readiness; `Pool.Run` errgroup also hosts the conversations auto-archive sweep goroutine when `Config.ConversationsRegistry` is non-nil (#243); `Config.SweepInterval` overrides the sweep cadence for testing (zero = `conversations.SweepInterval`, #262); `Config.BootstrapEvicted` parks the bootstrap evicted (no eager claude) + `Pool.Ready()` gates the first-`Create` startup race, for embedded pool hosts (#761, ADR 026); `SessionSettings{Model,Effort,YOLO}` + `claudeSettingsArgs` persist per-session claude spawn settings and apply them at both spawn sites, byte-identical argv at the zero value (#833, security-sensitive, ADR 030); `Pool.UpdateSettings` + `SettingsUpdate{*string,*string,*bool}` make those settings mutable post-creation — `Rename`-shaped partial merge under `Pool.mu`, YOLO fail-safe-OFF via nil-check (#840, security-sensitive), and on a real change triggers a live-restart of the session's supervisor via `Session.spawnBase`/`spawnArgs` + `Supervisor.Restart` so the change takes effect on the *currently running* child without waiting for its next spawn (#842, security-sensitive, ADR 031); `Pool.DefaultSettings() (SessionSettings, bool)` mirrors `Default()`'s lock discipline (RLock, fresh-resolve `p.bootstrap` so it stays rotation-safe) to expose those persisted settings across the package boundary — the read counterpart to `UpdateSettings`, existence bool for the no-bootstrap fallback, wired by #848 into the `screen_snapshot` reply's `model`/`effort`/`yolo` fields via an optional `SnapshotSettings` closure on `internal/relay`'s `V2SessionConfig` (#847, not security-sensitive; see [v2-session-manager.md](v2-session-manager.md)); `Pool.BootstrapID() SessionID` (same RLock-fresh-resolve shape) backs `supervisor.Config.ResolveSessionID` so the bootstrap always spawns with its own deterministic `--session-id`, never `--continue`, resolved fresh each spawn so a `/clear` rotation (`Pool.RotateID`) is picked up on the next restart with no extra wiring — closes a shared-sessions-dir confused-deputy restart-resume gap and retires the startup adopt-by-mtime scan (#839, security-sensitive; see [jsonl-reconciliation.md](features/jsonl-reconciliation.md)); `sess.id` is a two-lock field — `RotateID` writes it under both `Pool.mu` (W) and `Session.lcMu` (reusing the `lcMu` section already held for `lastActiveAt`), a read is race-clean under either, and lifecycle-goroutine readers (the eviction-transition notify, the idle-eviction warn log, `Session.ID()`) go through a new `currentID()` helper — fixes a live `/clear`-rotation-vs-lifecycle-goroutine data race on the id string header that the #839 live-watcher wiring made possible; not security-sensitive (id lock-hygiene, not a trust surface); a pre-existing, nil-impact, capMu-only id read in `Pool.Activate`'s LRU path is a known non-blocking gap (#866; see [codebase/866.md](codebase/866.md)); `writeMCPSettings(registryPath, id) (string, error)` (new `settings.go`) + `Session.settingsPath` write a per-session `--settings <path>` file carrying `{"enableAllProjectMcpServers":true}` and join it into `spawnBase` (not `claudeSettingsArgs`), so it survives every recompose (backoff restart, #842 live restart) — fixes claude 2.1.199's "N new MCP servers found" startup modal wedging every interactive spawn's PTY readiness check; mirrors the un-gated agent-run compat fix (`d10ce87`) without importing its deny-default posture (deliberately not a reuse of `internal/agentrun/settings`); write failure at construction is a hard error; not security-sensitive at introduction; also touches `cmd/pyry`'s ACP-embedded-pool argv-equality tests since they spawn through the same `buildSession` (#943, see [codebase/943.md](codebase/943.md)); relocated (#1518, security-sensitive: id-to-path traversal gate) off `os.TempDir()` to `<dataDir>/session-settings/<id>.json` when a registry path is configured (unchanged `os.TempDir()` behaviour otherwise) — id-gated via `ValidID`, written atomically (scratch file, fsync, rename, matching `saveRegistryLocked`), name derived from the session id so restarts overwrite rather than accumulate (bounds on-disk count by session, not restart, count); a multi-day daemon could otherwise have the temp copy reaped out from under a live session, wedging the #943 modal or backoff-looping the child; both write sites plus `CreateIn`'s `saveLocked` rollback now remove the file on every construction error return (a `defer`-and-success-flag, no longer merely best-effort) since the data dir has no reaper to bound an orphan the way `os.TempDir()` used to — `materialise`'s discard branches deliberately don't, since the race loser's path is the live winner's; see [codebase/1518.md](codebase/1518.md); `Runner` interface (`State`/`WriteUserTurn`/`WaitForPTY`/`Run`/`Restart`) + `Config.RunnerFactory` is the injection seam retyping `Session.sup` off the concrete supervisor; not security-sensitive, no consumer migrated yet, unblocks the Streamrunner Interactive T4/T7 work (#1077, see [codebase/1077.md](codebase/1077.md)) — **superseded**: #1348 deleted `internal/supervisor` entirely, so `RunnerFactory` is now mandatory (nil is a construction error, no PTY default to fall back to) and there is exactly one production `Runner` (`cmd/pyry`'s `streamRunner` adapter over `*streamsup.Runner`); `SetSpawnArgs(args []string)` (#1580) widens the interface with a swap-only argv installer (`Restart`'s install half without the kill), and the doc corrections + capability-type-assertion dispatch mechanism (`Session.Runner()` + an anonymous method-set assertion, not a `Session.Supervisor()` accessor) are pinned in [codebase/1580.md](codebase/1580.md); `Config.SessionID string` is an additive, eager id field set unconditionally at both construction sites (`Pool.New`'s `bootstrapID` local, `Pool.buildSession`'s `id` param) so a future stream-json `RunnerFactory` can read a non-empty session id at construction — `streamsup.New` requires it eagerly and has no lazy-resolve path; the PTY supervisor structurally cannot read it (`buildClaudeArgs` takes no such param), so it stays inert on that path (rollback guarantee) and keeps rotating solely via `ResolveSessionID`; construction-fixed and does NOT mirror a `/clear` rotation on the stream-json path, accepted-and-documented since `streamsup.New` itself has no rotation mechanism (#1108, not security-sensitive, see [codebase/1108.md](codebase/1108.md)); `Pool.RotateForNewSession(oldID) (SessionID, error)` (#1125, security-sensitive) is the daemon-driven twin of `AdoptAnnouncedID` — mints a fresh id, re-keys under `Pool.mu` via a shared `rekeyLocked` helper extracted byte-identically from `RotateID`, then fires the same off-lock `notifyTransition(ReasonClear)` (it used to also register the new id in a freshly-allocated skip-set before the caller spawned `--session-id <newID>`, the load-bearing asymmetry against the rotation watcher's CREATE handler; #2137 retired the watcher and deleted the skip-set) — see [codebase/1125.md](../codebase/1125.md); `newProbePreferredTranscriptResolver` / `newTranscriptResolver` in `reconcile.go` (#838 delivery-confirm baseline) are rewritten as thin adapters over `internal/transcript` (#1149, security-sensitive) — the local `jsonlExt`/`uuidStemPattern`/`mostRecentJSONL` are deleted, the `("", 0, nil)` no-baseline convention and AC4 guard preserved verbatim behind `transcript.Newest`/`CanonicalDir`/`StatByID`/`Probed`, no signature change — see [codebase/1149.md](codebase/1149.md) and [transcript-package.md](features/transcript-package.md); `ResolveSessionID` widens again to `func() (id string, resume bool)` (#1164, not security-sensitive) — claude 2.1.199 refuses `--session-id` for a transcript that already exists (a hard daemon restart survives `<bootstrapID>.jsonl`), so the bootstrap closure now probes `transcript.StatByID` fresh each spawn and emits `--resume <id>` (reattach, preserves the idle conversation) instead of crash-looping on `--session-id`; absent transcript keeps #839's create path byte-identical; decided per-spawn (not `firstRun`-gated) so cold start / in-process respawn / daemon restart share one rule — see [codebase/1164.md](codebase/1164.md) and [ADR 032](decisions/032-bootstrap-resume-per-spawn-existence-probe.md); `Pool.Revive(id, label, spawnDir) (*Session, error)` (#1487, security-sensitive) re-materialises a conversation's dropped session back into the pool **without spawning** — no `ctx` param is the API signal for that — sharing `GetOrCreateIn`'s register/persist/schedule core via a newly-extracted `materialise` helper (`took bool` replaces what used to be an inline early return); zero `SessionSettings` on the revive path is deliberate fail-closed (a phone-persisted YOLO does not survive a restart); see [codebase/1487.md](codebase/1487.md) and [ADR 035](decisions/035-lazy-revive-not-registry-rehydrate.md); `Pool.UpdateSettings`'s live-apply is now partitioned (#1581, security-sensitive): a model/effort-only change (`inBandDeliverable`) is delivered in-band as `/model`/`/effort` commands via `WriteUserTurn` on the child's already-open stdin (no kill), while a present YOLO or a clear-to-default keeps #842's restart — both branches install the recomposed argv via `SetSpawnArgs` first (#1580), so a failed in-band write still reaches the next spawn; see [codebase/1581.md](codebase/1581.md); `Pool.SettingsFor(id) (SessionSettings, error)` (#1585, security-sensitive, split from #1577) is the missing per-session read half of `UpdateSettings` — mirrors its lookup-and-refuse shape (bare `ErrSessionNotFound`, no empty-id-to-bootstrap fallback, unlike `Lookup`) rather than `DefaultSettings`'s existence-bool, one `p.mu.RLock()` per call, deliberately no delegation to/from `DefaultSettings` (reentrant `RLock` self-deadlock hazard); shipped unwired on purpose, no consumer yet; see [codebase/1585.md](codebase/1585.md); `Pool.UpdateSettings`'s bypass posture now splits on the *direction* of the change, not merely its presence (#1604, security-sensitive): a YOLO revoke (`true→false`) joins the in-band branch as a `set_permission_mode` control request via the widened `Runner.RevokeBypass() error` (#1603's writer), applying to the running child with no respawn, while a YOLO enable (`false→true`) keeps #842's restart because claude gates the escalation on the launch argv and refuses the control request (#1595); `Restart` therefore keeps a live production caller and #1574 may not delete it; see [codebase/1604.md](codebase/1604.md) |

| [rotation-watcher.md](features/rotation-watcher.md) | **Retired #2137.** Historical note only: live `/clear` detection via fsnotify + per-PID FD probe (Linux `/proc/<pid>/fd`, macOS `lsof`) drove `Pool.RotateID` by guessing which session rotated; claude's own `conversation_reset` announcement (#2134-2136) answers the same question directly and replaced it — see [`streamsup-package-announced-reset-follower.md`](features/streamsup-package-announced-reset-follower.md) and [`sessions-package-key-types-adoptannouncedid.md`](features/sessions-package-key-types-adoptannouncedid.md) for the live mechanism |
| [control-plane.md](features/control-plane.md) | `internal/control` — Unix-socket JSON server, `SessionResolver` seam, one-shot-per-conn verb dispatch (no verb hands off connection ownership since #1535 deleted `VerbAttach`/`VerbResize`/`AttachPayload`/`ResizePayload`/`control.SendResize`, the wire surface #1348's server-side attach-handler deletion had already orphaned); § "Rekey: V2 conn re-key trigger seam" (#459 + #462 + #463) covers the `Rekeyer` interface, `SetRekeyer` post-construction install pattern, `VerbRekey` wire shape, `ErrCodeConnNotFound` mapping, `control.Rekey` client helper, and the `pyry rekey <conn_id>` operator-facing consumer's three-way error routing (typed sentinel + known untyped server rejects → `os.Exit(1)` clean `pyry rekey:` prefix; transport → wrapped-return → main's outer `pyry: ` prefix); § "Lifecycle" (#863) covers the three-layer shutdown-abort — `Server.Close` closes tracked streaming conns (read-parked pump), `Bridge.Shutdown()` closes a terminal `shutdownCh` the input pump's buffered send selects on (send-parked pump), `Session.Run` defers the latter — that fixes daemon shutdown hanging on an attached client (see [codebase/863.md](codebase/863.md)); § "Handshake Deadline: per-conn timeout and the session-verb extend" (#865) covers the per-conn `s.handshakeTimeout` field (defaults to `defaultHandshakeTimeout`=5s, injectable in same-package tests), `handleApprove`'s clear-for-the-wait vs. `handleSessionsNew`/`handleSessionsRm`'s **extend**-to-`sessionOpTimeout+sessionOpConnGrace` (35s) before their long `Pool.Create`/`Remove` call — fixes `sessions.new`/`sessions.rm` silently EOF-ing a client after 5s despite the mutation succeeding (see [codebase/865.md](codebase/865.md); `handleAttach` was the other clearer until #1535 deleted it along with the rest of the orphaned attach/resize wire surface — see [control-plane.md](features/control-plane.md)); § "Approve: mcp.approve verb — forward to permbridge, block, default-deny" (#1104) covers `VerbMCPApprove`, the `SetApprovalRegistry`/`Approver` setter seam (mirrors `SetRekeyer`), `ApprovePayload`/`ApproveResult` (snake_case tags matching the T1 approval-contract spike; `ApproveResult` byte-identical to `permbridge.Verdict`'s wire shape), `handleApprove`'s blocking-verb shape (clears the handshake deadline, blocks on `permbridge.Pending.Await`, guaranteed to return within `mcpApprovalTimeout`), the `watchApproveConn` disconnect/shutdown watcher that maps caller-disconnect and `s.closedCh` into a fail-closed `Resolve(id, Deny(...))`, and the shared `*permbridge.Registry` instance created once in `cmd/pyry`'s `runSupervisor` and installed via the setter so #1080's modal-resolve wiring threads the same instance (see [codebase/1104.md](codebase/1104.md)); § "Approve: ..." also now covers the **landed** `SetApprovalSurfacer` seam (#1080) — `handleApprove` calls the optional `surface(permbridge.Request) func()` right after `Register` and defers the returned `retire` for guaranteed cleanup + client-modal dismissal on every terminal `Await` path, production-implemented by `cmd/pyry`'s `streamApprovalBridge.Surface` (see [v2-session-manager.md](features/v2-session-manager.md) § Stream-json approval bridge and [codebase/1080.md](codebase/1080.md)); § "Attachment.file: file a claude-named host file under the calling session's conversation" (#2164) covers `VerbAttachFile`, the `SetFileAttacher` post-construction seam (plain-func shape like `SetApprovalSurfacer`, not a typed registry — the destination lookup needs `cmd/pyry` composition-root state), and the confinement recipe for a model-chosen path: `agentrun.ResolveWorkdir` canonicalises both the conversation's `Cwd` root and the target before a `withinDir` boundary test, `os.SameFile` closes the check-then-open TOCTOU window, and every refusal is a content-free static sentence; #2168 shipped the MCP tool that calls it (`pyry mcp-files`, see [pyry-mcp-files-command.md](features/pyry-mcp-files-command.md)), and #2169 registered that tool on the interactive spawn and threaded the calling session's id onto each child's environment, composed per-spawn rather than at construction so it survives a `RestartFresh` rotation (a `/clear` rotation, which never respawns, is the one case it still can't track) (see [control-plane-attachment-file-confine-and-store-a-claude-named-path.md](features/control-plane-attachment-file-confine-and-store-a-claude-named-path.md)) |
| [idle-eviction.md](features/idle-eviction.md) | Per-session active↔evicted state machine; idle timer + concurrent-active-cap (LRU) eviction triggers; `Activate` / `Evict` primitives; eviction commits `stateEvicted` (signal+flip via `beginEvict`) BEFORE child teardown, persists+closes (`endEvict`) after — fixes an `Activate` racing the teardown window from silently no-op'ing against a dying child (#1186, ADR 034) |
| [e2e-harness.md](features/e2e-harness.md) | `internal/e2e` (build tag `e2e` or `e2e_install`) — `Harness` + `Start(t)` / `StartIn(t, home, flags...)` spawn pyry in temp / caller-supplied HOME with optional variadic flags (last-wins), `cap_test.go` (#116) drives cap-eviction LRU + cap/idle interleave via `sessions.new` over the control socket against a tiny shell-script claude stand-in (`Pool.Create` appends `--session-id` to ClaudeArgs, breaking sleep), `StartExpectingFailureIn(t, home)` for failed-start assertions, `Harness.Run(t, verb, args...)` auto-injects socket, `RunBare(t, args...)` drives daemon-free verbs, `Harness.Stop(t)` graceful mid-test teardown (idempotent with `t.Cleanup`); sibling `AttachHarness` + `StartAttach(t, sessionID)` (#125) wraps `pyry attach` in a `creack/pty` master/slave pair with `TestHelperProcess`-echo (via the `echoClaudeScript` argv-dropping shell wrapper as of #257 — see below) as supervised claude for interactive PTY round-trip tests; sibling `StdioAttachClient` + `startStdioAttach(t, label)` (#161) wraps `pyry attach --stdio` in plain `os.Pipe()` ends (no PTY anywhere on the client side — the SDK consumer shape) over the same helper-as-echo daemon, with `Write` / `ReadUntil(needle, deadline)` / `Close(t) int` and clean-detach via `inputW.Close()` → child exit 0; the proof-of-life `TestE2E_AttachStdio_BytesRoundTrip` was `t.Skip`'d pending #167 (`pyry attach --stdio` rejected by `parseClientFlags`), then skipped on #257 since #167's fix exposed a separate `--session-id`-vs-test-binary harness bug, then unskipped and passing once #257 landed (migrated `spawnAttachableDaemon` onto the `echoClaudeScript` / `writeEchoClaude` wrapper `auto_attach.go` (#163) already used, dropping `Pool.Create`'s appended `--session-id <uuid>` before it reaches the test binary's flag parser; also guarded `StdioAttachClient.Stderr` with `safeBuffer` against the os/exec stderr-copy race — see [codebase/257.md](codebase/257.md)); sibling `TestE2E_AttachStdio_NoPTYInProcessTree` (#162, unskipped by #257 alongside the byte-flow test — its stale `#167` gate had long since landed) inspects the attach child's open fds via `/proc/<pid>/fd/` (linux) / `lsof -p <pid> -Fn` (darwin) and asserts no `/dev/ptmx` / `/dev/pts/*` / `/dev/ttys*` / `/dev/tty` is held — binary-level defence against a refactor that pulls a PTY into `cmd/pyry/runAttach`'s `--stdio` branch; sibling `ForegroundAutoAttachClient` + `startForegroundAutoAttach(t, label)` (#163) drives `pyry --session-id <uuid> …` (no `attach` verb) over plain `os.Pipe()`s, exercising the 1.3c-2 foreground-binary auto-attach path through `runSupervisor` → `tryAutoAttach` → `control.AttachStdio` (bypassed both #167 and #257's bug from the start — calls `AttachStdio` in-process, no `parseClientFlags`, no `Pool.Create` argv-append against the test binary since the shell-wrapper claude absorbs `--session-id`); pairs the byte round-trip with a `pgrepChildren(pid)` (`pgrep -P`, identical surface on linux + darwin) zero-direct-children assertion that catches a hypothetical bug wiring auto-attach correctly *and* spawning a competing supervisor; uses an `echo-claude.sh` shell wrapper as supervised claude to absorb `Pool.Create`'s `--session-id` append (Go test binary's `flag.Parse()` would otherwise reject it); `safeBuffer` (mutex-wrapped `bytes.Buffer`) for `cmd.Stderr` to dodge the os/exec stderr-copy goroutine race; sibling `ForegroundSupervisedClient` + `startForegroundSupervised(t, sessionID, extraEnv)` (#164) is the inverse-direction peer that pins `tryAutoAttach`'s three fall-through arms (no matching session, no daemon, `PYRY_NO_AUTO_ATTACH=1`) — foreground gets its own socket path distinct from any daemon, structurally falls through via stat ENOENT, and `pgrepChildren ≥ 1` + `SessionsList` snapshot equality assert "supervisor mode reached, daemon registry untouched"; gate-discrimination stays at unit tier (six tests in `cmd/pyry/auto_attach_test.go`), e2e tier pins system-level fall-through-reaches-supervisor invariant; `spawnDaemonWithRegisteredSession` wraps `spawnAutoAttachDaemon` + `SessionsNew` for the "daemon present" tests; sibling `StartRotation(t, home, sessionsDir, initialUUID, trigger)` (#123) wires the fake-claude binary as the supervised child via `spawnWith(spawnOpts)` core + three `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_*` env vars for rotation-watcher e2e; `AttachHarness.WaitDetach` + `AttachHarness.Run` (#127, sharing `runVerb` with `Harness.Run`) drive the documented `Ctrl-B d` detach + post-detach `pyry status` for the triple-invariant clean-detach proof; covers `stop`/`logs`/`version`/`status` (running + stopped), three restart-survival proofs (active, evicted-state, `lastActiveAt`), corrupt-registry fail-loud, missing-`~/.claude/projects/` clean-startup, idle-eviction + lazy-respawn (raw `VerbAttach` over the control socket), bootstrap warm-start ignores persisted `lifecycle_state` (#253, two-phase mutate-and-restart pinning ADR 016's carve-out plus the non-bootstrap-evicted boundary; negative-greps the v0.10.1 zero-time + max-int64 uptime sentinels), conversations sweep loop ticks + clean SIGTERM shutdown (#263, `conv_sweep_test.go` seeds promoted+unpromoted 60-day-idle entries, drives the daemon at `-pyry-conv-sweep-interval=100ms`, polls `conversations.Load` for the unpromoted removal under a 5s budget, then asserts `processAlive(pid) == false` + panic/`runtime/`/`goroutine ` substring scan over stderr after `h.Stop` — closes the daemon-wiring gap that the in-package `pool_conv_sweep_test.go` cannot reach: a regression in `cmd/pyry/main.go`'s `sessions.Config` construction silently sets `p.convReg == nil` and the in-package test still passes), attach round-trip bytes, attach clean-detach, attach-survives-claude-restart (the last surfaced + fixed the bridge input-pump leak — see [ADR 007](decisions/007-bridge-iteration-boundaries.md)), and attach SIGWINCH propagation (#126, e2e cover for the #133/#137/#136 live-resize chain via a `pty.Setsize`-on-master + `winsize rows=N cols=M` helper marker) at the binary boundary; SIGTERM/SIGKILL teardown, leak verification via re-exec; the default zero-value spawn stand-in (`Start`/`StartIn`/`StartInWithEnv`, `spawnWith`'s `spawnOpts` zero value) switched from a bare `/bin/sleep` to the same argv-ignoring `sleep-claude.sh` wrapper `cap_test.go` (#116) already used for `Pool.Create`'s new-session path (#918) — #839 started appending `--session-id <uuid>` to every bootstrap spawn too, which crash-looped a bare `/bin/sleep` under backoff; `writeSleepClaude`/`sleepClaudeScript` relocated verbatim from `cap_test.go` into `harness.go` so the shared spawn core can reach it without breaking the `e2e_install` build |
| [fakeclaude-binary.md](features/fakeclaude-binary.md) | `internal/e2e/internal/fakeclaude` — test-only `package main` that opens a `<uuid>.jsonl` and rotates it on a triggerable signal (rotation-watcher e2e primitive); optional modes layer on top — `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_STDIN_LOG` (#323) appends stdin to a file, `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_ASSISTANT_TRIGGER` (#311) scripts an assistant chunk on stdout (coarse path), `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_JSONL_TRIGGER` (#642) appends captured claude-format turn events to the live session JSONL the structured producer tails (structured path — fed the live two-phone structured-receive capstone; needs sessions-dir alignment + a pre-created initial JSONL, see [codebase/642.md](codebase/642.md); a zero-byte read of the trigger (the producer's `O_TRUNC`-before-`write` window) now skips-without-removing instead of unlinking the fixture, closing a full-suite `-race` structured-stream flake — see [codebase/958.md](codebase/958.md)), `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_IDLE_TRIGGER` (#792) inverts TUI's startup posture — claude comes up **busy** (no startup `❯`, never the spinner, so `WaitReady` blocks) and frees only when the trigger appears (emit `❯` once, then stays idle so a queued backlog drains back-to-back) — the controllable busy→free window that fed the live queue-drain capstone `/bin/sleep` couldn't (never idle → never drains); mutually exclusive with TUI, relies on `STDIN_LOG` for the per-turn growth-commit, see [codebase/792.md](codebase/792.md), `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_ESC_ENDS_TURN` (#794) enters raw mode and watches stdin for the remote interrupt's **bare ESC** (a `0x1b` not followed by `0x5b` — unambiguous vs the `ESC[200~`/`ESC[201~` paste markers) and, on the first one, appends the interruption entry so the Esc *causes* the turn to stop — the harness piece that made the live remote-interrupt capstone confirm causally (not vacuously via #792's file-driven flip); coexists with TUI, single-writer-of-`f` preserved via `escPending`, see [codebase/794.md](codebase/794.md) (through #1244 the appended line was a canned assistant `end_turn` line, provably causal but not reason-bearing; #1244 restaged it as claude's own `type:"user"` interruption marker — byte-shaped after the repo's one recorded capture — so the mapper's #1243 arm fires and the minted-tier oracle asserts `turn_end{cancelled}` on the wire, see [codebase/1244.md](codebase/1244.md)), `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_MODAL_CLEAR_ON_ANSWER` (#793) **extends** modal mode (`PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_MODAL_TRIGGER`, #791 — the permission-prompt raiser): after the modal is shown it clears it on the first post-modal stdin byte (the local `pyry attach` head's answer keystroke) by scrolling the `"Do you want to proceed?"` anchor out of the bottom-12 `DetectModalClass` window (16 `\r\n` + a load-bearing trailing `❯` — `trimGridText` drops trailing empty rows) so tui-driver fires `EventKindPtyModalHidden` and the daemon's #706 **local** first-answer-wins arm resolves it and broadcasts `modal_dismissed{local}` — the harness piece behind the live two-head capstone; a **new** default-off env (not a `MODAL_TRIGGER` change, else #791's remote arm would race), byte-identical when unset, single-writer preserved via `clearPending`, see [codebase/793.md](codebase/793.md), and `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_TUI` (#603) emits the idle `❯` (U+276F) / thinking `✻` (U+273B) glyphs so tui-driver `IsIdle`/`IsThinking` and #594's `WaitReady→DeliverPrompt→commit` contract can confirm a turn against it (the file is on the `cmd/substrate-guard` allowlist for carrying the two glyphs, mirroring the `ptyrunner/helper_test.go` exemption; consumers drain the spinner `message` envelope that races the ack — see [codebase/603.md](codebase/603.md)), and **on-turn transcript growth** (#673): whenever the stdin reader is active (TUI/`STDIN_LOG`), a delivered turn grows the live session JSONL by one inert `{}\n` line so #668's transcript-growth commit-confirm (`confirmViaTranscriptGrowth`) observes growth and acks instead of timing out with `ErrTurnNotCommitted` — a cross-goroutine `turnPending atomic.Bool` keeps the actual `f` write on the main goroutine (signal-only across the boundary), preserving the single-writer-of-`f` invariant; the five turn-driving tests also align `sessionsDir` to the daemon's computed `<HOME>/.claude/projects/encode(workdir)` since the resolver has no env override (see [codebase/673.md](codebase/673.md)), and `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_CLEAR_ROTATES` (#1004) watches stdin for the phone-driven `new_session` verb's `/clear` keystroke (`supervisor.StartNewSession`'s `ClearInputLine`+`TypePrompt`) and, on the first match, performs the same close-old/open-new JSONL rotation the file trigger does — the harness piece proving the `new_session` v2 control verb's on-disk rotation is *caused by* the frame (the file trigger points at a never-created path so `/clear` is the only rotation source); shares the `rotated` one-shot gate with the file trigger, no raw mode needed (the trailing `\r` commits the canonical line), byte-identical when unset — see [codebase/1004.md](codebase/1004.md), and `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_STREAM_JSON` (#1140) skips the PTY/TUI surface entirely (checked FIRST in `main()`, above every `mustEnv` call — structurally mutex with every mode above and binds no sessions dir/transcript) and instead hand-mirrors the daemon's `internal/streamsup` wire: reads `{"type":"user",…}` envelopes on stdin and, per turn, emits one `assistant` line (echoing the prompt) + one `result{subtype:"success"}` line on stdout, byte-compatible with what `streamsup.Parser` maps to `TextChunk`/`TurnEnd` — the fake the stream `interactive_runner` path (#1081) needs to run without a real `claude` binary; feeds the sibling #1135 harness + `send_message` e2e spec, see [codebase/1140.md](codebase/1140.md); and `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_STREAM_INTERRUPT` (#1136, default-off, a rider on `STREAM_JSON`) withholds the per-turn `result` on a user turn (turn stays in flight) and answers an interrupt `control_request` with `result{subtype:"error_during_execution"}` — which `streamsup.Parser` maps to `TurnEndReasonCancelled` — the fake behaviour the live per-conversation interrupt e2e needs (a minted, non-bootstrap conversation whose `turn_end{StopReason:"cancelled"}` is both the interrupt proof and the #1121 routing-target confirmation), see [features/fakeclaude-binary.md § Interrupt mode](features/fakeclaude-binary-stream-json-mode.md#interrupt-mode-honorinterrupt-1136) and [codebase/1136.md](codebase/1136.md); and, on the stream-json branch, `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_STDIN_LOG` (#1137, extending the PTY-only var above) tees the stream child's stdin via `io.TeeReader` at the `main()` call site (`runStreamJSON` signature untouched) — the runtime oracle proving a stream `new_session` never types `/clear` (it's a `RestartFresh` re-spawn), see [features/fakeclaude-binary.md § Stream-path stdin tee](features/fakeclaude-binary.md#stream-path-stdin-tee-pyry_fake_claude_stdin_log-1137) and [codebase/1137.md](codebase/1137.md); and `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_STREAM_HOLD` (#1138, default-off) parks the stream child BEFORE it consumes any stdin until a trigger file appears (`waitForTriggerFile`, an `os.Stat` poll at the `main()` call site above `runStreamJSON`, signature untouched) — the stream-path analog of idle-trigger mode (#792): the daemon's queued turns buffer in the child's stdin pipe during the hold (`streamsup.WriteTurn` returns on write, not commit, so a msgqueue backlog isn't otherwise observable here) and drain FIFO on release, one turn cycle per turn, in submission order — the fake behaviour the live stream queue-drain-in-order e2e needs, see [features/fakeclaude-binary.md § Stream-path startup hold](features/fakeclaude-binary.md#stream-path-startup-hold-pyry_fake_claude_stream_hold-1138) and [codebase/1138.md](codebase/1138.md); and `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_STREAM_APPROVE` (#1139, default-off, a rider on `STREAM_JSON`, mutually exclusive with the interrupt rider) *originates* one permission request per user turn by calling `control.Approve` directly — the same client `pyry mcp-approve` calls — against a control-socket path read lazily from a file (`PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_APPROVE_SOCKET_FILE`, the test writes the daemon's real random socket path post-spawn) and reflects the daemon's verdict into a distinct assistant needle (`approve-allow`/`approve-deny`/the fail-closed `approve-error` on any client-side failure) — the fake behaviour the live stream permission-round-trip e2e needs to prove `modal_shown`/`modal_answer`/verdict/deny-on-timeout end-to-end without depending on the still-open `--permission-prompt-tool`/`--mcp-config` interactive-runner wiring gap; pairs with a new `cmd/pyry` `approvalTimeout()` env seam (`PYRY_APPROVAL_TIMEOUT`, falls back to the existing 2-minute `mcpApprovalTimeout`) that shrinks only the daemon's fail-closed timer duration for the timeout case, see [features/fakeclaude-binary.md § Approve rider](features/fakeclaude-binary.md#approve-rider-pyry_fake_claude_stream_approve-1139), [features/control-plane.md § Wiring](features/control-plane.md), and [codebase/1139.md](codebase/1139.md); and `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_SESSION_ID_FROM_ARGV` + `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_JSONL_TRIGGER_DIR` (#1195, default-off) close the PTY tier's structural turn-lifecycle gap for a **minted** per-conversation session — every prior mode binds its stem to the process-wide `PYRY_FAKE_CLAUDE_INITIAL_UUID`, so a minted child wrote `<sharedDir>/<INITIAL_UUID>.jsonl` while the daemon tailed `<mintedSessionID>.jsonl` and the subscription never opened; the first knob instead resolves the stem from this spawn's own argv (`argvSessionID`, last `--session-id`/`--resume` wins, guarded against escaping the sessions dir — a laxer stand-in for the daemon's `transcript.ValidStem`, not a literal mirror), falling back to the env var on no match so byte-identical behaviour is preserved when unset (including for the pinned-since-#839 bootstrap spawn, which never reads `os.Args` otherwise); the second gives each child its own `<dir>/<stem>.jsonl.trig` (vs the shared trigger's single-poll-goroutine-only claim protocol, a coin flip across children) so a test can re-drop an `end_turn` line at exactly the minted child until the #929 subscription-offset kicker lands it — together they enable `TestRelayV2_PerConversationTurnEnd`, the PTY tier's first proof that conversation-scoped `turn_end` exists at all, verified non-vacuous by an on-disk assertion pair (marker text present in the minted transcript, absent from the bootstrap one), see [features/fakeclaude-binary.md § Argv-derived stem mode](features/fakeclaude-binary-argv-derived-stem-mode.md#argv-derived-stem-mode-1195), [§ Per-child JSONL trigger mode](features/fakeclaude-binary.md#per-child-jsonl-trigger-mode-1195), and [codebase/1195.md](codebase/1195.md) |
| [fakephone-harness.md](features/fakephone-harness.md) | `internal/e2e/internal/fakephone` (#296, extended #308) — in-process WS client speaking the phone half of the mobile↔relay protocol; `Dial(ctx, baseURL, serverID, token, deviceName)` opens `/v1/client` with the three required upgrade headers verbatim; `Send(env protocol.Envelope)` writes one text frame, `Receive(timeout)` reads one and unmarshals (deadline → `ErrReceiveTimeout`), `Close()` is idempotent + unblocks in-flight Receive with `ErrClosed`; pre-check + post-I/O recheck of a `closed` bool maps Close-mid-call to `ErrClosed` rather than wrapped library errors; speaks raw envelopes only (routing wrap is the relay's job); `coder/websocket` matching #247 / #295; timed-out Receive cannot be retried on the same Client (library closes conn on Read-ctx-cancel — documented constraint, fresh Client per timeout-plausible attempt); no consumers wired yet (roundtrip e2e test lands in follow-up ticket); #308 adds `LastCloseStatus() (websocket.StatusCode, bool)` — `Receive`'s error path captures the peer-side close status via `websocket.CloseStatus(err)` (skips `-1` for non-CloseErrors) into `lastCloseStatus` under the existing mutex; originally consumed by v1's `TestRelay_AuthReject_4401`, retired with the v1 leg (#941) — the auth-reject `4401` assertion now lives on `testV2BadToken` (`bad_token_4401`) in `relay_v2_handshake_test.go`, over Noise. |
| [fakerelay-harness.md](features/fakerelay-harness.md) | `internal/e2e/internal/fakerelay` (#295, extended #301/#308/#371/#581; binary-direct hello path removed #583) — in-process WS server speaking the routing half of the mobile↔relay protocol; `New(logger) → Server` with `/v1/server` (first-claim-wins on `x-pyrycode-server`) and `/v1/client` (requires `server`/`token`/`device-name` headers, mints `c-N` `conn_id`s) endpoints; wraps phone→binary frames as `protocol.RoutingEnvelope` and unwraps binary→phone frames; pre-upgrade HTTP 400/409/503 substitutes the production 4409/4404 WS close codes (deliberate test-ergonomic deviation, documented in the package comment); cleanup-cascades-on-binary-loss drops orphaned phones so `Close()` leaves no goroutines behind under `-race`; `coder/websocket` matching #247; #301 added binary-direct `hello`/`hello_ack` dispatch (envelopes with empty `conn_id` decode-twice as `protocol.Envelope`, dispatch by `Type` — only `TypeHello` handled, captured under `LastBinaryHello(serverID)`, replied with routing-wrapped `HelloAckPayload{ProtocolVersion:"v1", ServerID, ConnID:"-"}`; **this entire binary-direct hello path — the dispatch branch, `handleBinaryDirect`, the `lastBinaryHello` map, and the `LastBinaryHello` accessor — was removed in #583** once #582 retired the binary↔relay handshake, so a stray no-conn_id frame now just falls through to the unknown-`conn_id` Debug-drop), `RejectNextBinaryWith4409()` one-shot opt-in for the production WS-close-4409 code (next `/v1/server` upgrade accepts then closes with `websocket.StatusCode(4409)`, flag clears after one use; coexists with the existing HTTP-409 first-claim-wins path), and `ForceCloseBinary(serverID) bool` (closes with `StatusInternalError` for transport-reconnect e2e). First production consumer: the daemon-side relay e2e tests at `internal/e2e/relay_test.go` (#301); #308 adds token-injection on the first phone→binary frame per `conn_id` (`phoneConn` grows `token`/`firstFrameSent`/`tokMu`; `phoneRecvPump` reads-and-clears the upgrade-time `x-pyrycode-token` under `tokMu` and embeds it in `RoutingEnvelope.Token` on that one frame) and `CloseCode` honor on the binary→phone path (`binaryRecvPump` queues a `phoneSend{frame, closeCode}` tuple onto `pc.sendCh`; `phoneSendPump` writes `frame` first so the phone observes the error envelope before close, then issues `pc.conn.Close(websocket.StatusCode(closeCode), "")` — serialising close through the same send pump eliminates the race a direct `conn.Close` at the `binaryRecvPump` site would have introduced against an in-flight write); v1 consumer `internal/e2e/relay_auth_test.go` (`TestRelay_AuthReject_4401`) drove the auth-reject path end-to-end until it retired with the v1 leg (#941, superseded by the Noise-path `bad_token_4401`); `/v2/server` shares this same `handleBinary` dispatch, so the surviving `TestRelay_4409`/`TestRelay_1011` route-swap (#941) is transport-agnostic — `RejectNextBinaryWith4409`/`ForceCloseBinary`/`WaitBinary` key on `serverID`, not path. **#371 (race fix):** adds `WaitBinary(ctx, serverID) bool` alongside `LastBinaryHello` — blocks until `s.binaries[serverID]` is registered or ctx fires (single fast-path check under `s.mu`, then `time.NewTicker(2ms)` polling so cancellation is responsive). Closes the upgrade→registration race in fixture probes: `websocket.Accept` writes the 101 response and unblocks the test's `websocket.Dial` *before* `handleBinary` finishes `s.binaries[serverID] = bc` under `s.mu` (`fakerelay.go:231-260`), so a test calling `ForceCloseBinary` immediately after `dialBinary` could land in that window under `-race` on a contended CI worker and observe a not-yet-registered binary. `WaitBinary` first served raw-dial unit tests while e2e tests polled `LastBinaryHello`; **#581 migrates every e2e readiness gate onto `WaitBinary` too** (decoupling the suite from the relay-leg hello ahead of #582 retiring it — the consumer slice of #569's Strangler Fig; `LastBinaryHello` then survived only for `TestRelay_Hello`'s payload assertion until #582 deleted that test and #583 removed the accessor itself). `TestForceCloseBinary` now calls `WaitBinary` between `dialBinary` and `ForceCloseBinary`; ghost-case assertion unchanged. `ForceCloseBinary` semantics deliberately not modified (wrapping it internally would slow the ghost case or require two variants); `handleBinary`'s Accept-then-register order deliberately not modified (pre-Accept registration would impose new ordering constraints on the routing dispatcher). |
| [install-e2e.md](features/install-e2e.md) | `internal/e2e/install_{linux,darwin}_test.go` (build tag `e2e_install`) — `pyry install-service` × `systemctl --user` (Linux) / `launchctl bootstrap gui/<uid>` (macOS) round-trip + bug-#19 PATH inheritance regression guard + cleanup-on-fatal re-exec verification on both platforms |
| [e2e-realclaude.md](features/e2e-realclaude.md) | `internal/e2e/realclaude` (#361, build tag `e2e_realclaude`) — sibling Go package to `internal/e2e`, opt-in via `make e2e-realclaude`, scaffold home for the real-`claude`-binary trust-boundary tests #362/#363 will populate; `smoke_test.go` asserts `exec.LookPath("claude")` succeeds and `claude --version` runs zero-exit under a 10 s `context.WithTimeout` (fatal-not-skip — suite was opted into); single-tag `//go:build e2e_realclaude` deliberately distinct from `e2e`/`e2e_install` so a future `make e2e` won't pull real-claude tests in; `make test` skips via tag exclusion alone (no path filter), `make check` unchanged, per-PR CI continues to run only `make check`; no `-race` on the make target (I/O-bound exec, not goroutine-stress). **CI cadence:** no GitHub Actions workflow — the suite runs locally during the code-review phase of every dispatched ticket via the pipeline (see the code-review agent's `CLAUDE.md` for the invocation contract). The earlier nightly workflow (`.github/workflows/e2e-realclaude-nightly.yml`, #362) was removed in #379 the same day it landed (Max-plan tokens used locally are free vs. a paid `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` GitHub secret; failure surface synchronised to dispatch cadence beats unpredictable 04:00 UTC failures; one fewer CI file to keep in lockstep with `self-check-daily.yml`). `make e2e-realclaude` is unchanged — still the entry point, just no longer invoked by CI. **Fixture helpers (#372):** `fixtures.go` adds `WithWorktree(t) string` (`t.TempDir()` + `t.Setenv("HOME", dir)`; pins HOME for both the in-test process and any subprocess so `os.UserHomeDir()` resolves identically on both sides — `t.Setenv` not `os.Setenv` is load-bearing for parallel-safe cleanup; no `.claude/` pre-creation, the runtime owns it), `ReadJSONL(t, workdir, sessionID) []JSONLEntry` (delegates path composition to `tuidriver.SessionJSONLPath` (#508) then loops `jsonl.NewReader(f, jsonl.Config{}).Next()` until `io.EOF`; empty file → empty slice; open/parse failures `t.Fatalf` with the resolved path embedded in the message; private `resolveAndOpenJSONL(workdir, sessionID) (*os.File, string, error)` split keeps the missing-file path unit-testable as a returned error rather than via a `*testing.T`-wrapper trick), and `type JSONLEntry = jsonl.Event` (type **alias**, not wrapper struct — preserves `entry.EndOfTurn` / `entry.Usage.OutputTokens` field access without forcing callers to import `internal/agentrun/jsonl` directly). Five tagged tests in `fixtures_test.go` cover happy path / empty file / missing file (via the private split) / HOME-restore-after-subtest / alias compile-check; **Subprocess invocation helper (#373):** `fixtures.go` adds `RunPyryAgentRun(t, RunOpts) RunResult` — synchronous invoker that builds `pyry` once per test process via `sync.Once` (`PYRY_E2E_BIN` short-circuit + temp-dir cache, structurally cloned from `internal/e2e/harness.go:118-141` because disjoint build tags `e2e` vs. `e2e_realclaude` block direct reuse), writes `<Workdir>/prompt.txt` + `system.txt` from `opts.Prompt`/`opts.SystemPrompt` (0o600), invokes `pyry agent-run --prompt-file=… --system-prompt-file=… --allowed-tools=<comma-joined> --max-turns=<n> --effort=<…> --model=<…> --workdir=<…> --output-format=stream-json` (eight flags in `--flag=value` form so the argv-contract test's substring matches are order-independent), captures stdout/stderr into `bytes.Buffer`s, and returns `RunResult{ExitCode, SessionID, Stdout, Stderr}` where `SessionID` comes from a `bufio.Scanner` over stdout stopping at the first decoded `{"type":"system","subtype":"init","session_id":"…"}` line with non-empty session_id (init-only by design — lands earliest in stdout so failing tests still see the id even when the run aborted before producing a `result` trailer; non-JSON and non-init lines skip silently; `parseInitSessionID` is inline ~14 LoC, no dependency on `internal/agentrun/streamjson` to keep the dependency direction one-way). **Non-zero subprocess exit is NOT a `t.Fatalf`** — callers assert on `RunResult.ExitCode` themselves so the real CLI's error paths can be tested; only structural failures (validation, build, exec start, `context.DeadlineExceeded`) call `t.Fatalf`, with the timeout branch embedding both stdout and stderr in the message so the operator sees what was running. `RunOpts` mirrors `cmd/pyry/agent_run.go:66-152`'s flag surface 1:1 (`Workdir`, `Prompt`, `SystemPrompt`, `AllowedTools`, `MaxTurns`, `Effort`, `Model`, `ExtraEnv`, `Timeout` defaulting to 5 min) — **no `SessionID` input** (pyry agent-run has no resume flag; session_id is an output, not an input), **no `Mode` enum** (post-#391 architecture has no second mode at this layer — `pyry agent-run` IS the stream-json forwarder). Private `validateRunOpts(opts) error` returns first-violation-wins on the seven required fields (`Workdir`/`Prompt`/`SystemPrompt`/`AllowedTools` non-empty/`MaxTurns > 0`/`Effort`/`Model`), exposed as a returned error so tests assert on it without intercepting `t.Fatalf`; the `--effort` enum is NOT re-validated locally — pyry rejects bad values and the helper surfaces that as a non-zero exit (one place to update). Test infrastructure: `TestMain` re-execs the test binary as a fake `pyry` when `GO_TEST_HELPER_PROCESS=1`, pins `PYRY_E2E_BIN=os.Args[0]` for every other test so `ensurePyryBuilt` short-circuits to the fake, and the fake selects behaviour from `PYRY_E2E_FAKE_MODE` (`happy` emits init + assistant + result trailer, `fail` exits 2 before any stream-json, `sleep` blocks 30s, `argv` echoes its argv to stderr). Seven tests cover: happy-path session_id + stdout asserts, `validateRunOpts` positive + negative table (Workdir/Prompt/SystemPrompt/AllowedTools/MaxTurns≤0/Effort/Model), init-event-absent (ExitCode 2, SessionID empty, no fatal), timeout (re-execs itself with `PYRY_REALCLAUDE_TIMEOUT_INNER=1` so the inner `t.Fatalf` is captured as a subprocess failure — cleaner than wrapping `*testing.T`), argv-contract (eight flags substring-matched in stderr), and a `parseInitSessionID` table (empty / init / empty-session_id / non-init-first / trailer-only / malformed-before-init). Production code lands at ~140 non-comment LoC vs. the spec's ~95 LoC budget — two exported structs (9+4 fields) + public helper + validator + build-cache clone + parser renders close to this footprint by construction; flagged at commit time, reviewer accepted. Composition pattern downstream tests use: `WithWorktree` → `RunPyryAgentRun` → `ReadJSONL`. **First consumer (#364):** `prompt_fidelity_test.go` adds `TestRealClaude_PromptFidelity` — runs `pyry agent-run` against the real `claude` CLI with a distinctive ASCII-only literal (`const distinctivePrompt = "INTEGRATION_TEST_PROMPT_PROMPTFIDELITY_2N7Q4R8W"`, JSON-safe by construction so substring matching is deterministic), then asserts the literal survives byte-for-byte into the first `user`-kinded entry of claude's JSONL session via `bytes.Contains(event.Raw, []byte(literal))`. **`Event.Raw` substring-match instead of decoding `message.content`** — `jsonl.Event` only decodes assistant-shaped fields, and user lines carry `content` as either a string OR `[{type:"text",text:…}]` (see `internal/agentrun/jsonl/reader.go:137` comment); matching the verbatim line bytes covers both shapes without owning a second decoder. Run-level asserts (`ExitCode == 0`, `SessionID != ""`) come BEFORE the JSONL assert so setup/auth/network failures surface as themselves rather than as confusing "no user entry" messages; per-shape failure-message dump policy is **kinds for "missing", body for "found-but-wrong"** (no event bodies in the missing-kind dump — kind tags alone redirect debugging without bulk noise or sensitive bodies). Private `jsonlPathFor(workdir, sessionID)` recomputes the resolved JSONL path for failure messages via `os.UserHomeDir()` + `agentrun.EncodeProjectDir(workdir)` and returns a `<unresolved: …>` sentinel on error rather than escalating diagnostic-helper failures into test failures. `RunOpts` uses `--max-turns=1`, `--effort=low`, `--model=claude-haiku-4-5` (literal model name, matching `fixtures_test.go:163`, not the `haiku` short alias) to minimise per-run cost (~$0.01); `SystemPrompt` discourages prompt echo, `AllowedTools: []string{"Read"}` is the minimum non-empty value satisfying `validateRunOpts`. Pins the streamrunner envelope's text round-trip end-to-end — would catch any future preprocessing, escaping drift, or wiring change that bypasses streamrunner (the original `/doctor` poisoning class, made structurally impossible by #391). One real haiku call per `make e2e-realclaude` run; `make test` still excludes via build tag. No new exports, no helper additions — entire test body is a fixture composition. **Second consumer (#365):** `allowed_tools_enforcement_test.go` adds `TestRealClaude_AllowedToolsEnforcement` — runs `pyry agent-run --allowed-tools Read` against the real `claude` CLI with an explicit Bash-attractive prompt (`"List the files in the current working directory. Use the Bash tool to run \`ls -la\`."`) and asserts no assistant entry in the resulting JSONL emits a `tool_use` content block with `name == "Bash"`. Pins the **runtime behavior** of the agent-run argv shape that `cmd/pyry/agent_run_test.go:484` already pins statically (no `--settings`, no `--permission-mode`, no `--session-id`): with `--dangerously-skip-permissions --allowed-tools Read`, the real claude binary refuses Bash. File-local 12-LoC detector `bashInvokedInRaw` mirrors `internal/agentrun/selfcheck/selfcheck.go:284-302` exactly (exact-case `Type=="tool_use" && Name=="Bash"` match, decode-error → skip silently per selfcheck.go:283) — **duplicated rather than imported** to keep the dependency direction one-way (e2e tests don't depend on the daemon's audit package); the lockstep coupling is named in a one-line comment so a future `tool_use` → `tool_invocation` rename moves both fixtures together. Complements `internal/agentrun/selfcheck` (#336), which probes the boot-time `--settings`/`defaultMode=deny` path; this probes the spawned agent-run `--allowed-tools` + `--dangerously-skip-permissions` path. **Obsolete-matrix history:** originally framed as a `defaultMode ∈ {deny, default, dontAsk}` matrix; under post-#391 architecture the per-spawn settings file is gone and `--allowed-tools` is the sole enforcement configuration on the agent-run path, so the matrix collapsed to one row (named in a header comment so a future reader who finds the original framing in git history understands why). Prompt is intentionally explicit ("Use the Bash tool to run …") and the `SystemPrompt` is minimal/neutral with no "don't use Bash" guidance — the property under test is the **gate**, not the model's judgment; a weak prompt would risk a green test for the wrong reason. Failure-message discipline on a positive hit: cite the resolved JSONL path (via the package-private `jsonlPathFor` helper #364 introduced — now two consumers but stays file-private, YAGNI bar for promoting to a fixture export visible but not yet met), never echo `e.Raw` (mirrors selfcheck.go:217-219 — never log raw assistant content, pattern consistency across the codebase). `RunOpts` uses `--max-turns=2` (one turn for the model to attempt-and-fail, a second for a final reply so a hung run is distinguishable from a refused run in diagnostics), `--effort=low`, `--model=claude-haiku-4-5` (~$0.01). Reuses the canonical four-phase shape from #364 (worktree allocate → run → run-level asserts → property assertion); no new exports, no helper additions, ~94 LoC. **Operator-visible signal extension (#420):** appends a phase-5/6 disjunctive assertion to the same test after the gate-held loop completes — channel-agnostic by design (the contract is "operator gets *some* interpretable signal on denial", NOT "operator gets *this specific* signal"). Two new file-local detectors: `assistantTextRefusalHit(events) (bool, int)` walks `e.Kind=="assistant"` entries decoding `message.content[].{type,text}` and returns true on the first lowercased-substring match against fixed set `denialKeywords = ["cannot","can't","unable","not allowed","permission"]`; `structuredDenialHit(stdout) (bool, int)` scans the captured stream-json stdout with `bufio.Scanner` (default 64 KiB buffer — `result` lines are well under that, confirmed against the spike fixture set + `internal/agentrun/streamjson/testdata/captured_run.jsonl`) and returns true on the first `{"type":"result", "is_error":bool, "permission_denials":[]json.RawMessage}` line where `len(PermissionDenials) > 0 || IsError`. Combine point is `if !textHit && !structHit { t.Fatalf(...) }` — either alone is sufficient; both miss is the silent-no-op failure mode being guarded against. The `is_error` branch is forward-compat tolerance for a future claude that routes denial through `is_error=true` + a subtype change without touching `permission_denials` (canonical today). Detector deliberately stops there — accepting `subtype != "success"` would conflate denial with unrelated failure modes (max-turns hit, timeout, API rejection), muddling the contract. Keyword set kept narrow on purpose (broader markers like "available"/"access" false-positive in non-refusal contexts); the disjunctive design tolerates a keyword miss as long as the structured channel fires, so the right discipline if the test ever flakes is to expand the keyword set in a follow-up, NOT weaken to "any non-empty assistant text". JSON unmarshal errors in either detector skip silently (selfcheck.go:283 pattern). Both detectors return scanned-entry counts so the disjunctive-miss `t.Fatalf` names `denialKeywords` literally, assistant-entry count `N`, stdout-line count `M`, and the resolved JSONL path — never raw bytes (selfcheck.go:217-219 discipline applied even though the prompt is synthetic). **Architect picked extension, not sibling test**: same argv/model/prompt/RunResult as #365 — a sibling would re-run the same haiku call (~$0.01) with no behavioural difference and invite asymmetric drift where a future maintainer fixes the prompt for one test and forgets the other exists. Co-locating the two assertions makes the one contract (`pyry agent-run --allowed-tools=Read` is a deny-by-default gate that surfaces some signal to the operator) visible in one place. Companion to #418 (captured-trace null-findings fixture-walk on the direct-`claude` spike argv) — no overlap, different argv. Adds +111 LoC to `allowed_tools_enforcement_test.go`, zero per-run cost increase (two synchronous in-memory walks over the same `events` slice and `result.Stdout` bytes; no extra subprocess, no extra model call). See [`codebase/420.md`](codebase/420.md) for the disjunctive-design rationale. **Third consumer (#376):** `tool_loop_test.go` adds `TestRealClaude_ToolLoopIntegrity` — positive-path counterpart to #365. Seeds the worktree with `hello.txt`/`world.txt` (`os.WriteFile` 0o600), runs `pyry agent-run --allowed-tools Bash --max-turns=3 --effort=low --model=claude-haiku-4-5` with `"Use the Bash tool to run \"ls -1\" in the current directory, then tell me how many .txt files you see."`, and asserts the full internal tool loop end-to-end. Two assertion phases — (a) single-pass state machine over on-disk JSONL events with three flags (`bashToolUseID`, `sawToolResult`, `sawFinalText`) advanced in event order so the first missing piece is the one reported on failure: capture `tool_use` id from the first `{Type:"tool_use", Name:"Bash"}` content block under an `assistant`-kinded entry's `message.content[]`, then on `user`-kinded entries scan for matching `{Type:"tool_result", ToolUseID==bashToolUseID}`, then on subsequent `assistant` entries scan for `{Type:"text", Text:non-empty}`; (b) `bufio.Scanner` over `result.Stdout` for the `type:"result"` trailer line with `subtype=="success"`, `stop_reason=="end_turn"`, `num_turns>=2`. **Two load-bearing insights pinned structurally:** `tool_use`/`tool_result` are content blocks INSIDE `assistant`/`user` entries (not top-level events — `jsonl.Event.Kind`'s whitelist technically permits those values but that path is unreachable for production claude on disk), and the `type:"result"` trailer is exclusive to pyry's stdout (emitted by `streamjson/emitter.go.Close`), NOT on disk — assertions on the trailer therefore read `result.Stdout`, never `events`. The AC's "result event" wording did not disambiguate; the spec resolved it explicitly and pinned `parseResultTrailer` to stdout. Two file-local helpers: `parseContentBlocks(raw json.RawMessage) ([]contentBlock, error)` decodes the verbatim line into `{Message:{Content:[]contentBlock}}` with a single struct (`Type`, `Name`, `ID`, `Text`, `ToolUseID`, all `omitempty`-tagged) that handles `tool_use`/`tool_result`/`text` blocks one-shot — parse errors are a per-line skip mirroring `bashInvokedInRaw`/`selfcheck.go:283`; `parseResultTrailer(stdout []byte) (*resultTrailer, error)` scans stdout via `bufio.NewScanner(bytes.NewReader(stdout))` and returns the first decoded line where `Type=="result"` (non-JSON and non-result lines skip silently; missing the entire trailer is a hard `t.Fatalf` with stdout prefix because the trailer is a structural guarantee of every successful run). Default 64 KiB scanner line limit is sufficient vs. pyry's ~400-byte trailer; helper comment notes the `scanner.Buffer(make([]byte, 1<<20), 1<<20)` extension recipe for a future >64 KiB line. **`resultTrailer.PermissionDenials *[]json.RawMessage` pointer-typed** with `omitempty` so callers distinguish "field absent" (nil) from "field present, empty slice" (non-nil, len 0); pyry's emitter (`streamjson/emitter.go:251-275`) emits eleven trailer fields and `permission_denials` is NOT in that set (claude-only field, dropped by pyry's re-emitter), so the conditional check is dormant today — retained per the AC ("if present in the schema") as a forward-compat guard against either pyry's emitter growing the field or a future test variant comparing against claude's native stdout. **`num_turns >= 2`** lower bound: a tool-loop run necessarily has ≥2 assistant entries (tool_use turn + final text turn); `≥` absorbs any post-tool clarification turns within `MaxTurns: 3`. **Seeded fixtures (`hello.txt` "hello\n", `world.txt` "world\n", 0o600)** exist so the Bash invocation has deterministic targets to enumerate; not asserted against — the test pins protocol shape, not response content. **Prompt-as-enforcement-fixture discipline (#365 pattern)**: the property under test is the loop completing, not the model's judgment, so the prompt phrases Bash as the obvious tool and gives the model a concrete numeric question to answer in its final turn — exercising both the `tool_use`→`tool_result` hop AND the subsequent text turn. **SystemPrompt** minimal and neutral (`"You are an e2e regression-guard test. When asked to inspect the filesystem, use the Bash tool."`) — non-empty load-bearing per `validateRunOpts`. `MaxTurns: 3` is one turn of slack against haiku occasionally running a verification command. **Failure-message discipline:** on-disk-walk failures cite the resolved JSONL path via the file-package-private `jsonlPathFor` (#364, third consumer now — YAGNI bar for promoting to exported fixture helper visible but still not met); stdout-trailer failures embed the first 1 KiB of stdout; never echo `e.Raw` (consistent with #365 + `selfcheck.go:217-219`).
