Reuses canonical phase shape extended to five (worktree allocate → **seed text files** → run → run-level asserts → property assertion split into JSONL-walk + stdout-trailer-parse); no new exports, no helper additions, ~202 LoC. One real haiku call per run, ~$0.02. **Fourth consumer (#381):** `per_agent_test.go` adds five named top-level tests — one per dispatcher role — `TestRealClaude_PO_RoleLoop` / `_Architect_RoleLoop` / `_Developer_RoleLoop` / `_CodeReview_RoleLoop` / `_Documentation_RoleLoop`. Per-role-drift regression guard: the 2026-05-14 `/doctor` poisoning bug failed all five roles together; the next failure of this class will likely hit one role only, so five separate top-level functions (not a table loop) give five independent pass/fail signals on the nightly board. File-private `dispatcherBaseTools []string` mirrors `agents/dispatcher/src/dispatch.ts:1183` from the sibling `agent-dispatcher` repo — 21 entries (Bash/Read/Write/Edit/Glob/Grep/TodoWrite + 4 qmd + 2 context7 + 7 codegraph; **no figma entries** despite the ticket-body parenthetical — the dispatcher source-of-truth literal has none, see "Lessons learned" in `codebase/381.md`). `dispatcherAllowedToolsForRole(role)` returns `dispatcherBaseTools` for po/developer/documentation, `dispatcherBaseTools + "Agent"` for architect/code-review, mirroring `dispatch.ts:1184-1186`'s `needsAgent` membership check; cite comments above both are the renumber-detection breadcrumbs. **Defensive `make+copy` on both branches** so a caller's slice mutation cannot corrupt the package-level constant; unknown role panics (programmer error at the 5 call sites, not a runtime condition). System prompts are deliberate 1-2 line stand-ins ("You are a Pyrycode product-owner agent. You refine issue bodies. Use Read/Edit/qmd/codegraph for research."), NOT verbatim from `agents/<role>/CLAUDE.md` — the property under test is the (allowed-tools, system-prompt shape) wiring, not operator-prompt fidelity (which would make the suite expensive and falsely sensitive to prompt-edit churn). User prompts are role-appropriate one-shot tasks ("Rewrite this one-line ticket title…", "Implement a Go function `Add(a, b int) int`…", "Summarize this one-line commit message…") that resolve in ≤4 turns with haiku/low and produce a single end-of-turn assistant text block (no `tool_use`); each explicitly constrains reply shape to keep haiku from drifting into exploratory tool use. Shared `runRoleSmokeTest(t, role, systemPrompt, userPrompt)` body consolidates the six identical assertions — `ExitCode == 0`, `SessionID != ""`, `parseResultTrailer(result.Stdout)` succeeds, `PermissionDenials` empty (nil-or-zero-length, pointer-vs-empty distinction from #376 preserved), `NumTurns >= 1` (single-shot no-tool minimum; #376's `>= 2` is tool-loop specific), LAST `assistant`-kinded JSONL event has `EndOfTurn == true && TextChars > 0`. **Shared helper is not a table loop** — each `Test…_RoleLoop` is its own top-level function so per-role failure attribution surfaces on the nightly board. Reuses #376's package-private `parseResultTrailer` + `resultTrailer` directly (second consumer; no fixture-export widening) and #364's `jsonlPathFor` (fourth consumer). File-local `truncate([]byte) string` (1 KiB cap, suffixed `... (truncated)`) — extracted file-locally because the five assertion blocks invoke it 2-3 times each; the analogous inline pattern in #376/#365 is unchanged. `RunOpts: MaxTurns=4, Effort="low", Model="claude-haiku-4-5"`, no `Timeout` override (5-minute default is the runaway guard, not the SLO). Production dispatch uses opus/high; this deliberate haiku/low downgrade is the cost/coverage trade — a future nightly opus suite is a separate file, not an additive flag here. `t.Parallel()` is NOT called — matches the existing realclaude convention, keeps cost predictable, avoids API rate-limit interactions. ~$0.10 total per run (5 haiku calls, cache warm); ~30 s per test, ~150 s aggregate wall time. **Lesson:** first-pass implementation pulled three figma read-only entries into `dispatcherBaseTools` from the ticket body's parenthetical; the spec named `dispatch.ts:1183` as the source of truth and that literal has zero figma entries. Code review caught this as a MUST FIX (commit `be2d1a0`); shipping a pre-drifted mirrored constant defeats the suite's purpose. The cite comment was also corrected from the ticket-body line numbers (1190/1191-1193) to the actual source-of-truth lines (1183/1184-1186) — the cite is the renumber-detection breadcrumb and must land on the correct line. ~174 LoC. **Opt-in real-API fixture (#409, dual-credential contract extended in #490):** `fixtures.go` adds `WithWorktreeAuthenticated(t) string` — sibling to `WithWorktree` for tests that need a real Anthropic API response (not argv-shape probes). Reads BOTH `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` AND `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` from the outer test-runner env and proceeds if either is non-empty (post-#490: closes the silent-skip masquerade flagged in #487's post-mortem — Max-only Macs have no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` because the credential lives in macOS Keychain (`security find-generic-password -s 'Claude Code-credentials' -w | jq -r '.claudeAiOauth.accessToken'`) and `claude` accepts the extracted access token via `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`; the helper now unblocks the 7 opt-in tests on that configuration). If both are unset/empty, calls `t.Skipf` with a single named-variable message that names BOTH variables AND embeds the Keychain extraction recipe verbatim **before** allocating `t.TempDir()` so a skipped run never strands an empty workdir (AC#5 mandates skip-not-fail so the suite stays green on contributor machines without API keys; AC#4 reconciles with #5 only by routing the absent-credential branch through `t.Skip` inside the helper rather than at every consumer). When at least one var is present, delegates to `WithWorktree(t)` for `$HOME` isolation, then re-pins only the non-empty env var(s) via `t.Setenv` — an absent variable is NOT re-pinned to `""`, preserving the original outer-env shape so `os.LookupEnv` keeps distinguishing unset from set-empty for any downstream tooling that switches on it. **Mechanically redundant** with `RunPyryAgentRun`'s `cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), opts.ExtraEnv...)` inheritance at `fixtures.go:169` — the re-pin is intent-documenting, not bug-fixing: it forecloses silent regression if a future refactor inserts an env-scrubbing helper between this call and the subprocess spawn, and the `t.Setenv` cleanup ordering restores the value on test exit even if other in-test code mutates it. **No credential-shape validation** (no `strings.HasPrefix("sk-ant-")`) — claude is the only authority on key validity and surfaces bad values as a non-zero subprocess exit; same one-place-to-update discipline `validateRunOpts` uses for `--effort`. **No imports added** (`os`/`testing` already there). Doc comment (5 lines) names `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` literally, restricts the helper to real-API tests, and surfaces the skip path — `go doc` is the only documentation surface required by AC#3. Closes the gap that #383's permission-protocol spike surfaced: a `$HOME`-pinned subprocess can't find `~/.claude/credentials` and returns a synthetic `assistant` envelope with `"text":"Not logged in · Please run /login"`, `error:"authentication_failed"`, `exit_code: 1`, `duration_ms: 402`, zero tool_use frames, zero permission events — claude exits at the auth gate before any model/tool/permission logic runs. Plain `WithWorktree(t)` is unchanged. **End-to-end test `TestWithWorktreeAuthenticated_RealAssistant`** (`fixtures_test.go:60-102`, ~43 LoC) self-skips when credential absent, otherwise runs `pyry agent-run` with minimum-viable shape (`Prompt:"Reply with the single word 'pong' and nothing else."`, `SystemPrompt:"You are a minimal e2e authentication probe. Keep replies under 10 words."`, `AllowedTools:["Read"]` minimum-non-empty, `MaxTurns:1`, `Effort:"low"`, `Model:"claude-haiku-4-5"`, no Timeout override) and asserts the canonical five-phase shape from `runRoleSmokeTest`: `ExitCode==0` → `SessionID!=""` → **negative-pin** against the #383 failure mode (`bytes.Contains(stdout, "\"model\":\"<synthetic>\"")` and `bytes.Contains(stdout, "\"error\":\"authentication_failed\"")` both rejected, with stdout in the diagnostic capped via `truncate` at 1 KiB) → walk `ReadJSONL` for the first `Kind=="assistant" && EndOfTurn && TextChars > 0` event, citing the resolved JSONL path via the file-local `jsonlPathFor` (fifth consumer now) on failure. The structural asserts would already fail on a synthetic-auth envelope (`ExitCode != 0`, no `EndOfTurn` text); the substring checks produce a clearer diagnostic for the auth-failed mode specifically — belt-and-suspenders against a *named* failure mode (#383's literal trace), not generic defensive coding. The 1 KiB stdout truncation isn't a leakage defense against today's stream-json (which does not echo the API key) but cheap insurance against any future claude diagnostic change. `t.Parallel()` is NOT called — matches the existing realclaude convention. ~$0.01 per `make e2e-realclaude` run when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is exported, ~10-30 s wall time; zero cost / time when absent (Go's standard `SKIP` output). No change to `RunPyryAgentRun`; AC#1 satisfied by the existing inheritance path, AC#3/#4 by the in-helper gate, AC#5 by the test's pass-on-credentials / skip-on-absence behaviour. ~55 LoC total. **Fifth consumer (#382):** `resilience_test.go` adds four named top-level protocol-resilience tests — `TestRealClaude_BashTool_NonZeroExit` (RunPyryAgentRun + AllowedTools:["Bash"] + prompt `"Use the Bash tool to run \"ls /nonexistent-path-for-pyrycode-382\". Report what you see."`; walks JSONL events with three independent observation flags `bashToolUseID` / `sawToolResult` / `sawFinalText` so a failure message names the actual missing event rather than a downstream consequence — first-pass coupled `sawFinalText` on a derived `toolResultErr` flag and review caught this as a SHOULD FIX (`0e8e364`); five JSONL assertions then `parseResultTrailer` → `trailer.Subtype == "success"` (do NOT assert on `StopReason` — `end_turn` and `tool_use_error` both satisfy the AC, the "structured signal not pipe break" contract IS `Subtype == "success"`)), `TestRealClaude_PrematureStdinClose` (bypasses pyry, calls `claude` directly via `exec.CommandContext` with 60 s deadline; writes the production-shape user-turn envelope `{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"…"}]}}` via local `buildUserTurnEnvelope` then closes stdin — exactly the streamrunner production pattern; asserts `ctx.Err() != context.DeadlineExceeded` AND `exitCode == 0`; `WithWorktreeAuthenticated(t)` required because claude actually processes the envelope), `TestRealClaude_MalformedStreamJSON` (direct exec, plain `WithWorktree(t)` — claude rejects before reaching the model so no API cost; writes garbage `[]byte("this is not stream-json\n}}{{not json either\n")`, asserts `ctx.Err() != DeadlineExceeded`, `exitCode != 0`, `len(stderr) > 0`, **stderr predicate is OR-of-keywords** `{"json","parse","input","format","envelope"}` case-insensitive — pinning a specific phrase couples the test to claude's diagnostic copy; if a future claude version emits zero matches, expand the keyword set, keep the OR-shape, never pin a phrase), `TestRealClaude_LargePromptNearContextWindow` (RunPyryAgentRun + `strings.Repeat("All work and no play makes Jack a dull boy. ", 1200)` → ~52 KB prompt + `WithWorktreeAuthenticated`; disjunctive assertion as a single `if !branchA && !branchB { t.Fatalf(...) }` so the failure message lists both branches on "neither" — branch A = `trailer.Subtype == "success"` AND `trailer.StopReason NOT IN {"context_overflow","max_context_length"}`, branch B = `trailer.Subtype != "success"` AND `result.ExitCode != 0`; an unknown new stop_reason is treated as the structured-error branch by virtue of the explicit-set membership check; the failure mode being guarded against is "neither" — no result trailer at all (crash) or success-with-overflow-shaped-stop-reason (silent truncation)). **File-local helpers (`resilience_test.go:271-378`):** `resolveClaudeBin(t)` honours `PYRY_CLAUDE_BIN` unless its absolute path equals `os.Args[0]`'s (defense against the 2026-05-16 fork-bomb pattern that `RunOpts.UseTestBinaryAsFakePyry` also encodes — second occurrence of the pattern; `t.Fatalf`-not-skip because pointing the env var at the test binary is a misconfigured run, not an absent claude); falls back to `exec.LookPath("claude")` + `t.Skipf` on miss. `directClaudeArgs() []string` returns the eight-flag argv mirroring `buildClaudeArgs` minus `--append-system-prompt-file` (direct-exec tests don't need a system prompt). `runClaudeDirect(t, ctx, bin, workdir, stdinBytes) (int, []byte, error)` wraps `exec.CommandContext` + `StdinPipe` + `Start` + `Write` + `Close` + `Wait`; **stdin-write errors are `t.Logf`-not-`t.Fatalf`** because Test 3's fast-reject path produces an EPIPE-wrapped `*os.PathError` from claude having already closed its end — first-pass had `errors.Is(err, io.ErrClosedPipe)` predicate which doesn't match the actual wrapping chain, review caught this as a NIT and the fix drops the predicate entirely. Stdout is captured but not returned — neither caller asserts on response content, capturing prevents pipe backpressure. `buildUserTurnEnvelope(prompt) ([]byte, error)` builds the envelope locally rather than importing `internal/agentrun/streamrunner` to keep dependency direction one-way. **Two-field extension to `contentBlock` in `tool_loop_test.go`:** added `Content json.RawMessage \`json:"content,omitempty"\`` and `IsError bool \`json:"is_error,omitempty"\``. Both `omitempty`-tagged; no behavior change to #376's `TestRealClaude_ToolLoopIntegrity` (does not read either field). Doc-comment gained a four-line note explaining why `tool_result` content uses `json.RawMessage` — the body is a string OR an array of nested content blocks per claude's stream-json contract, and `RawMessage` holds either shape. **MUST FIX in code review (`0e8e364`):** first-pass read `b.Text` for the tool_result content non-emptiness check; `b.Text` is always `""` for `tool_result` blocks (the field is populated only for `text` blocks), so the assertion would have fired on every nightly regardless of claude's output. Fix adds `Content json.RawMessage` to the struct and asserts on its length. Lesson: when extracting a field from a foreign CLI's structured output, verify the field name + shape against real captured testdata (`internal/agentrun/jsonl/testdata/*.jsonl` — 19 `"is_error":...` occurrences confirmed the `is_error` field presence without spending a real API call). Bounded timeouts: 60 s on Tests 2 + 3 (tight, property is "no hang"), 5-min `RunPyryAgentRun` default on Tests 1 + 4 (runaway guard, not SLO). Three real haiku calls per run (Tests 1, 2, 4; Test 3 rejects pre-model), ~$0.08 total with cache warm. `t.Parallel()` NOT called — matches the existing realclaude convention. No edits to `fixtures.go` (spec explicitly forbade it); `runClaudeDirect` stays file-local until a third raw-exec test materialises. ~380 LoC. **MCP server smoke suite (#384):** `mcp_smoke_test.go` adds four named top-level tests, one per MCP server the dispatcher agents depend on (`TestRealClaude_MCP_QMD` → `mcp__qmd__query`; `TestRealClaude_MCP_Context7` → `mcp__plugin_context7_context7__resolve-library-id`; `TestRealClaude_MCP_CodeGraph` → `mcp__codegraph__codegraph_search`; `TestRealClaude_MCP_Figma` → `mcp__plugin_figma_figma__get_metadata`). Each drives a haiku turn through `pyry agent-run` and asserts the named MCP tool fires with non-empty `tool_result`; protocol drift on any server (renamed tool, changed parameter shape, broken stdio handshake) surfaces as a structured failure rather than silently wedging a dispatched agent. **HOME is deliberately NOT pinned** — `WithWorktree`/`WithWorktreeAuthenticated` both pin `$HOME` to a tempdir, which disables claude's MCP server discovery (claude reads `$HOME/.claude.json` + `$HOME/.claude/plugins/...`); tests allocate `t.TempDir()` workdir and leave HOME pointing at the outer operator's home so claude resolves the same MCP server set the operator has configured. Per-test JSONL files land under `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-tempdir>/`, bounded by tempdir uniqueness; acceptable because the suite is not fully hermetic today (`WithWorktreeAuthenticated` already leaks `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) and CI runners have controlled HOME. Inline `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`-unset skip per test (mirrors `WithWorktreeAuthenticated`'s message shape, inlined because the helper's HOME pin is incompatible). File-local pre-flight probe `mcpServerHealthy(t, displayName) (bool, string)` runs `claude mcp list` under a 10 s `context.WithTimeout`, scans for `<displayName>:` prefix + `✓ Connected` sentinel, returns `(true, reason)` for absent / `! Needs authentication` / `✗ Failed to connect` / probe-timeout so callers `t.Skipf` with operator-actionable context; `PYRY_CLAUDE_BIN` intentionally NOT honored (reserved for stubbed pyry tests; fork-bomb defense lives in `resilience_test.go:resolveClaudeBin`). Plugin-prefixed names (`plugin_context7_context7`, `plugin_figma_figma`) pinned because that's what claude actually emits in JSONL `tool_use.name`; the mismatch with the unprefixed `mcp__context7__*` form in `dispatcherBaseTools` (#381) is invisible there because role smoke prompts don't compel context7 calls — a follow-up to align the dispatcher list is scoped out. Codegraph test additionally seeds a one-symbol Go source file (`func PyrycodeSeedSentinel384() {}`) + runs `codegraph index .` (30 s timeout) in the workdir before driving the call — an MCP server with no index returns empty results regardless of protocol health, so the seed closes the false-negative window; `exec.LookPath("codegraph")` miss skips cleanly. Figma test pins a public Figma Community file URL (`figmaTestFileURL = "https://www.figma.com/community/file/1035203688168086460/material-3-design-kit"`) as a file-level const with a top-of-file swap-license comment ("if this URL stops resolving, replace with another stable public file — the test asserts the protocol round-trip, not any one file's content"). Shared `assertMCPToolUsed(t, workdir, result, toolName)` helper consolidates the six identical assertions (`ExitCode == 0`, `SessionID != ""`, assistant `tool_use` with `name == toolName` and non-empty `id`, matching user `tool_result` with `tool_use_id` equal and `len(content) > 0`, trailer `PermissionDenials` nil-or-empty); non-emptiness sufficient because content text is server- and version-dependent and pinning literal content would couple the test to one server's output shape. **Fourth consumer of `parseContentBlocks` + `contentBlock`** (#376 originated; #382 extended with `Content json.RawMessage` + `IsError` — both consumed here; the `Content` field is what makes the `tool_result` non-emptiness check correct per #382's lesson about `b.Text` always being `""` for tool_result blocks). **Fourth consumer of `parseResultTrailer`**; **seventh of `jsonlPathFor`**; **third of `truncate`**. Tight shared system prompt keeps the model on a single-call rail. `RunOpts: MaxTurns=3, Effort="low", Model="claude-haiku-4-5"`. Four real haiku calls per run (~$0.08 with cache warm, matching AC). `t.Parallel()` NOT called — keeps cost predictable, avoids two concurrent `claude mcp list` invocations contending on the same MCP server stdio sockets. ~290 LoC, zero edits to `fixtures.go`, zero production-source changes. **Budget guardrails (#385):** `budget_test.go` adds two top-level tests pinning cost-relevant guarantees the suite did not previously exercise end-to-end. `TestRealClaude_CacheHitWarmsAcrossRuns` runs the same `RunOpts` skeleton twice through `RunPyryAgentRun` against `WithWorktreeAuthenticated(t)` and asserts the second invocation's trailer reports `Usage.CacheReadInputTokens > 0` (primary, `t.Fatalf`) — pinning Anthropic prompt-cache alignment within the 1-hour TTL when (system-prompt, allowed-tools, model, effort) are identical; a regression that breaks cache-key alignment (dynamic content in the system prompt, per-invocation tool-list churn) shows `== 0` here. Diagnostic-only check on the first run's `CacheCreationInputTokens > 0` uses `t.Errorf` (not `t.Fatalf`) so a sub-threshold system prompt surfaces as a soft signal rather than masking the primary on a passing run; the (0+pass vs. 0+0) interpretation matrix is documented inline. `cacheHitSystemPrompt` is a deterministic 5-sentence string concatenation (~300 tokens) sized to clear Haiku 4.5's ~2048-token implicit-cache minimum by margin; the doc comment names the "no dynamic content" constraint (date, run id) explicitly because that is the regression class the test catches. `TestRealClaude_MaxTurnsHonored` runs `pyry agent-run --max-turns=2` against a prompt that natural-completion would require ≥5 turns (numbered 5-line Bash sequence with explicit "do NOT combine" guidance) and asserts five fields on the trailer: `Subtype == "error_max_turns"`, `TerminalReason == "max_turns"`, `NumTurns == 2` (exact — off-by-one fires here), `StopReason != "end_turn"`, `IsError == true`. **`ExitCode == 0` is correct** — `pyry agent-run` exits 0 on a successfully-emitted result trailer regardless of trailer `is_error`; the budget-exhaustion signal lives in the trailer fields, not the subprocess exit code, and a code comment pins this so a future maintainer doesn't "fix" the assertion to `!= 0`. Tool-call-collapse risk pinned in a comment above `maxTurnsPrompt`: if a future haiku revision is smart enough to fire all five `echo`s in one tool_use block (or otherwise complete in ≤2 turns naturally), the assertions fail loudly and the right fix is to bump the prompt to force more turns (e.g. 8 sequential `read X.txt` calls), not to weaken the assertion. Three-field extension to the `resultTrailer` struct in `tool_loop_test.go` adds `IsError bool`, `TerminalReason string`, and `Usage resultTrailerUsage` (plus the new `resultTrailerUsage` sub-struct emitting all four token-count fields); all `omitempty`-tagged so pre-#385 consumers (#376/#381/#382/#384) decode unchanged — single-source extension over duplicating the parser in `budget_test.go`. **Fifth consumer of `parseResultTrailer`**. File-local `truncateStdout([]byte) string` mirrors the inline pattern from `tool_loop_test.go:127`. `RunOpts`: cache-hit uses `MaxTurns=1, AllowedTools=["Read"]`; max-turns uses `MaxTurns=2, AllowedTools=["Bash"]`; both `Effort="low", Model="claude-haiku-4-5"`. Three real haiku calls per run (2+1), ~$0.04 total with cache warm, matching the AC. `t.Parallel()` NOT called — matches the existing realclaude convention; also load-bearing on the cache-hit test where concurrent runs would muddy the "cache warmed by run 1 specifically" diagnostic. ~199 LoC, zero edits to `fixtures.go`, zero production-source changes. **Long-running session JSONL append integrity (#421):** `long_session_test.go` adds `TestRealClaude_LongSessionJSONLIntegrity` — the **first ≥10-turn test in the suite** and **eighth consumer of the fixture trio**, closing the long-turn-count gap left by peer tests that cap at `MaxTurns ∈ {1,2,3,4}`. Seeds the workdir with a 10-line `numbers.txt` (`"1\n2\n…\n10\n"`, `0o600`) then drives a real `claude-haiku-4-5` session through ten distinct single-command Bash operations against it (`wc -l`, `head -n 3`, `tail -n 3`, `sort`, `uniq`, `cat`, `grep 5`, `wc -c`, `awk '{s+=$1} END {print s}'`, `ls -l`) under `RunOpts: MaxTurns=12, Effort="low", AllowedTools=["Bash"], Model="claude-haiku-4-5", Timeout=10*time.Minute`. `MaxTurns=12` is a deliberate 2-turn headroom above the ≥10 floor — early signal if haiku starts collapsing turns despite the anti-chain steering, never trips on the natural-completion path. `Timeout` bumped from the 5-minute `RunPyryAgentRun` default to 10 minutes to absorb cold-network/queue tail latency on a 10-turn run without inflating the success-path budget on short tests — per-test override over fixture-default mutation. Two file-scope prompt constants mirror `budget_test.go`'s `maxTurnsSystemPrompt` / `maxTurnsPrompt` shape (count bumped from 5 to 10, `error_max_turns` framing dropped): `longSessionSystemPrompt` is the one-paragraph anti-chain steering ("use the Bash tool one command at a time, wait for each result before continuing, do NOT combine commands, and do NOT chain commands with && or ;"); `longSessionUserPrompt` enumerates the ten numbered single-command operations and ends with an explicit "Do NOT combine these. Do NOT use && or ; to chain them. After all ten results, summarize what you saw in one short sentence" — the **summary tail is load-bearing for the last-event assertion**: it nudges the model to emit one more `assistant`-kinded `end_turn` text block, giving the last-event-`EndOfTurn && TextChars > 0` assertion something to land on without depending on the model spontaneously producing a closing turn (without it, the LAST assistant entry can be a `tool_use`-only message with `TextChars == 0`, failing the assertion on a non-regression). Six sequential `t.Fatalf` assertions in evaluation order: (1) `ExitCode == 0` (truncated stderr on failure); (2) `SessionID != ""` (truncated stdout on failure); (3) `parseResultTrailer(result.Stdout)` succeeds (truncated stdout on failure); (4) `trailer.NumTurns >= 10` — **failure message names the prompt-design recourse** ("if the model is collapsing turns, expand the prompt or strengthen anti-chain steering — do NOT lower the threshold") at the point of failure so the next maintainer reads the correct fix where it'll be seen, not in a doc buried under newer tickets; (5) single-pass JSONL walk via `ReadJSONL` counting `e.Kind == "assistant" && EndOfTurn` entries (must be ≥10) AND tracking the last `e.Kind == "assistant"` entry, which must satisfy `EndOfTurn && TextChars > 0` (failures include `jsonlPath` from `jsonlPathFor`, the observed counts, and the last entry's `EndOfTurn`/`TextChars` values — same shape as `per_agent_test.go:128-132`); (6) **forward-defensive negative tripwire** `!bytes.Contains(result.Stderr, []byte("bufio.Scanner: token too long"))` — the **literal stdlib error text pinned verbatim** (paraphrasing would silently disable the trip), expected to pass trivially today because no production `bufio.Scanner` exists in the relevant path and `ReadJSONL`'s underlying `jsonl.NewReader` has a 16 MiB cap (`internal/agentrun/jsonl/reader.go:27-30`, read-path is structurally safe); its job is to fire the day someone wires a stdlib `bufio.Scanner` into pyry's production stdout/stderr surface without bumping the buffer and hits a long claude line in this multi-turn run — failure message points the maintainer at the fix (`tool_loop_test.go:210` precedent: bump to 1 MiB). The Scanner tripwire deliberately anchors on the long-running multi-turn test, not a short one — a 1-turn test would not produce a long-enough line to trigger the regression class, so the assertion has to live where the symptomatic input shape exists, even though the assertion itself is forward-defensive. Regression classes guarded together: Scanner buffer downgrade (the literal-stderr trip), off-by-one trailer write (trailer `num_turns` assertion), trailing-newline drift (JSONL walk counting `EndOfTurn`), scanner reset across turn boundaries (≥10 distinct end-of-turn entries), buffer flush gap swallowing the last event (last-assistant-entry `EndOfTurn && TextChars > 0`). Seeded-Bash variant chosen over the documented text-only fallback ("list 10 facts about Helsinki, one per turn") because ten distinct shell commands give the model ten concrete, separable tasks (`wc -l` ≠ `head -n 3` ≠ `tail -n 3` etc.) that resist collapsing into a single combined turn even under brevity pressure; the fallback stays documented as recourse if a future haiku revision collapses the Bash prompt. Reuses `WithWorktreeAuthenticated` (eighth consumer) + `RunPyryAgentRun` + `ReadJSONL` + `parseResultTrailer` (sixth consumer) + `jsonlPathFor` (eighth consumer) + `truncate` (fourth consumer) unchanged — **zero new helpers, zero new exported types, zero new struct fields, zero edits to `fixtures.go` or any peer test file**. One real haiku call per `make e2e-realclaude` run, ~$0.05–$0.10 (higher per-test than peers because of the turn count, but bounded — same order of magnitude as `budget_test.go`'s cache-hit + max-turns pair); `t.Parallel()` NOT called — matches the existing realclaude convention. ~140 LoC including the two prompt constants and inline comments, zero production-source changes. See [`codebase/421.md`](codebase/421.md) for the design rationale. **SIGTERM-mid-tool_use cleanup regression guard (#422, re-specified twice by #1219):** `sigterm_mid_tool_use_test.go` adds `TestRealClaude_SigtermMidToolUse` — the **ninth consumer of the fixture trio** and the SIGTERM-mid-tool_use cell of the resilience matrix `resilience_test.go` (#382) opened. Pins four invariants when pyry receives SIGTERM with a Bash subprocess in flight: (1) full-subtree cleanup — no orphan claude or Bash descendant survives pyry's exit; (2) the on-disk session JSONL ends at a complete envelope boundary; (3) pyry exits within 5 s of SIGTERM (`streamrunner.killGrace`); (4) the signal genuinely landed mid-tool_use. **#1219 replaced the original `sleep 30`/`tail -f /dev/null` fixture and the invariant-4 discriminator twice** after claude's evolving Bash-tool policy defeated both — see [`codebase/1219.md`](codebase/1219.md) for the full history and `sigterm_mid_tool_use_test.go`'s file header (source of truth for the current shape, kept current across future claude-side defeats). Current mechanics: a test-owned FIFO (`holdFIFO`) inverts ownership of the open window — claude's `cat <fifo>` blocks on a write end only the test can release (`t.Cleanup`), so a claude-side timeout/backgrounding policy can no longer end the call early; an event-driven rendezvous (FIFO open, subprocess appears, `tool_use` flushed to disk) times the SIGTERM instead of a fixed sleep. Invariant 4's on-disk check is `classifyBashToolResult`, a four-way classifier (absent / interrupted-by-shutdown / unbounded / bounded-by-claude) that **rejects only on positive evidence claude's own bound ended the call** (read from two surfaces — the `toolUseResult` sidecar and the `tool_use` params, since neither alone covers both of claude's model-chosen and client-default bounding branches) and accepts every other shape, including claude's teardown-interruption marker and an ordinary non-zero exit written during pyry's own reaper race. A pre-SIGTERM JSONL snapshot backs the post-exit check with different fabric (a timing fact, no claude field). `internal/e2e/realclaude/sigterm_mid_tool_use_test.go` grew ~280 LoC → ~1500 LoC (`TestClassifyBashToolResult_ProbeEnvelopes`, 11 credential-free fixture rows including verbatim probe captures, is the classifier's oracle). No production source touched by #1219 (test-only). Same family as the 2026-05-16 fork-bomb incident (`ca8b688`, different shape, same cleanup-paths-leaking class). See [`codebase/422.md`](codebase/422.md) for the original design and [`codebase/1219.md`](codebase/1219.md) for the rework. **Large tool-output regression sensor (#423):** `large_tool_output_test.go` adds `TestRealClaude_LargeToolOutput_ExceedsDefaultScannerBuffer` — the **tenth consumer of the fixture trio** and the >64 KiB-on-one-line cell of the Scanner-buffer regression matrix. Orthogonal to #421's long-session test: that test fires when many short lines accumulate; this one fires when a single line on pyry's stream-json stdout exceeds the 64 KiB stdlib `bufio.Scanner` cap. Today only `permission_protocol_spike_test.go:133` extends a Scanner past the default; if that buffer extension is ever dropped — or if a similarly truncating scanner is wired into pyry's stream-json forwarding path — large tool output gets silently corrupted (Scanner returns `Scan() == false` with no error on a too-long token); this test fails. Drives a real `claude-haiku-4-5` session through a single Bash invocation producing ~80 KiB of stdout in one `tool_result` content block via `RunPyryAgentRun(t, RunOpts{Workdir, Prompt:largeToolOutputUserPrompt, SystemPrompt:largeToolOutputSystemPrompt, AllowedTools:["Bash"], MaxTurns:2, Effort:"low", Model:"claude-haiku-4-5"})`. **Deterministic prompt** (`printf '%80000s' '' | tr ' ' 'A'` — exactly 80,000 literal `A` characters on a single line, no trailing newline) preferred over `/dev/urandom` per the AC so any future fixture-snapshot work doesn't churn; at ~80 KiB it clears the 64 KiB stdlib cap by ~16 KiB and stays comfortably under the 1 MiB extended cap and the 16 MiB on-disk reader cap. The `"exactly as given"` wording in `largeToolOutputSystemPrompt` is load-bearing because paraphrasing risks fewer bytes and a missed 70 KiB threshold; the system prompt also forbids chaining (`"Do not chain commands with && or ;"`) to keep the model on a single-call rail. `MaxTurns: 2` is the structural minimum for the loop `assistant tool_use → user tool_result → assistant text → end_turn`; bumping to 3 is the documented recourse if a future haiku revision collapses turns or refuses the literal command (do NOT weaken the byte-length thresholds or the exact-match assertion). Pins four contracts together: (1) trailer reports `subtype="success"` AND `stop_reason="end_turn"`, (2) the on-disk JSONL `tool_result` content block exceeds 70 KiB (headroom under the ~80 KiB target absorbs framing/encoding), (3) pyry's stdout-forwarded `tool_result` content block has **byte-equal length** to the on-disk twin (the regression sensor — the emitter is contracted to re-emit `ev.Raw` verbatim per `internal/agentrun/streamjson/emitter.go:149-150`, so any non-zero delta means a scanner truncated the forwarding path), (4) stderr does NOT contain `bufio.Scanner: token too long`. **Why a local scanner — NOT a call to `parseResultTrailer`**: the shared `parseResultTrailer` (`tool_loop_test.go:211`) uses the stdlib's 64 KiB default; with an 80 KiB `user`/`tool_result` line on stdout **before** the trailer line, the default scanner returns `Scan() == false` (no error) on the long line, exits the loop, and returns `"no type:result line in stdout"` — a false negative that would mask the very regression this test exists to catch. **The shared `parseResultTrailer` helper is NOT edited** — the regression scope stays one file; the local `findResultTrailer` walks pre-scanned 1 MiB-capped lines from `scanLargeStdoutLines` instead (canonical extension pattern: `scanner.Buffer(make([]byte, 0, 64*1024), 1024*1024)`, mirrors `permission_protocol_spike_test.go:133`). Each returned line is a fresh `make([]byte, len(scanner.Bytes())) + copy` so callers can retain it past subsequent reads (Scanner reuses its internal buffer between `Scan` calls). `scanner.Err()` is propagated so a future buffer-exhaustion regression (a line above 1 MiB) fails loudly rather than silently truncating — silently discarding the error would re-introduce the exact failure mode the test is meant to catch, just at a different threshold. **Exact byte-length match, not a tolerance band** — the emitter writes `ev.Raw + '\n'` verbatim, so the disk-side and stdout-side bytes are byte-identical modulo the trailing newline; comparing `len(stdoutContent) == len(jsonlContent)` is the strongest possible assertion and any non-zero delta IS the regression being guarded against. Tolerance bands have to be justified against an absent transformation step; here there is none, so the tolerance is zero. The failure message names this directly (`"tool_result content length mismatch: stdout=%d bytes, jsonl=%d bytes — this is the regression sensor for #423; a scanner in pyry's stdout forwarding path has truncated the tool_result content block (the emitter is contracted to re-emit ev.Raw verbatim, see internal/agentrun/streamjson/emitter.go:149-150)"`) AND cites the emitter line so a future PR that introduces a transformation step has to update both the assertion and the contract together. **Invariant to `Content` shape**: claude may emit `tool_result.content` as either a bare JSON string (common for raw Bash stdout) OR a nested `[{type:"text",text:"..."}]` array; both shapes are byte-equal through `Emit`, so comparing raw `json.RawMessage` byte lengths between disk and stdout is shape-invariant — the test does NOT decode `Content` further. The 70 KiB threshold is on the raw byte length including JSON framing (quotes for the string shape, the wrapping array for the nested shape) and absorbs both. **Four file-local helpers** (`scanLargeStdoutLines`, `findResultTrailer`, `findBashToolResultBlock`, `findToolResultContentByID`, ~70 LoC combined) and two file-scope prompt constants (`largeToolOutputSystemPrompt`, `largeToolOutputUserPrompt`); helpers stay file-local until a second test needs the same shape, mirroring `resilience_test.go`'s precedent (`resolveClaudeBin`/`runClaudeDirect`) and #422's `spawnPyryAgentRun`/`processesInProcessGroup`. `findBashToolResultBlock` walks JSONL events in a single pass: on each `assistant` entry, decode content blocks via the shared `parseContentBlocks`, capture the first `{Type:"tool_use", Name:"Bash", ID:!=""}`; on subsequent `user` entries, find the first `{Type:"tool_result", ToolUseID==bashID}` and return its `Content` (`json.RawMessage`) plus block index. `findToolResultContentByID` walks the pre-scanned stdout lines and decodes each via `parseContentBlocks`, returning the first `tool_result` block whose `tool_use_id` matches. Per-line parse errors are silently skipped in both (matches `tool_loop_test.go:75-80`) — user envelopes with a plain-string `content` (not an array) fail the array-decode and are correctly ignored. **Reuses unchanged** (no new fixture-package surface, no struct extensions): `WithWorktreeAuthenticated` (tenth consumer — chosen over plain `WithWorktree` because the assertions depend on claude actually invoking Bash with the literal command; a synthetic-auth envelope would skip the `tool_use` event entirely and the "no Bash tool_use observed" assertion would fire on a non-regression), `RunPyryAgentRun`+`RunOpts`+`RunResult`, `ReadJSONL`+`JSONLEntry`, `parseContentBlocks`+`contentBlock` (#376 originated, #382 extended; **sixth consumer** — the existing fields `Type`/`Name`/`ID`/`ToolUseID`/`Content` cover exactly what the helpers need, and **`Content json.RawMessage` extended in #382 is the load-bearing field** that makes the byte-length equality assertion work without a struct extension — pre-#382 the test would have needed a struct extension), `resultTrailer` (the local `findResultTrailer` decodes into it directly; the `parseResultTrailer` function itself is bypassed for the 64 KiB-cap reason above), `jsonlPathFor` (#364, **tenth consumer**), `truncate` (#381, **sixth consumer**). **Failure-message discipline** distinguishes "test setup wrong" (`"no Bash tool_use observed in JSONL assistant entries (model may have refused to run the literal command; sharpen the system-prompt's \"exactly as given\" wording or bump MaxTurns to 3)"`, `"JSONL tool_result content byte length = %d, want > 70000 (target ~80 KiB; model may have paraphrased the printf/tr pipeline — inspect the JSONL for the actual command and stdout it ran)"`) from "production broken" (the byte-length mismatch message named above) — the next maintainer routes the response correctly at the point of failure rather than triage at first by guess. **Forward-defensive stderr substring tripwire** (`!bytes.Contains(result.Stderr, []byte("bufio.Scanner: token too long"))`) mirrors `long_session_test.go:135-139` — pins the literal stdlib error text verbatim (paraphrasing would silently disable it); failure message points the maintainer at the fix ("bump its buffer to 1 MiB (see tool_loop_test.go:210 note)"). One real haiku call per run, ~$0.02 with cache warm (large `tool_result` inflates token count above peer haiku-low tests but is bounded). `t.Parallel()` is NOT called — matches the existing realclaude convention. ~252 LoC including the package-level comment block, two prompt constants, and inline rationale, zero edits to `fixtures.go` or any peer test file, zero production-source changes. See [`codebase/423.md`](codebase/423.md) for the design rationale. **`/doctor` prompt-injection regression sensor (#487):** `doctor_poisoning_regression_test.go` adds `TestRealClaude_DoctorPoisoningRegression` — the **eleventh consumer of the fixture trio**, guarding that the per-spawn settings JSON pyry writes is one claude accepts at startup. Walks the JSONL for the first `Kind == "user"` entry, decodes its `Raw` content via file-local `decodeUserContent` (coerces both observed claude shapes: string literal AND `[{type:"text", text:"..."}]` array; mirrors the silent-skip policy of `bashInvokedInRaw`), asserts the content does NOT contain the verbatim `/doctor` template opening substring `"Help me fix the issues reported by /doctor below."` (observed in the ticket's reproduction `out.jsonl`). Defence-in-depth: requires at least one `assistant` event in the JSONL — empty assistant set under a non-poisoned session indicates a different upstream failure that masks the regression-guard's signal (`t.Fatalf` with diagnostic context, not silent pass). Failure diagnostic includes the verbatim user-entry content (truncated to ~512 bytes), the JSONL path, and the operator-visible direction `"claude is rejecting the per-spawn settings JSON at startup — see #487"`. Does NOT assert `stop_reason: end_turn` — that AC item is satisfied by the manual reproduction in the ticket Context, not by the automated test (pinning it would couple the test to upstream model-behaviour detail beyond the contract being guarded). `RunOpts: AllowedTools=["Read"], MaxTurns=1, Effort="low", Model="claude-haiku-4-5"`. One real haiku call per run (~$0.01). Reuses unchanged fixtures (`WithWorktreeAuthenticated`, `RunPyryAgentRun`, `ReadJSONL`, `jsonlPathFor`) with zero new helper surface, zero edits to `fixtures.go` or any peer test file. **Post-mortem note**: `ptyrunner_byte_equivalence_test.go` (#482) should have caught the invalid `"deny"` literal but silently skipped under Max-only environments (`WithWorktreeAuthenticated` requires `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`); the fixture-wide auth-skip cleanup to recognise Max-plan credentials is the explicit architectural follow-up. See [`codebase/487.md`](codebase/487.md) for the design rationale. **#854** adds `interactive_bootstrap_liveness_test.go` — the only test in the suite that drives the daemon's interactive relay path (a real spawned `pyry` + real claude + a headless phone over the v2 wire) rather than `pyry agent-run`; RED/GREEN oracle for the fresh-daemon bootstrap-reply deadlock fix in `cmd/pyry/interactive_turn_stream_v2.go`'s `resolveTarget`. See [`codebase/854.md`](codebase/854.md). **#1028** adds `interactive_conversation_lifecycle_test.go` — first real-claude coverage of the conversation-management verbs (create → rename → archive → unarchive → delete on one conversation, liveness `send_message` between unarchive and delete), reusing the #997/#854 harness verbatim; only new code is `readConversationIDsOnDisk` for the delete on-disk-registry assertion; test-only, zero production files touched. See [`codebase/1028.md`](codebase/1028.md). **#1030** adds `interactive_modal_resolution_test.go` — first real-claude coverage of the modal-resolution verbs (`modal_answer`/`modal_cancel`), the hardest #963 family since the trigger is real claude choosing to call a gated Bash tool under default permission mode rather than a test env-var; new `spawnPermissionDaemon` drops `--dangerously-skip-permissions` from #854's spawn to let a real permission modal surface, phone pairs WITH `--allow-remote-permissions`; Phase A answers and proves a continuation `assistant_delta`, Phase B cancels and observes the `modal_dismissed{cancelled,remote}` broadcast via the new Type-only `drainForControlEvent` (fire-and-broadcast, no correlated reply); zero production files touched, #798 daemon surfacer needed no change. See [`codebase/1030.md`](codebase/1030.md). **#1031** adds `interactive_session_control_liveness_test.go` — first real-claude coverage of the session-control respawn verbs (`new_session` rotate via `/clear`, `set_session_settings` respawn via a live restart), the last #963 family; a sequential spine on one daemon/one conversation (turn 1 → new_session-rotate → turn 2 → settings-respawn → turn 3) pairs each verb's liveness send with a deterministic on-disk `sessions.json` bootstrap-row anchor (baseline-id-then-changed / persisted `Model=="haiku"`) so the assertion is non-vacuous against a silently no-op'd respawn — mirrors #1004/#1005's fake-tier non-vacuity design one layer up the stack; `Model:"haiku"` is the credential-safe choice because it duplicates the daemon's base `--model haiku` (last-wins) rather than spawning a different real model in a pre-ship gate; real claude rotates on every `/clear` (unlike fakeclaude's one-shot trigger), so the test settles the id to a stable value before each post-control `send_message`; only new code is the `readBootstrapRow`/`waitBootstrapIDSettled`/`waitBootstrapModel` on-disk reader family, zero production files touched. See [`codebase/1031.md`](codebase/1031.md). **#1153** adds `interactive_stream_liveness_test.go` — first real-claude coverage of the stream-json interactive runner (`interactive_runner: "stream-json"`, #1081; every prior interactive-relay real-claude test ran the default PTY runner); transcribes #854's daemon body with three deltas — `writeStreamInteractiveConfig` flips the production config toggle before spawn, drives one turn (parity with #1141), and drains to completion via the new two-milestone `drainForCompletedTurn` (non-empty `assistant_delta` then terminal `turn_state{idle}`, no echo assertion since real claude's words are non-deterministic) where #854's drain stops at the first delta; config-writer is a standalone helper (not a spawn wrapper) so the blocked-by permission rider #1154 can compose it with `spawnPermissionDaemon`; fresh package-private seed constants avoid redeclaring #854's file-private ones. Zero production files touched. See [`codebase/1153.md`](codebase/1153.md). #1172 adds the running-turn trigger infra consumed by #1176 (interrupt): a bounded, silent, foreground Bash loop (port of desktop `e1fe219`) holds a turn in `turn_state{responding}`, proven by `drainForResponding`/`assertNoIdleWithin` failing loud on an early `idle`; see [`codebase/1172.md`](codebase/1172.md). #1175 adds the security-relevant permission **deny** round-trip (security-sensitive, architect review PASS): reuses #1154's harness, swaps to `reject_once`, and pins the fail-closed proof to the explicit answer via a `modal_dismissed{Source=="remote", Outcome=="reject_once"}` attribution assertion (else a timeout-deny could masquerade as the reject) plus a workdir walk proving the gated `Write` never executed; rework `denyModalsUntilIdle` answers every retry modal since real haiku retries a denied tool at least once, bounded by a retry-count cap and wall-clock budget; see [`codebase/1175.md`](codebase/1175.md). **#1582** adds `interactive_stream_inband_model_test.go` — live proof that #1581's in-band model-change delivery reaches a running child with no respawn; reads claude's own per-turn `system`/`init` announcement off the `streamsup.Config.Stdout` seam, upstream of the parser (which must keep dropping `init` in silence, per the #833 no-log posture). Two assertion pairs (model changed / no teardown), each the sole red for a distinct `-overlay` mutant, proven against both a pre-#1581 tree and a mutated current tree. Zero production files touched. See [`codebase/1582.md`](codebase/1582.md). `set_permission_mode_probe_test.go` (#1595) drives four direct-`exec` claude children through an identical two-turn probe to measure whether a `set_permission_mode` control-channel request lands in-band on the bypass posture the way `/model` does; see [`set-permission-mode-inband-probe.md`](features/set-permission-mode-inband-probe.md) and [`codebase/1595.md`](codebase/1595.md). |
| [e2e-liverelay.md](features/e2e-liverelay.md) | `internal/e2e/liverelay` (#968, build tag `e2e_liverelay`) — opt-in single-test suite (`TestLiveRelay_ListConversationsRoundTrip`) that proves the daemon ↔ **real** `pyrycode-relay` pairing (device identity → WS connect → Noise IK handshake → `list_conversations` round-trip), closing the gap left by every other relay e2e test running against the in-process `fakerelay`. Design choice: spawns a locally built `pyrycode-relay` binary from the sibling `../pyrycode-relay` checkout (resolved via `PYRY_LIVERELAY_BIN` → `PYRY_RELAY_REPO` → default sibling path, `t.Skipf` loud if none found) on a loopback-bound, `--insecure-listen`, credential-free listener — NOT the production URL + a persisted device identity, deferring that audit to a future ticket. Ports `testV2DaemonListConversationsRoundTrip` (`internal/e2e/relay_v2_daemon_test.go`) 1:1 (bare base relay URL so `resolveDialURL` appends `/v1/server`; imports `fakephone` directly since it speaks the real phone wire); transcribes daemon-spawn/Noise-driving helpers from `internal/e2e/realclaude`. Readiness gate diverges from the spec's dial-retry proposal: the real relay's `ClientHandler` accepts-then-4404s an unbound `/v1/client` upgrade, so a dial-retry can't detect registration — `waitBinaryRegistered` polls `/healthz`'s (unauthenticated, un-rate-limited) `connected_binaries` field instead. Wired into `preship` (offline-capable, no credentials), not `check`/`e2e`. See [`codebase/968.md`](codebase/968.md). |
| [set-model-inband-probe.md](features/set-model-inband-probe.md) | `set_model` control-channel request (#2279) — measured against claude 2.1.259, on pyry's stream-json argv pinned `--model claude-haiku-4-5`: the subtype is accepted and **validates nothing at the control layer**. An unservable model string still draws `control_response.subtype:"success"`, is echoed back as the session's model on the next `system/init` line, and only fails one turn later as a turn-level error (`result.is_error:true`, but `result.subtype` stays `"success"`) — unlike `set_permission_mode`'s genuine `error`-subtype refusal, so a caller cannot read a rejected model change off the ack or the response subtype. All three reset spellings (key omitted, explicit `null`, string `"default"`) behave identically and return to the **account default**, not to the session's pinned launch flag — so #2280's encoder needs no pointer, a plain string suffices. #2281 has to read the following turn's `is_error` (or stderr's `unrecognized_model` line) or validate against the published model list before sending. No production code changes in this ticket. |
| [set-permission-mode-inband-probe.md](features/set-permission-mode-inband-probe.md) | `set_permission_mode` control-channel request (#1595) — measured against claude 2.1.220, on pyry's actual stream-json argv (no `--permission-prompt-tool`, no `--allowed-tools`): **revoke** (`bypassPermissions → default`) lands in-band, no respawn, confirmed on `control_response`, the next `init.permissionMode` echo, and a behavioural probe against a same-posture control child; **enable** (`default → bypassPermissions`) is refused with `Cannot set permission mode to bypassPermissions because the session was not launched with --dangerously-skip-permissions` — a third error string not previously read out of the binary. So a bypass-posture *escalation* is NOT reachable over the daemon's stdin control channel, but a *revocation* is. `inBandDeliverable` (`internal/sessions/pool.go`) can therefore split on the direction of a YOLO change; #1596 decides whether to add the production writer. No production code changes in this ticket. **#1603 added the production writer** (`RevokeBypass`/`WriteBypassRevocation`, revoke only, mode fixed in code); no production caller yet (#1604). |
| [bypass-reescalation-probe.md](features/bypass-reescalation-probe.md) | In-band bypass RE-escalation (#2060) — a child launched *with* `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, downgraded in-band to `default`, then asked back into `bypassPermissions` in the same child: RE-ESCALATION APPLIED at claude 2.1.239, the first committed capture of #1686's uncaptured hand observation. `enable` (#1595's no-flag negative) still refuses, byte-identical, so the probe still discriminates. Together: claude gates the escalation on the **launch argv**, not the session's current mode. Three turns per arm (not two), because the post-re-escalation read is a turn 3 and #1595's index-symmetry rule requires a matching-index control; a downgrade gate (`reescalateDowngrade`) confirms the child actually left bypass before the second request, without which the verdict is vacuous. No production writer, no daemon behaviour change — `permissionModeAllowed` is untouched; #1686 decides what is built on the answer. |
| [bypass-approval-argv-probe.md](features/bypass-approval-argv-probe.md) | Bypass launch argv vs. production's approval flags (#2061) — measures #1686's proposed argv (bypass **plus** the four approval flags production actually spawns with) at claude 2.1.239. Both questions came back "yes": the approval bridge survives the combined argv and comes back after an in-band downgrade (verdict taken on whether the stub socket was *consulted*, never on behaviour — a bridge-gone child and a bridge-allowed child read identically otherwise), and no race window was observed across three consecutive spawns that wrote the downgrade before turn 1. Neither answer closes #1686 as answered-no. Records two reusable traps: a "was it acked before X" read needs the driver to report state at the stage boundary rather than inferring order from stdout (claude's `system/init` line arrives *after* an early downgrade lands, not before); and a per-run redactor can only scrub paths the test itself puts on the argv, not paths claude echoes back (its `cwd` in `system/init`). |
| [permission-mode-switch-inband-probe.md](features/permission-mode-switch-inband-probe.md) | In-band permission-mode switch (#2041) — `acceptEdits`/`dontAsk`/`plan`/`auto` all switch on a running child in-band at claude 2.1.239, extending #1595's `default`/`bypassPermissions` pair. `auto` is refused **per model** (a model not publishing `supportsAutoMode: true`), never per mechanism; the model is picked from the run's own live `initialize` list, never a fixed table, since the committed capture and a live run drifted on one row's name at the same binary version. |
| [permission-protocol-spike.md](features/permission-protocol-spike.md) | `--permission-prompt-tool stdio` compatibility findings: Claude Code 2.1.143 silently bypassed the gate in the historical six-mode capture, while 2.1.259 emits `control_request/can_use_tool` and accepts a correlated deny response before Bash executes. The current assertion-based live test keeps stdin open and proves denial with a side-effect witness; the historical raw fixtures and their regression contract remain preserved. |
| [update-package.md](features/update-package.md) | `internal/update` — building blocks for `pyry update`: `ParseLatestRelease` + `CompareVersions` (#179, leading-`v` + pre-release suffix tolerance); `AssetName` + `ParseChecksumsFile` + `VerifySHA256` (#180, GoReleaser tarball filename for `linux/darwin × amd64/arm64` plus SHA-256 verify); `DetectRestartCommand` (#181, `RestartProbe` → launchctl/systemctl argv, launchd wins on tie); `Fetcher` (#182, zero-value-usable `net/http` wrapper with `FetchLatestRelease` + `FetchAsset`); `ExtractBinary` (#186, in-memory tar.gz → binary bytes via `filepath.Base` matcher); `AtomicReplace` (#187, write-temp-in-same-dir + fchmod + fsync + rename); `VerifySignature` (#776, pure `crypto/ed25519.Verify` over the raw checksums bytes; length-checks `pub`/`sig` before `ed25519.Verify` so a wrong-length key returns a sentinel instead of panicking); sentinels `ErrMalformedRelease` / `ErrInvalidVersion` / `ErrUnsupportedPlatform` / `ErrAssetNotInChecksums` / `ErrMalformedChecksums` / `ErrChecksumMismatch` / `ErrBinaryNotInArchive` / `ErrMalformedArchive` / `ErrInvalidSignature` / `ErrInvalidPublicKey` for `errors.Is` branching |
| [install-script.md](features/install-script.md) | `install.sh` — curl-piped release downloader (`PYRY_VERSION`, `PYRY_INSTALL_DIR`) + post-install smoke check (#203) that detects a default-named running service (`launchctl print | grep 'state = running'` on darwin, `systemctl --user is-active --quiet pyry` on linux), restarts it, and probes `${INSTALL_DIR}/pyry status` for the `Uptime: 2562047h47m16.854775807s` sentinel from #202; four exit codes (0 healthy / 2 sentinel / 3 dial-fail / 4 restart-fail); leans on `pyry status`'s built-in dial retry (#199) + 5s deadline as the grace period (no extra `sleep`); `internal/install/test_smoke_classify.sh` is the bash-only unit test for `classify_status` |
| [config-package.md](features/config-package.md) | `internal/config` — `Config` (`RelayURL`, `DebugCapture`, `InteractiveRunner`), `DefaultConfig()`, `Load(path)` overlay-decode loader for `~/.pyry/config.json`; `InteractiveRunner` (#1081) toggles `"pty"`/`"stream-json"`, validated + selected at the `cmd/pyry` composition root, not in `Load` |
| [identity-package.md](features/identity-package.md) | `internal/identity` — `ServerID` (UUIDv4-shaped string newtype), `NewServerID()` (crypto/rand + version/variant nibbles, panic-on-rng-fail), `ParseServerID(s)` returning `ErrInvalidServerID` for malformed input, `LoadOrCreate(path)` (#207, first-run mint + atomic-rename persist at mode 0600 / parent 0700, single trailing newline tolerated, never rewrites on parse failure — corruption is operator-escalated, never auto-regenerated, since paired devices bind tokens to a specific server-id); foundation for Phase 3 pairing/relay routing, no consumers wired in this slice |
| [keys-package.md](features/keys-package.md) | `internal/keys` (#438) — binary-side X25519 static keypair for Mobile Protocol v2 (Noise_IK). `StaticKey` (unexported `priv`/`pub [32]byte`) + by-value `PrivateKey() [32]byte` / `PublicKey() [32]byte` accessors (mutating the return does not alias internal storage, pinned by `TestStaticKey_AccessorsReturnByValue`); `LoadOrCreate(baseDir, daemonName string) (*StaticKey, error)` as the package's single entry point, owning the full `(baseDir, daemonName) → filepath.Join(baseDir, daemonName, "static_key.json")` mapping so a caller-precomputed path cannot bypass the allowlist; `validDaemonName` (unexported, white-box tested) — length 1..64, every byte in `[a-z0-9_-]`, no leading `-`, character-by-character scan with no regex, rejects empty/`.`/`..`/`/`/`\`/uppercase/whitespace/NUL/multi-byte UTF-8/leading hyphen/oversize-65; reject path returns `fmt.Errorf("keys: invalid daemon name %q: %w", daemonName, ErrInvalidDaemonName)` and performs zero filesystem operations (pinned by `TestLoadOrCreate_InvalidDaemonName` which asserts `baseDir` has zero entries after every reject row); `newStaticKey()` via stdlib `crypto/ecdh.X25519().GenerateKey(rand.Reader)` (panics on `crypto/rand` failure mirroring `identity.NewServerID`'s discipline — zero-entropy keys would break the Noise_IK authentication model), `.Bytes()`/`.PublicKey().Bytes()` give the raw 32-byte material wire-compatible bit-for-bit with `flynn/noise.DHKey`. On-disk schema locked to `docs/protocol-mobile.md` § Static keys — binary side: `onDiskKey{Version int, Algorithm string, PrivateKey, PublicKey string (base64.StdEncoding padded), CreatedAt time.Time (RFC 3339 UTC via MarshalJSON after t.UTC() to strip monotonic-clock)}` with constants `schemaVersion=1`, `algorithmName="Noise_25519"`, `filename="static_key.json"` — `base64.StdEncoding` chosen deliberately over `RawURLEncoding` (opposite of `internal/pair`'s QR/wire encoding) because on-disk JSON gets padded base64. Atomic-write recipe: `os.MkdirAll(dir, 0o700)` → `os.CreateTemp(dir, ".static-key-*.tmp")` → **`os.Chmod(tmp, 0o600)` BEFORE write** (defeats macOS umask collapsing the requested mode, same defence as `internal/identity/store.go:77`) → `json.Marshal` body → `f.Write` → `f.Sync` → `f.Close` → `os.Rename(tmp, path)`; `defer os.Remove(tmp)` cleans partial state on any earlier failure; rename is the commit point so no observer sees partial bytes. Three-way switch on `os.ReadFile` error (`nil` → parse; `errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist)` → mint+persist; else → wrapped non-`ErrCorruptKeyFile` error — preserves I/O-vs-corruption sentinel distinction, mirrors `internal/identity/store.go:38-46`, trapped by `TestLoadOrCreate_NonENOENTReadErrorIsNotCorruption` which makes `static_key.json` itself a directory). Seven-step load-side validation (all fail-fast returning `ErrCorruptKeyFile`-wrapped errors): `json.Unmarshal` syntax → `version == schemaVersion` → `algorithm == algorithmName` → `base64.StdEncoding.DecodeString(private_key)` succeeds + length 32 → same for `public_key` → `!created_at.IsZero()` (encoding/json's `time.Time` unmarshaller already rejects non-RFC3339 strings at step 1 so one `IsZero()` covers both absent and malformed) → `ecdh.X25519().NewPrivateKey(priv).PublicKey().Bytes()` matches stored `pub` via `crypto/subtle.ConstantTimeCompare` (anti-tamper; constant-time buys nothing for public material but matches project hygiene posture per `internal/devices.VerifyToken`). **Existing files are NEVER overwritten on the load path even on validation failure** — paired phones bind to a specific keypair and silent regeneration would invalidate every pairing without operator awareness (same invariant as `internal/identity.LoadOrCreate`; pinned by `TestLoadOrCreate_CorruptJSONReturnsSentinel` which asserts post-call bytes are byte-identical to the seeded fixture). Sentinels `ErrInvalidDaemonName` + `ErrCorruptKeyFile` (both `errors.Is`-matchable); bare wrapped `fmt.Errorf` (no sentinel) covers filesystem I/O errors so an operator can distinguish corruption from permissions/disk-full. Error messages include the file path (operator-actionable) but NEVER include file contents, base64 fields, or decoded bytes — pinned by `TestLoadOrCreate_CorruptJSONErrorDoesNotLeakPrivateKey` which seeds a known base64 private key, triggers `algorithm` mismatch, asserts `err.Error()` substring-free of the known base64 (single defensive assertion against future error-message refactors). SECURITY contract in package doc-comment + `StaticKey.PrivateKey()` doc-comment: returned `[32]byte` is the X25519 static secret, MUST NOT be logged / wrapped into error / emitted on a wire (mirrors `internal/devices`'s plaintext-token contract); no `slog` calls in the package at all. Daemon-name validator deliberately NOT consolidated with `cmd/pyry/main.go:sanitizeName` — `sanitizeName` is a transformer that replaces bad chars with `_` and permits `.`/uppercase, whereas this REJECTS; the difference that matters is posture, not charset — a transformer always returns *some* usable name (`sanitizeName("MyBox") == "MyBox"`, `sanitizeName("../etc") == ".._etc"`, both refused by `validDaemonName`), so it can never satisfy this package's zero-write reject contract (`sanitizeName` itself was hardened in #1508 to never return `.` or `..`, see `docs/knowledge/codebase/1508.md`). Two-file split (`static_key.go` pure type + validator + generator; `store.go` `LoadOrCreate` + JSON schema + atomic write) mirrors `internal/identity` (`server_id.go` + `store.go`); same-package (white-box) tests so unexported `validDaemonName`/`newStaticKey`/`onDiskKey`/`schemaVersion` are directly testable. Concurrency: not safe for concurrent use against the same path; bootstrap runs once at daemon startup before any goroutines fan out. Package owns the full `(baseDir, daemonName) → absolute-path` mapping (caller-precomputed path would bypass the allowlist); package does NOT validate `baseDir` (the allowlist defends against `daemonName` injection, not against an attacker-controlled `baseDir`). Fifth instance of the per-daemon JSON-file pattern + atomic-write recipe (after `internal/sessions`, `internal/identity`, `internal/devices`, `internal/conversations`); `docs/PROJECT-MEMORY.md` § Project-level conventions named "a fifth registry forces extraction" but the architect re-evaluated at this boundary and concluded the helper stays duplicated until a sixth — the umask-defence variant matters and the recipe is ~25 lines, extraction risks one helper shared across stores where the defence matters and stores where it does not. **Filesystem hardening landed in #439** as the hard prerequisite for any production consumer (#432 QR payload, #433 Noise wrapper): two new `errors.Is`-matchable sentinels `ErrInsecureKeyDirMode` / `ErrInsecureKeyFileMode` (distinct because the operator's chmod target differs — lumping under one `ErrInsecurePermissions` would force parsing the wrapped message); `ensureSecureKeyDir(dir)` rejects parent-dir `mode & 0o077 != 0` (or non-directory) with a wrapped sentinel naming the path and observed mode in `%#o`, runs `MkdirAll(0o700)` + re-stat on first run (defensive against ACLs / future stdlib changes widening the requested mode — `mkdir(2)` cannot itself widen under a normal umask); `LoadOrCreate` switched from `os.ReadFile` to `os.Lstat` + strict-equality `0o600` check + `openSecureKeyFile` (`os.OpenFile(path, O_RDONLY|syscall.O_NOFOLLOW, 0)` then `io.ReadAll`); belt-and-suspenders symlink defence is two-fabric — `Lstat` catches symlink-at-rest (a symlink's own mode `0755`/`0777` fails the `== 0600` check before the open ever runs), `O_NOFOLLOW` catches the dynamic swap during the TOCTOU window between Lstat and open with `ELOOP`; **no auto-`chmod` ever** (the loud-failure contract IS the security feature — auto-repair would hide packaging bugs that unpacked the key as `0644`, and would hand a hostile sibling process under the same UID the exact moment of vulnerability they need); error wrapping `keys: %s: mode %#o: %w` names path + observed mode + sentinel and never the file contents, decoded bytes, or resolved symlink target (`*os.PathError.Path` is the caller-supplied path, not the resolved target; no `os.Readlink` enrichment); `syscall.O_NOFOLLOW` canonical on both Linux + macOS (no `golang.org/x/sys/unix` dep added). Directory check uses `& 0o077 != 0` (permits narrower modes like `0600`/`0500` — security contract is "no g/o access", not "exactly 0700"; narrower modes are non-functional and the daemon fails loudly with `EACCES` on subsequent operation); file check is strict `!= 0o600` because the package writes at exactly `0600` and anything else (including narrower) indicates tampering. Pre-existing `TestLoadOrCreate_NonENOENTReadErrorIsNotCorruption` continues to pass after the refactor — the directory-at-key-path trap now surfaces as `ErrInsecureKeyFileMode` (Lstat sees directory mode ≠ `0600`) before the read happens, neither `Is(ErrCorruptKeyFile)` nor `Is(ErrInvalidDaemonName)` per the test's assertions. New tests `TestLoadOrCreate_InsecureDirModeRejected` (table `0700` accept / `0750`/`0755`/`0701` reject; asserts message contains dir path + observed mode `%#o` and no private-key leak, file bytes unchanged), `TestLoadOrCreate_InsecureFileModeRejected` (table `0644`/`0640`/`0660`/`0666` reject; chmod-back-to-`0600` proves the reject path didn't mutate content), `TestLoadOrCreate_FreshCreateUnderHostileUmaskStill0700` (`syscall.Umask(0)` is process-global so non-parallel; asserts post-create dir mode `0700` and file mode `0600`), `TestLoadOrCreate_SymlinkRefusedOnRead` (symlink decoy with sentinel keypair; asserts `errors.Is(ErrInsecureKeyFileMode)` and message contains neither decoy path nor key bytes), `TestLoadOrCreate_InsecureFileModeErrorDoesNotLeakPrivateKey` (extension of the existing no-leak shape for the new sentinel). `io.ReadAll` on the opened file is uncapped — CR `[INFO]` finding consistent with spec § Security threat #7 (hostile process under same UID is out-of-scope today); revisit `io.LimitReader` if the threat model expands. |
| [v2-session-manager.md](features/v2-session-manager.md) | `internal/relay` v2 session manager (#445, extended #446/#452/#454/#453/#450/#462/#549/#571/#588/#618/#626/#610/#647/#663/#727/#717/#725/#707/#723/#774/#777/#812/#813/#831/#845/#848/#874/#875/#877/#909/#911/#912/#1008/#965/#1121/#1125/#1525/#1505) — fourth surface of `internal/relay` (alongside `connection.go` outbound dial, `auth.go` shared WS close-code constants, `handlers/` per-envelope-type processors): per-`conn_id` state machine that completes a Mobile Protocol v2 Noise_IK handshake, validates the AEAD-sealed device-token piggybacked in IK message 1 early-data, emits `noise_resp` (IK message 2 carrying `hello_ack`) on success, and refuses every out-of-state inner frame at the WS-close layer. **No application dispatch** — `noise_msg` frames that reach `open` are dropped silently; the open-state handler chain in `internal/relay/handlers/` is structurally unreachable from this slice (the load-bearing invariant the gating test pins for AC #4). Four-state lifecycle `V2StateAwaitingInit` → `V2StateHandshakeComplete` → `V2StateOpen` → `V2StateClosed` with `handshakeComplete` observably distinct from `open` even though both can be set inside the same `noise_init` handler — the externally-controlled `state` field exists so a same-package gating test (`TestV2Session_Gating_NoiseMsgInHandshakeComplete_4401`) directly assigns the substate + injects hand-rolled `(send, recv)` CipherStates and structurally proves the "handler chain unreachable from handshakeComplete" invariant; any future refactor that defers token validation cannot silently remove the gating row. Two new exported WS close-code constants `StatusProtocolMismatch websocket.StatusCode = 4421` (state-machine / discriminator violations) and `StatusHandshakeFailure websocket.StatusCode = 4426` (Noise_IK failure before CipherStates exist) declared alongside the existing `StatusUnauthorized = 4401` (reused unchanged from `auth.go`); style mirrors `auth.go`'s exported-close-code pattern. New wire-format types in `internal/protocol/v2envelope.go` (~28 LOC): `V2Version = 2` const, three discriminator constants `TypeNoiseInit` / `TypeNoiseResp` / `TypeNoiseMsg`, `InnerFrameV2{Version int, Type string, Data string}` struct (Data is `base64.StdEncoding`-padded; decoded length cap 65535 bytes = Noise framework per-message limit, enforced at the JSON-decode boundary so oversized payloads never reach `Responder.ReadInit`). One-line additive modification to `internal/protocol/handshake.go`: appends `Token string \`json:"token,omitempty"\`` to `HelloClientPayload` — in-band carrier of the device-pairing token under v2 (`docs/protocol-mobile.md` § Authentication line 420 moves the token from the WS-header / routing envelope into AEAD-sealed early-data); `omitempty` keeps v1 round-trip byte-identical for existing fixtures and tests; the v1 `RoutingEnvelope.Token` field is NOT removed in this slice (v1 dispatcher still consumes it). Manager surface: `V2Session` (unexported fields incl. `*noise.Responder` + paired `*noise.CipherState` send/recv), `(*V2Session).State() V2SessionState` accessor, `V2SessionConfig{Frames, Outbound, StaticPriv, Devices, ServerID, Logger}`, `V2SessionManager`, `NewV2SessionManager(cfg) (*V2SessionManager, error)` (panics on nil Frames/Logger like `dispatch.New`; wrapped error on nil Outbound/Devices, empty ServerID, wrong-length StaticPriv), `(*V2SessionManager).Run(ctx) error` (blocks until Frames closes or ctx is cancelled; every per-conn session is dropped on return). Outbound is a function (not channel) so production wires `(*relay.Connection).Send` directly and tests substitute an in-memory recorder; non-nil errors from Outbound are log-and-drop (mirrors v1's `cmd/pyry/relay.go` forwarder posture). **Single dispatch goroutine** owns all `*V2Session` mutation; `m.sessions` map mutated exclusively by `Run`; no lock — the loop IS the lock for the non-goroutine-safe `flynn/noise` `CipherState` mutable nonce counter (same contract as #433 pushed to the per-conn-dispatch boundary). Intentionally simpler than `internal/dispatch.Dispatcher`'s per-conn fan-out — this slice runs no handlers, every frame is synchronous Noise (~100 µs) or sub-µs JSON, so single-goroutine fan-in is correct; per-conn fan-out is a #446 concern. State transitions: `noise_init` in `awaitingInit` runs the handshake — lazy-construct `noise.Responder` from `cfg.StaticPriv` (one X25519 derivation per real handshake, not at session-creation; zero cost for conns that never send a frame) → `Responder.ReadInit(data)` → `protocol.Envelope` decode + `Type == TypeHello` check + `HelloClientPayload` decode → marshal `HelloAckPayload{ProtocolVersion:"v2", ServerID, ConnID}` envelope → `Responder.WriteResp(ackJSON)` returns response bytes + paired `(send, recv)` CipherStates → state → `handshakeComplete` → marshal `InnerFrameV2{Type: noise_resp, Data: base64(respMsg)}` → `cfg.Devices.Validate(hello.Token)`: hit ⇒ emit noise_resp envelope + state → `open` (log `event=v2.handshake.accept`, `conn_id`, `device_name`); miss ⇒ emit noise_resp first (so AEAD channel exists on wire), then AEAD-seal an `auth.invalid_token` error envelope under `s.send`, wrap as `noise_msg`, emit ONE `RoutingEnvelope{ConnID, Frame: noiseMsg, CloseCode: 4401}` so the error frame and the close are **atomic on the wire** (phone observes error envelope BEFORE WS close per spec § Failure modes line 436; two-call `Send+CloseConn` would race the relay's output paths — mirrors v1 dispatcher's `outcome.Code` → `Response.CloseCode` mapping in `cmd/pyry/relay.go:38` + `dispatch.go:498-512`). Step-5 reconciliation: spec originally read "decode early-data as Envelope; on err OR non-hello: take AEAD-sealed `auth.invalid_token` path" — implementation follows the reconciled rule that at step 5 CipherStates do NOT yet exist (no key to seal under), so early-data decode failure or non-hello at handshake-time is a 4421 handshake-layer protocol violation (close-only); the AEAD-sealed-error path is reserved for after step 7 when CipherStates exist. `Responder.ReadInit` failure (MAC fail, wrong static pubkey, malformed IK message 1) → close(4426). `Responder.WriteResp` failure (defensive; practically unreachable under correct flynn/noise) → close(4426). The "gating" row `noise_msg in handshakeComplete`: AEAD-decrypt-then-decode-as-Envelope; decrypt failure → close(4421); decrypt success → seal `auth.invalid_token` under live `send` + emit ONE envelope `{Frame, CloseCode: 4401}` regardless of envelope type — handler chain unreached structurally. `noise_resp` from a phone (only the binary writes it) → close(4421) in every state with `reason=noise_resp_from_phone` log field. `noise_msg` in `awaitingInit` (no CipherStates) → close(4421). `noise_init` after `handshakeComplete` or `open` → close(4421) (rekey is #435, not here). Unknown `type` / bad `v` / malformed JSON / oversized `data` → close(4421). State `closed` drops late frames silently. `V2SessionConfig.StaticPriv` is the binary's 32-byte X25519 static private key; doc-comment declares MUST-NOT-log / MUST-NOT-wrap-into-error / MUST-NOT-emit-on-wire (same contract as `internal/keys` + `internal/noise`). Log discipline: ACCEPT logs `device_name` (operator-actionable); REJECT logs `conn_id` + `close_code` only, **NO `device_name`** even when early-data carried one — anti-enumeration of paired-device names from binary logs (mirrors `auth.go:129-132`). `HelloClientPayload.Token`, `cfg.StaticPriv`, raw `Frame` bytes, AEAD ciphertext, plaintext payload bytes, and base64 forms thereof are MUST-NOT-log at any level (including `%w`-wrapped errors). AEAD-sealed error envelope on 4401 emits static `MsgInvalidToken` + fixed `CodeAuthInvalidToken` — no attacker-influenced content echoed; close-only paths (4421/4426) emit no envelope. **Wired into the daemon in #549** — with `PYRY_MOBILE_V2=1` set, `cmd/pyry/relay.go` builds the `V2SessionManager` over `conn.Frames()` (via `startRelayV2`) instead of the v1 `internal/dispatch.Dispatcher`; default-unset keeps the v1 `/v1/server` path unchanged. Gated operationally by [`pyry pair preflight`](features/pyry-pair-command.md) (#436) as the operator's pre-flip safety check — see [`codebase/549.md`](codebase/549.md). Fakerelay extensions: `mux.HandleFunc("/v2/server", s.handleBinary)` shares the existing handler (relay-side routing-envelope wire unchanged in v2), and `binaryRecvPump` now treats `json.RawMessage` that marshals to the literal token `"null"` as "no frame to forward" — close-only `{ConnID, CloseCode: code}` envelopes marshal with `Frame: nil` (which round-trips through the WS as `"frame": null`); without the guard fakerelay would forward `null` as a phone-side WS text payload, breaking the production close-only contract. Fakephone extensions: `SendBytes(data []byte)` / `ReceiveBytes(timeout)` byte-oriented siblings of `Send(env)` / `Receive(timeout)` so v2 tests build the `InnerFrameV2` (not a `protocol.Envelope`) as raw bytes and bypass the envelope marshal/unmarshal; v1 envelope-oriented variants delegate to the byte variants so v1 behaviour is byte-identical. Same-package unit tests in `internal/relay/v2session_test.go` (~730 LOC, stdlib only): happy-path / bad-token-AEAD-error-then-4401 / IK-reject-4426 / noise_init-after-open-4421 / gating-noise_msg-in-handshakeComplete-4401 / out-of-state-rejections table-driven / config-validation panics + wrapped errors. E2E in `internal/e2e/relay_v2_handshake_test.go` (~354 LOC, build tag `e2e`): wires `relay.Connect` + `V2SessionManager` inline against fakerelay's new `/v2/server` (no daemon — `cmd/pyry/relay.go` still wires v1), dials a fakephone against `/v1/client` (unchanged routing wire), drives a Noise_IK handshake from the phone side via `internal/noise.Initiator` + an in-test `crypto/ecdh.X25519` keypair; three subtests cover happy / bad-token (phone reads AEAD-sealed `auth.invalid_token` then `LastCloseStatus() == 4401`) / IK-reject (phone reads close 4426, no prior frame). Out of scope: open-state handler dispatch (#446), encrypted echo round-trip + tampered-`noise_msg` AEAD-failure 4421 teardown (#446 — no dispatch path exists here to fail), rekey timer + `rekey_request` envelope (#435), per-phone-conn 10s handshake timeout (requires relay→binary "phone connected" forward signal which doesn't exist in v2 wire today), `V2Session` cleanup on phone-initiated WS close (same root cause), `RoutingEnvelope.Token` removal (v1 hard-cutover slice). **#446 fills the open-state `noise_msg` row**: `V2SessionConfig` gains optional `Handlers map[string]dispatch.Handler`; `V2Session` captures the matched device snapshot (`s.device`) in the token-accept branch of `handleNoiseInit` (right before state advances to `V2StateOpen`) and surfaces it through `*dispatch.Conn.Auth()` on each per-frame dispatch.
