New `dispatchAppFrame` AEAD-decrypts the inbound `noise_msg` via `s.recv.Decrypt`, allocates a per-frame `outbound chan protocol.RoutingEnvelope` of capacity `handlerOutboundBuf = 8` and a per-frame `*dispatch.Conn` via `dispatch.NewConn` (the new production-allowed sibling of `NewTestConn` — same body, different policy), calls `dispatch.Route(ctx, logger, conn, cfg.Handlers, plaintext)` synchronously, then drains `outbound` non-blockingly (`select { case env := <-outbound: ...; default: return }` — the channel is intentionally NOT closed so a misbehaving handler that forks a sender after dispatchAppFrame returns writes into a leaked-but-capacity-bounded channel the GC reclaims, rather than panicking on a closed-channel send), AEAD-encrypts each reply under `s.send.Encrypt`, wraps as `noise_msg` via `marshalInnerFrameV2`, and emits via `m.send` with `CloseCode: 0` (the reply's `CloseCode` is ignored — handlers do not signal closes through `c.Send`/`c.Reply`, that field is reserved for the manager's own `closeWith` close-intent emissions). AEAD-failure teardown on tampered/replayed/truncated `noise_msg`: `s.recv.Decrypt` returns non-nil → log `v2.aead.fail` with `conn_id`+`close_code=4421` (NO error text — flynn/noise error may carry counter indices that aren't operator-actionable) → `closeWith(ctx, s, StatusProtocolMismatch, nil)` emits one close-only routing envelope AND deletes the session entry from `m.sessions`, so the next `noise_init` for the same `conn_id` lazy-creates a fresh `awaitingInit` with no carry-over CipherStates (AC #3, structurally provided by reusing the existing `closeWith` rather than adding a parallel cleanup path). The handler chain is structurally unreachable on AEAD failure: the decrypt-failure branch returns BEFORE `dispatchAppFrame` is called — pinned by `TestV2Session_OpenState_TamperedFrame_HandlerNotReached`'s `atomic.Bool` side-effect flag. Transition table updated: `noise_msg, decrypt succeeds` in `open` becomes "dispatch via handler chain; sealed reply emitted; state stays open" (previously "drop, open-state dispatch deferred"); `noise_msg, decrypt fails` in `open` becomes "close(4421), → closed (AEAD-failure teardown; session entry dropped)" (previously "drop"). Concurrency: handlers run synchronously on the single dispatch goroutine — a slow handler stalls dispatch for ALL `conn_id`s (worst-case `send_message`'s 30s `Activate` timeout). Per-conn fan-out (one goroutine per `conn_id` with a per-session mutex guarding `s.send`/`s.recv`, mirroring `dispatch.Dispatcher.runConn`) is the priority follow-up before production cutover, NOT in this slice. Log policy extension: AEAD plaintext, ciphertext, handler reply envelope bytes (pre-encrypt), encrypted reply bytes (post-`Encrypt`), and base64 forms of any of those MUST NOT appear in any logged field; no per-envelope log on the open-state happy path (high-frequency message traffic would spam the log channel — existing per-handler logs surface that diagnostic instead). Refactor companion in `internal/dispatch`: extract `Dispatcher.handleOne` body into the new exported `Route(ctx, logger, conn, handlers, frame)` free function and `Dispatcher.sendError` into a package-private free `sendError(ctx, logger, conn, ...)` — mechanical (≤10 LOC moved), preserves behaviour, existing dispatcher tests stay green as the regression guard; `Dispatcher.handleOne` becomes a 1-line wrapper over `Route`. New tests: `TestV2Session_OpenState_EncryptedRoundTrip` (paired-device happy path through `dispatchAppFrame`, stub handler keyed by `TypeListConversations` replies via `c.Reply`, phone-side decrypt asserts inner envelope `Type`/`InReplyTo`/`Payload` match), `TestV2Session_OpenState_TamperedNoiseMsg_4421` (flip one byte of real ciphertext → exactly one outbound envelope with `CloseCode == uint16(StatusProtocolMismatch)` and nil Frame, registered handler's flag stays false, `mgr.sessions[v2TestConnID]` absent), `TestV2Session_OpenState_FreshNoiseInitAfterAEADClose` (after 4421+cleanup, second `noise_init` on same `conn_id` completes a fresh handshake; ciphertext sealed under OLD `initSend` fails against new session's `s.recv` — proof of no carry-over), `TestV2Session_OpenState_UnknownEnvelopeType_SealedUnsupportedReply` (open-state envelope with `Handlers = nil` → AEAD-sealed `Envelope{Type: TypeError, Payload.Code: CodeProtocolUnsupported}`, state stays `open`), plus dispatch unit tests `TestRoute_StandaloneInvocation` + `TestRoute_NoHandler_UnsupportedReply` pinning the new exported contract, and e2e `testV2EncryptedEchoRoundTrip` + `testV2TamperedNoiseMsg_4421` extending `TestRelayV2_Handshake` (the `v2Harness` constructor grows a `handlers map[string]dispatch.Handler` parameter — nil for existing tests; the fresh-`noise_init`-after-tampered-close case stays unit-shape only because `fakerelay` assigns a new `conn_id` per dial). Production daemon wired to the v2 manager in #549 (`PYRY_MOBILE_V2=1` → `startRelayV2` constructs `V2SessionManager` instead of `Dispatcher` and registers the same three handlers against `V2SessionConfig.Handlers`). **#454 lands the v2 control-envelope discriminator** at the top of `dispatchAppFrame`: a five-line `json.Unmarshal` probe on the AEAD-decrypted plaintext intercepts `Envelope.Type == protocol.TypeRekeyRequest` and routes it to a new `handleRekeyRequest` method, short-circuiting `dispatch.Route` so the application handler chain never sees the envelope. The probe is a re-decode (`dispatch.Route` decodes the same plaintext again); architect chose against refactoring `Route` to accept a pre-decoded `Envelope` so `internal/dispatch` stays v1-pure. JSON decode failures deliberately fall through to preserve #446's `protocol.malformed` reply path. `handleRekeyRequest` is logs-only — no `m.send`, no `m.closeWith`, no `s.state` mutation, no outbound frame: the binary is the IK responder per [ADR 024](decisions/024-noise-ik-mobile-e2e.md) and re-keys are initiated by phone-side `noise_init`, so an inbound `rekey_request` is informational. Decodes `env.Payload` as `struct{ Reason string }`; logs at INFO for the closed set `{scheduled, manual, compromise}` from `docs/protocol-mobile.md` § Re-key, WARN for empty / unknown / non-string / decode-failure (forward-compat for future mobile reasons the binary doesn't recognise yet). Three structured log fields: `event="v2.rekey.request.received"`, `conn_id`, `reason`. Architectural asymmetry "`TypeRekeyRequest` exists as a `Type*` constant but is deliberately absent from `inboundAppTypeSet`" pinned in three structurally-different places: doc-comment on the constant ("MUST NOT add to inboundAppTypeSet"), doc-comment **above** `inboundAppTypeSet` ("v2-control types like TypeRekeyRequest stay out"), and a deterministic `TestTypeConstants_V1V2Partition` partition test in `compat_test.go` that asserts every `Type*` constant lives in exactly one of `inboundAppTypeSet` / `v2OnlyTypes` (never both, never neither). The `v2OnlyTypes` allowlist literal lives test-side so production callers can't accidentally import it for dispatch logic. Tests under the `// --- v2 control-envelope tests (#454) ---` header: `TestV2Session_OpenState_RekeyRequest_Intercepted` (handler-chain `atomic.Bool` stays false; session stays open; no outbound close), `TestV2Session_OpenState_RekeyRequest_UnknownReasonTolerated` (buffer-logger captures `level=WARN`, `event=v2.rekey.request.received`, `reason=lunar-eclipse`), `TestV2Session_OpenState_RekeyRequest_RecognisedReasons` (table-driven over the three recognised reasons, asserts INFO + correct field). New `bufferLogger()` / `syncLogBuffer` test helpers (mutex-wrapped `bytes.Buffer` so the dispatch goroutine writes while the test goroutine reads). Concurrency unchanged — probe and handler both run on the manager's single dispatch goroutine. **#453 lands the v2 re-key responder swap** on open conns: the top of `handleNoiseInit` is refactored from `if s.state != V2StateAwaitingInit` into a `switch s.state` that routes the `V2StateOpen` arm to a new `handleRekeyInit` method. `handleRekeyInit` runs IK responder again against `cfg.StaticPriv` (`noise.NewResponder` → `ReadInit` → peer-static continuity check → `WriteResp` → marshal `noise_resp` → atomic CipherState swap → emit) with empty early-data on both directions per spec § Re-key (hello validation and token re-check are skipped — they ran at initial handshake; the per-rekey identity gate is the peer-static check, not the token). The peer-static continuity check is `bytes.Equal(resp.PeerStatic(), s.peerStatic)` (the field captured by #452 at initial handshake); mismatch closes the conn at WS 4426 with `reason=rekey_peer_static_mismatch`, implementing the threat-model's "no impersonation succeeds" residual-risk claim on the relay-MITM surface (`docs/protocol-mobile.md` § Threat #3). `bytes.Equal` is intentionally variable-time-acceptable (both operands are public keys); a one-line code comment names the choice to forestall a "should be `subtle.ConstantTimeCompare`" review nit. The CipherState swap is the single tuple assignment `s.send, s.recv = newSend, newRecv` on the manager's single dispatch goroutine — no half-mixed state where one direction uses new keys and the other uses old, because the loop IS the lock for `flynn/noise`'s non-goroutine-safe `CipherState`; a tuple assignment on this goroutine cannot be observed half-applied by any other code path, so the spec's atomic-switchover requirement is structural. The old `*CipherState` pointers are dropped from the struct; Go's GC reclaims the underlying memory. No explicit `Wipe()` of the key bytes is exposed (would require touching #433's surface — deferred); the single-owner-goroutine invariant means no code path reads the old state after the swap, which is the practical zeroisation property — documented in the `V2Session` and `V2SessionManager` package comments. State stays `V2StateOpen`; `s.device` and `s.peerStatic` are preserved (the #452 lifetime contract — a successful re-key MUST NOT overwrite `s.peerStatic`); log `v2.rekey.accept` at INFO with `conn_id` + `device_name` (operator-actionable; SAME device as initial handshake by construction). Five reject branches in `handleRekeyInit` (`rekey_responder_init_failed`, `rekey_read_init_failed`, `rekey_peer_static_mismatch`, `rekey_write_resp_failed`, `rekey_marshal_noise_resp`) all close at **4426** (the SAME code initial handshake uses for IK-pattern failure — no new close code introduced) via `closeWith`, which removes the session entry so the next inbound frame lazy-creates a fresh `awaitingInit`. The mismatch reject log line deliberately omits `device_name` (anti-enumeration discipline — the re-key initiator's identity is unknown / hostile). `noise_init` in `V2StateHandshakeComplete` still rejects at 4421 (the `default` arm of the `switch s.state`, with `reason=noise_init_in_handshake_complete` — the previous `reason=noise_init_after_handshake` label is dropped). **Old-key frames after the swap fail AEAD and tear down the conn via the inherited #446 tampered-frame branch** — no new code path; once `s.recv` points at the new CipherState, any frame sealed under the old keys fails `s.recv.Decrypt` and lands in `closeWith(StatusProtocolMismatch, nil)` → session removal. Tests under `// --- re-key responder tests (#453) ---`: `TestV2Session_RekeyResponder_HappyPath_RoundTripUnderNewKeys` (SAME initiator static; round-trips an application frame under the new CipherStates; asserts state stays open + device + peerStatic snapshot preserved), `TestV2Session_RekeyResponder_DifferentPeerStatic_4426` (DIFFERENT initiator static → close 4426; reject log contains `reason=rekey_peer_static_mismatch` but NOT `device_name` — security-load-bearing for Threat #3), `TestV2Session_RekeyResponder_OldKeyFrameAfterSwap_4421` (pre-rekey ciphertext fed AFTER swap → inherited #446 tampered-frame branch closes at 4421; verifies inheritance at the new authenticated-state boundary). The pre-#453 test `TestV2Session_NoiseInitAfterOpen_4421` is **removed** — it pinned exactly the behaviour this slice intentionally changes. Out of scope: binary-initiated re-key (1h timer + emit + 30s reply timeout) is #450 using the same `protocol.TypeRekeyRequest` constant landed by #454; the `pyry rekey <conn_id>` operator verb is #451; explicit `Wipe()` of old CipherState key bytes is deferred (#433 surface touch); persistence of new CipherStates across binary restarts is out for v2 per ADR 024 § Re-key policy (in-memory only). **#450 (initiator-side re-key, ships parallel with #453):** each `V2Session` that reaches `V2StateOpen` arms a per-session 1-hour `*time.Timer` (`rekeyInterval`, lowercase package var so tests substitute via `t.Cleanup` like `connection.go:43`'s `handshakeTimeout`); on fire the `time.AfterFunc` callback pushes a `wakeSignal{s, wakeRekeyEmit}` onto a per-manager buffered channel `m.wake` (cap `wakeBufferSize = 16`) instead of touching session state directly — the dispatch goroutine pops the wake on a new third select arm in `Run` and runs `emitRekeyRequest` under the single-owner-goroutine invariant for `s.send`. The emit AEAD-seals `Envelope{ID: 1, Type: protocol.TypeRekeyRequest, TS: now, Payload: {"reason": "scheduled"}}` under `s.send`, wraps as `marshalInnerFrameV2(TypeNoiseMsg, ciphertext)`, forwards via `m.send`, sets `s.awaitingRekeyReply = true`, and arms `s.rekeyReplyTimer` (`rekeyReplyTimeout = 30s`); ID=1 because there is no `rekey_ack` to correlate by InReplyTo per spec (the next AEAD round-trip under the new keys is the implicit ack); only `"scheduled"` is emitted by this slice — `"manual"` and `"compromise"` belong to the future `pyry rekey <conn_id>` verb (#451). On phone-side reply, `handleRekeyInit`'s success tail now calls a new `s.rekeyComplete(m, ctx)` hook (one line added at #453's existing line :632 success tail, unconditional on the swap path — spontaneous phone-initiated re-keys that the binary did not request still re-base the 1-hour cadence; clearing an unset `awaitingRekeyReply` is a no-op, stopping a nil `rekeyReplyTimer` is a no-op) which clears `awaitingRekeyReply`, stops `rekeyReplyTimer`, and re-arms `rekeyTimer` from the swap moment. On reply-timeout (`rekeyReplyTimer` fires and the wake-channel arm finds `awaitingRekeyReply` still true), the manager emits a `noise.rekey_failed` WARN log line with `conn_id` + `close_code=4426` (NO `err=` field — anti-leakage of flynn-noise error text per security review) and calls `closeWith(ctx, s, StatusHandshakeFailure, nil)`; the existing `closeWith` cleanup path now ALSO stops both per-session timers before the existing `delete(m.sessions, s.connID)` — `Stop()` is safe on a fired timer (returns false, no-op); the callback's `ctx.Done` arm is the load-bearing teardown. `Run` derives `runCtx, cancelRun := context.WithCancel(ctx); defer cancelRun()` and passes `runCtx` downstream so `time.AfterFunc` callback goroutines unblock cleanly on Frames-channel close (which doesn't cancel `ctx`); `Run`'s observable behaviour is unchanged (still returns `ctx.Err()`). Three new `V2Session` fields all owned by the dispatch goroutine: `rekeyTimer *time.Timer`, `rekeyReplyTimer *time.Timer`, `awaitingRekeyReply bool` — the bool is load-bearing AND distinct from `rekeyReplyTimer != nil` because `Stop()` returns false on a fired timer whose callback may still be in flight, and the bool gives `handleWake`'s timeout arm a stable late-arrival predicate to ignore stale wakes after `rekeyComplete` already won. Emit-side seal/marshal failures Warn-and-drop (three distinct events: `v2.rekey.emit.seal_failed`, `v2.rekey.emit.marshal_failed`, `v2.rekey.emit.skipped_already_awaiting`, carrying `event` + `conn_id` only — no error text), NEVER close the conn (same posture as `sealError`'s line 498-502: internal seal failures are not wire-protocol failures). No new close code introduced — reply-timeout reuses `StatusHandshakeFailure` (4426). No new exported symbols. Tests under `// --- re-key initiator tests (#450) ---`: `TestV2Session_RekeyInitiator_Emit_ReArmViaResponder` (joint AC#5 bullets 1+2 — substitute `rekeyInterval = 20ms`, drive to open, wait for first emit and decode under `sess.initRecv` asserting `Type == TypeRekeyRequest` + `reason == "scheduled"`, construct second `noise.Initiator` reusing the SAME `initPriv`, feed fresh `noise_init`, derive `initRecv2` via `initiator2.ReadResp`, wait for SECOND emit and decode under post-swap `initRecv2`; merger of bullets 1+2 because the only natural production caller of `rekeyComplete` is `handleRekeyInit` — a test-goroutine call would violate the single-owner-goroutine invariant), `TestV2Session_RekeyInitiator_ReplyTimeout_4426` (substitute `rekeyReplyTimeout = 40ms`, `bufferLogger()`, drive to open, do NOT feed reply, wait via new `waitForOutboundCount(t, rec, n, deadline)` helper, assert third envelope `CloseCode == uint16(StatusHandshakeFailure)` + nil Frame + `mgr.sessions[v2TestConnID]` absent, log contains `event=noise.rekey_failed` + `close_code=4426` + `conn_id=…`, and the `noise.rekey_failed` line specifically does NOT contain `err=` via per-line substring check), `TestV2Session_RekeyInitiator_TimerCleanup_NoGoroutineLeak` (two sub-tests `close_via_manager_exit` and `close_via_reply_timeout`, both assert `runtime.NumGoroutine()` returns to within +1 of the pre-test baseline after `runtime.Gosched(); runtime.GC(); time.Sleep(20ms)`). Tests are explicitly NOT `t.Parallel()` — they mutate package-level vars that the dispatch goroutines of other parallel tests would read. Concurrency invariant preserved: `Run` is still the only long-term goroutine the manager owns; `time.AfterFunc` callbacks are transient (spawned only when the timer fires, exit after one channel send) and NEVER touch session state directly — the wake-channel routing IS the single-owner-goroutine guarantee. Closes the last v2 re-key piece on the binary modulo the operator verb #451. **#462 (slice B1 of the #460 re-split of #451) lands the manager-side `control.Rekeyer` implementer**: `*V2SessionManager` exposes `Rekey(ctx context.Context, connID string) error` (the interface-pinned signature — the AC text said `TriggerRekey` but the slice A `control.Rekeyer` interface declared `Rekey`; the interface wins because it lets `*V2SessionManager` satisfy `control.Rekeyer` directly with no adapter, pinned by `var _ control.Rekeyer = (*V2SessionManager)(nil)`). The method funnels each request onto Run's single dispatch goroutine via a new unbuffered `manualRekey chan manualRekeyReq` field + a fourth `Run` select arm + a private `handleManualRekey` method — `Rekey` itself does only channel I/O on the caller's goroutine, every session-state read or write happens on Run's goroutine, preserving the single-owner invariant for `s.send` / `s.state` / `s.rekeyTimer` / `s.awaitingRekeyReply` with no new lock, no new atomic, no new long-lived goroutine. `manualRekeyReq{connID, reply chan error}` carries a per-request cap-1 reply channel so the manager's `req.reply <- err` send is non-blocking even if the caller's ctx fires between enqueue and reply; the `manualRekey` channel is unbuffered (backpressure is correct — Run busy means Rekey waits) and never closed by the manager on Run exit (in-flight callers unblock via `ctx.Done` instead, same posture as `m.cfg.Frames`). `handleManualRekey` looks up `m.sessions[connID]` and rejects with three sentinels: `relay.ErrConnNotFound` (`= fmt.Errorf("relay: conn not found: %w", control.ErrConnNotFound)` — the `%w` wrap keeps slice A's `errors.Is` mapping to `ErrCodeConnNotFound` firing without further plumbing; the `relay → control` import is non-cyclic), `relay.ErrSessionNotOpen` (`= errors.New("relay: session not open")` — has no wire-code analogue today, dispatcher surfaces it verbatim through `Response.Error` with no `ErrorCode`) for both `s.state != V2StateOpen` AND `s.awaitingRekeyReply` (the two states collapse externally because from the operator's perspective both mean "the conn is not in a state where a manual rekey can be initiated" — distinguishing them would leak internal state-machine vocabulary with no operator-actionable consequence, and the collapse also imposes a natural per-conn rate limit of one manual rekey per `rekeyReplyTimeout` since a second `Rekey` arriving within that window hits the awaiting-reply branch). On the happy path `handleManualRekey` stops + nils `s.rekeyTimer` (re-bases the scheduled 1-hour cadence — `Stop()`'s bool return is intentionally ignored; the stale-wake race where the scheduled timer fired and queued a wake before `Stop()` ran is benign because `emitRekeyRequest`'s existing defensive `awaitingRekeyReply`-skip catches it and logs `v2.rekey.emit.skipped_already_awaiting` — no double emit, one spurious WARN) then calls `m.emitRekeyRequest(ctx, s, "manual")` and returns nil; the natural #453 responder cycle on the phone's reply re-arms a fresh `rekeyTimer` via `rekeyComplete` from the swap moment, so the next scheduled emit lands at T_swap + `rekeyInterval` not at the original T+60min boundary. The `emitRekeyRequest` function gains a `reason string` parameter — body deltas are mechanically minimal (struct literal field `Reason: reason` and Info log field `"reason", reason` replace the previous `"scheduled"` literals); the two call sites (`handleWake`'s `wakeRekeyEmit` arm passes `"scheduled"`, `handleManualRekey` passes `"manual"`) share one body, satisfying the AC's "no parallel emit machinery" by sharing the function not the channel routing (the manual path skips `m.wake` entirely; it runs `emitRekeyRequest` synchronously inside the `manualRekey` select arm on the same goroutine). Mobile Protocol v2 (`docs/protocol-mobile.md` § Re-key) wire-pins `payload.reason = "manual"` as *"operator-triggered via `pyry rekey <conn_id>`"*; the literal is the only semantic difference between the scheduled and manual emits. The defensive `awaitingRekeyReply`-skip inside `emitRekeyRequest` is structurally unreachable from the manual path (the explicit pre-check in `handleManualRekey` fires first); it stays because the scheduled path still relies on it for the stale-wake race. AEAD-seal-failure posture inherited from #450 (Warn-and-drop, do NOT close the conn) means a sub-emit failure on the manual path leaves the conn with `rekeyTimer = nil` and `awaitingRekeyReply = false` — automatic scheduled re-keying is paused indefinitely until a phone-initiated rekey rebases the timer via `rekeyComplete` — acceptable per the architect's security review (seal failures realistically unreachable; the `v2.rekey.emit.seal_failed` log is the operator-visible signal; re-arming the timer in the seal-failure branch would mask the error AND introduce a code path the scheduled emit doesn't have). Tests under `// --- re-key manual-trigger tests (#462) ---`: `TestV2Session_RekeyManual_HappyPath_EmitsManualReason` (long substituted rekeyInterval/rekeyReplyTimeout so the scheduled boundary cannot fire; Rekey returns nil; second envelope decrypts under `sess.initRecv` to `TypeRekeyRequest` with `reason == "manual"`; log buffer contains `event=v2.rekey.emit` + `reason=manual`), `TestV2Session_RekeyManual_UnknownConn_ReturnsErrConnNotFound` (Run started with empty sessions map; `errors.Is(err, relay.ErrConnNotFound)` AND `errors.Is(err, control.ErrConnNotFound)` both fire — the wire-mapping invariant — and no outbound side-effect), `TestV2Session_RekeyManual_AlreadyAwaitingReply_ReturnsErrSessionNotOpen` (long rekeyReplyTimeout; first Rekey succeeds and emit lands; second Rekey returns `errors.Is(err, relay.ErrSessionNotOpen)`; only one manual emit recorded; state stays `V2StateOpen` with `awaitingRekeyReply == true`), `TestV2Session_RekeyManual_RebasesScheduledTimer` (substitute `rekeyInterval = 100ms`/`rekeyReplyTimeout = 1s`; manual Rekey then drive a fresh responder cycle reusing the SAME initPriv → load-bearing **negative** assertion that at T ≈ original boundary + jitter the envelope count is still 3, no stale scheduled emit landed between manual emit and the re-based boundary; then positive assertion that at T_rekeyComplete + rekeyInterval + jitter a fourth envelope arrives decoding to `TypeRekeyRequest` with `reason == "scheduled"` under post-swap `initRecv2`). All four #462 tests are NOT `t.Parallel()` — they mutate package-level rekeyInterval/rekeyReplyTimeout vars. Daemon construction of `*V2SessionManager` landed in #549 (the `PYRY_MOBILE_V2=1` cutover), so `NewV2SessionManager` now has a production caller; but #549 deliberately does NOT call `ctrlServer.SetRekeyer(mgr)`, so the `Rekey` method is reachable from `internal/relay` tests only until that control-socket wire-up lands in a separate ticket. Sibling slice B2 ships the operator-facing `pyry rekey <conn_id>` CLI verb that calls slice A's `control.Rekey` client helper; once both B2 and the `SetRekeyer` wire-up land on top of #549, the verb is end-to-end functional. **#571 adds the concurrency-safe server-initiated push surface** — `(*V2SessionManager).Push(ctx, connID, env protocol.Envelope) error` seals a caller-owned envelope under the addressed open session's send CipherState, wraps it as the existing `noise_msg` transport frame, and forwards it to the phone; safe to call from any goroutine other than the dispatch goroutine. Structural twin of #462's `Rekey` funnel: a new unbuffered `push chan pushReq` field + a fifth `Run` select arm route each request onto the single dispatch goroutine, where private `handlePush` looks up the session, gates on `V2StateOpen` (refusing un-authenticated `handshakeComplete` peers with `ErrSessionNotOpen` — security gate), then reuses `emitRekeyRequest`'s `json.Marshal → s.send.Encrypt → marshalInnerFrameV2(TypeNoiseMsg) → m.send` seal sequence. A funnel (not a mutex) because `flynn/noise`'s `CipherState` nonce counter is not concurrency-safe and every `s.send.Encrypt` already runs on `Run`; the unbuffered channel makes a cross-goroutine push wait its turn rather than race the counter, and composes with the re-key swap for free (both run on `Run`). Reuses `ErrConnNotFound` (wraps `control.ErrConnNotFound`; covers unknown AND torn-down sessions since `closeWith` already deleted them) / `ErrSessionNotOpen`; no new exported type, no new wire shape, no goroutine, no change to v1 or the inbound dispatch path. The missing primitive behind server-initiated delivery (the "broadcast layer" deferred at the `V2Session`/`State()` comments) — consumed by #572's v2 assistant-turn bridge (`blocked-by` #571), which owns connected-session enumeration + outbound envelope-ID policy + production wiring. Push is reachable from `internal/relay` tests only until #572. Six new `-race`-clean tests: interleaved-push+reply-decrypt (AC#1/#4), N=8 concurrent-pushes + M=8 in-flight-replies no-nonce-corruption (AC#2), unknown-conn `ErrConnNotFound` + unrelated-session-unaffected (AC#3), not-open `ErrSessionNotOpen` (AC#3), closed-session `ErrConnNotFound` (AC#3), ctx-cancelled returns `ctx.Err()`. See [`codebase/571.md`](codebase/571.md). **#588 adds the concurrency-safe open-session enumeration snapshot** — `(*V2SessionManager).ActiveConnIDs(ctx) []string` returns the `conn_id`s of every session currently in `V2StateOpen`, the enumeration half #571 deferred to #572 (`Push` can address one phone but cannot discover which phones are connected). Fourth instance of the single-writer funnel idiom and the structural twin of #571's `Push`, with the seal/marshal steps dropped (a snapshot reads the map, touches no CipherState): a new unbuffered `snapshot chan snapshotReq` field (`snapshotReq{reply chan []string}`, cap-1 reply) + a sixth `Run` select arm route each request onto the single dispatch goroutine, where private `handleActiveConnIDs` ranges `m.sessions` and appends every `connID` whose `s.state == V2StateOpen`. `ActiveConnIDs` itself does only channel I/O on the caller's goroutine (send onto `m.snapshot` then receive on `req.reply`, both with `ctx.Done` escape arms). The map read is serialised by `Run`'s select against every map write (lazy-create, `delete` in `closeWith`, handshake/re-key transitions), so no torn read of `m.sessions` or `s.state`; no new lock, atomic, or goroutine. The `V2StateOpen` filter is the load-bearing security gate (`security-sensitive`) — excludes still-handshaking / token-unvalidated peers exactly as `handlePush` does, so a server push never reaches an un-authenticated phone; belt-and-suspenders holds because the consumer calls `Push` per id and `Push` independently re-gates on `V2StateOpen` (two deterministic checks in two methods). Returns `[]string` not `([]string, error)` — a snapshot has no actionable failure; `nil` on ctx-cancel or after `Run` exited (Frames closed, no receiver on `m.snapshot`) is equivalent to "no open sessions" for the broadcast consumer, which re-enumerates next turn. Result is an unordered set (Go map-iteration order; no sort — the consumer fans out order-independently); `handleActiveConnIDs` logs nothing and returns only non-secret conn-id routing keys (never a `*V2Session` or key/plaintext bytes). v2 analog of v1's `dispatch.Dispatcher.ActiveConns()` (which needs no ctx because it reads under a mutex; v2 has no mutex, so the read must funnel through `Run`). The `V2Session.State()` no-cross-goroutine-reader invariant is preserved — `handleActiveConnIDs` reads `s.state` *on* `Run`, so `State()` still needs no atomic/mutex. With `Push` (#571), both primitives #572's assistant-turn bridge consumes now exist; only the bridge wiring + outbound envelope-ID policy remain. Five `-race`-clean white-box tests: open-only enumeration (AC#1, mixed-state injection), torn-down-session-absent (AC#2, 4421 AEAD teardown then re-enumerate), concurrent-with-dispatch (AC#3, hammer `ActiveConnIDs` while M=16 inbound replies drive `dispatchAppFrame` — every snapshot is `{connID}` or empty, replies still decrypt in order), empty-manager (`len 0`, no block), ctx-cancelled (Run not started → `nil` deterministically). Reachable from `internal/relay` tests only, pending a #572 wire-up that never landed — #572 closed NOT_PLANNED and #1542 deleted `ActiveConnIDs`, retargeting its coverage onto `ActiveConns` directly. See [`codebase/588.md`](codebase/588.md). **#618 adds the inbound screen-snapshot handler** (`security-sensitive`) — ADR 025's on-demand, parser-independent live-view escape hatch. `dispatchAppFrame`'s discriminator becomes a `switch probeEnv.Type` with a `TypeRequestSnapshot` arm beside the `TypeRekeyRequest` arm; private `handleRequestSnapshot` validates the `conversation_id` via a new optional `KnownConversation func(string) bool` config seam (AC#4 — unknown/foreign/empty/nil-seam ⇒ `error{conversation.not_found, retryable=false}` *before any render*), renders via a new optional `Snapshotter ScreenSnapshotter` config seam (one-method consumer-declared interface that `*supervisor.Supervisor` satisfies, so the relay imports neither `internal/supervisor` nor tui-driver; nil-seam or `live==false` ⇒ `error{server.binary_offline, retryable=true}`, AC#3), and pushes a `screen_snapshot{conversation_id, text, ts, model, effort, yolo}` — the three settings fields populated from an optional `SnapshotSettings func() (model, effort string, yolo bool)` config seam (#848; nil-seam ⇒ empty model/effort + `yolo:false`, byte-identical to the pre-#848 zero-value reply; production wires a closure over `*sessions.Pool.DefaultSettings` built in `cmd/pyry/main.go` so `internal/relay` still imports no `internal/sessions` type) — every branch via the existing `m.handlePush` seal-and-forward path (#571), **never the public `Push`** (which self-deadlocks on the dispatch goroutine). Both new `V2SessionConfig` fields are optional and unvalidated, so the ~42 existing construction sites compile unchanged. The supervisor seam `(*Supervisor).ScreenSnapshot() (text string, live bool)` captures the live `*tuidriver.Session` under `sessMu` (mirroring `WriteUserTurn`) then renders `tuidriver.Render(sess.Snapshot(), 0, 0)` **inside the tui-driver seal** — raw bytes consumed in one expression, never named in pyrycode, so `cmd/substrate-guard` stays green. SECURITY: the rendered text is NEVER logged; error replies carry only a static message constant; the `conversation_id` is validated before any render. Deferred to #596+: per-conversation screen routing (this single-bootstrap-conversation phase renders the one live screen for any registered id). Table-driven unit tests (`TestV2Session_OpenState_RequestSnapshot[_Repeat|_NeverLogsScreenText]`) + an e2e round-trip at the real daemon seam (`testV2DaemonRequestSnapshotRoundTrip`, AC#5). See [`codebase/618.md`](codebase/618.md). **#626 closes the daemon-side capability trust decision #607 deferred** (`security-sensitive`): `handleNoiseInit` now intersects the phone's advertised `HelloClientPayload.Capabilities` with the daemon's authoritative supported set `supportedV2Capabilities = {CapabilityInteractive}` via a pure `negotiateCapabilities(advertised) []string` that **iterates the supported set** (so a spoofed/unsupported advertisement can never be echoed or flagged — security by loop shape, not runtime check), echoes exactly the intersection in `hello_ack.Capabilities` (computed before the ack literal — single source of truth; `omitempty` keeps a no-capability phone v1 byte-identical), and records a new set-once `s.interactive bool` on `V2Session` in the token-OK tail between `s.device = &device` and `s.state = V2StateOpen` (fail-closed `false` on every non-authenticated path; re-key preserves it like `device`/`peerStatic`). The snapshot funnel is widened one level — `snapshotReq.reply` becomes `chan []ActiveConn`, `handleActiveConnIDs` → `handleActiveConns() []ActiveConn` (same `V2StateOpen` gate, appends the flag), new exported `ActiveConn{ConnID string; Interactive bool}` + public `ActiveConns(ctx) []ActiveConn`, and `ActiveConnIDs(ctx) []string` is reimplemented as a thin projection (explicit `nil` in → `nil` out preserves #588's nil-on-cancel contract; the #589 consumer compiles untouched). The future #596 structured-stream fan-out filters `ActiveConns` on `Interactive` to route interactive events only to granted conns. Two independent deterministic gates (token-OK flag-write + `V2StateOpen` enumeration filter) keep an un-authenticated peer's flag unobservable; security review verdict PASS. One production file (`internal/relay/v2session.go`, +121/−40), no wire-type/emitter change. Tests: `TestNegotiateCapabilities` (AC#2/#3 matrix), `TestV2Session_Handshake_CapabilityNegotiation` (echo/drop/spoof/advertise-nothing, decoding the captured hello_ack), `TestV2Session_ActiveConns_MixedInteractive`, `TestV2Session_CapabilitySpoof_TokenFail_NeverEnumerated` (security); the existing `TestV2Session_ActiveConnIDs_*` suite passed unchanged at the time (AC#5) — later renamed `TestV2Session_ActiveConns_*` and retargeted directly onto `ActiveConns` by #1542, once the deferred `ActiveConnIDs` projection was deleted. See [`codebase/626.md`](codebase/626.md). **#610 rewrites the #571 push surface non-blocking under relay backpressure** (`security-sensitive`; EPIC #596 Phase 2) — closing the ADR-025 line-220 open risk that a slow relay wedges the daemon's event producer (#633's JSONL drainer). The synchronous `push chan pushReq` funnel (where `handlePush` waited for the seal) is removed; `Push` now enqueues the **unsealed** envelope onto a per-session bounded FIFO (`m.queues[connID]` → `pushQueue{items, dropped}`, guarded by the leaf mutex `m.pushMu`, bounded at `pushQueueCap = 256`) and returns immediately, signalling a cap-1 `m.drainCh`; `Run`'s `drainOnce` select arm pops one envelope per pass (FIFO; rough map-range fairness) and seals it via `forwardEnvelope` (the renamed `handlePush`) — the seal stays single-writer on `Run`, so one slow `Outbound` interleaves with rather than monopolises `Run`'s servicing of inbound frames / `ActiveConns` / wakes. **Drop-before-seal is load-bearing**: the queue holds unsealed envelopes so the class-aware drop policy (`pushQueue.enqueue`; the only droppable class is `assistant_delta`, drop-oldest under pressure; control events `turn_state`/`tool_use`/`tool_result`/`turn_end`/`stall` + the coarse `message` are admitted by evicting a droppable, never dropped; a documented **soft overflow** admits control past nominal cap only in the unreachable all-control-saturated case) never gaps the phone's strictly-sequential `recv` nonce — a dropped *sealed* frame would MAC-fail → 4421. Surviving order is preserved (`enqueue` only removes existing entries + tail-appends, AC#4). Error-contract change: the public `Push` collapses not-open → `ErrConnNotFound` (queue-exists ⟺ `V2StateOpen`), and the `V2StateOpen` security gate moved to the drain-side `forwardEnvelope`, so a buffered push to a conn that de-authed/closed before drain is dropped there, never delivered to an un-authenticated peer (the two snapshot handlers still call `forwardEnvelope` directly — `InReplyTo`-correlated, sealed in-line). One production file (`internal/relay/v2session.go`, +249/−65), no new exported type, no wire change; the live `Push` callers are #632's structured emitter + #589's coarse `message` bridge. Drop policy is a pure table-tested unit surface (`TestPushQueue_Enqueue_*`); `TestV2Session_Push_NonBlockingUnderStall` proves AC#1 end-to-end against a stalling outbound. See [`codebase/610.md`](codebase/610.md). **#647 adds the inbound mid-turn reconnect-replay consumer** (`security-sensitive`, EPIC #596 Phase 2): a phone advertising `hello.last_event_id` (new additive `HelloClientPayload.LastEventID *uint64`) is replayed its conversation's missed tail from the #646 event ring — late-bound via `SetReplaySource(ring, currentConv)` (a `pushMu`-guarded setter that breaks the emitter↔manager construction cycle, not a `V2SessionConfig` field) — on its own conn before the live stream resumes, via `replayMissed` run inline at the `handleNoiseInit` success tail (`After` classifies caught-up/gap/replay); an aged-out position, or one beyond the conversation's id space (#1494), gets a `resync` marker (`TypeResync`, inline `{conversation_id}` payload) instead of a gap-ful replay; a per-conn `replayThrough` watermark + a `forwardEnvelope` guard dedup the transient replay/live overlap; #777 then paces the forward loop one event per `Run` pass (Run-owned `replayQueue` drained by `drainReplayOnce` via a cap-1 `replayCh` pump mirroring `drainCh`/`drainOnce`, plus a `drainOnce` gate holding a replaying conn's live events until its tail empties) so a large mid-turn replay no longer monopolises the dispatch goroutine while replay-before-live ordering and the single-writer seal are preserved. `last_event_id` is untrusted (range/shape-validated by the `*uint64` decode, ring-bounded, scoped to the daemon-resolved conversation). **#663 closes the caught-up-watermark MUST FIX** #647 shipped with: the caught-up branch sourced the watermark from the untrusted `last_event_id` (silently suppressing the live stream after a `/clear`-rotated reconnect or a hostile-large id); it now clamps to `min(afterID, NewestID(convID))` so a remote id beyond the conversation's id space can never mute the live stream. See [`codebase/647.md`](codebase/647.md) / [`codebase/663.md`](codebase/663.md). **#727 adds the inbound modal-control interception + `ModalResolver` seam + `modal_dismissed` broadcast** (`security-sensitive`, EPIC #597 Phase 3, the seam-introduction slice of the daemon-side modal bridge): `dispatchAppFrame`'s discriminator gains `TypeModalCancel`→`handleModalCancel` and `TypeModalAnswer`→`handleModalAnswer` arms (beside `request_snapshot`/`rekey_request`; no `dispatch.Route` handler). A new consumer-declared `ModalResolver` interface (`ResolveCancel`/`ResolveAnswer`, declared beside `ScreenSnapshotter` so `internal/relay` imports neither supervisor/modalbridge/audit nor cmd/pyry) + a `ModalDismissal{Outcome, Source}` type + an optional nil-safe `V2SessionConfig.ModalResolver` field (nil ⇒ both frames inert no-ops — no consumer cascade, the reason the slice stays S). A `modal_cancel` for an outstanding modal resolves via the seam (consume the registry entry → fail-safe ESC → audit `{cancelled, remote}`) and then fans a `modal_dismissed{modal_id, outcome, source}` to **every** interactive-capable open conn via the manager-internal `broadcastModalDismissed`, which **reads `m.sessions` directly** (the `handleActiveConns` pattern) and `Push`es per conn — it MUST NOT call `ActiveConns` (which funnels back onto the same Run goroutine via `m.snapshot` → **deadlock**); the fan-out test drives the cancel through the real `Frames`/`Run` loop, so an accidental `ActiveConns` call would hang it (structural no-deadlock proof). `modal_answer` is intercepted and routed through the same seam but is a **deferred no-op** in this slice (no keystroke, no mutation, no dismissal, no audit) — the escalating ALLOW path stays inert until #717's per-device gate; the `handleModalAnswer` broadcast line is present-but-unreachable so #717 changes only the resolver impl. AC-4 idempotency = the registry's atomic `Resolve` consume (first cancel wins; unknown/already-resolved → `(zero,false)` no-op). Envelope `ID: 1` non-load-bearing (phone correlates on `modal_id`; `modal_dismissed` is a control event, `EventID == nil`, never in the #647 ring). The `cmd/pyry` `modalResolverV2` (`cmd/pyry/modal_resolve_v2.go`) implements the seam over the daemon-singleton `modalbridge.New()` registry (the #716 `Resolve`), the #726 `SendEsc` safe-answer seam, and the #712 audit sink; wired in `cmd/pyry/relay.go`'s `startRelayV2`. Cancel is intentionally **ungated** (dismiss = fail-safe deny). Producer wiring landed in #798 (live surfacer); #725 (deny-on-timeout) reuses this broadcast + audit path. Tests: relay-side fan-out/no-op/nil-resolver (`internal/relay/v2session_modal_test.go`) + resolver-side consume/keystroke/audit/no-body-leak (`cmd/pyry/modal_resolve_v2_test.go`); code-review PASS, real-claude e2e green. See [`codebase/727.md`](codebase/727.md) + [`features/modalbridge-package.md`](features/modalbridge-package.md) (outbound half). **#725 adds deny-on-timeout** (`security-sensitive`, the fail-closed leg of #717's 3-way split, unblocked #791/#793): a surfaced modal with no `modal_answer`/`modal_cancel` within a bounded window (`modalDenyTimeout`, 2 min default) is safe-denied via a daemon-global timer — `ArmModalTimeout` arms `time.AfterFunc` off the Run goroutine on `reg.Record`, the callback funnels the `modal_id` onto a new `modalTimeout` channel, and a new Run select arm fires `handleModalTimeout` → `ResolveTimeout` (one-shot `Resolve` → ESC → audit `{denied_timeout, timeout}` with empty device) → reuses `broadcastModalDismissed` unchanged. Exactly-once is structural (answer/cancel/timeout resolution are all arms of the same Run select, gated by the one-shot registry `Resolve`); the `*time.Timer` is deliberately discarded (never Stopped). Live in production since #798 wired the surfacer. Tests: `cmd/pyry/modal_resolve_v2_test.go` (resolver) + `internal/relay/v2session_modal_test.go` (`…_ModalTimeout_FanOut` -race arm→fire→broadcast) + `cmd/pyry/interactive_modal_v2_test.go` (`fakeArmer`); code-review PASS. See [`codebase/725.md`](codebase/725.md). **#707 adds the inbound `interrupt` control + Esc routing** (`security-sensitive`, EPIC #597 Phase 3): the remote interrupt — a paired phone stops the running turn, the remote equivalent of pressing Esc locally. `dispatchAppFrame`'s discriminator gains a `TypeInterrupt`→`handleInterrupt` arm (beside `modal_cancel`; no `dispatch.Route` handler). A new consumer-declared `Interrupter interface{ SendEsc() error }` (declared beside `ScreenSnapshotter`, so `internal/relay` imports neither supervisor nor tui-driver) + an optional nil-safe `V2SessionConfig.Interrupter` field (nil ⇒ inert, no Esc — the no-cascade S-holding shape) wired, pre-#1121, to `*supervisor.Supervisor`'s **sealed `SendEsc`** (#726, zero new supervisor code) via one line in `cmd/pyry/relay.go` — **since #1121** rewired instead to a `cmd/pyry` `activeInterrupter` adapter that resolves the **active conversation's bound runner** (mirrors `boundHost`'s `CurrentSessionID → Pool.Lookup` shape, copying `sessionRouter.resolve`'s empty-`CurrentSessionID` guard so an unbound conversation never falls through to the bootstrap session) and dispatches by concrete runner type — `SendEsc` for `*supervisor.Supervisor`, `Interrupt` for the stream-json `streamRunner` (#1120) — fixing a latent mis-routing bug where every interrupt actuated the idle bootstrap child instead of the runner running the active conversation's turn; `internal/relay` itself is unchanged, keeping the `SendEsc` method name. `handleInterrupt(s)` (signature takes only `s` — no payload to decode, no reply, no broadcast, fire-and-forget) is **capability-gate-first**: `if !s.interactive` → inert (the **first** inbound frame whose authorization IS the `interactive` capability — a one-line check, NOT a reusable inbound-gate abstraction since it is the only consumer; dequeue is ungated, `modal_answer` uses the per-device gate, `modal_cancel` a nonce); then nil-`Interrupter` → debug-log inert; else best-effort `SendEsc()` (error Warn-logged + tolerated). The frame carries **no payload** (no `conversation_id`/`modal_id`/`answer_token`/idempotency key) — a replayed `interrupt` just sends another Esc (no-op in claude), so no nonce/dedup. Maps to the neutral `turnevent.Cancel` (declared #707, routed via the seam not constructed — the `modal_cancel` precedent; #600 maps ACP `session/cancel` onto the same shape). Multi-phone: any interactive paired phone interrupts the single live claude (no per-conn binding — the broadcast fan-out trust model). Tests: `internal/relay/v2session_interrupt_test.go` (`fakeInterrupter`; capability-by-path / nil-inert / SendEsc-error-tolerated, synced via a barrier conn opened after the frame); code-review PASS. See [`codebase/707.md`](codebase/707.md). **#831 adds the inbound `new_session` control + `/clear` routing** (`security-sensitive`, EPIC #597 Phase 3, split from #824): the remote start-new-session — a paired phone starts a fresh session, the remote equivalent of typing `/clear` locally. `dispatchAppFrame`'s discriminator gains a `TypeNewSession`→`handleNewSession` arm (beside `interrupt`; no `dispatch.Route` handler). A new consumer-declared `SessionStarter interface{ StartNewSession() error }` (declared beside `Interrupter`, so `internal/relay` imports neither supervisor nor tui-driver) + an optional nil-safe `V2SessionConfig.SessionStarter` field (nil ⇒ inert, no `/clear` — the no-cascade S-holding shape) wired to `*supervisor.Supervisor`'s **sealed `StartNewSession`** (#830, zero new supervisor code) via one line in `cmd/pyry/relay.go`. `handleNewSession(s)` (signature takes only `s` — no payload to decode, no reply, no broadcast, fire-and-forget) is a line-for-line `handleInterrupt` mirror, **capability-gate-first**: `if !s.interactive` → inert (reuses the `interactive`-capability-is-the-authorization posture #707 established); then nil-`SessionStarter` → debug-log inert; else best-effort `StartNewSession()` (error Warn-logged + tolerated). The frame carries **no payload** (no `conversation_id`/nonce/idempotency key) — a replayed `new_session` just drives another `/clear` (harmless), so no nonce/dedup. **Unlike `interrupt` it maps to no neutral `turnevent` command** — no announced ACP counterpart needs one, so it drives the seam directly (the `modal_cancel` precedent). **No new emitter, no ack path** — the client observes the resulting break via the pre-existing #656/#657 `session_transition` marker (`reason: clear`). Multi-phone: any interactive paired phone can start a new session on the single live claude (no per-conn binding — the broadcast fan-out trust model). Tests: `internal/relay/v2session_newsession_test.go` (`fakeSessionStarter`; capability-by-path / nil-inert / StartNewSession-error-tolerated, synced via a barrier conn opened after the frame); code-review PASS. See [`codebase/831.md`](codebase/831.md). **#723 adds the inbound `dequeue_message` control + `msgqueue.Remove`** (`security-sensitive`, EPIC #597 Phase 3, the capstone of the queued-backlog wire side; split from #705): a paired phone cancels a queued `send_message` before it drains. `dispatchAppFrame`'s discriminator gains a `TypeDequeueMessage`→`handleDequeueMessage` arm (beside `interrupt`/`modal_cancel`; no `dispatch.Route` handler). A new consumer-declared `QueueRemover interface{ Remove(conversationID string, queuedMsgID uint64) bool }` (declared beside `Interrupter`, so `internal/relay` imports neither `internal/msgqueue` nor cmd/pyry) + an optional nil-safe `V2SessionConfig.QueueRemover` field (nil ⇒ inert, no Remove) wired to the concrete `*msgqueue.Queue`'s existing `Remove` (#719, zero new msgqueue code) by widening the relay `queue` param in `cmd/pyry/relay.go`. `handleDequeueMessage(s, env)` (signature takes `(s, env)` — no ctx, the `handleInterrupt` deviation) is **capability-gate-first**: `if !s.interactive` → inert (the **second** inbound frame gated on the `interactive` capability — still a one-line check, not an abstraction); then nil-`QueueRemover` → debug-log inert; then decode-tolerantly (never echo payload bytes); then the one-line `Remove(conversation_id, queued_msg_id)`. `false` from `Remove` (unknown/already-delivered/in-flight-head id, or unknown/foreign conversation_id) is **success of a valid request** (AC-2): no reply, no error, no broadcast. **AC-4 convergence is automatic** — `Remove`'s `notify` fires the #719 `OnChange` seam on a successful removal, driving the #722 `queueStateEmitterV2` (the sole `queue_state` emitter) to push the updated backlog; the handler MUST NOT re-emit `queue_state`. Security: a hostile `conversation_id` is confined by `Remove`'s convID-scoping (`Remove(A,…)` provably never touches conversation B); **deliberately no `KnownConversation`/`Route` membership gate** — the frame only mutates the named conversation's own existing backlog and returns nothing, so the deterministic scoping is self-validating and a stochastic gate would risk a false-negative drop (Belt-and-Suspenders → different fabric; Evidence-Based Fix Selection). Reconciles the now-stale `handleInterrupt` "dequeue is ungated" comment. Tests: `internal/relay/v2session_dequeue_test.go` (`fakeQueueRemover`; capability-by-path / nil-inert / decode-tolerant-never-echo / intercepted-not-routed, barrier-conn synced) + e2e `internal/e2e/relay_v2_dequeue_test.go` (AC-5 capstone: enqueue two under sleep-claude → dequeue the non-head → backlog `[msg1]`, no error envelope); code-review PASS. See [`codebase/723.md`](codebase/723.md). **#774 adds the in-repo idle sweep that tears down a dropped/backgrounded phone's encrypted session** (`security-sensitive`): the relay↔binary leg is a single multiplexed WebSocket with no per-connection disconnect frame, so a silently-gone phone's `V2Session` — two Noise `CipherState`s + an armed 1-hour `rekeyTimer` — lingered up to an hour before its scheduled rekey went unanswered and closed at 4426. A new per-session `idleTimer` (armed at `V2StateOpen` beside `rekeyTimer`) chases `lastActivityAt` (a Run-owned field stamped on every inbound frame in `handleFrame`); on fire it either reschedules for the remaining window (a frame arrived after it was armed — `time.Since(lastActivityAt) < idleTimeout`) or tears the session down via the existing `closeWith` at a new WS close code `StatusIdleTimeout = 4408` (echoes HTTP 408, extending the 44xx←HTTP convention), which drops both `CipherState`s, stops+nils all three per-session timers, and deletes the session + push queue. Wired through the **existing** `time.AfterFunc → wakeSignal → handleWake` machinery (#450) with a new `wakeIdleTimeout` `wakeKind` — the `armIdleTimer` callback touches only `m.wake`, and the reschedule/teardown decision runs on the Run goroutine (the loop is the lock). Stale-wake safety = two deterministic guards composed (`state != V2StateOpen` + `idle < idleTimeout`), so no active session is torn down by a stale wake; a swept-then-returning phone re-handshakes cleanly via the normal lazy-create path (no #647 replay reconciliation needed). `idleTimeout` is a lowercase test-overridable package var (15 min default, well short of `rekeyInterval`), mirroring the `rekeyInterval`/`modalDenyTimeout` idiom; not yet config-driven. One production file (`internal/relay/v2session.go`, ~103 LOC) + the `4408` row in `docs/protocol-mobile.md`; no new package/goroutine/exported-type-beyond-the-close-code, no signature changes. This is the in-repo coarse backstop for the "phone-disconnected cleanup" gap the Out-of-scope section previously tracked as unaddressed; the event-driven exact-disconnect signal remains cross-repo relay work (future ticket). Four `-race`-clean same-package tests (full-teardown/AC-1, activity-reschedule, churn-returns-to-baseline/AC-3, swept-then-reconnect/AC-3); security review PASS. See [`codebase/774.md`](codebase/774.md). Optional `V2SessionConfig.DevicesPath` (#782): when set, the handshake `Reload`s `devices.json` before `Validate` so a post-startup pairing is accepted without a daemon restart; `""` disables the reload (byte-stable opt-out); reload errors fail closed (log `path`+static reason, never the wrapped `err`; proceed against the retained set). **`StreamBundle(ctx, connID, blob)` (#812, `security-sensitive`)**: the debug-bundle streaming primitive — moves an arbitrarily-large `[]byte` (the #811 assembler's output, routinely multi-MB) to one open, authenticated conn as ordered, cap-respecting `debug_bundle_chunk` frames ending in a `debug_bundle_done{total}` marker, by looping `Push` (the async `Push`→`drainOnce`→`forwardEnvelope` path), **never** the synchronous handler-reply channel (`handlerOutboundBuf = 8`, deadlocks on >8 sends from a handler). Clears both walls that stop a bundle riding a normal reply: the 65535-byte AEAD frame cap and the handler-buffer deadlock. New file `internal/relay/v2bundlestream.go`: pure `bundleEnvelopes` chunker (0-based ascending `Seq`, trailing `done`, `EventID` nil so the replay dedup guard is inert); `bundleChunkBytes = 48000` conservative const (~1.3 KB headroom under the cap — belt) enforced by `TestStreamBundle_EveryFrameWithinCap` (per-frame ciphertext-≤-cap — suspenders); exported pure `ReassembleBundle` receiver-contract oracle (no daemon inbound caller — production use is phone-side) that fails cleanly (`nil, err`) on reorder/gap/duplicate/`total`-mismatch/missing-marker, skipping interleaved non-bundle frames. Bundle chunks are **control-class → never dropped** (all delivered, in order, AC#2); an integrity contract of two nets (AEAD per-frame content + `Seq`/`Total` structural). Logs one content-free line (`conn_id`,`chunks`,`bytes` — never the bytes, AC#4). **Ships unwired**; the request verb is #813. Architect + code-review security passes both PASS. See [`codebase/812.md`](codebase/812.md). **#857 adds a fifth `handleRequestSnapshot` seam**, `SnapshotUsage func() (usedTokens, windowTokens int)` (optional, primitive-typed, same shape as `SnapshotSettings`), populating the `screen_snapshot` reply's `used_tokens`/`window_tokens` fields from [`internal/contextwindow.Read`](features/contextwindow-package.md) (#856). Read only on the success path, nil-guarded to `(0, 0)`. The `cmd/pyry` closure is built in `relay.go` (not threaded from `main.go` like `snapshotSettings`) over a **dedicated** `resolveOwnBootstrapJSONL` instance (`cmd/pyry/interactive_turn_stream_v2.go`, the turn-stream's own probe-preferred resolver, reused via a second instance rather than `internal/sessions`' `ResolveTranscript` seam — see [codebase/857.md](codebase/857.md) for why); any resolver or `Read` error collapses to `Read("")`'s deterministic fresh-session report. **Not `security-sensitive`.** See [`codebase/857.md`](codebase/857.md). **Transport-down hold on the push drain (#874, `security-sensitive`, layer 1 of the reconnect-reliability design, umbrella #829):** an additive `V2SessionConfig.Connected func() bool` transport level-poll (nil-optional; production wires `(*Connection).Connected` → `(*transport.Client).IsConnected`, a new synchronous poll sibling to the existing edge-triggered `Connected() <-chan struct{}`) is consulted by `drainOnce` **before** the pop — a `false` result holds the queue head un-popped and unsealed instead of sealing-and-dropping it, so no Noise send-nonce is burned for a control envelope that cannot reach the phone during a daemon↔relay reconnect blip (a burned nonce previously gapped the phone's recv nonce and tore the still-live session down at WS 4421). Session-level failures (`ErrConnNotFound`/`ErrSessionNotOpen`/seal failure) still drop in `forwardEnvelope`, downstream of the probe, unchanged. Lazy re-flush only (the next `Push`'s `drainCh` signal); immediate flush-on-reconnect is #875. Security review PASS, net-positive. See [`codebase/874.md`](codebase/874.md); the eager reconnect trigger — `V2SessionConfig.Reconnect` optional seam + one `Run` select arm re-signalling `drainCh` (never calling `drainOnce` directly), fed by `relay.Connection.Reconnected()`'s fan-out of the single-observer transport edge — lands in #875, not security-sensitive (wake timing only, every nonce increment stays gated by #874's `Connected` probe). See [`codebase/875.md`](codebase/875.md). **Connect-time modal reconcile (#877, `security-sensitive`):** an optional `V2SessionConfig.OutstandingModals func() []protocol.ModalShownPayload` seam (nil ⇒ inert) + `reconcileModals` helper — a structural sibling of `broadcastModalDismissed` minus the fan-out — unicasts the current outstanding `modal_shown` set to `s.connID` from `handleNoiseInit`'s success tail, ahead of the #647 replay hook, so a phone that (re)connects while a permission prompt is pending is brought to current modal truth instead of riding the deny-on-timeout window unseen; mints no nonce, re-arms no timer, consumes the #876 `Registry.Snapshot()` wired via `cmd/pyry`'s `modalReg.Snapshot`. See [`codebase/877.md`](codebase/877.md). Twin `security-sensitive` `OutstandingQueues func() []protocol.QueueStatePayload` seam (nil ⇒ inert) + `reconcileQueues` helper — structural twin of `reconcileModals`, `TypeQueueState`/`QueueStatePayload` substituted — unicasts the current per-conversation `queue_state` for every non-empty backlog to `s.connID` immediately after `reconcileModals` in the same success tail, so a phone that connects/reconnects between backlog changes sees current queue truth instead of the stale/empty view #722's push-on-change leaves; mints no id, dequeues nothing, consumes the new `msgqueue.Queue.SnapshotAll()` enumeration seam via `cmd/pyry`'s `outstandingQueues` adapter and reuses the #722 `toQueueStatePayload` mapping (no new payload type) (#878). See [`codebase/878.md`](codebase/878.md). **Concurrent app-frame reply drain (#909, `security-sensitive`):** `dispatchAppFrame` no longer runs `dispatch.Route` synchronously then drains `outbound` only after it returns — `Route` now runs on a short-lived goroutine while `Run` interleaves draining (sealing via the extracted `forwardAppReply`) with waiting for it, so a handler emitting more than `handlerOutboundBuf` (8) replies can no longer fill the channel and wedge the manager for every conn; `s.send.Encrypt` stays reachable only from `Run` (security review PASS, no nonce/counter-corruption path), single-sender/single-receiver channel semantics preserve FIFO emission order, and `outbound` is still never closed (#446). See [`codebase/909.md`](codebase/909.md). Bounds repeated `request_debug_bundle` requests on a stalled/slow transport to one in-flight bundle's chunks per conn via a `pushMu`-guarded `bundleInFlight` scan checked before assembly — closes a retry-driven unbounded-memory amplification the control-class bundle chunks opened, and corrects the now-false "unreachable in practice" premise on `pushQueue`'s soft-overflow comment (#911, `security-sensitive`); #1491 moves the `DebugBundler` seam call itself off `Run` onto a short-lived per-request goroutine (`assembleBundle` → `m.bundleReady` → `handleBundleReady`, every `s.send.Encrypt` staying on `Run`) so an uncapped recording read+gzip can no longer stall the dispatch loop, and gives `bundleInFlight`'s queue-derived gate its missing accept-side half, `V2Session.bundleAssembling` (`security-sensitive`). See [`codebase/911.md`](codebase/911.md). **Scheduled + manual rekey emit gated behind `transportDown()` (#912, `security-sensitive`):** extends the #874 hold to `emitRekeyRequest`'s two callers — a `transportDown()` gate at each call site (not inside the primitive, since `handleManualRekey` stops the scheduled timer before emitting). Scheduled path (`handleWake`'s `wakeRekeyEmit` arm) defers instead of sealing: re-arms a new `armRekeyRetryTimer` at a short `rekeyRetryInterval` (1m, bounded under the 15m `idleTimeout`) so the emit self-heals once the transport recovers, with no nonce burned, no `awaitingRekeyReply`, no reply timer, and no more mislabelled `noise.rekey_failed`/4426 teardown of a healthy session. Manual path (`handleManualRekey`) returns a new `ErrTransportDown` sentinel, distinct from `ErrSessionNotOpen`, leaving the scheduled cadence untouched. `emitRekeyRequest` itself is unchanged beyond a documented precondition doc-line. See [`codebase/912.md`](codebase/912.md). **Handler execution offloaded off `Run` (#965, `security-sensitive`):** extends #909 — `dispatchAppFrame`'s application-frame tail is now a non-blocking enqueue onto a new per-conn `V2Session.appFrames` FIFO (`appFrameQueueDepth` = 16; overflow closes the conn at 4421 rather than block `Run`), drained by a new per-conn worker goroutine (`appFrameWorker`, one per open session, spawned in `handleNoiseInit`'s open tail, stopped via `close(s.done)` in `closeWith`) that runs `routeAppFrame` (the #909 concurrent-Route-drain shape, unchanged) off `Run` and posts each reply to a new unbuffered `V2SessionManager.appReply` channel; `Run`'s new `m.appReply` select arm calls `forwardAppReply` (now gated on `s.state == V2StateOpen`, dropping replies for a torn-down session) to seal under `s.send`, so every `s.send.Encrypt`/`s.recv.Decrypt` stays on the single-owner `Run` goroutine while a slow handler (e.g. `create_conversation`'s 30s spawn) no longer stalls other conns' frames or `Run`'s timers (`m.wake`/`m.modalTimeout`/`m.manualRekey`). Per-conn FIFO ordering holds structurally: the single worker fully drains frame N's replies before dequeuing N+1. Closes the reply-*latency* head-of-line stall #909 explicitly deferred (#909 fixed the reply-*count* deadlock). Security review PASS: no off-`Run` seal, no nonce-reuse path, bounded inbound queue, no dead-session emission. See [`codebase/965.md`](codebase/965.md). § "Stream-json approval bridge — the verdict arm" (#1080) covers `ResolveAnswer`'s second actuation arm: `modalResolverV2.streamApprovals` (optional, #1014-shaped) dispatches to `cmd/pyry`'s `streamApprovalBridge.ResolveStream`, which resolves a `permbridge`-parked completer (allow echoes `Input` verbatim, deny uses the fixed `reasonRemoteDeny`) instead of routing a tui keystroke; `streamApprovalBridge.Surface`/`retire` (installed via `internal/control`'s new `SetApprovalSurfacer` seam) own the `modal_id ⇄ tool_use_id` correlation and the single-arbiter dismissal across answer/timeout/disconnect/shutdown — see [codebase/1080.md](codebase/1080.md); **since #1125** the `new_session` `SessionStarter` seam is wired not to the bootstrap supervisor but to a `cmd/pyry`-side `activeSessionStarter` adapter that resolves the active conversation's bound runner and dispatches by concrete type — a bound `*streamsup.Runner` gets a **direct** rotation (`Pool.RotateForNewSession` mints+re-keys+registers-allocated *then* `RestartFresh(newID)`, **no `/clear` keystroke**), while a bound `*supervisor.Supervisor` keeps the original `/clear`-and-let-the-watcher-rotate behavior for zero PTY regression — the `new_session` routing twin of `interrupt`'s #1121 fix, see [codebase/1125.md](codebase/1125.md). **Since #1192** the interrupt route's three signature-stable inert arms — `handleInterrupt`'s non-interactive-conn return and `activeInterrupter.SendEsc`'s no-active-conversation and no-bound-runner returns — each emit an `Info`-level `v2.interrupt.*` record (`conn_id` / `conversation_id`) instead of returning silently, closing the ambiguity a live 2m10s-silent daemon log left between "frame never arrived," "conn not interactive," "route inert," and "keystroke ignored"; **since #1193** the successful actuation and the neither-method inert arm each also emit (`v2.interrupt.dispatched` naming the arm dispatched — `interrupt` or `send_esc`; `v2.interrupt.no_actuator` for neither), closing the invariant: every path through the route now records, so an empty `v2.interrupt.*` log on a wired daemon means the frame never arrived — see [codebase/1192.md](codebase/1192.md), [codebase/1193.md](codebase/1193.md). |
| [noise-package.md](features/noise-package.md) | `internal/noise` (#433, extended #452) — narrow Go wrapper around `github.com/flynn/noise v1.1.0` for the `Noise_IK_25519_ChaChaPoly_BLAKE2s` cipher suite pinned by Mobile Protocol v2. Owns the suite name in exactly one source location (`var cipherSuite = flynnNoise.NewCipherSuite(DH25519, CipherChaChaPoly, HashBLAKE2s)`) — a future suite migration is a one-line edit. Eleven exports total: `KeyLen = 32` const, `ErrInvalidKeyLength` sentinel, `Responder` / `Initiator` / `CipherState` types, `NewResponder(staticPriv []byte) (*Responder, error)` / `Responder.ReadInit(initMsg []byte) ([]byte, error)` / `Responder.WriteResp(earlyData []byte) (respMsg []byte, send, recv *CipherState, err error)` (the binary side), `NewInitiator(staticPriv, peerStaticPub []byte) (*Initiator, error)` / `Initiator.WriteInit(earlyData []byte) ([]byte, error)` / `Initiator.ReadResp(respMsg []byte) (earlyData []byte, send, recv *CipherState, err error)` (the phone side — production initiator is the mobile-team's Kotlin/Swift impl, this Go side exists for full round-trip tests without a phone), `CipherState.Encrypt(plaintext []byte) ([]byte, error)` / `Decrypt(ciphertext []byte) ([]byte, error)`. No `HandshakeState` / `Config` / `HandshakePattern` / `CipherSuite` / `DHKey` re-export — flynn types do not leak through the API; a migration off `flynn/noise` touches only `noise.go`. **Empty associated-data is enforced at the type system** — `Encrypt`/`Decrypt` have no AD parameter, internally call flynn's `Encrypt(nil, nil, plaintext)` / `Decrypt(nil, nil, ciphertext)`; the v2 spec mandate (`docs/protocol-mobile.md:197` — "Implementations MUST NOT pass a non-empty AD without a corresponding spec amendment") is structurally impossible to violate without editing this package. Per-handshake key derivation + per-session nonce-counter discipline make outer-envelope binding redundant; the outer routing envelope (`conn_id`, `frame`) is plaintext and intentionally not AEAD-bound. **Constructors length-check before any flynn call** — `len(staticPriv) != KeyLen` (or `len(peerStaticPub) != KeyLen` on initiator) returns wrapped `ErrInvalidKeyLength` (`errors.Is`-matchable) with prefix naming which key tripped (`"noise: responder static key:"`, `"noise: initiator peer static pub:"`). Public-key derivation via stdlib `crypto/ecdh.X25519().NewPrivateKey(staticPriv).PublicKey().Bytes()`, not flynn — matches `internal/keys.StaticKey.PrivateKey()` byte-for-byte (same `crypto/ecdh` source). **Defensive `append([]byte(nil), key...)` copy of every key argument** into the `DHKey{Private, Public}` struct so a caller-side mutation after construction (or hypothetical future `StaticKey` zeroisation) cannot corrupt the live handshake state. `Random: rand.Reader` explicit in both `Config` literals — flynn falls back to a default when nil, but the explicit wire removes one "did the default change?" question and pins entropy source visibly. **Responder swaps cs1/cs2; initiator does not** (flynn returns `(cs1, cs2)` where cs1 carries initiator→responder traffic and cs2 carries responder→initiator traffic — asymmetric per role; the wrapper collapses to symmetric `(send, recv)` shape on both sides). Swap pinned structurally by `TestRoundTrip_BothDirections` (if `WriteResp` returned `(cs1, cs2)` instead of `(cs2, cs1)`, the very first cross-side `Decrypt` fails with a MAC error) — no comment-only assertion; the test is the load-bearing pin. Single sentinel `ErrInvalidKeyLength`; everything else (MAC failure, malformed message, out-of-order handshake call, AEAD failure, counter exhaustion, oversize message) returns bare wrapped `fmt.Errorf` because the caller (#434) closes the WS with one close code per surface (4426 handshake, 4421 transport) regardless of the underlying flynn reason — finer-grained sentinels are deferred until a caller needs to branch. **Zero `slog` calls** in the package; every error has shape `"noise: <op>: <flynn message>"` and never echoes plaintext / ciphertext / key bytes / early-data payload (pinned by `TestErrorMessages_DoNotLeakPlaintextOrKey` which encrypts a 16-byte high-entropy `crypto/rand` probe, tampers the ciphertext, asserts the resulting error string contains neither the hex nor the base64 encoding of the probe). **Not safe for concurrent use** — each `Responder` / `Initiator` / `CipherState` is owned by one goroutine (flynn's `CipherState` carries a mutable 64-bit nonce counter; concurrent access would corrupt it); the wrapper adds no locks because adding them would mask programming errors at the caller layer (#434 maintains a single dispatch loop per `conn_id` satisfying this structurally). Single production file `internal/noise/noise.go` (~215 LOC) — splitting into `responder.go` / `initiator.go` / `cipher.go` was rejected (each section is ~30 LOC, three-file overhead buys no readability). Package location `internal/noise/` chosen over `internal/encryption/noise/` (the alternative offered by the ticket body) — every internal-package occupant is one level deep; an `encryption/` intermediate for a single occupant breaks the established layout. `flynn/noise v1.1.0` is the only new direct dependency (latest stable, released Feb 2024, pure Go, no CGo, used by Tailscale's control protocol with the same cipher suite — production precedent); `golang.org/x/crypto v0.51.0` promoted from indirect to direct by `go mod tidy` (flynn imports `chacha20poly1305` + `blake2s`). Tests are same-package (white-box) with `t.Parallel()` everywhere, stdlib `testing` only, no mocks — the test "phone" is a real `Initiator` built in the same test; 11 tests cover full round-trip + both-directions transport + 32-frame loop + tampered/truncated handshake messages + wrong-responder-static rejection (test doc-comment cites the spec reconciliation: AC said "initiator's `ReadResp` errors" but the natural failure surface in IK is responder's `ReadInit` because DH outputs disagree when decrypting the encrypted-`s` field of message 1 — the responder never produces a `respMsg`) + bad-key-length matrices for both constructors + out-of-order / replayed transport frame rejection + the no-leak hygiene assertion. No production consumer in this PR — `internal/relay`'s per-`conn_id` state machine (#434) and the re-key state machine (#435) are the planned consumers. |
| [dispatch-package.md](features/dispatch-package.md) | `internal/dispatch` — single-inbound-frame routing primitive (#307; extended #318/#319/#446). Current surface: `Handler func(ctx, *Conn, protocol.Envelope) error`; `Conn{ConnID() string, NextID() uint64 (atomic.Uint64, starts at 1), Auth() *devices.Device, Send(ctx, env), Reply(ctx, req, respType, payload)}`; `NewConn`/`NewTestConn(id, outbound, auth) *Conn` (auth is write-once at construction — the v2 session manager passes its handshake-matched device, live reader `internal/relay/handlers/register_push_token.go`); `Route(ctx, logger, conn, handlers, frame)` — synchronous, runs on the caller's goroutine, decodes frame, refuses frames whose type is not a known inbound app-frame type via `protocol.IsKnownAppType`, dispatches by `Envelope.Type`: malformed→`protocol.malformed` (no `in_reply_to`), encrypted→`protocol.unsupported`, unknown-type→`protocol.unknown_type`, no-handler→`protocol.unsupported`; `sendError` (unexported) seals error envelopes to `Code*` + static `Message` only. Goroutine-free — no demux, no per-conn goroutines; the v2 session manager (`internal/relay/v2session.go`) owns whatever goroutine calls `Route`. **The v1 `Dispatcher`/`FirstFrameGate` gate-and-demux state machine (#307–#446: `Config`, `FirstFrameGate`+`FirstFrameOutcome`, `Dispatcher`, `New`/`Register`/`Run`/`Outbound`/`ActiveConns`, the per-conn auth-slot writer `setAuth`, one demux goroutine + N per-conn goroutines) was orphaned when #913 removed the v1 relay branch (0 production callers thereafter) and physically deleted by #1039** — not security-sensitive (unwired gate, nothing live to audit); `dispatch_test.go`'s four kept-branch tests (`TestMalformedInnerFrame`/`TestUnknownType`/`TestEncryptedRefusal`/`TestIDCounter_MonotonicPerConn`) were re-homed to direct `Route`/`Conn` calls rather than deleted, `gate_test.go` deleted whole. `relay.AuthenticateFirstFrame` (in `internal/relay`) was untouched by #1039 and remained an unreferenced exported function until **#1040 deleted it** (plus `AuthOutcome`/`ErrMalformedHelloFrame`/`buildResponse`); `auth.go` now holds only the `StatusUnauthorized`/`MsgInvalidToken` WS close-code constants consumed by the live Noise_IK v2 handshake. See [codebase/1039.md](codebase/1039.md), [codebase/1040.md](codebase/1040.md) for the removals; [codebase/307.md](codebase/307.md), [codebase/308.md](codebase/308.md), [codebase/311.md](codebase/311.md), [codebase/318.md](codebase/318.md) for the removed design's history. |
| [devices-package.md](features/devices-package.md) | `internal/devices` — `Device` struct (`TokenHash`, `Name`, `PairedAt`, `LastSeenAt`; snake_case JSON tags; #282 appends optional `Platform string json:"platform,omitempty"` (`"fcm"`/`"apns"`) and `PushToken string json:"push_token,omitempty"` — the on-disk counterpart of `protocol.RegisterPushTokenPayload` from #275, written by the future #250 handler; `omitempty` keeps pre-#282 disk files round-trip-clean, zero-migration change; #702 appends optional `AllowRemotePermissions bool json:"allow_remote_permissions,omitempty"` — the per-device remote-permission gate, default OFF/denied), `HashToken(plain) string` (lowercase SHA-256 hex), `VerifyToken(plain, hash) bool` (constant-time via `crypto/subtle.ConstantTimeCompare`, length-mismatch falls out via the same path); #702 adds the remote-permission gate in `auth.go` — `(*Device).MayAnswerRemotePermission() bool` (nil-guarded eligibility), `AuthorizeRemotePermission(d, RemotePermissionOutcome) bool` (fail-closed decision; sole ALLOW = eligible × `OutcomeAllow`, every other outcome incl. no-answer/timeout/cancel denies), `RemotePermissionOutcome` enum — both pure predicates, consumed by the #703 modal control loop, set only by `pyry pair --allow-remote-permissions`; pure functions, stdlib only, foundation for Phase 3 `pyry pair` + WS-handshake auth, no runtime consumers wired in this slice; #1527 appends optional `RedeemBy time.Time json:"redeem_by,omitzero"` — deadline at which an unredeemed pairing record stops being acceptable, stamped by `runPairDefault` at mint time as `PairedAt + RedemptionWindow` (`const RedemptionWindow = 15 * time.Minute`); `omitzero` not `omitempty` because `omitempty` is a silent no-op on a `time.Time`; nothing reads the field this slice (`Validate`, `reconcileDevices`, the v2 handshake, `pair list`, `pair revoke` all unchanged) — see [`codebase/1527.md`](../codebase/1527.md); follow-ons #1528 (record redemption) and #1529 (enforce) |
| [devices-registry.md](features/devices-registry.md) | `~/.pyry/<name>/devices.json` — `internal/devices.Registry` (`Load` / `Save` / `Add` / `Remove` / `List` / `FindByTokenHash` / `Validate` / `UpdatePushRegistration`); mutex-guarded slice + atomic-rename save (`{"devices": [...]}` envelope, sort by `PairedAt` then `Name` for byte-deterministic output, parent `0o700` / file `0o600`); `Save` snapshots under lock + writes outside (auth path stays unblocked, see [ADR 020](decisions/020-devices-registry-snapshot-then-write.md)); `(*Registry).Validate(plain)` (#210) is the WS-perimeter auth predicate — empty-plain early-out before lock/hash, then `HashToken` outside the lock + scan + in-memory `LastSeenAt = time.Now()` advance + snapshot under `mu`, no `Save` (caller schedules durability); `(*Registry).UpdatePushRegistration(tokenHash, platform, pushToken, name) bool` (#250) is the in-memory mutator keyed by `TokenHash` — sets `Platform` / `PushToken` / `Name` together under `r.mu` (phone is source of truth for self-reported name), returns `true` iff a row matched, caller chains `Save` for durability; consumed by `internal/relay/handlers.Handle`; empty-or-missing file → empty registry, malformed → wrapped `registry: parse` error; storage primitive for Phase 3 `pyry pair` / `pyry pair revoke` / WS-handshake auth, no consumers wired in this slice; `(*Registry).Reload(path)` (#782) reconciles the on-disk set into memory (keep-in-memory-survivor keyed on `TokenHash`, membership == disk, fail-closed on malformed/unreadable) so a device paired after daemon startup authenticates at its next v2 handshake without a restart — called before `Validate` at the handshake and before `Save` in `register_push_token` (clobber guard — narrows only the daemon's own window, not `pyry pair`'s side; corrected 2026-08-19, see [ADR 029](decisions/029-devices-registry-reload-at-handshake.md#correction-2026-08-19-1530)); cross-process exclusion for all three writers is `internal/devices.WithLock` (#1530, [`codebase/1530.md`](codebase/1530.md)), a sidecar `flock(2)` primitive not yet wired into any caller |
