| [conversations-auto-archive.md](features/conversations-auto-archive.md) | `internal/conversations.ShouldArchive(c, now)` pure predicate (#219; unpromoted ≥30 days idle archives, promoted exempt; threshold const, `>=` boundary, `now` injected; #1488 adds an `IsArchived` guard exempting manually-archived rows from the destructive sweep — manual archive is durable until the user unarchives, see [codebase/1488.md](codebase/1488.md)) + `Sweep(reg, now) int` pure iterate-and-apply primitive (#237; snapshots `reg.List()`, applies the predicate, calls `reg.Delete`, returns the count; no I/O, no `Save`) + `RunSweepLoop(ctx, reg, path, interval, log) error` ticker wrapper (#242; ticks every `interval`, calls `Sweep(reg, time.Now())`, conditionally `reg.Save(path)` only on non-zero count, INFO on success / ERROR-and-continue on Save failure, returns nil on ctx cancellation, no final on-shutdown sweep) + `SweepInterval = time.Hour` exported const + `sweepOnce` unexported single-tick body factored for tests + daemon wiring (#243): `cmd/pyry/main.go` resolves `~/.pyry/<name>/conversations.json` via `resolveConversationsRegistryPath` (sibling of `resolveRegistryPath`), calls `conversations.Load` after `tryAutoAttach` (malformed JSON wraps as `loading conversations: %w` and fails startup; missing file is benign cold start), plumbs `*Registry` + path through `sessions.Config.ConversationsRegistry` / `ConversationsRegistryPath` into matching unexported `Pool` fields; `Pool.Run` gates `g.Go(RunSweepLoop)` on `p.convReg != nil` so existing tests stay untouched + sweep-interval seam (#262): `sessions.Config.SweepInterval time.Duration` (zero = use `SweepInterval` default) plumbs through to the unexported `Pool.convSweepInterval` field consulted by `Pool.Run`; surfaced via the `cmd/pyry` `-pyry-conv-sweep-interval` flag (visible, `(testing; 0 = production default of 1h)`) for out-of-process e2e tests downstream of #251; the original `var convSweepInterval` package-level seam + `withConvSweepInterval` helper are removed (one seam, not two) |
| [conversations-registry.md](features/conversations-registry.md) | `~/.pyry/<name>/conversations.json` — `internal/conversations.Registry` (`Load` / `Save` / `Create` / `Get` / `List` / `Update` / `Delete` / `Promote`); mutex-guarded slice + atomic-rename save (`{"conversations": [...]}` envelope, sort by `LastUsedAt` then `ID` for byte-deterministic output, parent `0o700` / file `0o600`); `Save` snapshots under `r.mu` for the in-memory copy but serializes the *entire* snapshot→encode→fsync→rename sequence under a dedicated `saveMu` (#868, [codebase/868.md](codebase/868.md)) so snapshot order always equals rename order (no cross-`Save` lost update) while read/mutate concurrency during the write is preserved (`saveMu → mu` one-directional lock order) — diverged from `devices`'s plain snapshot-then-write-unlocked posture, see [ADR 020](decisions/020-devices-registry-snapshot-then-write.md)'s 2026-07-10 update; `Update(id, fn func(*Conversation)) bool` runs `fn` with `r.mu` held + pointer to slice element (no-callback-back, no-pointer-retention contract — see [ADR 022](decisions/022-conversations-update-callback-under-lock.md)); `List(filter ...ListFilter)` variadic-for-ergonomics with `IsPromoted *bool` filter (`filter[0]` only); `Promote(id, name) error` (#218) flips `IsPromoted` and sets `Name` under `r.mu` with a same-lock uniqueness scan against other *promoted* records, four exported sentinels (`ErrConversationNotFound` / `ErrConversationAlreadyPromoted` / `ErrPromotionNameInUse` / `ErrPromotionNameEmpty`) for `errors.Is` mapping to wire codes by later CLI/wire bindings, no implicit `Save`; empty-or-missing file → empty registry, malformed → wrapped `registry: parse` error; `Delete(id) bool` (#237) is the slice-element-removal mutation primitive originally consumed by `Sweep` (also #237 — see [conversations-auto-archive.md](features/conversations-auto-archive.md)) and, since #822, by the `delete_conversation` daemon handler's hard (permanent) delete — no `Save` (caller's concern), order-preserving, snapshot-safe (mid-iteration `Delete` does not disturb a `List()` snapshot already in flight); storage primitive for Phase 3 auto-archive (#219/#237), no daemon-side consumers wired in this slice (CLI `pyry conv promote` remains deferred; the mobile `promote_conversation` frame landed as a consumer in #949 — see [relay-package.md](features/relay-package.md) / [codebase/949.md](codebase/949.md)); `RebindSession(oldID, newID string) bool` (#739) re-points the conversation bound to `oldID` (matched by `CurrentSessionID`, first-match-and-stop) at `newID` and appends `oldID` to `SessionHistory` in a single `r.mu` critical section (no implicit `Save`, empty-`oldID` short-circuits to `false` so an unbound row is never swept) — the first production writer of `SessionHistory`, consumed by the pool's `/clear` rotation path (see [conversation-session-binding.md](features/conversation-session-binding.md)); `SetArchived(id, archived bool) bool` (#880) flips the durable `IsArchived` flag under `r.mu`, structurally touching only that one field (deterministic enforcement, not convention), hit/miss + no implicit `Save`, symmetric one-arg archive/restore toggle, consumed by the `archive_conversation`/`unarchive_conversation` handler (#881); `ListFilter` gained `IsArchived *bool` (#880), ANDing with `IsPromoted` when both set on one filter (orthogonal to the separate "only filter[0] of multiple ListFilter args" variadic rule); distinct from the auto-archive `Sweep`'s hard-delete but no longer independent of it — since #1488 `ShouldArchive` reads `IsArchived` and the sweep exempts archived rows (see [conversations-auto-archive.md](features/conversations-auto-archive.md), [codebase/1488.md](codebase/1488.md)) — see [codebase/880.md](codebase/880.md) |

| [conversation-session-binding.md](features/conversation-session-binding.md) | How each phone-created discussion gets its own dedicated claude session (EPIC #672). **Create half (#677, per-`Cwd` spawn #685, scratch creation #696):** `create_conversation` eagerly mints + binds a session via `sessions.Pool.CreateIn`, recorded on `Conversation.CurrentSessionID`, via a narrow `handlers.SessionCreator` seam (`Create(ctx, label, spawnDir)`) adapted at `cmd/pyry` (`sessionMinter`); 30s mint timeout → retryable `server.binary_offline`. The bound session now spawns in the conversation's own phone-influenced `Cwd` (#685): the cmd-layer `sessionMinter`/`resolveSpawnDir` is the sole validator — a set `Cwd` is confined to `$HOME` + trust-marked to its realpath (mirroring the bootstrap confine→trust posture, AC#3 byte-identity) before `CreateIn`; a `$HOME`-escape (incl. a symlinked ancestor) → non-retryable `protocol.malformed` (`ErrSpawnDirRejected`) with no half-bound row; a null `Cwd` → shared trusted workdir (AC#4, unchanged). #696 lets the phone's default `~/.pyrycode/scratch` open on a fresh host: `expandTilde` (leading `~`/`~/` → `$HOME`, no `$VAR`/`~user`) + `confineWorkdirToHomeCreating` (canonicalise the longest existing ancestor via an `os.Lstat` walk, `MkdirAll 0700` the missing suffix only after the `$HOME` check, then re-confine) — narrowly reversing #685's "non-existent path rejected" posture for the phone path; `confineWorkdirToHome` + daemon startup unchanged, symlinked-ancestor escape never created. **Routing half (#678):** `send_message` resolves the frame's `ConversationID` → bound session via a `handlers.SessionRouter` seam (`sessionRouter`/`boundSession` at `cmd/pyry`) and delivers the inbound turn *there* instead of the bootstrap, so discussions are isolated; an unbound conversation is rejected *before* any `Pool.Lookup` (`Lookup("")` returns bootstrap — the AC#4 hazard) → retryable `server.binary_offline`; re-activation funnels through cap-enforcing `Pool.Activate`. **Outbound attribution (#687):** a `cmd/pyry` *active-conversation* signal (`activeConversation`, stamped by `sessionRouter.Route` on success) re-keys the structured turn stream's two cursor readers (live emitter + #647 replay) off the now-empty bootstrap cursor, so the routed reply stream emits again and stamps the right `conversation_id`; making reply *content* follow the bound transcript is #679 (`blocked-by` #687), and the coarse v1/v2 bridges still tap the bootstrap cursor. **Per-`Cwd` turn bridge (#686):** the #679 by-id reply resolver now reads from the conversation's own `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/` directory (derived from the bound session's captured spawn `WorkDir` via `Supervisor.WorkDir()` → `perConversationSessionsDir` → `sessions.DefaultClaudeSessionsDir`) once #685 spawns sessions in distinct dirs; a default null-`Cwd` session keeps resolving from the shared dir, an underivable dir is a retryable miss (never a fallback) — confidentiality now layered on filename (UUID) × directory (spawn workdir). Per-operator create quota, the coarse-bridge re-key, and full per-conversation outbound routing deferred to #672 follow-ups. **Rotation maintenance (#739):** a `/clear` rotation now re-points the owning conversation's `CurrentSessionID` at the new id and appends the retired id to `SessionHistory` via `conversations.Registry.RebindSession`, driven from a `ReasonClear` branch in `sessions.Pool.notifyTransition` *before* the observer fan-out (the single observer slot is taken — the off-lock chokepoint co-locates the rebind and makes #741's ordering structural); persisted on hit via atomic `Save` (best-effort, `Warn`-and-swallow). Eviction is binding-neutral by control flow (`NewID == ""`, keeps its id, never enters the branch — AC#2), guarding the `send_message` respawn path. `security-sensitive` (the attribution #741 routes on); foundation for the #741 reverse lookup. **Read-and-stamp (#741):** the `session_transition` producer (`cmd/pyry`) resolves the transitioning session's owning conversation via a duplicated cmd-side read scan (`conversationForSession` over `CurrentSessionID` + `SessionHistory`, empty-`sid`-guarded, first-match) and stamps `conversation_id` onto every emitted envelope — resolving by `payload.NewSessionID` (the live id for both reasons: rotated id post-#739-rebind for `clear`, evicted id mirrored onto `new_session_id` for `idle_evict`); an unresolvable binding drops the **whole event** fail-closed (no guessed/empty key on the wire, `Run` survives) and `conversation_id` is kept out of every log. The resolver is an injected closure built in `startRelayV2` where `convReg` is already in scope (no `main.go` threading → XS, not S), so the producer never imports `internal/conversations` and `toWirePayload` stays pure. `security-sensitive` (the outbound dispatch decision a mis-resolution would leak across conversations); fills the #740 wire field, unblocks `pyrycode-mobile#336`. **Restart scope closed (#1487, security-sensitive):** a `Pool.Lookup` miss inside `resolve` (the daemon-restart case — `Pool.New` drops every minted session) now falls through to `sessionRouter.revive`, which re-validates `conv.Cwd` through `resolveSpawnDir` (the mint-time validator, re-run because a path valid at mint can become a `$HOME` escape before the restart, #696) and calls the new non-spawning `Pool.Revive`; see [sessions-package.md](sessions-package.md), [codebase/1487.md](codebase/1487.md), [ADR 035](decisions/035-lazy-revive-not-registry-rehydrate.md) |
| [conversation-session-binding-routing.md](features/conversation-session-binding-routing.md) | `send_message`'s read side, split out of `conversation-session-binding.md` (2026-09-08, over the 50000-byte cap): the `SessionRouter` seam, the empty-binding-guard and `Pool.Activate` invariants, the error-mapping table, and the enqueue-and-ack contract — including the #2159 lesson that a step run between `EnqueueDelivery` and `replyAck` races the async drain the turn was just handed to, so any handler side effect with its own latency belongs after the ack, not before it |
| [pair-package.md](features/pair-package.md) | `internal/pair` — `Payload{Server identity.ServerID, Relay, Token, ServerStaticPubkey string}` (#432 added `ServerStaticPubkey` for Mobile Protocol v2 Noise_IK trust anchor) + `Encode(p) string` (JSON → base64url no-pad) + `Decode(s) (Payload, error)` round-trip with `ErrInvalidPayload` sentinel; rejects non-base64, non-JSON-object, trailing bytes, missing/empty fields, invalid server-id, **non-base64 pubkey, wrong-length pubkey (≠ 32 bytes)**; error strings never echo input or decoded fields (token visibility discipline). Alphabet asymmetry: outer envelope is `base64.RawURLEncoding` (URL-safe, no pad) but the `server_static_pubkey` *value* inside the JSON is `base64.StdEncoding` (padded, standard alphabet) — same string form as `internal/keys`' on-disk encoding for cross-system debugging. Plus `Render(p, w io.Writer) error` (#212, extended #432) drawing a `qrterminal.GenerateHalfBlock` QR symbol + blank line + `Encode(p)` line + **`Static-key fp: aa:bb:cc:dd:ee:ff:11:22  (verify this matches the fingerprint shown on your phone)` line** + fixed instruction line; unexported `errTrackingWriter` adapter propagates writer errors through qrterminal's no-error-return API and short-circuits subsequent writes; non-panicking `<invalid>` fallback for hand-built payloads with malformed pubkey (Decode-validated payloads cannot reach this branch). Plus `Fingerprint(pubkey [32]byte) string` (#432, exported) returning `BLAKE2s-256(pubkey)[:8]` as colon-separated lowercase hex (exactly 23 chars: `8*2 + 7`); `[32]byte` (not `[]byte`) parameter makes wrong-length input a compile error. **The 8-byte / 64-bit truncation is load-bearing security** per `docs/protocol-mobile.md` § *Security review* → *[Cryptographic primitives]* — a 32-bit fingerprint is brute-forceable (≈ 2³² preimage search ≈ a few CPU-hours); test vector hardcodes the BLAKE2s digest of 32 zero bytes (`32:0b:5e:a9:9e:65:3b:c2`) rather than recomputing at test time from the same primitive (which would be tautological). Deps: stdlib + `internal/identity` + `github.com/mdp/qrterminal/v3` + `golang.org/x/crypto/blake2s` (#432; same hash mandated by `Noise_IK_25519_ChaChaPoly_BLAKE2s` per [ADR 024](decisions/024-noise-ik-mobile-e2e.md), forward-compatible with the upcoming #433 Noise wrapper). Consumed by `cmd/pyry/pair.go`'s `runPairDefault`. |
| [pyry-pair-command.md](features/pyry-pair-command.md) | `cmd/pyry/pair.go` — `pyry pair` CLI verb family (no daemon). Bare `pyry pair [-pyry-name] [--name] [--relay] [--allow-remote-permissions]` (#213, extended #432/#702) mints a 256-bit `crypto/rand` token, hex-encodes it, persists `Device{TokenHash, Name, PairedAt: now.UTC(), AllowRemotePermissions}` to `~/.pyry/<name>/devices.json` (sanitized name) — the `--allow-remote-permissions` bool flag (#702, default OFF) records the per-device remote-permission gate and is its only writer (never set over the wire), and renders `Payload{Server, Relay, Token, ServerStaticPubkey}` (QR + paste-fallback + fingerprint) to stdout — loads config + relay + devices.json + server-id + **static keypair (`keys.LoadOrCreate(resolveStaticKeyBaseDir(), sanitizeName(name))` after `identity.LoadOrCreate`, before `crypto/rand`; #432)** BEFORE the token draw, and `Save`s BEFORE `Render` so the plaintext token never escapes the process if any I/O fails (see [ADR 021](decisions/021-pair-cli-order-of-operations.md)); `keys.LoadOrCreate` failures (`ErrInvalidDaemonName` if `sanitizeName` produces a name `keys.validDaemonName` rejects — `PYRY_NAME=Foo` is the canonical case; `ErrInsecureKeyDirMode` / `ErrInsecureKeyFileMode` on weak perms; `ErrCorruptKeyFile` on parse failure) surface as `pyry: pair: keys: <wrapped>`; `staticKey.PublicKey() [32]byte` is base64-StdEncoded into the new `ServerStaticPubkey` field; `StaticKey.PrivateKey()` is never read by this verb (grep-enforceable); auto-name `device-<hash[:8]>` when `--name` omitted; relay precedence `--relay` → `Config.RelayURL` → `DefaultConfig().RelayURL`. `resolveStaticKeyBaseDir()` returns just `~/.pyry`; the `<sanitized-name>/static_key.json` suffix is owned by `internal/keys` (cannot bypass allowlist via caller-precomputed path). Sub-verb `pyry pair list [-pyry-name]` (#214) is read-only — `devices.Load` + `renderPairList` writes a `text/tabwriter`-padded `NAME / PAIRED / LAST SEEN / TOKEN-PREFIX` listing sorted by `(PairedAt, Name)` ascending, with `never` for zero `LastSeenAt` and the first 8 hex chars of `TokenHash` for visual ID; empty registry (file missing, zero-byte, or empty `devices` slice) prints exactly `No paired devices.\n` and exits 0; pure formatter `renderPairList(list, w io.Writer) error` is unit-testable byte-for-byte. Sub-verb `pyry pair revoke [-pyry-name] <name>` (#215) is destructive — `devices.Load` + `Registry.Remove(name)` + (only on `true`) `Registry.Save`, prints `Revoked <name>.\n` to stdout on success; not-found (incl. missing/empty registry) exits 1 via direct `os.Exit` with byte-exact `pyry pair revoke: no device named <name>\n` and leaves the on-disk file untouched; I/O errors exit 1 via wrapped returned error (`pyry: pair revoke: …`); usage errors exit 2. `runPair` is a sub-router (mirrors `runSessions` per [ADR 010](decisions/010-sessions-cli-sub-router.md), without `parseClientFlags` since pair doesn't dial the daemon); flags-leading args (`pyry pair --name=foo`) fall through to bare-pair via a `strings.HasPrefix(args[0], "-")` check; `pairVerbList` const updates in lockstep when sub-verbs land. Exit codes 0 / 1 (I/O or render error) / 2 (parse, empty relay, or unknown sub-verb). Sibling `RunBareIn(t, home, args...)` helper in `internal/e2e/harness.go` for HOME-isolated daemon-free e2e. Sub-verb `pyry pair preflight [-pyry-name]` (#436) is the v2 release gate — strictly opt-in, silent stdout, exit 0 on empty registry, exit 2 with byte-pinned stderr line `pyry pair preflight: <N> paired device(s); v2 release gate requires zero.` on non-empty, exit 1 (wrapped via main) on registry I/O / malformed JSON; 0/1/2 split mirrors `grep(1)` so CI can distinguish "check itself failed" from "gate fired"; pure verdict helper `preflightVerdict(count) (exitCode, stderrLine)` extracted so byte-exact contract is unit-testable without `os.Exit` subprocess; `pair list` table output unchanged. |
| [transport-package.md](features/transport-package.md) | `internal/transport` (#247, extended #248/#290/#631) — long-lived WSS client to the relay: native ping/pong heartbeat (30s/30s), exponential backoff with ±20% jitter (1s/2s/4s/8s/16s/30s cap, reset to attempt 1 after ≥60s stable connection), `Connect(ctx)` blocking lifecycle, `Send([]byte)` / `Receive(ctx) []byte` channel-proxy methods, generic over frame payload (no envelope/handshake — those live in `internal/relay`); `Config{URL, Headers, WriteTimeout, Logger, FatalCloseCodes}` (ReadTimeout dropped: heartbeat is the inactivity contract; FatalCloseCodes #248 lists WS close codes that terminate the reconnect loop with `ErrFatalClose` — relay passes `{4409}`); three pump goroutines per conn (`recvPump`/`sendPump`/`pingLoop`) under a child ctx, installed BEFORE `setConn` makes the conn observable to concurrent `Send` callers (lessons.md:290 precedent); `conn.SetReadLimit(1 MiB)` as the inbound DoS cap; `1011` close-code on reconnect (`1006` is received-only); function-typed `dialFn` seam + per-Client RNG with `newClientForTest` constructor for sub-second cadence assertions; sentinels `ErrNotConnected` / `ErrClosed` / `ErrDisconnected` (#248, surfaced from `Receive`/`Send` when the live conn drops, via a per-conn `connDone` channel; pre-closed at construction so calls before `Connect` return the sentinel rather than blocking) / `ErrFatalClose` (#248); `Connected() <-chan struct{}` buffer-1-drop-on-full signal (#248) fires on every fresh conn for the relay's forward loop (the binary↔relay handshake it once gated was retired #582); `DropConn()` (#248) force-closes via `conn.CloseNow()` (abrupt 1006-equivalent) so a transport-healthy-but-application-stuck conn can be recycled through the dial loop's reconnect (its original relay handshake-failure caller was retired #582); fatal-close classification PREFERS the recvPump error among the three pump-return errors (sendPump/pingLoop return generic `use of closed network connection` while recvPump observes the `CloseError`; without preferring, 4409 classification flakes under `-race`); new dep `github.com/coder/websocket v1.8.13` (MIT, ~2k LOC, context-first; first network-protocol dep in the project); `IsConnected() bool` (#874) — a new synchronous level poll (`false` iff `closeCh` closed or `c.conn == nil`, agrees with `Send`'s predicate) sibling to the edge-triggered `Connected()` channel, wired through `relay.Connection.Connected` into the v2 push drain's pre-seal transport-down probe, see [v2-session-manager.md](features/v2-session-manager.md); `Config.WriteTimeout` left unset (non-positive) now defaults to `defaultWriteTimeout` (10s) inside `New` (#916) — a zero value previously made `sendPump`'s per-write context instantly deadline-exceed, reconnect-looping the client |
| [relay-package.md](features/relay-package.md) | `internal/relay` (#248, extended #249/#250/#301/#303/#307/#308/#318/#319/#582/#631/#666) — binary side of the binary↔relay wire protocol. Two surfaces: (1) outbound dial, established on WS upgrade (binary↔relay handshake retired #582) — wraps `internal/transport.Client`: `Connect(ctx, Config{ServerID identity.ServerID, RelayURL, BinaryVersion, Logger})` validates (`wss://`-only, all fields required → `ErrInvalidConfig`) and spawns a `run` goroutine; builds `x-pyrycode-server` / `x-pyrycode-version` / `user-agent: pyry/<v>` upgrade headers; the relay is content-blind and registers the binary's server-id from the `x-pyrycode-server` header on WS upgrade (it sends no `hello_ack`), so on every `transport.Client.Connected()` signal the conn is treated as established and `run` goes straight to `forwardFrames` (one `Info "relay: conn established"` breadcrumb, no `hello` send and no 5s ack wait — the `handshake()` method and `handshakeTimeout` package var are deleted, as is the `HelloServerPayload` construction); `Frames() <-chan protocol.RoutingEnvelope` exposes inbound frames in order, transparently across reconnects; `Wait() error` blocks on terminal classification (`ErrServerIDConflict` for fatal 4409 — rides the transport's `FatalCloseCodes:[4409]`, independent of any frame exchange / `ctx.Err()` / wrapped transport error); `Close()` idempotent via `sync.Once`. The *phone↔binary* `hello`/`hello_ack` is a different leg and survives (Noise_IK early-data, relay-blind; validated by surface (2)). (2) `AuthenticateFirstFrame(env, token, reg, serverID, logger) (AuthOutcome, error)` (#249, `auth.go`) — pure per-conn token-validation predicate composing A5's `devices.Registry.Validate` with #271's `HelloAckPayload` / `ErrorPayload`. Carrier-agnostic (`token` is an explicit `string` argument; the function never parses WS headers / `env.Frame` payload / hello payload — it only reads the outer envelope's `id` to echo into `in_reply_to`); two-state semantics — `Validate` returns `(Device, bool)` and the handler folds empty / never-paired / removed-after-pair all into the same `auth.invalid_token` reject (spec § Error codes line 535 same-UX equivalence locked by `TestAuthenticateFirstFrame_RevokedTokenSameUX`); `AuthOutcome{Response protocol.RoutingEnvelope, CloseConn bool}` carries protocol-level intent and the relay-conn caller closes the phone WS with the exported `StatusUnauthorized websocket.StatusCode = 4401`; outer envelope `ID` fixed at 1; `MsgInvalidToken` const pins the spec sentence; `ErrMalformedHelloFrame` sentinel for the JSON-undecodable-frame path only (missing/empty/unknown/revoked tokens are valid `auth.invalid_token` outcomes with `nil` error); logging discipline forbids `token` everywhere and forbids `device_name` on reject to prevent name-enumeration probes from binary logs (name IS logged on accept — operator-actionable, attacker already holds a valid token); supervisor wiring + per-envelope dispatch + outbound `Send` + the relay-conn wiring that actually delivers the token to `AuthenticateFirstFrame` are deferred to consuming tickets. (3) `internal/relay/handlers` (#250, sub-package `handlers/`) — first per-envelope-type processor: `Handle(routing, *Device, *Registry, registryPath, nextID, logger) (RoutingEnvelope, error)` for `register_push_token`. Five behavioural branches: malformed-frame → `ErrMalformedFrame` sentinel (dispatcher owns the `protocol.malformed` response); unauth (`device == nil`, dispatcher bug) → `auth.invalid_token` error envelope, no registry touch; dedupe (`(payload.Platform, payload.Token, payload.DeviceName) == (device.Platform, device.PushToken, device.Name)`) → `ack` envelope, **structurally NO `UpdatePushRegistration` and NO `Save`** (the load-bearing optimisation: ~100B/conn rewrite avoided, test pins `errors.Is(os.Stat, fs.ErrNotExist)` with the file pre-state set to never-existed as the spy); gone-mid-conn (`UpdatePushRegistration` returns `false`, concurrent revoke between auth-accept and frame) → `auth.invalid_token` (same UX); save-failure → `server.binary_busy` error envelope, `Retryable=true`, `RetryAfterS=nil`, **in-memory IS mutated** (post-condition: in-memory is the runtime source of truth, next successful Save catches disk up — phone retries, dedupe will succeed on retry); happy write → `ack`. `Name` is part of the dedupe triple because the protocol's `device_name` makes the phone source-of-truth for its self-reported name (iOS Settings rename propagates). Sub-package depends on `internal/devices` + `internal/protocol` only — no import of `internal/relay`, keeping the future dispatcher cycle-free; `auth.go` stays in `internal/relay` proper because it is the gate into the dispatcher, not a per-type handler. `wrap(connID, inReplyTo, nextID, envType, payload)` is a file-local helper deliberately duplicating `relay.buildResponse` rather than importing across the sub-package boundary (premature to lift for one handler). Logging: push token NEVER logged (FCM/APNs registration id is opaque infra data, not a secret on par with the auth-time device token, but no operational signal worth the noise); `device_name` IS logged on every path that has one (write/dedupe/save-failed/gone-mid-conn) because handler runs post-auth — caller already cleared the auth gate, nothing to enumerate (inverse of #249's reject-path discipline). `msgUnauthorized` / `msgBinaryBusy` are unexported file-local consts (AC asserts `Code` + `Retryable` only, never `Message`). Tests: six flat (`first-time write` + reload assertion, `re-register identical` no-write spy, `re-register changed` content-equality not mtime to dodge CI flakes, `save-failure` via regular-file-blocks-parent `MkdirAll` failure + in-memory-still-mutated post-condition, `unauth` no-write + count-unchanged, `malformed-frame` pins sentinel). #301 adds `Config.AllowInsecureScheme bool` (test-only seam letting `ws://` pass `Connect`'s scheme check when true; env-gated via `PYRY_ALLOW_INSECURE_RELAY=1` at the cmd/pyry layer) and wires the daemon-side consumer: `cmd/pyry/main.go` + `cmd/pyry/relay.go` (new file) resolve `-pyry-relay` > `PYRY_RELAY_URL` > `cfg.RelayURL`, call `relay.Connect` against the `identity.LoadOrCreate`'d server-id, and spawn one supervisor-owned goroutine that drains `Frames()` and reads `Wait()` — `ErrServerIDConflict` cancels the daemon ctx (4409 → clean exit), other terminals log warn and exit without restart (transport already handled non-fatal closes), empty URL = disabled; #307 adds `(*Connection).Send(env protocol.RoutingEnvelope) error` (`connection.go:170-184`, JSON-marshal + `transport.Client.Send` wrapper; returns `transport.ErrDisconnected`/`ErrNotConnected`/`ErrClosed` verbatim, frames sent while disconnected are lost per v1 reconnect semantics — first consumer is `internal/dispatch`'s outbound forwarder in `cmd/pyry/relay.go`); #308 adds `(*Connection).CloseConn(connID string, code uint16) error` (`connection.go:203+`) — close-only `RoutingEnvelope{ConnID, CloseCode}` (no `Frame`) marshal + `transport.Client.Send`; fire-and-forget at this layer; the dispatcher's auth-reject path does NOT call `CloseConn` (it publishes one `Frame+CloseCode` envelope so the wire op is atomic), `CloseConn` is the surface reserved for direct callers that want close-without-payload (idle/inactivity sweep future consumer); cmd/pyry/relay.go grows the registry load (`devices.Load(resolveDevicesPath(instanceName))` between `relay.Connect` and `dispatch.New`; ENOENT → empty registry, malformed → wrapped `load device registry: %w` fails fast) and the `authGate` closure that bridges `dispatch.FirstFrameGate` → `relay.AuthenticateFirstFrame` mapping `outcome.CloseConn` to `Code: uint16(relay.StatusUnauthorized)` (4401); `AuthenticateFirstFrame`'s deferred wire-mechanism choice is settled here as option (a): the token rides `RoutingEnvelope.Token` populated by the relay from the phone's `x-pyrycode-token` header on the first frame per `conn_id`. #303 adds a second per-envelope-type processor to `internal/relay/handlers/`: `ListConversations(ConversationLister) dispatch.Handler` (`list_conversations.go`) — closure over a minimal-surface `ConversationLister` interface (`List(filter ...conversations.ListFilter) []Conversation`, declared at the handler call site so `*conversations.Registry` satisfies it structurally with no new export); reads the registry, `sort.SliceStable` by `LastUsedAt` asc / `ID` asc mirroring `Registry.Save`'s on-disk order byte-for-byte, projects each row into a `protocol.ConversationSummary` (collapsing `LastMessageTS` onto `LastUsedAt` since `Conversation` has no distinct last-message timestamp today — comment at projection site names the collapse), `json.Marshal`s `ConversationsPayload{Conversations: out}` with `make([]ConversationSummary, 0, len(list))` so empty registries serialise as `[]` not `null`, and replies via `c.Reply` so `id`/`ts`/`in_reply_to` are stamped by the dispatcher (handler never stamps them itself); `cmd/pyry/relay.go`'s `startRelay` grows a trailing `convReg *conversations.Registry` parameter (threaded from the existing `runSupervisor` load) and registers `d.Register(protocol.TypeListConversations, handlers.ListConversations(convReg))` after `dispatch.New` and before `go d.Run(ctx)`; relay-disabled early-return still fires when `relayURL == ""` (uniform signature, `convReg` accepted but unused on that path); tests use the real dispatcher as the seam (`dispatch.New` + `Register` + `Run` goroutine, frames pushed on input channel, response decoded from `d.Outbound()`), four flat tests cover empty-registry-as-empty-array / single-row-round-trip / deterministic ordering with `LastUsedAt` tie broken by `ID` / two-consecutive-requests-per-conn `in_reply_to` correctness + monotonic per-conn `id`. #319 rewrites the #250 pure handler against `dispatch.Handler`: factory `RegisterPushToken(reg *devices.Registry, registryPath string, logger *slog.Logger) dispatch.Handler` returning a closure with `func(ctx, *dispatch.Conn, protocol.Envelope) error`; `wrap()` helper and `ErrMalformedFrame` sentinel are gone (the dispatcher's `handleOne` already decoded `env`, the only remaining decode is the inner `RegisterPushTokenPayload` which now replies `protocol.malformed` via `c.Reply` — static `msgMalformed` message, decode-error text NOT echoed); the device is read via `c.Auth()` (per #318) with a nil-device defence-in-depth branch that still emits `auth.invalid_token`; `replyAck` / `replyError` are the file-local helpers that marshal the payload and call `c.Reply(ctx, env, type, payloadJSON)` so the dispatcher stamps `id`/`in_reply_to`/`ts`; registered in `cmd/pyry/relay.go`'s `startRelay` immediately after `list_conversations` with `d.Register(protocol.TypeRegisterPushToken, handlers.RegisterPushToken(registry, resolveDevicesPath(instanceName), logger))`; the new `internal/dispatch` import edge in `internal/relay/handlers` is cycle-free (`dispatch` doesn't import the sub-package). Test surface rewritten as seven flat tests (six behavioural branches + the new malformed-payload case replacing the deleted malformed-frame-sentinel test), driving a real `*dispatch.Conn` constructed via the new `dispatch.NewTestConn(testConnID, out, dev)` with `_ = c.NextID()` called once after construction so the first handler reply observes `id=2` (mirrors the gate's `hello_ack=1` accounting); new e2e `internal/e2e/register_push_token_test.go` (build tag `e2e`) pairs a device via `RunBareIn pair`, dials a `fakephone` against a `fakerelay` with `PYRY_ALLOW_INSECURE_RELAY=1`, sends `hello`→`hello_ack` (`*InReplyTo == 1`) then `register_push_token`→`ack` (`*InReplyTo == request.ID`, `env.ID >= 2` — strictly-greater leaves room for future dispatcher-side replies), and reloads `~/.pyry/test/devices.json` to assert the `(Platform, PushToken, Name)` triple is persisted. #322 adds a third handler `SendMessage(w TurnWriter, logger *slog.Logger) dispatch.Handler` in `internal/relay/handlers/send_message.go` — inbound-only slice (phone → ack or `conversation.not_found` refusal; assistant-turn delivery deferred to #311). Handler-owned `TurnWriter interface { WriteUserTurn(conversationID string, payload []byte) error }` keeps the `handlers` sub-package free of `internal/sessions` / `internal/supervisor` imports; `*sessions.Session` satisfies it structurally via a one-line `(*Session).WriteUserTurn` passthrough added in the same slice (`internal/sessions/session.go`, alongside `State()` in the delegation cluster). Four behavioural branches: pre-decode malformed → `protocol.malformed` (writer NOT reached, stub.calls==0 in tests); `WriteUserTurn` nil → `ack` (INFO log with `conversation_id` + `message_id` — phone-supplied opaque ids, not credentials); `errors.Is(err, conversations.ErrConversationNotFound)` → `conversation.not_found` error envelope `Retryable=false`; any other err → return wrapped to dispatcher (NO wire reply — load-bearing contract pinned by a 50ms drain-window assertion in `TestSendMessage_WrappedError_PassesThroughNoWireReply`). `payload.Text` is passed verbatim — no transformation, no length cap beyond transport's 1 MiB ceiling, no trailing newline appended (child's parser owns turn-framing), NEVER logged at any level. Sentinel-to-wire-code mapping at the consumer per `docs/PROJECT-MEMORY.md` convention (`internal/conversations` does NOT depend on `internal/protocol`). Writer is invoked BEFORE sentinel-mapping — validation lives upstream at `Supervisor.WriteUserTurn`'s `ValidateConversation` closure wired by `pool.go:355-362`; the not-found test pins `stub.calls == 1` to keep that discipline. Factory name `SendMessage` (mirrors `RegisterPushToken` / `ListConversations`, not the AC's literal `NewSendMessageHandler`); `replyAck` / `replyError` reused directly from `register_push_token.go:106-124` (same `handlers` package — no helpers.go extracted). Static messages `msgSendMessageMalformed = "malformed send_message payload"` + `msgConversationNotFound = "conversation not found"` — decode-error text NEVER echoed back to phone. Dispatcher registration in `cmd/pyry/relay.go` adds one line `d.Register(protocol.TypeSendMessage, handlers.SendMessage(sess, logger))` after the `register_push_token` line; `startRelay`'s signature grows a trailing `sess handlers.TurnWriter` parameter (typed as the interface so `relay.go` does not import `internal/sessions`); `cmd/pyry/main.go` reorders `runSupervisor` so `sessions.New` runs BEFORE `startRelay` and passes `pool.Default()` (the bootstrap session) — none of `sessions.New`'s inputs depend on `startRelay`'s outputs, two-line reorder, zero behavioural fallout. Test surface (4 flat `t.Parallel()` tests in `send_message_test.go`, all race-clean, no e2e in this slice — assistant-turn roundtrip is #311): success-ack with stub-captured `(conversation_id, text)` round-trip + `id==2`/`InReplyTo==requestID` shape; `conversations.ErrConversationNotFound` → error envelope + `stub.calls==1`; wrapped-err pass-through with 50ms drain-window for no-outbound; malformed payload → `protocol.malformed` + `stub.calls==0`. Helpers `newSendMsgConn` / `assertSendMsgEnvelopeShape` mirror `register_push_token_test.go`'s pattern (per-file copies, not shared — Go does not propagate `_test.go` symbols across files in the same package by convention). Two named-out-of-scope security follow-ups inherited from the protocol surface: (a) mobile-origin system-message prepend (`docs/protocol-mobile.md` threat #1, requires `Device.Name` plumbing through `TurnWriter`); (b) per-conn rate-limiting + ctx-aware `WriteUserTurn` cancel (`send_message` is the first verb to perform potentially-blocking PTY I/O; N hostile phones × 1 MiB frames pin N goroutines on backpressure; residual risk bounded by auth gate + revocation). #666 adds a fourth handler `CreateConversation(reg ConversationCreator, registryPath, defaultCwd string, logger *slog.Logger) dispatch.Handler` in `internal/relay/handlers/create_conversation.go` — the daemon side of the mobile "New discussion" tap (diagnosed live 2026-06-18: a `create_conversation` frame hit `dispatch.Route`'s "no handler registered" branch → `protocol.unsupported`, so the app never navigated into the new thread). Decodes `protocol.CreateConversationPayload`, resolves its three `*`-nullable fields to effective values (`promoted` default false, `cwd` default the new `defaultCwd`, `name` pointer passthrough), mints a server-side id via `conversations.NewID()` (never phone-supplied → no collision/overwrite, `Create` appends), `reg.Create`s the row, **eagerly** `reg.Save`s (best-effort/non-fatal — diverges from `RunSweepLoop`'s lazy archive-tick Save so a created conversation survives a daemon restart, matching the `pyry pair` Add→Save precedent), and replies `conversation_created` via `c.Reply` (dispatcher stamps `id`/`ts`/`in_reply_to`). Declares its own minimal `ConversationCreator` interface (`Create` + `Save`) satisfied structurally by `*conversations.Registry` (same consumer-declared-interface discipline as `ConversationLister`). No session spawn at create time — a `Create`d row validates immediately via `convReg.Get` (the pool's `ValidateConversation`), so a follow-up `send_message` on the new id is admitted with no claude session (lazy bind on first turn); no split needed. Registered on **both** dispatch paths: v1 `d.Register(protocol.TypeCreateConversation, …)` in `startRelay` and the v2 `Handlers` map in `startRelayV2`; `startRelay`/`startRelayV2` grow a `defaultCwd string` param threaded from a new `resolveDefaultCwd(workdir)` helper in `cmd/pyry/main.go` (mirrors `resolveClaudeSessionsDir`: absolute `*workdir` or `os.Getwd()`). `security-sensitive` — the phone-influenced `cwd` is **inert stored metadata** today (sole reader is `list_conversations`'s echo to the operator's own paired devices; no code path spawns/chdirs/path-joins from `conversation.Cwd`), so it is stored verbatim with a code comment that any future per-conversation session-spawn consumer MUST canonicalise + boundary-check it (deferred SHOULD FIX). No new protocol types, no registry edits. Five flat `t.Parallel()` handler tests via `dispatch.NewTestConn` (all-null defaults / explicit-fields-verbatim / eager-persist-survives-reload / malformed-payload-static-message-empty-registry / created-id-validates-for-send_message). `conversation_updated` multi-phone broadcast out of scope. See [codebase/666.md](codebase/666.md). **#820 adds a fifth handler** `RenameConversation(reg ConversationRenamer, registryPath string, logger *slog.Logger) dispatch.Handler` in `internal/relay/handlers/rename_conversation.go` — clones `CreateConversation`'s shape but simpler (no session mint, no cwd): decode → empty-title guard (`strings.TrimSpace(p.Name) == ""`, runs BEFORE the mutation so a blank rename leaves the stored name untouched) → `reg.Update` (single locked callback both sets `Name` and snapshots the reply `ConversationUpdatedPayload` — no find-then-read TOCTOU, no second lock acquisition) → not-found on an `Update` miss → eager best-effort `Save` → `c.Reply(conversation_updated)`. Declares its own minimal `ConversationRenamer` interface (`Update` + `Save`) satisfied structurally by `*conversations.Registry` (same consumer-declared-interface discipline as `ConversationCreator`/`ConversationLister`). `LastUsedAt` is **not** bumped — rename is a metadata edit, not a "use", mirroring `Registry.Promote` and keeping `list_conversations` ordering stable. All three reject branches (`protocol.malformed` decode, `protocol.malformed` empty-name, `conversation.not_found`) reply with a fixed static string; the title is user content and is never logged, only `conn_id`/`conversation_id` appear in structured log fields. Registered on **both** dispatch paths (`startRelay`'s v1 block and `startRelayV2`'s `Handlers` map), reusing `resolveConversationsRegistryPath(instanceName)`. **Not `security-sensitive`** at the label-worthy level per the architect's self-review (PASS, no MUST-FIX) — `conversation_id` is used only as an exact-match registry key, the title is opaque display text echoed only to the requester, no subprocess/crypto/file-path surface. Live fan-out to *other* connected clients is explicitly out of scope (reply goes to the requester only; other clients see the new title on their next `list_conversations`). See [codebase/820.md](codebase/820.md). **#822 adds a sixth handler** `DeleteConversation(reg ConversationDeleter, registryPath string, logger *slog.Logger) dispatch.Handler` in `internal/relay/handlers/delete_conversation.go` — clones the same shape for a **hard (permanent)** delete (the reversible path is archive/unarchive, #880/#881, a distinct verb): decode → `reg.Delete` (exact-match registry-key removal, a miss is `conversation.not_found` with no state change — idempotent-on-miss) → eager best-effort `Save` → `c.Reply(conversation_deleted, {id})`. Declares its own minimal `ConversationDeleter` interface (`Delete` + `Save`) satisfied structurally by `*conversations.Registry` (same consumer-declared-interface discipline as `ConversationRenamer`/`ConversationCreator`/`ConversationLister`). Reply is a dedicated `conversation_deleted` ack, not a `conversation_updated` reuse — the record no longer exists post-delete, so there's no row to project `name`/`cwd`/`last_used_at` from. **Deliberately diverges from the rename/create malformed-branch logging template**: logs `conn_id` ONLY, never the decode `err` or `conversation_id` — a `*json.SyntaxError` can embed offending input bytes and a decode failure leaves the struct at most partially populated, so `p.ConversationID` may hold raw attacker bytes (mandated by AC #5's "no attacker-controlled payload bytes ... logged"); the not_found/success branches log `conversation_id` as a structured field per the rename/create precedent, since by then it's a proven-real registry key. Registered on **both** dispatch paths (`startRelay`'s v1 block and `startRelayV2`'s `Handlers` map), reusing `resolveConversationsRegistryPath(instanceName)`. `security-sensitive` (label carried for the logging divergence above; architect self-review PASS, no open MUST-FIX) — `conversation_id` is used only as an exact-match registry key (no path/argv/query construction), a paired phone can already enumerate all conversations via `list_conversations` so delete grants no new capability, and live fan-out to *other* connected clients is explicitly out of scope (same posture as rename/create). See [codebase/822.md](codebase/822.md). **#881 adds a seventh handler**, `ArchiveConversation(reg ConversationArchiver, registryPath string, logger *slog.Logger, archived bool) dispatch.Handler` in `internal/relay/handlers/archive_conversation.go` — a **single parameterized factory** registered twice (once per `archived` value) rather than two near-identical handlers, the reversible symmetric-toggle counterpart to `DeleteConversation`'s permanent removal. Flow: decode → `reg.SetArchived(id, archived)` (#880's deterministic single-field mutator; a miss is `conversation.not_found`) → `reg.Get(id)` to snapshot the post-flip record for the reply (a concurrent `delete_conversation` racing between the flip and the read also surfaces here as a `Get` miss, folded into `conversation.not_found`) → eager best-effort `Save` → `c.Reply(conversation_updated)` (now carrying `is_archived`). Declares its own minimal `ConversationArchiver` interface (`SetArchived` + `Get` + `Save`) satisfied structurally by `*conversations.Registry`. Deliberately does NOT snapshot via an `Update` closure like `RenameConversation` — honors #880's dedicated single-field mutator instead, which structurally cannot touch another field. Copies `DeleteConversation`'s malformed-branch logging divergence (`conn_id` only, never `err`/`conversation_id`) and carries the same `security-sensitive` label, though the architect's self-review notes archive is strictly lower stakes than delete since it's reversible. `rename_conversation.go` gains a one-line populate (`IsArchived: cv.IsArchived`) since it shares the now-extended `ConversationUpdatedPayload`. Registered on both dispatch paths, reusing `resolveConversationsRegistryPath(instanceName)`; four wiring lines total. Live fan-out to other connected clients out of scope, same posture as rename/delete. See [codebase/881.md](codebase/881.md). **#949 wires the previously-unwired eighth handler**, `PromoteConversation(reg ConversationPromoter, registryPath string, logger *slog.Logger) dispatch.Handler` in `internal/relay/handlers/promote_conversation.go` — the `promote_conversation` message type, payload, and reused `conversation_updated` reply all pre-existed (as did `Registry.Promote`), but no `V2SessionConfig.Handlers` entry ever called it, so every `promote_conversation` frame fell through to `protocol.unsupported` and "Save as channel" silently failed. Clones `RenameConversation`'s shape with one extra step: `Registry.Promote` returns only an `error`, not the updated row, so the handler `reg.Get`s it back before building the reply. Declares its own minimal `ConversationPromoter` interface (`Promote` + `Get` + `Save`) satisfied structurally by `*conversations.Registry`. **The design decision**: `PromoteConversationPayload` carries a required `Cwd` that the handler deliberately does NOT consume — Option B over Option A — the promoted channel inherits the scratch conversation's existing (already `$HOME`-confined) `Cwd` rather than treating the wire value as a new workspace to confine-and-store, so unlike `ChangeWorkspace` this verb opens no untrusted-path surface; `TestPromoteConversation_PayloadCwd_IsIgnored` is the regression guard. Adopts `ChangeWorkspace`'s malformed-branch logging divergence (`conn_id` only, never decode `err`/`conversation_id`) over `RenameConversation`'s. Registered in `cmd/pyry/relay.go`'s `V2SessionConfig.Handlers` map — the **sole** dispatch table since #913 removed the legacy v1 `d.Register` block (`startRelay` is now a thin wrapper calling `startRelayV2`); no v1 registration exists to mirror. `security-sensitive` (architect self-review PASS, no MUST-FIX — the label anticipated Option A's untrusted-path surface, and the central review finding is that Option B never builds it). Proven end-to-end by `internal/e2e/relay_v2_promote_test.go`'s `TestRelayV2_Promote` (RED on main, GREEN after). See [codebase/949.md](codebase/949.md). `Connection` also exposes `Connected() bool` (#874, level poll) and `Reconnected() <-chan struct{}` (#875, edge signal re-broadcasting the transport's single-observer fresh-conn edge) — both consumed solely by `internal/relay.V2SessionManager`'s push-drain wake seams, wired in `cmd/pyry/relay.go`. See [codebase/875.md](codebase/875.md). **#1040 removed surface (2) above**: `AuthenticateFirstFrame`/`AuthOutcome`/`ErrMalformedHelloFrame`/`buildResponse` are gone — zero production callers since #913 slice 1, orphaned machinery deleted by #1039, this predicate itself deleted by #1040. `auth.go` now holds only `StatusUnauthorized`/`MsgInvalidToken`, consumed by the live Noise_IK v2 handshake (see [features/v2-session-manager.md](features/v2-session-manager.md)); current detail lives in [features/relay-package.md](features/relay-package.md) § "Auth: WS close-code constants". See [codebase/1040.md](codebase/1040.md). |
| [relay-package-handlers.md](features/relay-package-handlers.md) | `internal/relay/handlers` split out of `relay-package.md` (2026-09-08, over the 50000-byte cap): `register_push_token`'s full behavioural contract, dedupe, logging discipline and test surface, plus `send_message`'s #2159 conversation auto-naming seam (`ConversationAutoNamer` / `ConversationAnnouncer`) and the package convention that a narrow consumer-declared interface keeps its own name even when its method set duplicates a sibling's, because the doc comment carries a distinct contract |
| [protocol-package.md](features/protocol-package.md) | `internal/protocol` (#255, extended #271/#272/#273/#274/#275/#308/#454/#607/#617/#638/#649/#647/#656/#701/#720/#707/#740/#812/#813/#831/#844/#1007/#967/#1074) — wire-format leaf package for the mobile WebSocket protocol v1: `Envelope` (id/type/ts/payload + omitempty in_reply_to/event_id/payload_encrypted; `Payload json.RawMessage` for deferred decode; `TS time.Time` so the dispatcher's clock-skew check doesn't re-parse), `RoutingEnvelope` (binary↔relay-only `{conn_id, frame}` wrapper, `Frame json.RawMessage` so the relay splices byte-for-byte without parsing), `IsKnownAppType(env) error` predicate (sentinel `ErrUnsupported` when `payload_encrypted=true`, `ErrUnknownType` when `Type` empty/unknown, `nil` otherwise; encrypted-wins-over-unknown check order pinned by test); 21 `Code*` error-code string constants (14 + the 7 `attachment.*` reject codes added #1751) + 16 `Type*` envelope-type string constants grouped by spec table order; refusal-to-wire-code mapping stays at the dispatcher call site (consumer convention pinned by `docs/PROJECT-MEMORY.md` § "Refusal-to-wire-code mapping is the consumer's job"); package-private `inboundAppTypeSet` map literal as the predicate's O(1) lookup; deliberate triple-copy of the type list (constants + map + test slice) as three explicit drift detectors that fail loudly in CI; pure data — no I/O, no goroutines, no `context`, no `slog`; sentinel error strings carry no input bytes and no payload contents (no token leakage via error message); golden round-trip tests against `testdata/envelope_full.json` / `envelope_minimal.json` / `routing_envelope.json` pin the wire bytes (canonical `json.Compact` comparison; `time.Time.Equal` for `TS` to dodge monotonic-clock strip); first per-type payload `RegisterPushTokenPayload` (#275, `push.go`, three required `string` fields `platform`/`token`/`device_name`, pure DTO no methods, golden round-trip via `Envelope.Payload` against `testdata/register_push_token.json`) is the first slice of the #256 catalog — sibling slices land per-ticket in their own `*.go` files; second slice `messaging.go` (#272) carries `SendMessagePayload` / `MessagePayload` (two pure DTOs; `MessagePayload.Role` stays `string` not an enum type) — the slice originally also shipped the v1 bulk-history backfill payloads `BackfillSincePayload` / `MessageChunkPayload` / `BackfillDonePayload` (`backfill_since`/`message_chunk`/`backfill_done`), dead code with zero emitters and zero handlers, **removed in #967** (see below); golden round-trip against `testdata/*.json` fixtures; third slice `conversations_read.go` (#273) carries `ListConversationsPayload` (empty `struct{}` for the request) / `ConversationsPayload` (slice of summaries for the response) / `ConversationSummary` (row type — `ID`, `Name *string` WITHOUT `omitempty` for the same `null`-on-wire discipline as `BackfillSincePayload.ConversationID` (removed in #967; `SessionTransitionPayload.WorkspaceCwd` is now the canonical example), `IsPromoted`, `Cwd`, `LastMessageTS time.Time`, `LastUsedAt time.Time`; declaration order matches fixture key order for byte-stable round-trip; `ConversationsPayload` does not reorder — binary owns ordering); golden round-trip against `testdata/list_conversations.json` and `testdata/conversations.json` (two rows, one `name=<string>` one `name=null`, envelope rides `in_reply_to: 3`); fourth slice `conversations_write.go` (#274) carries `CreateConversationPayload` (phone → binary, three `*T` fields all WITHOUT `omitempty` — the maximal-optionality case, with `IsPromoted *bool` proving the pointer-to-zero round-trip) / `ConversationCreatedPayload` (binary → phone reply, `{ID, IsPromoted, Cwd, Name *string, LastUsedAt time.Time}`) / `PromoteConversationPayload` (phone → binary, three required `string` fields, no optional anywhere) / `ConversationUpdatedPayload` (binary → phone broadcast, `{ID, IsPromoted, Name *string, Cwd, LastUsedAt time.Time}` — note `Name` / `Cwd` order swap vs `_created`, dictated by spec example); declaration order matches each spec example verbatim, four flat test functions in `conversations_write_test.go`, `conversation_created.json` is the only fixture in the slice carrying `in_reply_to: 4`; fifth slice handshake/control payload structs (#271; `HelloServerPayload` / `HelloClientPayload` / `HelloAckPayload` / `ErrorPayload` / `AckPayload` in `handshake.go` — pure DTOs, optional fields are `*T` + `omitempty`, two `Hello*Payload` structs over a union, `AckPayload` is `struct{}`, golden round-trip via per-type fixtures under `testdata/`); WS close codes (#247) and dispatch wiring (#248–#250) remain explicit out-of-scope. #308 extends `RoutingEnvelope` with two optional `omitempty` fields: `Token string` (phone→binary direction, first frame per `ConnID` only; populated by the relay from the phone's `x-pyrycode-token` HTTP header at WS upgrade; originally consumed only by `relay.AuthenticateFirstFrame`, deleted by #1040 — the field is retained with no live consumer, its own removal deferred as separate follow-on scope; doc-comment forbids logging at any layer) and `CloseCode uint16` (binary→relay direction only; when non-zero asks the relay to forward `Frame` (if non-empty) and then close that phone's WS with this code; dispatcher ignores on inbound — defends against a malicious relay injecting `CloseCode=4401` on a phone→binary frame); both fields omitempty so pre-#308 fixtures + wire bytes are byte-identical; `TestRoutingEnvelope_TokenOmitempty` + `TestRoutingEnvelope_CloseCodeOmitempty` pin the omit shape. **#454 adds the first Mobile Protocol v2 control-envelope type** `TypeRekeyRequest = "rekey_request"` to `codes.go` in a new "v2 control" group below the v1 `Push` group. The constant is **deliberately NOT** added to `inboundAppTypeSet` — a leak would route the envelope to `dispatch.Route`'s handler chain. The architectural asymmetry is pinned by belt-and-suspenders rails of different fabric: (1) doc-comment on the constant ("MUST NOT add to inboundAppTypeSet"), (2) doc-comment above `inboundAppTypeSet` ("v2-control types like `TypeRekeyRequest` stay out"), and (3) deterministic `TestTypeConstants_V1V2Partition` in `compat_test.go` — every `Type*` constant must be in `inboundAppTypeSet` OR in a test-local `v2OnlyTypes` allowlist; never both, never neither. The `v2OnlyTypes` literal lives test-side so production callers can't import it for dispatch logic (v2 dispatch switches on individual constants, not on partition membership). The interception happens at the v2 dispatch boundary in `internal/relay/v2session.go`'s `dispatchAppFrame` (not in this package). No v1 `Type*` constant changes; existing fixtures + drift detectors unchanged. **#607 lands the first v2 additive interactive application events** (EPIC #596 Phase 2 structured streaming): a new `interactive.go` with five binary→phone payload structs `TurnStatePayload` (`{conversation_id, state}`, state ∈ thinking/responding/idle) / `AssistantDeltaPayload` (`{conversation_id, turn_id, seq int, text}`, seq is a per-turn counter that resets each turn) / `ToolUsePayload` (`{…, tool_use_id, name, input_summary}`) / `ToolResultPayload` (`{…, tool_use_id, is_error bool, result_summary}`) / `TurnEndPayload` (`{conversation_id, turn_id, stop_reason}`) — **no `omitempty` on any field** (the deliberate inverse of the optional-field discipline) so boundary zero-values `seq:0` / `is_error:false` stay explicit on the wire and the fixtures pin the full shape; `state` / `stop_reason` stay plain `string` (the `MessagePayload.Role` precedent) and `stop_reason` carries `internal/turnevent.TurnEndReason` strings (`end_turn`/`max_tokens`/`max_turn_requests`/`refusal`/`cancelled`) **verbatim without importing `turnevent`** — the package stays a stdlib-only leaf, #608 produces the field via `string(turnevent.TurnEnd.Reason)`. Five matching `Type*` constants (`TypeTurnState`…`TypeTurnEnd`) land in their **own** v2-only const block in `codes.go` (separate from the `TypeRekeyRequest` control block so the comment distinguishes v2 control — intercepted before `dispatch.Route` — from v2 application events — outbound, never dispatched inbound; both "v2-only" for the partition). `compat_test.go` gains five `ErrUnknownType` rejection rows + the five in `v2OnlyTypes` (now 6 entries) and the `all` partition slice (`16 + 6 == 22`). On the handshake, both phone-facing payloads gain `Capabilities []string json:"capabilities,omitempty"` (`HelloClientPayload` = the phone's advertisement, `HelloAckPayload` = the daemon's supported-set echo) plus the `CapabilityInteractive = "interactive"` vocabulary constant in `handshake.go` — `omitempty` is the byte-identical lever (the `Token`/#308 precedent), so the unedited `hello_client.json` / `hello_ack.json` fixtures round-trip byte-identically (key absent, not `null`), pinned by two new programmatic round-trips in `handshake_test.go` while the pre-existing fixture round-trips stay unchanged as the byte-stability guard. **Wire vocabulary only** — no bridge, push, capability intersection/enforcement, or fan-out (all the daemon's trust decision, deferred to consumer #608); these payloads are the mobile wire representation of #606's neutral turn-event model. `interactive_test.go`'s `roundTripEnvelope` helper re-marshals the **decoded struct** (not the original `RawMessage`) to actually pin struct→wire tags. `docs/protocol-mobile.md` amended with the five table rows + an `Interactive events (v2, capability-gated)` subsection + a `Capability negotiation (v2)` note. **#617 lands the v2 screen-snapshot wire pair** (EPIC #596 Phase 2, backing ADR 025 § Safe degradation's parser-independent screen-snapshot floor): a new `snapshot.go` with `RequestSnapshotPayload` (`{conversation_id}`, phone→binary inbound control — structurally like `TypeRekeyRequest`, intercepted before `dispatch.Route`, **no `dispatch.Route` handler**) and `ScreenSnapshotPayload` (`{conversation_id, text, ts time.Time}`, binary→phone outbound event) — `text` is **plain rendered text only, never raw terminal control codes** (preserves ADR 025's no-raw-bytes invariant), `ts` compared with `time.Time.Equal` (monotonic strip), **no `omitempty` on any field**. Two matching `Type*` constants (`TypeRequestSnapshot` / `TypeScreenSnapshot`) land in their own cohesive v2-only const block in `codes.go`; `compat_test.go` gains two `ErrUnknownType` rejection rows + both in `v2OnlyTypes` (now 8 entries) and the `all` partition slice (`16 + 8 == 24`). **Wire vocabulary only** — the intercept/render(via tui-driver)/push is the consumer ticket's job (`security-sensitive`); this leaf declaration makes no trust decision. `snapshot_test.go` adds two round-trips (multi-line `text`, whole-second payload `ts` for byte-stable re-marshal) + an empty-`conversation_id` boundary; `docs/protocol-mobile.md` amended with two table rows + a `Screen snapshot (v2)` section. **#638 appends the sixth v2 interactive event** `stall` (EPIC #596 Phase 2): `StallPayload{conversation_id}` in `interactive.go` (no `omitempty`; the wire form of the internal-only `turnevent.Stall` onset marker — `conversation_id` only, **no `turn_id`** like `turn_state`, no clearing field since the phone self-clears on the next turn; the bridge #624-B/#608 injects the id because the internal `Stall` carries none; `internal/protocol` does **not** import `internal/turnevent` — decoupled, bridged only at the string `"stall"`) + `TypeStall = "stall"` appended to the v2-only interactive const block in `codes.go` (count comment five→six) + `testdata/stall.json` full-shape fixture + `TestStallPayload_RoundTrip` (re-marshals the **decoded** struct) + `compat_test.go` three-spot lockstep (`stall-rejected` `ErrUnknownType` row, `v2OnlyTypes` now **9** entries, partition `all` slice → `16 + 9 == 25`; `TestInboundAppTypeSet_CoversAllExportedTypeConstants` `len==16` left unchanged since `stall` is v2-only). **Wire vocabulary only** — the `stall_detected` → `Stall` → `stall` bridge + capability-gated fan-out is #624-B (`security-sensitive`); this leaf makes no trust decision. `docs/protocol-mobile.md` amended with a `stall` table row + `#### stall` subsection + intro five→six. See [codebase/638.md](codebase/638.md). **#649 surfaces the durable per-conversation event id on the wire** (EPIC #596 Phase 2; producer half of mid-turn reconnect): adds an additive `EventID *uint64 \`json:"event_id,omitempty"\`` field to `Envelope`, placed after `InReplyTo` (the precedent it mirrors exactly). It carries the durable per-conversation event id from #646's `internal/eventring` — `Ring.Append`'s previously-**discarded** return — distinct from the per-conn `ID` counter that resets each reconnect. Stamped **only** by the interactive emitter (`cmd/pyry/interactive_turn_v2.go`'s `emit`, capturing the id it already computed and sharing `&eventID` by reference across the fan-out so all conns get the identical id with no per-conn alloc); a reconnecting phone advertises the latest one it saw as `last_event_id`. Pointer + `omitempty` keeps every other `Envelope{...}` construction site nil → omitted → byte-identical (absent, not `null`/`0`; ring ids are always ≥ 1 so a non-nil pointer never encodes `0`) — AC-4's "v1 + non-interactive frames round-trip byte-identically" falls out with zero edits to those sites. `TestEnvelope_EventIDOmitempty` pins the omit/round-trip; the unchanged `envelope_full.json` / `envelope_minimal.json` fixtures are the byte-stability guard; three emitter tests cross-check the wire id against the ring (`e.ring.After`) for AC-1/2/3. Also corrects `emit`'s stale `#647 surfaces it on the wire` comment → `#649` (the producer/consumer split moved the surface here). **Not `security-sensitive`** — outbound, additive, non-secret monotonic id; the inbound `last_event_id` consumer (per-conn `last_event_id` in `hello`, ring replay, resync) is sibling #647 (`security-sensitive`, blocked-by #649). `docs/protocol-mobile.md` amended with an envelope-level `event_id` row + a replay-cursor note in § Interactive events. Carries **no** `event_id` (a session boundary is not a turn-stream event). `compat_test.go` lockstep: `v2OnlyTypes` now **11** entries (partition `16 + 11 == 27`), `session_transition-rejected` `ErrUnknownType` row. `TestSessionTransitionPayload_RoundTrip` over two struct-field-ordered fixtures (cwd-unset `null` + cwd-set). **Wire vocabulary only** — the producer is sibling #657 (`security-sensitive`, blocked-by #656); split from #655. `docs/protocol-mobile.md` amended with a `session_transition` table row + `#### session_transition` subsection (invariant stated). See [codebase/656.md](codebase/656.md). **#701 adds the v2 modal/permission wire vocabulary** (EPIC #597 Phase 3; SSOT for the remote-modal feature): four `Type*` constants in **one** new v2-only const block in `codes.go` — `TypeModalShown` / `TypeModalDismissed` (outbound binary→phone events) + `TypeModalAnswer` / `TypeModalCancel` (inbound phone→binary **control** envelopes intercepted at `v2session.go`'s `dispatchAppFrame` before `dispatch.Route`, **no handler** — the `TypeRekeyRequest`/`TypeRequestSnapshot` precedent; mixed inbound+outbound cluster under one rationale comment, the `request_snapshot`/`screen_snapshot` precedent) — plus five exported payload structs in `messaging.go`: `ModalOption` (`{ID, Label}`) + `ModalShownPayload` (`{ModalID, Class, Title, Prompt, Options []ModalOption (ordered — array order is display order), DefaultOptionID (MUST ∈ Options[].ID)}`) / `ModalAnswerPayload` (`{ModalID, OptionID, AnswerToken}`) / `ModalCancelPayload` (`{ModalID}`) / `ModalDismissedPayload` (`{ModalID, Outcome, Source}`, `Source` closed set `{remote, local, timeout}`). **No `omitempty` on any field**; no `time.Time` so no new import. `Class`/`Outcome` stay plain strings (producer #703 owns the exhaustive vocab, documented not enforced); only `Source` is pinned to a closed set (fully determined by the resolution mechanism). **`modal_id` is the SOLE correlation key** — no `conversation_id`, so the daemon resolves it against its own outstanding-modal state and a `{conversation_id, modal_id}` disagreement is impossible by construction (cross-conversation confusion foreclosed); **`answer_token` is an idempotency key, NOT a credential** (uniqueness/stability matter, secrecy doesn't — validity gate `modal_id`+#702 vs dedup key kept separate). `compat_test.go` three-spot lockstep: `v2OnlyTypes` now **15** entries (partition `16 + 15 == 31`), four `modal_*-rejected` `ErrUnknownType` rows. mixed inbound+outbound cluster under one rationale comment) — plus three exported payload structs in `messaging.go`: `QueuedItem` (`{QueuedMsgID uint64, Text string, TS time.Time}`, the nested-array element, the `ModalOption` precedent) + `QueueStatePayload` (`{ConversationID string, Queued []QueuedItem (ordered FIFO/enqueue)}`) / `DequeueMessagePayload` (`{ConversationID string, QueuedMsgID uint64}`). **No `omitempty` on any field**; `time` already imported (no new import). `QueuedItem.TS` RFC3339Nano compared via `.Equal`. **`queued_msg_id` is a plain per-conversation `uint64` counter (≥1), NOT a nonce** — matches `msgqueue.QueuedMessage.ID` exactly; mobile (#429) decodes it as an integer. **Empty-backlog `[]` vs `null` is the producer's (#722) call** — the leaf type can't force a non-nil slice (`[]QueuedItem(nil)` → `null`); docs recommend `[]`, both round-trip. **No `turnevent`/`turnbridge` neutral hop** (architect declined the original split body's `QueueState`/`DropQueued` proposal): queue backlog is daemon **state** not a turn-stream event, so the producer builds `protocol.*Payload` directly from engine state and the inbound frame is decoded at `dispatchAppFrame` — the `session_transition`/`modal` precedent, not the interactive-event path. `compat_test.go` three-spot lockstep: `v2OnlyTypes` now **17** entries (partition `16 + 17 == 33`), two `queue_state`/`dequeue_message`-rejected `ErrUnknownType` rows. `TestQueueStatePayload_RoundTrip` (N=2 items, `.Equal` on `TS`) + `TestDequeueMessagePayload_RoundTrip` + table-driven `TestDequeueMessagePayload_Malformed`. **Wire vocabulary only, `security-sensitive`: NO** (mirrors #656, not #701 — no nonce, dequeuing ungated per ADR 025 § Security model); the convID→authorized-conversation trust binding + capability-gated fan-out + live inbound handler are the labelled siblings #722 (producer) / #723 (handler) / #721 (`send_message` queue routing). `docs/protocol-mobile.md` amended with two table rows + a `### Queue (v2)` section. **Lesson:** JSON `null` into a non-pointer numeric is a `json.Unmarshal` **no-op (nil error)**, not a reject — the spec's proposed `queued_msg_id: null` malformed case was invalid; Golden round-trips in `interactive_test.go` against new `testdata/api_retry.json` / `compacting.json`. `docs/protocol-mobile.md` amended with two new `#### api_retry` / `#### compacting` subsections + the events-index count six→eight. See [codebase/1074.md](codebase/1074.md). **#1751 publishes the client-facing contract for `TypeAttachmentChunk`** (`security-sensitive`, docs-and-vocabulary only — #1752/#1753 already landed the Go): adds `docs/protocol-mobile.md` § Attachments (both directions of the shared `attachment_chunk` frame, the sender's chunking arithmetic, index-addressed reassembly, retrieval's two terminal signals, trust/content-hygiene obligations) and corrects two stale claims the document made against already-landed code (the envelope-cap paragraph's "chunking scheme is out of scope"; § Scope's outright attachments bullet). Adds seven `attachment.*` `Code*` constants to `codes.go` — `CodeAttachmentInvalidChunk` / `CodeAttachmentIntegrityFailed` / `CodeAttachmentTooManyUploads` / `CodeAttachmentTooLarge` / `CodeAttachmentStorageFailed` / `CodeAttachmentNotFound` / `CodeAttachmentStreamAborted` (error-code drift maps 14 → 21) — declared as vocabulary ahead of any consumer so #1741/#1743/#1744/#1746 answer from one shared list instead of each inventing a name. `attachment.not_found` deliberately merges "unknown id" and "resolves outside the conversation's directory" into one code (a disclosure decision against a traversal probe, not an imprecision); the receiver-resource condition splits into two codes on retryability (`too_many_uploads` transient, `too_large` permanent) rather than one code averaging both. Ships no producer, consumer or validator. See [features/protocol-package.md](features/protocol-package.md) § Constants (`codes.go`) for the full lessons record. #1746 (named above as a pending `attachment.*` consumer) closed `NOT_PLANNED` and split three ways: `request_attachment` (#2052) declares the retrieval request verb § Attachments named as a deliberate hole — `TypeRequestAttachment` + `RequestAttachmentPayload{conversation_id, attachment_id}`, phone→binary only, `conversation_id` a registry-validated lookup key rather than an authorization, correlated via `Envelope.InReplyTo` (no request-id key, `AttachmentStoredPayload`'s decision reused), filed in `cmd/pyry/relay_guard_test.go`'s `excludedTypes` as `"pending handler (#2054)"`; ships no handler. #2053 (the outbound stream) and #2054 (the handler) are still open. See [features/protocol-package-constants-codes-go-envelope-types-attachments.md](features/protocol-package-constants-codes-go-envelope-types-attachments.md). |
| [jsonl-reader.md](features/jsonl-reader.md) | `internal/agentrun/jsonl` (#348, extended #353) — pure stdlib JSONL line reader over claude session output. Surface: `Event{StopReason, TextChars, EndOfTurn, Raw json.RawMessage, Kind string, Usage *UsageBlock}` (#353 adds the trailing three as an additive extension for a future stream-json emitter split from #335: `Raw` = verbatim line bytes with trailing `'\n'` stripped, CRLF `'\r'` preserved, **freshly-allocated** via `make`+`copy` so subsequent `Next` calls cannot mutate caller-held bytes via backing-array reuse — pinned by `TestReader_RawByteEquivalence` which calls `Next` again before inspecting earlier `Raw` slices; `Kind` whitelisted to `"assistant"|"user"|"tool_use"|"tool_result"|"system"|"attachment"|""` — anything else / missing `type` field (incl. `{"type":"summary",…}`) collapses to `""` and bytes survive on `Raw` so downstream re-emitters can pass unrecognised kinds through byte-equivalent to `claude -p --output-format stream-json`; `Usage` non-nil **only** on assistant entries that carry a `usage` object — pointer-typed at both the inner-decode struct (`rawAssistantMessage.Usage *struct{…}` so `encoding/json` leaves it nil when absent) and the exported field (to distinguish "field absent" from "field present with all zeros"); `UsageBlock{InputTokens, OutputTokens, CacheCreationInputTokens, CacheReadInputTokens int}` mirrors the assistant `message.usage` JSON shape verbatim; non-assistant Events carry only `Raw`+`Kind` with all other fields zero/nil — existing `StopReason`/`TextChars`/`EndOfTurn` semantics for assistant entries preserved verbatim) + `Config{Logger, StartOffset}` + `NewReader(io.Reader, Config) *Reader` with `Next() (Event, error)` / `Offset() int64` / `AssistantCount() int` + sentinel `ErrLineTooLarge` (16 MiB single-line cap). #353 flipped one semantic: the previous `if raw.Type != "assistant" { continue }` is replaced by classification + early return, so every well-formed line surfaces as an Event (the only in-tree consumer `tail.Watcher` already forwarded every Event unconditionally; no `drain` code change, only test-assertion updates — `TestWatcher_LateCreate` 2→3, `TestWatcher_FixtureIntegration` 25→62 since `clean.jsonl` has 64 lines and the sole `end_turn` sits at line 62 where the watcher stops). Deterministic end-of-turn rule (Phase A spike #329, verified across 1151 real session JSONLs, zero false-positives / false-negatives): `EndOfTurn = Kind == "assistant" && StopReason == "end_turn" && TextChars > 0`. Empty-content `end_turn` entries (transitional thinking-block resolutions) are surfaced as `EndOfTurn=false` events but DO advance `AssistantCount` so sibling #349's max-turn enforcement counts every model output the operator paid for. Two-pass JSON decode (`rawLine{Type, Message json.RawMessage}` outer + `rawAssistantMessage{StopReason, Content[]{Type, Text}, Usage *struct{…}}` inner only when `Type == "assistant"`) — single-pass would fail on `user` lines whose `content` is a string not an array; non-assistant lines short-circuit before paying the inner decode. `TextChars = sum(len(c.Text) for c in Content)` — `thinking` / `tool_use` blocks have `text` absent, contribute 0 naturally, matches spec's literal `sum(len(content[i].text))`. Hand-rolled `[]byte` buffer + `scratch [4096]byte` struct field (no per-`Read` heap alloc); EOF is **not sticky** — only surfaced when `src.Read` returns `(0, io.EOF)` AND no complete line is buffered, so a later `Next` may pull more bytes from a growing `*os.File` (the standard `tail -f` contract sibling #349's fsnotify driver relies on). Resume contract: `Config.StartOffset` informational (caller must `Seek` first); `Offset()` reflects it pre-Next, advances past every consumed line's trailing `'\n'` (incl. silently-skipped non-assistant / malformed lines), never advances into a partial-line buffer. Malformed JSON line → Warn-logged (rate-limited: first + every 100th occurrence) + skipped, does NOT advance `AssistantCount`, does NOT terminate iteration; non-EOF read error wrapped `"jsonl: read at offset %d: %w"`. Trust boundary: package doc-comment carries verbatim `MUST NOT log file contents at any layer` from `internal/agentrun/trust.go:1-9` (claude session JSONL may contain user prompts, file contents, operator-pasted secrets); logs only offsets + the json package's error message string, never the source line bytes. Not safe for concurrent use — construct one Reader per source. No `context.Context` — cancellation is structural (consumer closes the underlying `*os.File`, next `Read` errors, `Next` surfaces it; the fsnotify driver owns the context). Why not `bufio.Scanner` / `bufio.Reader.ReadBytes('\n')`: Scanner has 64 KiB token cap (real lines reach ~80 KiB) AND cannot distinguish "drained mid-line, more may come" from "stream ended" — both surface as `Scan() returning false`; `ReadBytes` returns partial bytes + EOF mid-line, requiring an `io.MultiReader` re-feed dance. Hand-rolled buffer is ~15 lines and removes the dance. Fixtures (`internal/agentrun/jsonl/testdata/`): `clean.jsonl` (single `end_turn` last assistant entry, 25 entries, fires once), `double_end_turn.jsonl` (transitional 0-text `end_turn` + real `end_turn` with text, 6 entries, fires only on the second), `no_end_turn.jsonl` (max_turns-like, 20 entries observed — architect spec said 34, developer corrected from observation, same shape as #347's "ticket bodies on filesystem layout are advisory" lesson). Consumer: sibling #349 (fsnotify wrapper) wraps this reader with a `Watcher` over `~/.claude/projects/<EncodeProjectDir(workdir)>/`, calls `Next` synchronously on each Write event, persists `Offset()` as the resume point, surfaces `EndOfTurn` to the dispatcher's max-turn enforcement loop. Greenfield package — zero existing call sites, no migrations, no shims. |
| [agentrun-package.md](features/agentrun-package.md) | `internal/agentrun` — post-#508 the parent package hosts four small exported helpers: `ResolveWorkdir(workdir) (string, error)` (`filepath.Abs` → `filepath.EvalSymlinks` for the macOS `/var → /private/var` rule → (#910) an on-disk-case canonicalisation walk, so a case-insensitive-filesystem workdir whose configured case differs from disk resolves to the spelling claude derives, mirroring `internal/sessions/rotation/watcher.go`'s key shape; sole remaining caller is `internal/agentrun/trust` — [#516](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode/issues/516) tracks deleting it once trust migrates off). The pre-#508 `EncodeProjectDir` wrapper (#347 → delegated in #501 → deleted in #508) is gone; the dashed `~/.claude/projects/<encoded>/` encoder lives in `tui-driver/pkg/tuidriver.EncodeCwd` and consumers call `tuidriver.SessionJSONLPath` / `tuidriver.EncodeCwd` directly. The three surfaces this package once owned were deleted in #392 when stream-json subprocess mode replaced PTY drive: `MarkWorkdirTrusted` → resurrected as the `internal/agentrun/trust` subpackage (#475, slimmed); `Drive` / `DriveConfig` → resurrected as `internal/agentrun/ptyrunner` (#471); `WriteSettings` / `SettingsFilename` → resurrected as the `internal/agentrun/settings` subpackage (#476, slimmed). Also hosts `ExitErrIsBenign(err) bool` (#527, shared benign-teardown-error predicate consumed by `ptyrunner`/`budget`/`streamrunner`), `IsNewLogicalTurn(currentID, lastID) bool` (#574, shared logical-turn-boundary predicate consumed by `budget` and `streamjson`), and (#923) `ReapDescendantGroups(rootPid, logger)` — the claude descendant-process-group reaper, lifted verbatim (only the log prefix changed, `ptyrunner:`→`agentrun:`) from `internal/agentrun/ptyrunner` so the stream-json runner could consume it without importing a sibling `agentrun` subpackage; its enumeration helper `descendantPGIDs` relocated alongside it but stays unexported (no consumer outside the reaper). `ptyrunner` and, as of #924, `streamrunner` both import this parent for `ExitErrIsBenign` and `ReapDescendantGroups` (each via its own local `reapDescendantGroupsFn` seam); `jsonl/tail` and `settings` remain parent-import-free. See [`codebase/923.md`](codebase/923.md) / [`codebase/924.md`](codebase/924.md). |
| [agentrun-trust-subpackage.md](features/agentrun-trust-subpackage.md) | `internal/agentrun/trust` (#475, slimmed resurrection of #341 / #392) — `MarkWorkdirTrusted(workdir) (realpath string, err error)` ensures `~/.claude.json :: projects[<realpath(workdir)>].hasTrustDialogAccepted = true` so PTY-driven interactive `claude` (via `ptyrunner`, #471) skips the workspace-trust modal that the dispatcher has no human to dismiss. Returns the resolved realpath so the caller (#470's `runAgentRun`) hands it straight to `ptyrunner.Config.WorkDir` — eliminates a second `agentrun.ResolveWorkdir` call. Public wrapper is two lines: `os.UserHomeDir()` → delegate to unexported `markWorkdirTrustedIn(homeDir, workdir)` test seam (Go's `t.Setenv` forbids `t.Parallel` ancestors; the seam lets behavioural tests pass `t.TempDir()` directly and run in parallel). **Key delta from #341: no flock.** The original held `syscall.Flock` on `~/.claude.json.lock` across the entire RMW window to serialise concurrent pyry + foreground-claude writers. The slimmed version drops it because `ptyrunner.Run`'s post-idle `HasTrustModal(snap)` detector is the runtime safety net — if pre-marking lost the race the modal appears and `tuidriver` dismisses it.
