The single-writer crash-safety axis stays (`os.CreateTemp` → `Chmod` → encode → `Sync` → `Close` → `Rename`, mirrors `internal/devices/registry.go:Save` line-for-line). Pass-through preservation via `map[string]any` + `json.Decoder.UseNumber()` (**mandatory** — `~/.claude.json` may carry int64-sized values claude writes; default `float64` decoding silently corrupts above 2^53; pinned by `TestMarkWorkdirTrusted_PreservesNumericPrecision` with `1763123456789012345`). File mode preserved (existing file's mode copied; `0o600` on create — claude defaults to `0o644` in practice, pyry does not silently tighten). Error surface: `fs.ErrNotExist` on the data file → fresh skeleton; malformed input (unparseable JSON, `projects` not a JSON object, `projects[realpath]` not a JSON object) → wrapped error with file byte-identically untouched (pinned via three `bytes.Equal` pre/post tests); workdir-missing short-circuits via `ResolveWorkdir` BEFORE any `~/.claude.json` access (pinned by `errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist)` AND the data file not created). Logging discipline pinned at package-doc level: file contents MUST NOT be logged at any layer; no `slog` calls inside the helper; error wraps name a step (`encode` / `fsync` / `rename` / `stat` / `chmod` / `read` / `parse` / `home dir` / `create temp`) and the path only — never file bytes or unmarshalled fields. Concurrency: no goroutines, no `context.Context`, no in-process state — purely sequential within an invocation. Stdlib only (`bytes`, `encoding/json`, `errors`, `fmt`, `io/fs`, `os`, `path/filepath`) + `internal/agentrun` for `ResolveWorkdir`. Tests: ten cases, nine `t.Parallel()` behavioural drives through `markWorkdirTrustedIn` covering the seven AC rows plus numeric-precision + file-mode preservation + two non-object-shape rejections, plus one non-parallel `TestMarkWorkdirTrusted_PublicSmoke` via `t.Setenv("HOME", t.TempDir())` to pin the `os.UserHomeDir` plumbing without duplicating the matrix. Patterns established: **"slim resurrection"** when a deleted helper returns with a downstream safety net (enumerate what the old version did, identify what the new system covers, descope the now-redundant parts, name the trigger for re-introduction); **test seam for `os.UserHomeDir`-using helpers** — explicit-`homeDir` unexported sibling lets behavioural tests `t.Parallel`. Consumers: (1) `pyry agent-run` (#470) wires `MarkWorkdirTrusted(parsed.workdir)` into `cmd/pyry/agent_run.go::runAgentRun` immediately before the `ptyrunner.Run` spawn; (2) the **daemon serve path** (`runSupervisor`, #670) — the second consumer — pre-marks its workdir before any spawn and threads the realpath into `Bootstrap.WorkDir` so the marked key and the child cwd stay byte-identical (fixing the #421 clean-exit restart loop where the supervised claude wedged on the modal the bridge can't surface to the phone). **`$HOME` confinement is caller-side, not in this helper:** #670 added a strict-tightening deny-gate in `runSupervisor` only (a workdir resolving outside `$HOME` is rejected as a loud startup failure) — `MarkWorkdirTrusted` stays unconfined because the agent-run path shares it; the bound canonicalises both sides (`EvalSymlinks`) and uses a boundary-aware `filepath.Rel` test (#118/#221), errors content-free. See [codebase/670.md](codebase/670.md). **On-disk-case trust key (#910):** `ResolveWorkdir`'s output (the projects-map key this helper writes) is now canonicalised to the on-disk case, fixing the 2026-05-29 incident where a wrong-cased configured workdir on a case-insensitive filesystem pre-marked a key claude never read, rendering the trust modal and aborting every dispatch with `ErrTrustModalDetected`. See [codebase/910.md](codebase/910.md). |
| [agentrun-settings-subpackage.md](features/agentrun-settings-subpackage.md) | `internal/agentrun/settings` (#476, slimmed resurrection of #339 / #392; literal corrected #487) — `WriteSettings(allowedTools []string) (string, error)` writes a per-spawn deny-default permissions JSON file `{"permissions":{"allow":[...],"defaultMode":"dontAsk"}}` to `os.TempDir()` and returns the absolute path. The cutover caller (#470's `runAgentRun`) hands the path to [`ptyrunner.Config.SettingsPath`](features/ptyrunner-package.md) so PTY-driven interactive `claude` enforces the same deny-default tool whitelist `claude -p --allowedTools` had natively. Resurrection trigger: Phase A (2026-05-14) found `--allowedTools` is additive in interactive mode — only `--settings` with `defaultMode:"dontAsk"` replicates `-p` semantics. **Literal correction (#487):** the Phase A spike guessed `"deny"` and "verified" empirically with a prompt that happened to refuse under claude's silent fallback-to-`"default"` mode; claude 2.1.145 rejects unknown values at startup and prepopulates a `/doctor` repair template into the input buffer, reopening the same prompt-injection vector on the ptyrunner path. The fix is the Anthropic-documented value `"dontAsk"` (per [`agent-sdk/permissions`](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/permissions): "converts any permission prompt into a denial"), guarded against silent recurrence by `internal/e2e/realclaude/doctor_poisoning_regression_test.go`. **Key deltas from #339:** (1) subpackage `internal/agentrun/settings/` not sibling file; (2) signature drops `workdir` — tempfile lives in `os.TempDir()` with `os.CreateTemp("", "pyry-agent-run-settings-*.json")` random suffix instead of `<workdir>/.pyry-agent-run-settings.json`; (3) atomic-rename + `Sync` + explicit `0o600` chmod DROPPED — `os.CreateTemp`'s `O_CREAT\|O_EXCL` + Unix-default `0o600` + no pre-existing-file-to-overwrite + no second-reader-during-writes makes the recipe unnecessary; (4) empty input is an error before any file is created (the `nil → []string{}` normalisation #339 needed is no longer applicable); (5) caller owns `defer os.Remove(path)` on the success path — helper cleans up only on its own error path so callers never see a leaked path on `(_, err)`; (6) no stdout marker line — #470 owns any operator-visible signal. JSON shape compact (no `SetIndent`), trailing `\n` from `json.Encoder.Encode` pinned by golden-bytes test, field order load-bearing via two unexported struct types (`settingsFile.Permissions`, then `permissions.Allow` before `permissions.DefaultMode`); `DefaultMode` plain `string` not a typed enum. Order and duplicates in `allowedTools` are preserved verbatim — no sort, no dedup. Error surface: empty input → `errors.New("agentrun/settings: allowedTools required")`; `os.CreateTemp` failure → wrapped `agentrun/settings: create temp: %w` (no cleanup needed); Encode / Close failure → best-effort `_ = f.Close(); _ = os.Remove(tmpName)` then wrapped `agentrun/settings: encode: %w` or `agentrun/settings: close: %w`. Logging discipline pinned at package-doc level: MUST NOT log the `allowedTools` slice, the JSON payload, or the returned path — no `slog` calls, error wraps name a step + path only. Concurrency: no goroutines, no `context.Context`, no shared state; random tempfile suffix makes concurrent invocations collision-free by construction. Stdlib only (`encoding/json`, `errors`, `fmt`, `os`) — no `path/filepath`, no `log/slog`, no internal dependency on the parent `internal/agentrun` package (no workdir to resolve). Tests: six cases, all `t.Parallel()`, no helpers (each test reads at most one file so a one-line `os.ReadFile` is clearer than a wrapper — deliberate departure from `trust`'s `readJSON`/`writeJSON` pair); empty-input case uses a `filepath.Glob` pre/post set-diff rather than a count to detect tempfile leaks while parallel sibling tests create/clean their own files in `os.TempDir()`. Patterns established: **slim resurrection continues** (second instance after #475; deltas documented in a table in both per-ticket file and feature doc); **random tempfile + caller-owned cleanup is simpler than atomic-rename** when there's no pre-existing file to overwrite and no second reader before the writer closes; **`filepath.Glob` pre/post set-diff for no-leak assertions** when parallel tests share a directory. Lesson: don't follow the atomic-write recipe mechanically — check whether the failure modes it defends are even present. Production consumer: `#470` wires the writer into `cmd/pyry/agent_run.go::runAgentRun` immediately before `ptyrunner.Run`, paired with `defer os.Remove(settingsPath)`. **Deny extension (#411):** an additive sibling `WriteSettingsWithDeny(allowed, disallowed []string)` plus a shared unexported `writeSettings` core adds an optional `permissions.deny` list (`Deny []string \`json:"deny,omitempty"\``, positioned between `allow` and `defaultMode`); `agent-run`'s optional `--disallowed-tools` flow through it so claude's human-interaction tools (`AskUserQuestion`/`EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode`) are removed from the model's surface entirely. `omitempty` makes "absent deny → byte-unchanged" structural; `WriteSettings` stays a `(…, nil)` wrapper so `selfcheck` + the realclaude byte-equivalence pin + the existing golden tests are provably untouched (cleanest evidence for the back-compat AC). Deny is never emptiness-validated (empty = legal "no deny key"); the `allowedTools` non-empty guard applies through both doors. See [codebase/411.md](codebase/411.md). |
| [streamrunner-package.md](features/streamrunner-package.md) | `internal/agentrun/streamrunner` (#390, consumed #391) — no-PTY sibling of `agentrun.Drive`. `Run(ctx, Config) error` spawns claude as a plain subprocess (caller-assembled argv, expected to include `--input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --dangerously-skip-permissions`), writes one stream-json user-turn JSON envelope to stdin and closes stdin, forwards stdout/stderr to caller-supplied writers, waits for the child to exit. Envelope shape: `{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"<prompt>"}]}}` + `'\n'`; `PromptBytes` is **JSON-encoded** (`encoding/json`) — not shell-escaped — so embedded `"`, `\n`, `\\`, and control characters round-trip cleanly (pinned by `TestRun_StdinEnvelopeRoundTrip` with a deliberately tricky prompt). Stdin write is synchronous in the calling goroutine; `Write` / `Close` failures Warn-log-and-continue (child's exit code is the authoritative outcome — identical pattern to `drive.go`'s PTY writes). Return contract: `nil` on clean (exit 0) child termination; `nil` on ctx-cancel-driven teardown (operator shutdown is success — `ctx.Err() != nil` masks the post-`WaitDelay` signal-exit code); `*exec.ExitError` verbatim on non-zero exit not triggered by ctx cancel; wrapped `streamrunner: stdin pipe:` / `streamrunner: start:` for pre-Start setup failures; wrapped `streamrunner: marshal envelope:` only reachable if `encoding/json` rejects the input. ctx-cancel teardown: `cmd.Cancel = func() error { reapDescendantGroupsFn(cmd.Process.Pid, logger); return cmd.Process.Signal(syscall.SIGTERM) }` (#924) reaps claude's detached descendant Bash process groups — via the shared `agentrun.ReapDescendantGroups` (lifted from `ptyrunner` in #923) behind a local `reapDescendantGroupsFn` seam in `reap.go` — immediately before the existing graceful SIGTERM, which itself overrides stdlib's default SIGKILL; `cmd.WaitDelay = killGrace` (`5 * time.Second`; hardcoded per AC#2 "no timing knobs", mirrors `budget.GracePeriod` for symmetry) is stdlib's "after Cancel runs, wait this long for the process to exit, then SIGKILL and close pipes" — no package-owned goroutine, timer, or state machine beyond the idle-stall watchdog's (#360/#558). `cmd.Cancel` fires when `childCtx` is done, via either the operator ctx or the watchdog's `cancelChild`, so the one reap covers both teardowns unconditionally — the watchdog path is a harmless no-op (no tool in flight while claude owes an assistant turn). See [`codebase/924.md`](codebase/924.md). (Orthogonal to `budget.Counter`'s hand-rolled timer: that path terminates on a different signal — turn count — independent of ctx cancellation.) No `New() *Runner` constructor; pure function call shape. `Config` (required: `ClaudeBin` / `WorkDir` / `Stdout` / `Stderr`; optional: `Args` / `PromptBytes` / `Env` / `Logger`); writers MUST NOT block (stdlib forwarder blocks on writing into them while the child is still running → would deadlock `cmd.Wait`) — pinned in doc-comments. Logging discipline: Warn on stdin write/close failure (error message only); **NEVER** logs prompt bytes, the marshalled envelope bytes, or any substring of the event stream (writers are opaque `io.Writer` values; the package never sees event payload content in a parseable form — absence-asserted by construction). Dependency direction: stdlib (`context`, `encoding/json`, `errors`, `fmt`, `io`, `log/slog`, `os`, `os/exec`, `syscall`, `time`) plus the shared parent `internal/agentrun` (for `ExitErrIsBenign`, #527, and as of #924 `ReapDescendantGroups`); **does not import** `internal/supervisor` (the PTY helper) nor any **sibling** `internal/agentrun/*` subpackage — parent-only import keeps it a leaf primitive relative to `ptyrunner`/`budget`/etc; verifiable by `go list -deps ./internal/agentrun/streamrunner/... | grep pyrycode/internal/supervisor` (expected: empty). Tests: same-package `TestStreamRunnerHelperProcess` re-exec fake claude with four `GO_STREAMRUNNER_HELPER_MODE` modes — `clean` (three-line stream-json sequence: `system init` / `assistant text` / `result success` + exit 0), `exit1` (drain stdin + exit 1), `sleep` (SIGTERM handler prints `"got SIGTERM"` to stderr + exits 0 within ~50ms; 30s timeout fallback), `echo_stdin` (copies stdin to `GO_STREAMRUNNER_HELPER_STDIN_FILE` mode 0o600 + exit 0) — drive four `t.Parallel` test cases mapping one-to-one to the architect spec's AC trace: `TestRun_CleanExit` (stdout substring checks for `"type":"system"` / `"type":"assistant"` / `"type":"result"`; stderr empty), `TestRun_NonZeroExit` (`errors.As(&exitErr)` succeeds, `ExitCode() == 1`), `TestRun_CtxCancelMidRun` (parent cancels ctx 100ms after Run starts; Run returns nil, elapsed < 6s, `"got SIGTERM"` on stderr — sanity-checks SIGTERM not SIGKILL was the trigger), `TestRun_StdinEnvelopeRoundTrip` (prompt is `"hello \"world\"\n\\backslash\x01end"`; helper writes received stdin to a tempfile; test unmarshals into a local `userTurn` mirror and asserts byte-for-byte text equality + newline-terminated envelope). Consumer (#391): `cmd/pyry/agent_run.go` assembles the full claude argv (`--input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose --dangerously-skip-permissions --allowed-tools … --model … --effort … --max-turns … --append-system-prompt-file …`), passes prompt bytes through `Config.PromptBytes`, and forwards claude's stream-json stdout byte-for-byte to the dispatcher — eliminating the 2026-05-14 `/doctor` PTY-poisoning regression by structural design rather than schema validation. Sibling primitive `agentrun.Drive` (PTY-driven, interactive bridge mode) stays as-is — split rather than overloaded so each function has a clean test surface and a clean failure-mode story. |
| [streamsup-package.md](features/streamsup-package.md) | `internal/streamsup` (#1087, #1088, #1093, #1094, #1097) + `cmd/pyry`'s `newStreamRunnerFactory` (#1109, extended #1098) + the turnevent drain (`stream_turn_drain.go`, #1098) — stream-json sibling of `internal/supervisor` (the PTY path); process lifecycle + turn I/O + send-side turncommit gate + receive-side idle/stall watchdog + `sessions.Runner` satisfaction + the `RunnerFactory` that constructs a `streamRunner` from a `supervisor.Config` + a late-bound daemon-singleton fan-in (`streamTurnSink`) draining each session's parsed turnevents through the **unchanged** `interactiveTurnEmitterV2` via a single gated drain goroutine (`startStreamTurnDrainV2`, per-event session gate = AC2 cross-conversation scoping, no turnbridge reuse) have shipped, and are now **live in production**: the `interactive_runner: "stream-json"` config toggle (#1081) selects the factory onto `sessions.Config.RunnerFactory` and wires the drain's `activeSession` (`boundSessionIDForActive`, follow-active + fail-closed) / `SetReplaySource` at the relay leg, gating the three typed-nil `w.sup` readers off in stream mode. `New(Config) (*Runner, error)` / `Run(ctx) error` / `Stdin() io.Writer`. Spawns headless claude (`--input-format/--output-format stream-json --verbose`, never `-p`) and, unlike `streamrunner`, **holds its stdin open across many turns** instead of closing it after one (`cmd.StdinPipe()` opened pre-`Start`, stored under a leaf mutex, `Stdin()` returns `io.Writer` not `io.WriteCloser` so a consumer can't close a handle the runner owns). Restarts on crash via a `backoffTimer` copied verbatim from `internal/supervisor/backoff.go` (cannot import across the PTY/stream-json boundary — pure/lifted `backoff_test.go` ladder guards both). `buildArgs(base, firstRun, sessionID)` (pure) picks the id flag: first spawn `--session-id <id>`, every respawn `--resume <id>` — same id both times, so the on-disk session survives a kill-and-restart with no fork (`--fork-session` is the unused opt-in). `spawnAndWait` returns `(started bool, waitErr error)`; `Run` only advances `firstRun` when `started` is true — fixed in 66cc50e after the naive unconditional flip caused a permanent crash-loop when a spawn-**setup** failure (`StdinPipe`/`cmd.Start` erroring pre-launch) left the on-disk session never established, so the next retry's `--resume` errored "no conversation found" forever. Teardown mirrors `streamrunner`'s reap shape verbatim: `cmd.Cancel` reaps descendant Bash process groups (`reapDescendantGroupsFn` seam → `agentrun.ReapDescendantGroups`) then SIGTERMs, `cmd.WaitDelay` 5s SIGKILL fallback, fires only on ctx cancel (never a spontaneous crash — no speculative crash-path reaping). Imports stdlib + `internal/agentrun` (root) only — no `internal/supervisor`, no sibling `agentrun` subpackage; `go list -deps ./internal/streamsup/... \| grep pyrycode/internal/supervisor` expected empty. Test harness dispatches its fake child from `TestMain` on `GO_STREAMSUP_HELPER=1` **before `flag.Parse`**, not streamrunner's `-test.run` re-exec trick — `buildArgs` puts the package's fixed stream-json flags ahead of any caller args, so `-test.run` can never sort first and `go test` would exit 2 on the unknown leading flag. **Turn I/O (#1088):** `WriteTurn(ctx context.Context, w io.Writer, prompt []byte) error` marshals a user-turn envelope (streamrunner's shape, `json.Marshal`-escaped so an untrusted prompt can't forge a second stream-json control line — security-sensitive, pinned by an injection-resistance test) onto the held-open stdin, never closing it; `w == nil` → `ErrNoLiveChild`. `Parser` (`NewParser(sink func(turnevent.Event), logger) *Parser`, an `io.Writer` set as `Config.Stdout`) splits stdout on `'\n'`, decodes each line, and switches on the line's top-level `type` only (mirrors, does not import, `turnbridge/mapper.go`): `assistant`→`TextChunk`/`ThoughtChunk`/`ToolStart` per content block, `user`/`tool_result`→`ToolUpdate`, `result`→the turn boundary (`TurnEnd`), `system`/`rate_limit_event`/unknown/malformed→dropped. Turn-stateless (only a partial-line buffer, no turn counter) — makes zero cross-turn bleed structural. No mutex: `os/exec`'s single `io.Copy` forwarder goroutine is the only writer. No transcript file opened/watched (AC4). **Turncommit gate on send (#1093):** `WriteTurn` claims the `internal/turncommit` gate carried on `ctx` (mirrors `supervisor.deliverViaSession`), after the `w == nil` check and before marshalling — a false claim returns bare `turncommit.ErrDropped` and writes zero bytes, a nil gate delivers unconditionally; not security-sensitive (local sync claim). **Idle/stall watchdog (#1094, receive-side):** `Watchdog` (`NewWatchdog(WatchdogConfig{Idle, PendingPermission, OnStall, Logger}) *Watchdog`, `Writer() io.Writer` composed into `Config.Stdout` alongside the #1088 `Parser`) lifts `streamrunner`'s type-aware `stallTracker`/`watchdogTickFor` poll loop but **diverges to emit-not-kill**: on `awaiting && silent > Idle` (latched once per episode) it evaluates the content-free `PendingPermission() bool` hook and calls `OnStall(pending)` — never kills, enforced structurally (`Watchdog` holds no `context.CancelFunc`/process handle, so a kill can't be wired back in without changing the type). The hook annotates the signal, it does not gate it — both a genuine wedge and an approval-wait emit. Reads only each line's top-level `type`, never content (AC3). Not security-sensitive (the hook is local/content-free; its producer, the approval flow, lands in #1079/#1080 with its own review). **`sessions.Runner` satisfaction (#1097):** native `State()`/`Phase` (`state.go`, mirrors `supervisor.State` field-for-field), a ported live-restart seam (`Restart(args)` + `iterCtx`/`restartCh`/`liveArgs`/`drainRestart`, mirroring `supervisor.Restart`), `WriteUserTurn(ctx, conversationID, payload)` (a one-line wrap of `WriteTurn`, no new envelope logic), and `WaitForPTY(ctx) error` (bare `return nil`, no PTY on this path) — `internal/streamsup` must not import `internal/supervisor`, so `cmd/pyry/streamsup_runner.go`'s `streamRunner` adapter hosts the covariant `State()→supervisor.State` mapping and the `var _ sessions.Runner = streamRunner{}` compile assertion. **`streamRunnerFactory` (#1109):** `func(cfg supervisor.Config) (sessions.Runner, error)` — exactly the `sessions.RunnerFactory` shape — calls `streamsup.New(mapStreamsupConfig(cfg))` and wraps the result in `streamRunner`; on a `streamsup.New` error returns a genuine nil runner and a wrapped error, with **no PTY fallback branch** (never calls `supervisor.New`), so the primary interactive bootstrap can't silently diverge from stream-json intent. `mapStreamsupConfig` (pure) maps `supervisor.Config` → `streamsup.Config`, renaming `ClaudeArgs` → `Args` and running it through `stripSessionIDFlags` (drops `--session-id`/`--resume` in two-token, joined `--flag=value`, and dangling-flag forms, never mutating the input) so `streamsup.buildArgs`'s own id-flag re-injection doesn't double-inject on top of `Pool.buildSession`'s baked `--session-id <id>`. Consumes the #1108 `supervisor.Config.SessionID` seam. Does not wire into production — that's #1081. **Turnevent drain (#1098):** `newStreamRunnerFactory(sink *streamTurnSink) sessions.RunnerFactory` replaces the bare `streamRunnerFactory`, its closure setting `scfg.Stdout = streamsup.NewParser(sink.sinkFor(cfg.SessionID), cfg.Logger)` after `mapStreamsupConfig` (which stays pure, still nils `Stdout`). `streamTurnSink` is a daemon-singleton buffered-chan (256) fan-in; `sinkFor(sessionID)` is a non-blocking, drop-newest-on-full send (never wedges claude's stdout forwarder goroutine, never closed). `startStreamTurnDrainV2(ctx, sink, emitter, activeSession, logger) (cleanup func())` is the sole reader goroutine — the stream analogue of `startInteractiveTurnStreamV2`'s `OnEvent`/`FlushSignal`/`OnFlush` triple, reproduced without `turnbridge` (the Parser already emits `turnevent.Event`); it gates each envelope on `sessionID == active session` **before** calling `emitter.Handle` (AC2 cross-conversation scoping, `-race`-proven with concurrent sessions), and drives `emitter.flushC()`/`flushDelta` on the same goroutine (single-writer invariant). No transcript tailed (AC3, structural). The emitter is passed in, not built here — #1081 composes `activeSession` + wires `SetReplaySource`. **Interrupt send primitive + `result` subtype mapping (#1120):** `Runner.Interrupt() error` (`WriteInterrupt(w io.Writer, requestID string) error` + a per-`Runner` `atomic.Uint64`-minted `request_id`) writes one structured `control_request{subtype:interrupt}` line onto the live child's held-open stdin — `w == nil` → `ErrNoLiveChild`, zero bytes, no panic; concrete `*Runner` method, deliberately off `sessions.Runner` (#1077), mirrors `supervisor.SendEsc`. The parser's `result` handling now reads `subtype` via pure helper `resultTurnEndReason`: `error_during_execution` (claude's interrupt-terminated turn, spike T1 #1075) → `TurnEndReasonCancelled`, everything else unchanged → `TurnEndReasonEndTurn` — cascades to zero downstream wire-mapping edits since `outbound.go`/`acp_turn_stream.go` already string-cast `Reason` and already test `cancelled`. Not security-sensitive; routing an inbound interrupt frame to the right runner is #1121 (blocked-by #1120). **Fresh-restart under a new id (#1124):** `RestartFresh(newID string)` rotates the runner's persistent session id (a new `restartMu`-guarded `sessionID`/`rotatePending` pair, seeded from `cfg.SessionID` in `New`) and re-arms `firstRun` so the *next* spawn uses `--session-id <newID>` (fresh transcript, no fork) instead of `--resume`; `Run` reads the id via `beginSpawn()` (since #1481; originally a dedicated `nextSpawnID()` accessor, since deleted and folded into `beginSpawn`'s single-`restartMu`-section fix) in place of the old direct `r.cfg.SessionID` read. Mirrors `Restart`'s hint-before-cancel + coalescing discipline but leaves `r.args` untouched (id rotation only, not argv). The existing `started`-gated `firstRun` flip (66cc50e) then makes a successful fresh spawn `--resume` the *rotated* id on the next crash-respawn for free, and keeps retrying `--session-id <newID>` (never `--resume`) if the fresh spawn's setup fails — never a silent regression to the pre-rotation id. Empty `newID` is a `Warn`-logged no-op (deterministic guard; real validation is the pool/routing layer's job). Concrete method, off `sessions.Runner` (#1077), same discipline as `Interrupt` (#1120). Routing an inbound `new_session` frame to the right runner + `Pool.RotateID` is #1125 (blocked-by #1124). **Permission-approval-flag injection (#1168):** `newStreamRunnerFactory` gained a second `mcpApprovePath string` param; its closure now runs `scfg.Args = withApprovalArgs(scfg.Args, mcpApprovePath)` right after `mapStreamsupConfig`. `withApprovalArgs` reads `--dangerously-skip-permissions` off the args as the per-spawn yolo signal — present → args unchanged; absent → appends `permissionArgs(false, mcpApprovePath)` (`--permission-prompt-tool`/`--mcp-config`/`--strict-mcp-config`/`--permission-mode default`, #1106). Covers bootstrap and per-conversation runners alike (shared factory). `mcpApprovePath` is written once at daemon startup by `runSupervisor` (gated on `stream-json`, fail-closed, removed at shutdown) and threaded through `selectInteractiveRunner`; the PTY (`""`/`"pty"`) path never builds the factory, so its argv is untouched. Greens `TestInteractiveStreamModalResolution` (#1154). **Per-conversation turn-busy tracker (#1201, split child of #1198):** `cmd/pyry/stream_turn_busy.go`'s `turnBusyTracker` (`Busy(conversationID) bool` / `WaitIdle(ctx, conversationID) error`, self-synchronised, one mutex + a generation-broadcast channel) is fed by a new `busy.observe(env.sessionID, env.ev)` call `startStreamTurnDrainV2` makes as the first statement of its `sink.ch` arm — one line *before* the existing `activeSession()` gate, which is what makes a turn on a non-active conversation still report busy (the gate's identity source and the emitter's cursor-resolved state differ; feeding after the gate would inherit the emitter's blindness). Opener set is a whitelist (`ThoughtChunk`/`TextChunk`/`ToolStart`/`ToolUpdate` open, either-reason `TurnEnd` closes, everything else — including `Stall`, which cannot reach this tracker through the real sink — is a no-op), justified by the parser's tolerate-and-drop `default:` arm rather than a live stray event. Keyed by conversation (write-side `conversationForSession(w.convReg, sid)` resolve, the same closure `session_transition` frames use), never by session, so a `/clear`-rotated session's late events still resolve via `SessionHistory`. `Busy`'s signature is bare-`bool` (#1101 existence-oracle discipline — no error, no second `found bool`). Ships **unwired**: no consumer reads the signal, the 7 pre-existing drain-test call sites take a bare `nil` (the new drain parameter, `observe`'s nil-receiver no-op makes that safe), no v2 frame changes. **Session-teardown clear (#1202, split sibling):** `turnBusyTracker.clearForSession(sessionID)` composes onto the pool's single-valued `TransitionObserver` slot (alongside the incumbent wire emitter, which runs first) to close a turn on a `/clear` rotation or an idle/cap eviction — the two teardowns whose abandoned turn never produces a `result` line for `observe`'s `TurnEnd`-only clear to catch. Session-keyed (never conversation-keyed) and resolved via the same closure `observe` uses; runs synchronously on the transition-firing goroutine (cheaper than the fsync that goroutine already pays one line earlier); nil-safe so PTY mode's unconstructed tracker doesn't panic. The rotation-edge hazard #1201 flagged for this ticket turned out unreachable (one runner continues under a rotated id, tagging events identically) and the comment was corrected rather than guarded. One gap remains: a child dying mid-turn and respawning fires no pool transition. **Per-child-exit seam (#1206, split from #1203 alongside sibling #1207):** `Config.OnChildExit func()` — one unconditional call in `Run`, once per completed supervision iteration (also fires on a pre-launch setup failure, never per live child), sitting above both the shutdown-path `ctx.Err()` return and the `claude exited` log so it covers crash/deliberate-restart/shutdown from one call site; runs on the `Run` goroutine with no lock held but is not a drain barrier and must not block. Ships unwired (zero `cmd/pyry` diff — every `Config` literal is named-field). **Exit lane on the turn-busy fan-in (#1209, split from #1207 alongside sibling #1210):** `streamTurnEnvelope` gains an explicit `exit bool` discriminant (never a nil `ev` sentinel — `eventKind(nil)` returns `"unknown"` rather than failing, which would make a missed check silent) and `streamTurnSink.exitFor(sessionID) func()` mirrors `sinkFor`'s non-blocking drop-newest send but logs the drop at `Warn`, not `Debug` (a missed clear degrades a conversation permanently; a missed event self-heals on the next one), with exactly `event`+`session_id` — no `kind`, mirroring the existing content-free `clear_unresolved` shape. The drain's `sink.ch` arm handles `env.exit` as its first statement — `busy.clearForSession(env.sessionID)` then `continue` — ahead of `observe`, the active-session gate, and `emitter.Handle`, so an exit for a *background* conversation still clears it; riding the fan-in (FIFO, single reader) is what keeps this clear from overtaking events the dead child already pushed, which a direct call from #1206's callback could not guarantee. No new goroutine — the clear runs inline on the drain goroutine, serialised against `observe` by construction. Shipped unfired; **fired in production by #1210**, which assigns `streamsup.Config.OnChildExit = sink.exitFor(cfg.SessionID)` in `newStreamRunnerFactory`, one line below the `sinkFor` install and bound from the same `cfg.SessionID`. That closes the mid-turn-crash gap `stream_turn_busy.go`'s `KNOWN GAP` comment named: after #1210, no reachable sequence leaves a conversation reported busy forever — `TurnEnd` closes the normal turn, the #1202 pool transition closes a rotated or evicted one, and this lane closes a crashed one. AC2's ordering (not overtaken by, does not overshoot, the events around the dead child) holds structurally from the fire site's position in `Run`, with no runtime check. **Harness self-nudge suppression (#1247):** backgrounding a command produces a turn with no visible output, and claude's harness then injects a `user`/`text` block prodding the model to speak (reproduced 3 of 3 on the #1240 probe, claude 2.1.220) — previously surfaced as `Unrecognized{Site: user_block}` per the 2026-07-27 measurement, which never drove a backgrounded command. `emitUser` now drops exactly that one 100-byte string (`harnessNoOutputNudge`) by byte-exact equality, guarded on block type `text` so a `tool_result` (which decodes into `Content`, never `Text`) can't reach it, `continue` not `return` so a sibling `tool_result` in the same message still maps — the parser's first **block-level** suppression, a new tier beneath `ignoredLineTypes` (unchanged, top-level types only; its comment is corrected in place beside the measurement this amends) rather than an entry on it. Debug-logged with `site`+`type` only, never the text. Exact match, not prefix or substring: the wording is attested on one claude version, so drift must bring the row back rather than stay silently swallowed. See [`codebase/1247.md`](codebase/1247.md), [`codebase/1087.md`](codebase/1087.md), [`codebase/1088.md`](codebase/1088.md), [`codebase/1093.md`](codebase/1093.md), [`codebase/1094.md`](codebase/1094.md), [`codebase/1097.md`](codebase/1097.md), [`codebase/1109.md`](codebase/1109.md), [`codebase/1098.md`](codebase/1098.md), [`codebase/1120.md`](codebase/1120.md), [`codebase/1124.md`](codebase/1124.md), [`codebase/1081.md`](codebase/1081.md) (production wiring), [`codebase/1168.md`](codebase/1168.md) (approval-flag injection), [`codebase/1201.md`](codebase/1201.md) (turn-busy tracker), [`codebase/1202.md`](codebase/1202.md) (session-teardown clear), [`codebase/1206.md`](codebase/1206.md) (per-child-exit seam), [`codebase/1209.md`](codebase/1209.md) (exit lane on the fan-in), [`codebase/1210.md`](codebase/1210.md) (fires the exit lane in production), [`codebase/1199.md`](codebase/1199.md) (the delivery-seam consumer: `waitIdleForDelivery`/`openForDelivery` hold a mid-turn stream-json send in the msgqueue backlog and mark the conversation busy before writing, closing the queued-backlog-UI/drop-before-drain regression against `pty`). **`system` subtype dispatch (#1380):** the receive-side parser's `system`/`rate_limit_event` known-ignored tier is no longer wholesale — `emitSystemSubtype`, matched inside the existing `ignoredLineTypes` drop branch (so the surfaced/`Unrecognized` tier stays structurally unreachable from any `system` line), maps the first captured subtype, `system/task_started`, to a new `turnevent.BackgroundTaskStarted` (`TaskID`, `ToolCallID`, `Description`, `TaskType`, `TruncatedFields`), fixing #1240's symptom where a backgrounded command's lifecycle was indistinguishable from a genuine turn end. Every claude-derived field is truncated at construction (`maxTaskFieldID`/`maxTaskDescription`, mirroring `maxUnrecognizedRaw`); claude's `session_id`/`uuid` are deliberately not carried. Built and proven against the #1260 capture. `background_tasks_changed` (#1381) extends the same mapped set. See [`codebase/1380.md`](codebase/1380.md). **`task_updated` mapping (#1382):** second scalar subtype, one more `emitSystemSubtype` arm, mapping to a new `turnevent.BackgroundTaskUpdated` (`TaskID`, `Patch`, `TruncatedFields`). `Patch` (claude's `patch` object) is carried WHOLE and unparsed — `json.RawMessage` at the decode boundary (verbatim bytes, no key reordering or float64 rounding), a bounded `string` at the event boundary (`Unrecognized.Raw`'s precedent: a truncated object is no longer valid JSON). New independent cap `maxTaskPatch = 4096`, argued from the v2 envelope rather than the 24-byte observation (a single-key patch says nothing about a two-key one); worst case 4352 bytes/6.6% of the envelope, in line with `BackgroundTaskStarted`'s 7.4%. Discovered a genuinely new property of `truncateField`'s UTF-8 scrub: it is load-bearing on the *untruncated* path for a `json.RawMessage`-sourced field specifically (invalid bytes survive decode into `RawMessage` where they'd be U+FFFD-replaced in a `string`), corrected in-place in `truncateField`'s doc comment. See [`codebase/1382.md`](codebase/1382.md). **Swap-only spawn-argv installer (#1580):** `SetSpawnArgs(args []string)` is `Restart`'s argv-install half alone — no `restartCh` hint, no `iterCancel` call — for a caller that wants the *next* spawn to use new argv without ending the child running now; declining `Restart` is not a substitute (it loses the swap outright). Both methods now install through a new unexported `setArgsLocked` (the sole writer of `r.args`, `restartMu`-guarded), which is what lets `Restart` keep its single #1481 acquisition while `SetSpawnArgs` gets its own. Added to `sessions.Runner` (compile-checked across the one production impl + five test doubles) rather than reached by type assertion. See [codebase/1580.md](codebase/1580.md). **Class-aware fan-in reserve (#1496):** `sinkFor`'s drop-newest was class-blind — on a saturated 256-slot channel it could discard the newest `turnevent.TurnEnd`, which `turnBusyTracker.observe`'s lack of a `TurnStart` made unrecoverable rather than late, violating ADR 025 § Backpressure's never-drop-control promise that `pushQueue.enqueue` already honours downstream. `observe`'s type switch is extracted into a shared pure classifier, `turnMarkFor`, read by both `observe` and the new `sinkFor`: a closing-class event (`TurnEnd`, the `exit` envelope) now sends against the channel's full capacity, while the droppable class alone is refused at a `droppableCap` watermark (`buf - min(32, buf/2)`) that reserves the tail slots — the fan-in stays strictly bounded (the reserve partitions existing capacity, adds none), yielding never-drop-control rather than `pushQueue`'s strictly-bounded-via-soft-overflow, since claude's stdout has no analogue of #911's per-conn in-flight gate to bound an excursion. Residual closing-class loss logs at `Warn`, not `Debug`, visible at the daemon's default `LevelInfo`. Corrects the "no reachable sequence leaves a conversation reported busy forever" claim #1201–#1210 recorded as SATISFIED, which held for every clear-side feed but not for this fan-in precondition. See [`codebase/1496.md`](codebase/1496.md). **`control_response` ack consumed content-free (#1500)**, own `case` arm, no `subtype` dispatch. **Bypass revocation send primitive (#1603):** `(*Runner).RevokeBypass`/`WriteBypassRevocation`/`marshalBypassRevocationEnvelope` mirror the interrupt trio for a `set_permission_mode` control line that drops a live child's bypass posture with no respawn (#1595); mode is fixed in code, never a parameter; the interrupt id counter is renamed `interruptSeq`→`controlSeq`/`nextInterruptID`→`nextControlID` and shared across subtypes; **wired (#1604):** unlike `Interrupt`, `RevokeBypass` is ON `sessions.Runner` — its consumer, `Pool.UpdateSettings`, is inside `internal/sessions` with no `cmd/pyry` dispatch site to type-assert at, and a structural assertion there would fail open. See [`codebase/1500.md`](codebase/1500.md), [`codebase/1603.md`](codebase/1603.md), [`codebase/1604.md`](codebase/1604.md). |
| [ptyrunner-package.md](features/ptyrunner-package.md) | **Deleted 2026-08-16 (#1348):** `internal/agentrun/ptyrunner`, the interactive-TUI claude spawn primitive driven via [`github.com/pyrycode/tui-driver`](https://github.com/pyrycode/tui-driver), and the `pyry agent-run` entry point that drove it were removed together with the `PYRY_USE_STREAMJSON` rollback selector. `internal/agentrun/streamrunner` is the only `pyry agent-run` path now — see [pyry-agent-run-command.md](features/pyry-agent-run-command.md); doc kept as historical reference only. |
| [streamjson-package.md](features/streamjson-package.md) | `internal/agentrun/streamjson` (#354, extended #498, migrated #511) — leaf package that synthesises the leading `{"type":"system","subtype":"init","cwd":...,"tools":[...],"model":...,"session_id":...}` envelope on `New` (#498 — closes the [[Drop-In Contract]] gap so `parseInitSessionID` and every dispatcher parser keyed on the leading envelope work against ptyrunner unchanged; six required fields sourced from new `Config.Cwd` / `Tools` / `Model` plus pre-existing `SessionID`; key order pinned to `testdata/captured_run.jsonl:1` by `TestNew_InitLineKeyOrderMatchesFixture` byte-comparing the synthesised line against the fixture so a future struct-field reorder fails locally instead of only under `make e2e-realclaude`; `Tools` non-nil with empty slice OK and `"tools":[]` vs `"tools":null` pinned by `TestNew_EmptyToolsMarshalsAsEmptyArray`; on init-write failure `New` returns `(nil, err)` with the `streamjson: emit init` wrap pinned by `TestNew_InitWriteFailureReturnsError`; the unexported `initLine` struct is sibling to `trailer` and pins key order via struct-field declaration order — same convention `trailer` uses), re-emits the parsed entry stream onto stdout as line-delimited stream-json (byte-equivalent to `claude -p --output-format stream-json`), and composes a single `type:"result"` trailer on close. After #511 `Emit` takes `tuidriver.JSONLEntry` directly (was `jsonl.Event`): the five reads remap to `entry.Type` (was `Kind`), `entry.Message.StopReason` (was `StopReason` field, with a defensive `Message != nil` guard inside the assistant branch), `tuidriver.IsEndTurn(entry)` (was `EndOfTurn` field — function call not field), `readUsage(entry)` walking `Message.Raw["usage"].(map[string]any)` with `float64`→`int` truncation (was `*UsageBlock` typed gate), and `entry.RawLine` for the byte-passthrough (was `Raw`). `readUsage` returns `(zero, false)` on four absent paths (no `Message`, no `Message.Raw`, no `"usage"` key, non-map at `"usage"`) — preserving the pre-#511 `Usage == nil` semantics; **never** infer presence from "all four counters zero" (would drop legitimate zero-cost assistant entries). Post-[#512](codebase/512.md) `ptyrunner.Run` drains `tuidriver.TailJSONL` inline and feeds each entry straight into `emitter.Emit` — the throwaway `eventToEntry` adapter introduced in #511 (and the entire `internal/agentrun/jsonl/tail/` watcher package) are deleted. Post-[#513](codebase/513.md) that same `emitter.Emit(entry)` call moves under the `EventKindJsonlEntry` arm of a `for ev := range sess.Events(...)` switch-dispatch loop — wire-compatible because `Emit` reads only `ev.Entry`. `Emitter.Emit(entry)` writes `entry.RawLine + '\n'` verbatim — **no re-encoding under any circumstance** (re-marshalling `entry.Raw` would normalise key order and whitespace, breaking #506 byte-equivalence) — aggregates the four usage counters, counts logical turns for `num_turns` (distinct assistant `message.id`s via transition-counting — a split thinking/tool_use/text reply sharing one id is **one** turn, matching claude's native count streamrunner forwards; id-less entries each count via the empty-id floor; [#573](codebase/573.md)), tracks the last assistant's `Message.StopReason`, and records whether `IsEndTurn(entry)` was observed. Sticky write error (broken pipe → subsequent `Emit`s no-op, watcher drains to EOT or ctx-cancel without thrashing). `Emit` after `Close` no-ops (returns nil) — defends against the slow-watcher-unwind race. `Close()` writes the trailer line and is idempotent (second call returns the first's error, no double-write). `SetExitReason(ExitReason)` is the pluggable setter for #334's future budget Counter integration: `Counter.Terminate` will call `SetExitReason(ExitReasonMaxTurns)` before SIGTERM so the trailer reports `subtype:"error_max_turns"` / `terminal_reason:"max_turns"`. Default classification in `Close` when `SetExitReason` was never called: EOT observed → `ExitReasonCompletion` (`subtype:"success"`, `terminal_reason:"completed"`, `is_error:false`); EOT not observed → `ExitReasonError` (`subtype:"error_during_execution"`, `terminal_reason:""`, `is_error:true`). Trailer field set was **derived from captured `claude -p` fixtures, not invented** — emits the subset the v1 dispatcher parser (`agent-dispatcher/src/dispatch.ts`) reads plus the fields the v2 fixture-compare test asserts on; both `subtype` and `terminal_reason` are emitted because v1 reads one and v2 asserts on the other. Trailer struct fields are JSON-tagged with fixed order (`type, subtype, is_error, duration_ms, num_turns, result, stop_reason, session_id, total_cost_usd, usage, terminal_reason`) so `TestCapturedFixture_ByteEquivalence` diffs cleanly. `total_cost_usd` is always `0` (dispatcher owns the rate table) and `result` is always `""` (pyry doesn't synthesise final assistant text; dispatcher logs assistant events independently — defer adding it until a consumer requires it). `usage` always emits all four keys (`input_tokens` / `output_tokens` / `cache_creation_input_tokens` / `cache_read_input_tokens`) with integer totals — never `omitempty`, since a missing key is a different wire shape than zero. Single mutex held only for one `Emit` write+state-update or one `Close` write+flag-flip; no nested locks; concurrency-safe across `Emit` (the inline-drain goroutine in `ptyrunner.Run`), `SetExitReason` (budget Counter's Terminate hook), and `Close` (driver goroutine). Package doc-comment is load-bearing: MUST NOT log `entry.RawLine` bytes nor per-entry usage values — totals and counts only. Tests (after #511, 21 in `emitter_test.go`) covering raw-byte passthrough (incl. non-canonical whitespace preservation), usage aggregation across multiple assistants (incl. nil-Usage assistants), `num_turns` logical-turn counting (`TestEmit_NumTurnsCountsLogicalTurns` — distinct `message.id`s; split thinking+text reply is one turn; empty-id floor; #573), `stop_reason` last-wins (and `""` when no assistant seen), all three trailer compositions + the default-error fallback, idempotence of `SetExitReason` and `Close`, sticky-write-error, `Emit`-after-`Close` no-op, `cfg.Now` clock seam, `SessionID` round-trip, constant `total_cost_usd`/`result`, and the load-bearing `TestCapturedFixture_ByteEquivalence` which replays `testdata/captured_run.jsonl` (synthesised matching real captured shape) through `Emit`+`Close` and asserts non-result lines byte-equivalent + structural equality on `type`/`subtype`/`is_error`/`num_turns`/`stop_reason`/`terminal_reason` + four usage int fields, with known-expected diff list documented inline (timestamps, session ids, claude-only fields like `modelUsage`/`permission_denials`/`uuid`/`api_error_status`/`duration_api_ms`, extra usage subfields). Wire-up consumer: [pyry-agent-run-command.md](features/pyry-agent-run-command.md). |
| [budget-package.md](features/budget-package.md) | `internal/agentrun/budget` (#334) — `Counter` enforces the pyry-side `--max-turns` cap for `pyry agent-run` (interactive claude doesn't self-enforce `--max-turns` the way `claude -p` does, so pyry has to). Surface: `Reason{ReasonCompletion, ReasonMaxTurns}` (zero value `""` for ctx-cancel teardown) + `Config{MaxTurns int, Terminate func() error, Kill func() error, GracePeriod time.Duration, Logger *slog.Logger}` + `New(cfg) (*Counter, error)` + `(*Counter).OnEvent(tuidriver.JSONLEntry)` / `OnEndOfTurn()` / `Reason() Reason` / `Stop()` (migrated from `jsonl.Event` in [#512](codebase/512.md)). Post-#512 the caller (`ptyrunner.Run`) drains `tuidriver.TailJSONL` inline and invokes `OnEvent` per entry; post-[#513](codebase/513.md) the same calls move under the `EventKindJsonlEntry` and `EventKindJsonlEndOfTurn` arms of a unified `sess.Events(...)` switch-dispatch loop. End-of-turn classification is the **caller's** job — it calls `OnEndOfTurn` only when `tuidriver.IsEndTurn(entry)` returns true post-#512, or when `EventKindJsonlEndOfTurn` fires on the Events stream post-#513 (tui-driver applies the same `IsEndTurn` predicate internally). Wire `Terminate` / `Kill` to `cmd.Process.Signal(syscall.SIGTERM)` / `syscall.SIGKILL` in production (tests inject `signalRecorder` stubs). Counting rule from parent #329's Phase A spike (verified across 1151 real session JSONLs): each `entry.Type == "assistant"` JSONL entry counts as one turn, **including** empty-content transitional `end_turn` blocks; non-assistant kinds (`user`, `tool_use`, `tool_result`, `system`, `attachment`, `""`) return immediately without incrementing — defensive filter at the top of `OnEvent` because the upstream `tuidriver.TailJSONL` channel emits every well-formed line, not just assistant entries. `OnEvent` increments-then-branches under the lock: `fired=true` returns (idempotent — one SIGTERM per Counter); `count < MaxTurns` returns; else set `fired=true`, set `reason=ReasonMaxTurns`, arm `time.AfterFunc(GracePeriod, killAfterGrace)`, unlock, invoke `cfg.Terminate()` (Warn-log on error — grace timer is still armed so Kill follows even if Terminate fails with ESRCH). `OnEndOfTurn` is first-terminal-wins: sets `reason=ReasonCompletion` only if `reason==""`, never overwrites `ReasonMaxTurns`. **Budget-boundary case is `ReasonMaxTurns` post-[#512](codebase/512.md)** (deliberate semantic flip — the pre-#512 EOT early-return branch was deleted along with its pinning test `TestOnEvent_BudgetBoundaryEndOfTurnIsCompletion`): when the `MaxTurns`-th assistant entry is itself end-of-turn, `OnEvent` grabs the reason first under `c.mu`, fires `Terminate`, and the caller's subsequent `OnEndOfTurn` is a no-op; SIGTERM races claude's natural exit and both converge. `Stop()` cancels the pending SIGKILL timer (captures `killTimer` under the lock, nils it, releases the lock, calls `t.Stop()`); idempotent; called by the integration ticket after `cmd.Wait` returns. `killAfterGrace` re-checks `killTimer == nil` under the lock so a `Stop` racing a firing timer leaves the system consistent; external callbacks (Terminate, Kill) always invoked unlocked to avoid deadlocking a caller that holds the mutex indirectly. `defaultGracePeriod = 5 * time.Second` copied from `supervisor.spawnWaitDelay` per the spec ("the units are independent and we should not couple the budget package to the supervisor package") — same copy-don't-import discipline as `agentrun`'s atomic-write recipe vs `internal/devices/registry.go`. Single `sync.Mutex` guards `count`, `reason`, `fired`, `killTimer`; in production `OnEvent`/`OnEndOfTurn` fire from the agent-run caller's goroutine that drains `tuidriver.TailJSONL` (the `ptyrunner.Run` goroutine), the grace timer fires from `time.AfterFunc`, `Stop`/`Reason` fire from the driver goroutine. No `os/exec` / `syscall` imports — Counter is a pure budget enforcer; process lifecycle stays in supervisor/driver. Tests (12, all `t.Parallel`): `TestNew_Validation` row-table (zero/negative `MaxTurns`, nil `Terminate`, nil `Kill`); `TestOnEvent_NonAssistantKindsDoNotCount` (interleaves whitelist kinds, asserts Terminate fires only at the budget-th assistant event); `TestOnEvent_SIGTERMFiresExactlyAtBudget` (not at budget-1, exactly once at budget, not again at budget+1/+2); `TestOnEvent_SIGKILLFiresAfterGrace` (Kill not called at grace/2, called once after grace, elapsed `killAt - termAt >= grace`; `grace=80ms`); `TestStop_CancelsPendingSIGKILL` (hit budget → Stop → sleep 3×grace, no Kill; second Stop is no-op); `TestStop_WithoutBudgetHit`; `TestOnEndOfTurn_ReasonCompletion` (natural end before budget); `TestOnEndOfTurn_DoesNotOverwriteMaxTurns` (budget hit, then OnEndOfTurn — `Reason` stays `ReasonMaxTurns`); `TestReason_ZeroValueBeforeTerminalEvent`; `TestTerminateError_DoesNotBlockKill` (Terminate returns ESRCH-like error → Kill still fires after grace); `TestKillError_IsLogged` (asserts "kill failed" in slog Warn output via `syncWriter`-wrapped `strings.Builder` — slog handlers may write concurrently from `time.AfterFunc` and the test goroutine, so the buffer needs a mutex; `io.Discard` from `discardLogger()` is safe for concurrent use without wrapping). `signalRecorder` (mutex + counts + first-call timestamps for elapsed-time assertions) is the workhorse stub. Grace-period tests compress to 50-80ms; suite stays sub-second. No `Clock` interface — per evidence-based-fix-selection, defer until flakiness observed. Drive integration lives in [`ptyrunner-package.md`](features/ptyrunner-package.md) (#479 wired the Counter; #512 collapsed its OnEvent / OnEndOfTurn fan-out into the inline `tuidriver.TailJSONL` drain; [#513](codebase/513.md) collapsed that drain again onto the unified `tuidriver.Session.Events` switch-dispatch loop — both calls now live under JSONL-axis arms of the same switch). |
| [pyry-agent-run-command.md](features/pyry-agent-run-command.md) | `cmd/pyry/agent_run.go` — `pyry agent-run` verb that replaces `claude -p` in the dispatcher. **Single path since #1348**, which deleted the PTY-drive `ptyrunner` entry point and its `PYRY_USE_STREAMJSON` selector; `runAgentRunStreamRunner` is now the only runtime path (`TestRunAgentRun_SelectorVariableNoLongerDecides` pins the env var as a no-op). Flag surface (#337, `--disallowed-tools` added #411) covers `--prompt-file` / `--system-prompt-file` / `--allowed-tools` / `--disallowed-tools` (optional) / `--max-turns` / `--effort {low\|medium\|high\|xhigh\|max}` / `--model` / `--workdir` / `--output-format stream-json`, validated at parse time with one-line errors naming the offending flag. Tool boundary is the per-spawn deny-default settings file (`settings.WriteSettingsWithDeny`, delivered via `--settings`), **not** `--allowed-tools` on the argv — pyrycode#1387 (measured 2026-08-08) found `--dangerously-skip-permissions` defeats `--allowed-tools` outright, so every agent dispatched between the 2026-07-25 fleet switch and the fix ran unrestricted; the fix dropped the skip flag from this path in favour of `--permission-mode dontAsk` + `--settings`, both now negatively/positively pinned by `TestBuildStreamRunnerClaudeArgs_Shape`. Argv assembly (`buildStreamRunnerClaudeArgs` → shared [`streamrunner.BuildClaudeArgs`](features/streamrunner-package.md)) is also what the permission self-check (`--self-check`, [agentrun-selfcheck-package.md](features/agentrun-selfcheck-package.md)) builds and verifies, so the checked spawn and the production spawn cannot drift apart. #1555 reconciled six comments in `agent_run.go` that still described the deleted `ptyrunner` path as live; two sat inside the #1387 evidence block and the finding was preserved intact at both. Known residue (not yet fixed, flagged for a sibling #1348-residue ticket): `agent_run.go`'s `buildStreamRunnerClaudeArgs` doc comment and `agent_run_selfcheck.go`'s doc comments still describe `--dangerously-skip-permissions` / "the ptyrunner spawn path" as current, contradicted by the code and tests; `docs/knowledge/features/ptyrunner-package.md` still documents the deleted `internal/agentrun/ptyrunner` package as live. See [codebase/411.md](codebase/411.md) for the `--disallowed-tools` addition and [pyry-mcp-approve-command.md](features/pyry-mcp-approve-command.md) for the non-YOLO daemon-approval branch `buildStreamRunnerClaudeArgs` also supports. |
| [agentrun-selfcheck-package.md](features/agentrun-selfcheck-package.md) | `internal/agentrun/selfcheck` (#336 → rewritten #375 for stream-json → rewritten #473 for ptyrunner; literal corrected #487; argv `dontAsk` landed #538; probe-tool moved off Bash carveout #539; **detector moved to execution-layer sentinel #542**) — deterministic safety net for the per-agent tool-allowlist enforcement contract. Conceptual guard unchanged across all rewrites ("catch silent Claude Code regressions that would let our agents call tools we never allowed"); verification mechanism follows the production path. **Runtime-layer, NOT LLM-layer (#542):** per [permissions](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions) *"Permission rules are enforced by Claude Code, not by the model"* — the model emits `tool_use` blocks regardless of enforcement; the runtime denies execution *between* `tool_use` emission and `tool_result`. So the verdict is the **execution-layer side-effect** (a probe sentinel file does NOT appear on disk via post-run `os.Stat`), NOT the **LLM-layer output** (whether a `tool_use` block emitted). The JSONL stream is kept only for the end-of-turn liveness signal. Pre-#542 the JSONL `tool_use` scan watched the wrong layer and false-FAILed a healthy binary (the #532 "failing 5+ days" pattern); production enforcement always worked. Post-#473: verifies the deny-default settings file + `--settings <path> --permission-mode dontAsk` (post-#538 argv flip from `default`) under interactive-TUI PTY — the path the dispatcher actually uses after the #470 cutover. Surface post-#542: `Config{ClaudeBin, WorkDir, Logger, OverallTimeout (90s default), Env}` (`Prompt` removed — the prompt must name an internally-derived sentinel path a caller can't know) + `Result{SentinelWritten, SentinelPath string, EndOfTurnObserved, AssistantCount}` + sentinels `ErrSentinelWritten` / `ErrTimeout` + `SelfCheckDenyDefault(ctx, Config) (Result, error)`. **Four coupled hard-coded values (#539/#542)**: `canonicalProbeTool = "Write"` (single source of truth for the probe-tool name, now consumed only by the prompt — the detector stats a file); `probeSentinelName = "probe-sentinel.txt"` (#542; compile-time const, no separator/no `..` so `filepath.Join(realpath, …)` can't escape the workdir); `selfCheckMaxTurns = 2` (#542; MUST be `>= 2` so the runtime reaches the execute-or-deny step — turn 1 emits `tool_use`, deny happens between turns, turn 2 surfaces `end_turn`; `MaxTurns: 1` SIGTERM'd before the boundary = the original bug; ptyrunner rejects `<= 0`); `canonicalAllow = []string{"Read"}` (the deny-default whitelist; `canonicalProbeTool` MUST NOT appear in it, pinned by the 4-LOC `TestProbeToolIsNotInAllowList`). `canonicalPromptFor(sentinelPath)` (#542, replaced the `canonicalPrompt` const) returns `"Use " + canonicalProbeTool + " to create a file at " + sentinelPath + " with the content 'hello'. Be brief."` — probe-tool name stays a const, the absolute path is interpolated at runtime. The pre-#539 Bash echo exhibit rode `dontAsk`'s documented read-only-Bash carveout per [permission-modes](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permission-modes) and could not distinguish "boundary held" from "boundary bypassed via carveout"; `Write` sits off the carveout. **Composition (#473)**: four package-level seam vars — `trustMark = trust.MarkWorkdirTrusted`, `settingsWrite = settings.WriteSettings`, `newSessionID = sessions.NewID-wrapped`, `ptyRun = ptyrunner.Run` — let tests mock the entire spawn in-process; production never assigns. Lifecycle: validate → defaults → `trustMark(WorkDir)` (returns realpath; wraps `"mark workdir trusted: %w"`) → `settingsWrite(["Read"])` (wraps `"write settings: %w"`) + `defer os.Remove(settingsPath)` registered AFTER the err-check → `newSessionID()` (wraps `"mint session id: %w"`) → `context.WithTimeout(ctx, OverallTimeout)` → `io.Pipe` bridges spawner → watcher → errgroup with two goroutines. After `trustMark` returns `realpath`, derive `sentinelPath := filepath.Join(realpath, probeSentinelName)` and `prompt := canonicalPromptFor(sentinelPath)` — `realpath` is claude's cwd, so the prompt-named path and the post-run `os.Stat` path are byte-identical (one source of truth, no cwd-resolution ambiguity). **Spawner**: `ptyRun(gctx, ptyrunner.Config{ClaudeBin, WorkDir: realpath, SessionID, SettingsPath, SystemPrompt: "/dev/null", Model: "sonnet", Effort: "low", MaxTurns: selfCheckMaxTurns (2, #542), PromptBytes, Stdout: pw, Stderr: io.Discard, Env, Logger})`; `defer pw.Close()` so the watcher unblocks on EOF. The argv ptyrunner emits post-#538 is `--session-id <sid> --settings <path> --permission-mode dontAsk --append-system-prompt-file /dev/null --model sonnet --effort low` (no `--input-format` / `--output-format` / `--verbose` / `--dangerously-skip-permissions` / `--allowed-tools` / `--max-turns` — the settings file replaces `--allowed-tools`, the budget Counter replaces `--max-turns`). `WorkDir` is the symlink-resolved realpath from `trustMark`, not the parsed workdir — same realpath-not-parsed-workdir contract `runAgentRunPty` pins (#470) so claude's modal-check matches the trust pre-write's key. `/dev/null` as `SystemPrompt` is a portable 0-byte readable char device on Linux + macOS (the only targets); one fewer tempfile to manage vs. the empty-tempfile alternative. **Watcher (#542: liveness-only, no longer decides PASS/FAIL)**: `jsonl.NewReader(pr, …)` (#348) + loop on `reader.Next()`; on `Kind == "assistant"`: increment `AssistantCount`; on `ev.EndOfTurn`: set flag + `cancel()`; on `io.EOF`: return nil; `defer pr.Close()` so a stalled spawner-side `pw.Write` fails fast. The probe-detection branch (`probeToolInvokedInRaw` + `make+copy` Evidence) is **deleted** — a `tool_use` block is normal LLM output regardless of execution, so the verdict moves to a post-run `os.Stat`. The `streamjson.Emitter`'s trailing `type:"result"` line is filtered naturally by the `ev.Kind != "assistant"` continue — no new parser. **Pipe-close discipline is symmetric** — both ends `defer Close`; without `defer pr.Close()`, a stalled `pw.Write` from inside ptyrunner blocks forever and `errgroup.Wait()` hangs (silent regression class). **Execution-layer verdict (#542), stat-first** (the side-effect dominates the liveness signals): after `g.Wait()`, `os.Stat(sentinelPath)` — `== nil` → wrapped `ErrSentinelWritten` (FAIL, *unconditional* even if the run timed out: if the file landed the boundary leaked) > non-`ENOENT` stat error → wrapped `"stat sentinel: %w"` (infra) > sentinel absent + `EndOfTurnObserved` → `(result, nil)` (PASS) > `timeoutCtx.Err() == DeadlineExceeded` → `ErrTimeout` (INCONCLUSIVE) > non-Canceled `runErr` → wrapped infra error (incl. `ptyrunner.ErrTrustModalDetected` / `ErrMcpFailureBanner` / `ErrNetworkFailure` propagated verbatim so operators see the sentinel's embedded remediation hint) > defensive fallthrough `"terminated without end-of-turn or sentinel signal"`. The stat runs on the main goroutine after the `g.Wait()` barrier, inside `SelfCheckDenyDefault` (holds `realpath`), structurally guaranteed before the CLI wrapper's `defer os.RemoveAll(workdir)` reaps the file; `os.Stat`-only = no TOCTOU window. `SentinelWritten`/`SentinelPath` are written only after the barrier; `AssistantCount`/`EndOfTurnObserved` only before — no race. **Sub-package boundary preserved**: imports `cmd/pyry → selfcheck → {trust, settings, sessions, ptyrunner, jsonl}` — the streamrunner import is gone from the boot-time gate (streamrunner still exists in-tree for the `PYRY_USE_STREAMJSON=1` fallback but is no longer verified by the selfcheck). **Error-message hygiene is load-bearing (per #473 security review)**: the three new wrappers (`mark workdir trusted`, `write settings`, `mint session id`) MUST format with `%w` only — no workdir realpath / settings tempfile path / session id substituted into the message. Pinned by the package doc-comment so future contributors editing the helper see the rule. **CLI wrapper** `cmd/pyry/agent_run_selfcheck.go:runAgentRunSelfCheck(stdout io.Writer) error`: `os.MkdirTemp` + `defer RemoveAll` → resolve `PYRY_CLAUDE_BIN` (test seam) → `selfCheckGetVersion(claudeBin)` (best-effort 5s `claude --version`, returns `"<unavailable>"` on any failure; exposed as a `var` seam so CLI tests stub it) → `selfCheckFn(ctx, Config{ClaudeBin, WorkDir})` (also a `var` seam — CLI tests mock the entire selfcheck at the boundary instead of spawning a fake claude binary) → `errors.Is` switch rendering PASS (3 lines: marker + version + `"deny-default whitelist held: N assistant event(s)…; probe sentinel never appeared on disk."`), FAIL (`writeSelfCheckFailMessage(stdout, result.SentinelPath)` — signature `(io.Writer, string)` post-#542, multi-line operator affordance — "What was tested" describes `PTY-driven interactive-TUI mode` + `per-spawn deny-default settings file (permissions.defaultMode: "dontAsk", allow: ["Read"])` + `--settings <path> --permission-mode dontAsk` + the prompt instructing claude to `Use Write` to create a probe sentinel inside the throwaway workdir; "What was observed" reports `The probe sentinel file appeared on disk — claude's runtime executed Write despite the deny-default settings` + `Evidence (sentinel path on disk): <SentinelPath>` (a path pyry constructed, never claude output); "What to check" points at `internal/agentrun/ptyrunner/runner.go`'s `buildArgs` and `internal/agentrun/settings/settings.go`'s JSON shape, plus a sentence that the self-check now verifies RUNTIME-layer enforcement citing the permissions doc; references `#329/#336/#470/#473/#538/#539/#542`), INCONCLUSIVE (4 lines, advises retry-once before paging; body reads `Neither an end-of-turn signal nor a probe-sentinel write was observed …`), or propagate. **FAIL message required-substring pins (post-#542)**: `TestRunAgentRunSelfCheck_FAIL` asserts `permissions.defaultMode: "dontAsk"`, `["Read"]`, `PTY`, the execution-layer fragment `appeared on disk`, the sentinel path string itself, and ticket refs `#329` / `#336` / `#470` / `#473` / `#538` / `#539` / `#542` MUST be present; the pre-#542 LLM-layer substrings (`Use Write to create a file named probe.txt`, `"name":"Write"`) were removed. The settings-file literal was `"deny"` from #336 until #487; the argv literal was `default` until #538; the probe exhibit was `Use Bash to echo hello` until #539 moved it off the read-only-Bash carveout; the verdict was an LLM-layer JSONL `tool_use` scan until #542 moved it to the execution-layer sentinel `os.Stat`. **CI**: `.github/workflows/self-check-daily.yml` cron `13 6 * * *` (06:13 UTC daily, off-peak) + `workflow_dispatch`; exit-code contract unchanged across rewrites (`0` PASS, non-zero FAIL/inconclusive); no workflow-file edit required for the #473/#538/#539 cutovers — the contract is at the verb boundary, not in the workflow plumbing. **Alerting (#533, 2026-05-24):** a sibling `notify-failure` job (job-level `if: failure()` + `needs: self-check`, covers cancellation + `timeout-minutes` exhaustion) POSTs a Discord-flavoured markdown payload (workflow name + run URL wrapped in `<...>` to suppress link-preview + 12-char SHA + UTC date) to `${{ secrets.DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL }}` via `curl --fail-with-body` + `jq -n --arg` — no external action dep. Secret-unset = soft-fail (warning + exit 0) so the alert plumbing doesn't add a second red badge before the operator wires the secret; Discord-API rejection = strict-fail so "alert plumbing broken" surfaces. Provisioned after the 2026-05-15 → 2026-05-23 unread-badge incident where the safety net correctly went red the workflow's entire 9-day life while [#526](codebase/526.md) shipped to main undetected — failure mode was alert *delivery*, not alert *detection*. Tests (#473 deleted ~135 LOC of subprocess fixtures; #539 renamed Bash-specific test names; #542 swapped the FAIL-mechanism test + added the layer-swap regression net): helper-level `TestSelfCheck_{Pass, SentinelWritten (replaced ProbeToolInvoked in #542 — mock writes the sentinel inside the workdir + emits passLine; stat-first verdict FAILs even with end-of-turn), ToolUseInStreamDoesNotFail (new #542 — emits a Write tool_use but creates no file → PASS; the regression net proving an emitted-but-denied tool_use is normal output), PassesCanonicalAllowToPtyRunner (now also asserts cfg.MaxTurns >= 2, pinning AC2), Timeout, MalformedAssistantLineSkipped, ConfigValidation, TrustMarkFailure, SettingsWriteFailure, SessionIDFailure, SettingsCleanedOnLaterFailure, PtyRunnerError}` + `TestProbeToolIsNotInAllowList` (4-LOC `slices.Contains` invariant pin, new in #539); `TestProbeToolInvokedInRaw` **deleted in #542** (helper gone). All under an `installSeams(t)` helper that captures + restores the four seams and installs benign defaults (default `ptyRun` `t.Errorf`s if invoked); CLI-level `TestRunAgentRunSelfCheck_{PASS, FAIL}` (FAIL now builds `Result{SentinelWritten, SentinelPath}` + `ErrSentinelWritten`, asserts the path in stdout; the `selfCheckWriteLine` JSONL fixture was deleted) + `TestRunAgentRun_SelfCheckShortCircuit` under `installSelfCheckSeams(t)`. `TestSelfCheck_SettingsCleanedOnLaterFailure` is the defer-ordering invariant: `settingsWrite` mock calls real `os.CreateTemp`, `newSessionID` forces error, assertion is `os.Stat == ErrNotExist` (same shape as `TestRunAgentRun_PtyPath_SettingsRemovedOnFailure` from #470). No `TestSelfCheckHelperProcess` / shell-wrapper machinery — in-process seam-var mocking replaces the re-exec pattern on both sides. Daily workflow IS the e2e — fake mocks prove the harness; real claude proves the contract; `internal/e2e/realclaude/allowed_tools_enforcement_test.go` (#365) is the per-PR real-claude variant of the same boundary check on the production agent-run codepath (intentionally still Bash-specific — different contract, policy-mirror not name-mirror per the #539 spec), and #482 covers ptyrunner ↔ streamrunner wire-shape equivalence at the dispatcher boundary. |
| [pyry-update-command.md](features/pyry-update-command.md) | `cmd/pyry/update.go` (#189, extended #190) — user-facing `pyry update [--check] [--version <v>] [--no-restart]` CLI verb that wires the `internal/update` primitives into a single self-update flow: print current version → resolve target tag (latest or pinned) → short-circuit on `Same`/dev-build → `AssetName` + URL-template against the GitHub releases-download base → `Fetcher.FetchAsset` (tarball + checksums) → (#776) fetch `checksums.txt.sig` + `update.VerifySignature` against the baked-in `releaseSigningPublicKeyHex` **before any digest is parsed** (fail-closed: a missing `.sig` aborts like a missing tarball, no unsigned fallback; see ADR 028) → `VerifySHA256` → `ExtractBinary` → `AtomicReplace` at `os.Executable()` → (#190) probe `~/Library/LaunchAgents/dev.pyrycode.pyry.plist` + `~/.config/systemd/user/pyry.service` inline → `DetectRestartCommand` (#181, pure) → `==> Restarting daemon (<manager>: <unit>)...` + `exec.CommandContext` the launchctl/systemctl argv (skipped silently if no managed unit or `--no-restart` is set); `updateOptions` struct as the integration-test seam (httptest.NewServer + tempdir target + `probeRestart` / `runRestart` function fields); Homebrew hint when `os.Executable()` resolves under `/opt/homebrew/`; restart-failure error message foregrounds `binary replaced to <v>, but daemon restart failed: <inner>` so the user knows the new bytes landed and only the kick failed; e2e happy-path `cmd/pyry/update_e2e_test.go` (#260, build tag `(darwin || linux) && e2e_update`) drives the full chain end-to-end against an in-process fake release server (reusing `update_test.go`'s `fakeRelease`/`newFakeReleaseServer` verbatim), spawns a daemon from `<home>/bin/pyry`, calls `doUpdate` with a kill-and-respawn `runRestart` stand-in, and asserts inode-changed + PID-changed + `pyry status` Phase advances past `starting` (3s polling deadline catches the v0.10.1-shaped startup hang) + `pyry sessions list` exits 0 — release-acceptance gate without touching the operator's real launchd/systemd; extended #261 with three sibling failure-path tests (`TestUpdate_FetchFailure_E2E` HTTP 500 on asset download, `TestUpdate_VerifyFailure_E2E` 64-zero checksums body, `TestUpdate_BrokenNewBinary_E2E` tarball carrying `internal/brokenpyry`'s `os.Exit(1)`-stand-in) that pin "binary unchanged + daemon alive" on the pre-AtomicReplace paths and the no-rollback contract (`binary replaced to …` + `daemon restart failed`, version-agnostic) on the post-AtomicReplace path via a `BROKEN_PYRY_TOKEN` stderr-diagnostic guard; #776 added the signature gate (`signingPubKey` seam, auto-signing `newFakeReleaseServer`, `TestUpdate_MissingSignature`/`_BadSignature`/`_MissingSignature_E2E` + `TestReleaseSigningKey_Decodes`) and, in passing, fixed the pre-existing `cmd.Dir`-unset bug that had made the whole un-CI'd `e2e_update` suite un-runnable; #1498 closed the remaining downgrade gap the signature gate can't reach — an older release is validly signed too, so a yanked release or a stolen publishing token re-marking it as `/releases/latest` sailed through — with one guard between the `--check` early return and `AssetName`: refuse when `o.pinVersion == ""` and `cmp == update.Newer` (current newer than latest, the rollback direction), naming `--version <tag>` as the sanctioned unguarded downgrade route in the error text (`TestUpdate_UnpinnedDowngradeRefused`, `TestUpdate_CheckOnlyOlderLatest`) |
| [turnevent-package.md](features/turnevent-package.md) | `internal/turnevent` (#606) — daemon-owned **neutral turn-event model** for Phase 2/3 structured streaming (EPIC #596/#597); pure value types, stdlib only. Five ACP-shaped event structs (`TextChunk`, `ThoughtChunk`, `ToolStart`, `ToolUpdate`, `TurnEnd`) plus the internal-only `Stall` onset marker (#638) behind a sealed `Event` sum-type seam; four string-backed ACP enums (`ToolKind`/`ToolStatus`/`TurnEndReason`/`PermissionOptionKind`), each with a `Valid()` predicate scanning one unexported canonical slice (so const/slice/predicate drift is structurally impossible, asserted by per-enum exactness tests); a sealed `ToolContent` sum type (`TextContent`/`DiffContent`/`TerminalContent`, `nil` = status-only update). `ToolStart.RawInput` is opaque `json.RawMessage` (never parsed); `TurnEnd` carries `Reason` only — ACP's `stopReason` *return value* is the future `pyry acp` adapter's job (#600), not the model's. No errors, no constructors: out-of-taxonomy rejection is the consumer's job via `Valid()` (PROJECT-MEMORY refusal-to-wire-code convention). AC#5 stdlib-only import boundary enforced deterministically by a `go/parser` `ImportsOnly` test that rejects any import whose first path segment is dotted (no third-party linter). This is the stable contract the event-stream bridge (#608) maps tui-driver `Events()` **into** and the v2 wire types (#607) map **out of** — shaped ~90% like ACP but owned by us, so external ACP-spec churn stays inside the adapters and never reaches the daemon core or mobile wire. **#638** adds `Stall` — an internal-only, onset-only empty marker (mobile adapter *sends* it, the future ACP adapter *drops* it; mirrors tui-driver's one-shot `stall_detected`, no payload, no clearing edge), graduating it out of #606's "out of scope" list; its v2 `stall` wire peer lands in `internal/protocol` the same ticket. **#700** adds the permission **request/response** seam: `PermissionRequest` joins the outbound `Event` sum (reclassified from #606's internal-only list, following `Stall`'s precedent), `PermissionResponse` establishes the sealed **`Inbound`** sum type as its first member, and `PermissionOptionKind` (the four ACP `session/request_permission` kinds) is the fourth enum — pure value types + one non-validating constructor (`NewPermissionRequest`), no wire, no consumers; the gate/nonce/deny-on-timeout work is downstream and `security-sensitive`, not here. **#707** adds `Cancel` (`struct{}`) — the **second `Inbound` member**, the neutral remote-Esc / ACP `session/cancel` command, the slot reserved since #700. Fieldless (one live turn context, no correlation id yet; #600 adds one additively if ACP needs it); the marker stays in `permission.go` (the hinted relocation declined). `Cancel` is **declared vocabulary, not a live path**: the mobile `interrupt` frame routes to Esc directly via the `internal/relay` `Interrupter` seam, it does not construct a `Cancel` (the `modal_cancel`→`ResolveCancel` precedent); the wire→`Cancel` translator is the future ACP adapter (#600). **#1074** adds `ApiRetry{Active,Current,Total}` / `Compacting{Active}` — two more internal-only, PTY-derived status peers of `Stall`, but unlike `Stall` each carries an explicit `Active` falling edge (not onset-only), so a remote head can clear its indicator; both drop in the ACP adapter the same way `Stall` does. See [codebase/606.md](codebase/606.md), [codebase/638.md](codebase/638.md), [codebase/700.md](codebase/700.md), [codebase/707.md](codebase/707.md), [codebase/1074.md](codebase/1074.md). |
| [turnbridge-package.md](features/turnbridge-package.md) | `internal/turnbridge` (#615, #627, #639, #609, #679, #686, #1543) — since **#1543**, a **pure outbound-only** value-to-value adapter: `MapEvent` (#627) maps one neutral [`turnevent.Event`](features/turnevent-package.md) (#606) + an explicit `TurnContext` OUT to the matching v2 interactive wire payload (#607) — `TextChunk`→`assistant_delta`, `ToolStart`→`tool_use`, `ToolUpdate`→`tool_result`, `TurnEnd`→`turn_end`, `Stall`→`stall` (#639), `ApiRetry`/`Compacting`→`api_retry`/`compacting` (#1074), `ModelAnnounced`→`model_announced` (#1638), plus `BackgroundTask*`/`ThinkingProgress`/`RateLimited`/`Unrecognized`; `ThoughtChunk` and nil **drop** (#607 ships no thought-text envelope, per ADR 025). `BuildTurnState` shapes the `turn_state` payload (thinking/responding/idle) for the consumer's lifecycle machine to call. `inputSummary`/`resultSummary`/`truncate` derive the bounded single-line précis (`maxSummaryLen` 200 runes, rune-aware, exhaustive over the sealed `ToolContent`); `is_error` = `Status==ToolStatusFailed`. No I/O, no state, no envelope-ID minting, no clock read, no sealing — all the consumer's (`cmd/pyry/interactive_turn_v2.go`'s `interactiveTurnEmitterV2`). Not `security-sensitive` (pure value-to-value, no untrusted input, no capability decision). **#1543 deleted the package's inbound half** — `producer.go`+`mapper.go` (#615), the PTY-hosted tui-driver `Events()`-stream drain + its `follow-active` `NewTargetSubscriber` (#679) — along with the `tui-driver` dependency; #1348 had already orphaned it by deleting every terminal-driving claude path that fed and consumed it (including `internal/supervisor` itself), #1543 just removed the dead files and re-homed the package doc onto `outbound.go`. The live inbound counterpart today is [`streamsup-package.md`](features/streamsup-package.md)'s stream-json parser, a structurally different path with no `turnbridge` re-mapping step. See [codebase/627.md](codebase/627.md) (outbound adapter), [codebase/639.md](codebase/639.md)/[codebase/1074.md](codebase/1074.md) (stall/api-retry/compacting rows); the deleted producer's history is preserved (frozen, QMD-searchable, no live code left) at [codebase/615.md](codebase/615.md), [codebase/609.md](codebase/609.md), [codebase/679.md](codebase/679.md), [codebase/686.md](codebase/686.md), [codebase/854.md](codebase/854.md), [codebase/1062.md](codebase/1062.md), [codebase/1243.md](codebase/1243.md). |
| [eventring-package.md](features/eventring-package.md) | `internal/eventring` (#646) — bounded, in-memory, **daemon-resident** store of the recent structured turn events the interactive emitter fans out, keyed by a **durable, connection-independent per-conversation event id**; the replay source the mid-turn-reconnect path (#647, `security-sensitive`) consumes (EPIC #596 Phase 2, ADR 025 § Backpressure / replay). Ships with **no reconnect wiring** — storage primitive only; split from #611. 2 exported types: `Event{ID uint64, Type string, Payload json.RawMessage, TS time.Time}` (stores the durable id + the 3 replay-relevant envelope fields, **not** `protocol.Envelope.ID` which is the per-conn `nextID`, meaningless across conns) + `Ring` (`sync.Mutex` + `map[convID]*convRing`). `MaxEventsPerConversation = 1024` (named AC-2 bound, tunable; not load-tested — ADR 025 § Roadmap). `New(maxPerConversation int) *Ring` (panics if < 1, dispatch.New style) / `Append(convID, typ, payload, ts) uint64` / `After(convID, afterID) (events []Event, gap bool)`. **The durable id is assigned ONCE per logical event, before the per-conn fan-out**, in the emitter's `emit()` (`cmd/pyry/interactive_turn_v2.go`) after the single `json.Marshal`: `ts := time.Now().UTC()` hoisted out of the loop (one timestamp shared by the ring + every conn, was per-conn `time.Now()`), then `e.ring.Append(...)` — **unconditional, independent of conn count incl. zero** (the ring is the replay source for absent phones that reconnect later); returned id unused in #646 (#647 surfaces it on the wire).
