The ring's per-conversation counter (starts at 1, strictly increasing) is **distinct from** the per-conn envelope `nextID` — **not overloaded** (preserves the single-writer envelope-id invariant; keeps the slice `security-sensitive`-free). **Eviction:** hard per-conversation cap; oldest `assistant_delta` dropped first (sole droppable class), the five control types (`turn_state`/`tool_use`/`tool_result`/`turn_end`/`stall`) retained in preference; oldest-overall dropped only under all-control pressure. Slice kept id-ascending, non-contiguous after a middle delta eviction (by design — not a gap). **`After` is a pure 3-way classification:** caught-up `afterID==latestID → (nil,false)`; gap `afterID>latestID → (nil,true)` (an id this daemon never issued → resync, #1494) or `oldestRetainedID>afterID+1 → (nil,true)` (next-expected fell off the back → resync); replay `(events ID>afterID ascending, false)`. Unknown conversation: `afterID==0` caught-up, `afterID>0` gap (references events the daemon never had — usable across the daemon-restart boundary #646 scopes out). Isolation structural (`After` reads only `convs[convID]`). **Durability:** survives a supervised claude-**child** respawn + phone reconnects (emitter built once in `startInteractiveTurnStreamV2`, daemon stays up); does **not** survive a full daemon-process restart — purely in-memory by design, **no disk persistence**; that boundary is #647's resync via the AC-5 gap. **Ownership:** the emitter owns the ring (created in `newInteractiveTurnEmitterV2`, **signature unchanged** → 0 of 26 call sites cascade — retires the constructor-cascade red line); #647 reads `emitter.ring` via the seam reachable in `package main` (no accessor added). **Concurrency:** one `sync.Mutex` (leaf lock, never nested/across a channel op); `Append` on the emitter's single `Run` goroutine, `After` (future #647 query) on the manager goroutine — the cross-goroutine sharing lives in the ring's mutex, the emitter's unguarded-counter / single-`Run`-goroutine invariant ([codebase/632.md](codebase/632.md)/[codebase/633.md](codebase/633.md)) is preserved unchanged (belt-and-suspenders, different fabric). No goroutine spawned. See [codebase/646.md](codebase/646.md), [codebase/632.md](codebase/632.md) (emitter owner), [codebase/633.md](codebase/633.md) (daemon-resident wiring). |
| [mobile-live-e2e-runbook.md](features/mobile-live-e2e-runbook.md) | Manual operator runbook (#661) for the live emulator e2e (rung 3 of the mobile e2e ladder): the minimal daemon-side setup so the v2 structured turn bridge resolves a **real** (non-scripted) claude session transcript instead of spinning on `WARN turnbridge: resolve session jsonl, retrying error="no session jsonl found in ~/.claude/projects/-tmp"`. The four-step setup (a real workdir `W` → a real `claude` session in `W` so a `<uuid>.jsonl` exists → seed `conversations.json` to pass `WriteUserTurn`'s `ValidateConversation` gate (#312) → start the daemon `-pyry-workdir=W PYRY_MOBILE_V2=1`) leans entirely on **existing** daemon features — resolution rides the same `resolveClaudeSessionsDir` / `resolveLatestSessionJSONL` machinery #642 aligns against (newest-wins, **conversation-agnostic**), the only live delta being *who writes the transcript* (a real `claude` vs #642's scripted append). Documents why `/tmp`-with-no-seed spins forever (`encodeWorkdir`'s `/`-and-`.`→`-` → empty `~/.claude/projects/-tmp`), the cold-start producer-subscribe race (cite [codebase/642.md](codebase/642.md), don't re-derive), and the two **operator-verified** daemon-log observables (warning stops; producer opens its events stream on the resolved transcript). Live stack (emulator + real claude + relay) is operator-machine-only, **never CI**; AC verified via the runbook, not a CI gate. The manual sibling of the automated [e2e-harness.md](features/e2e-harness.md) / [e2e-realclaude.md](features/e2e-realclaude.md) suites. See [codebase/661.md](codebase/661.md), ADR 025 § Phase 2. |
| [audit-package.md](features/audit-package.md) | `internal/audit` (#712, EPIC #597 Phase 3, ADR 025 §6 "Audit") — zero-dependency leaf sink that writes **exactly one structured `slog.Info` record per resolved remote-permission decision** (which device, which modal, what outcome, from where). The forensic record of every time a phone was allowed/denied the right to answer a security-class modal. `Entry{DeviceHash, DeviceLabel, ModalID, ModalClass, Outcome, Source}` carries **only non-secret identity** — it has *no field that can hold a plain device token*, so the leak the `internal/devices` SECURITY contract forbids is impossible by construction (imports only `log/slog`, never `devices`; fixed six-attr key set pinned by an *exact-key-set* no-leak test). Self-contained `Outcome` (`allowed`/`denied_unauthorized`/`denied_timeout`/`cancelled`/`denied`/`dismissed_local`) + `Source` (`remote`/`timeout`/`local`, mirroring `protocol.ModalDismissedPayload.Source`) string-enums; `Log` records the already-decided outcome verbatim and **never consults the gate** (#702), keeping the sink decoupled from the authorization check. Live consumers (the #703 modal control loop split): #717/#727 (`remote`), #725 (`timeout`), and **#706** (`{dismissed_local, local}`, the local-TTY resolution arm — empty device identity, the no-device case). See [codebase/712.md](codebase/712.md), [codebase/702.md](codebase/702.md), [codebase/706.md](codebase/706.md). |
| [msgqueue-package.md](features/msgqueue-package.md) | `internal/msgqueue` (#704, extended #719/#869/#1000, EPIC #597 Phase 3, ADR 025) — in-memory, **daemon-resident** per-conversation inbound FIFO backlog + serial drain engine; the **inbound counterpart of [`internal/eventring`](features/eventring-package.md)** (eventring buffers what the daemon pushes OUT to phones; msgqueue buffers what phones send IN while claude is busy). Fixes synchronous `send_message`, where `WriteUserTurn`'s `WaitReady` gate blocks the per-conn handler mid-turn and fails past the 30s cap (#594), and concurrent messages race with no defined order. `Enqueue(convID, text) uint64` is **non-blocking** and assigns a stable per-conversation id (from 1); one serial **drain goroutine per active conversation** delivers the backlog one at a time, in enqueue order, through the injected `DeliverFunc(ctx, convID, payload) error` seam (the shape of `Supervisor.WriteUserTurn`, the #594 ready-gate→commit-confirm→recovery path). **The seam's blocking IS the pacing** — `WaitReady` blocks while claude is busy and returns only on a confirmed commit, so a mid-turn message is held until turn-end, an idle-time message drains promptly, and there's never >1 in-flight per conversation — **no separate turn-state detector** (drain trigger option (a) chosen over a `turnevent.TurnEnd` trigger). 3 exported types: `DeliverFunc`, `Config{Deliver, RetryInterval, MaxQueuedPerConversation, Logger}`, `Queue`; `New` **errors** (not panics) on a nil `Deliver` (caller-supplied wiring, contrast `eventring.New`'s panic). **Drain peeks-not-pops**: advances only after a confirmed commit, a delivery error retries the **same FIFO head** after `RetryInterval` (1s default) — **lossless**, and what bridges a claude-**child** respawn (`WriteUserTurn` returns `ErrNoLiveSession` during the window). Does **not** survive a full daemon-process restart (purely in-memory, same boundary as eventring; resync covers it; no disk persistence). **Concurrency:** single leaf `sync.Mutex` (guards `convs`, each `convQueue`, `started`, `closed`, `ctx`) never held across `deliver`, never nested; the lazy-spawn/exit-on-empty race + the `Run` shutdown join are both closed by a `closed bool` set under that lock before `wg.Wait()` (every `wg.Add` happens-before the `Wait` — a `ctx.Err()` gate would not give this). **Security (`security-sensitive`):** `text` is opaque transit, **never logged** (drain logs only `conversation_id`/`queued_msg_id`/`queued_at`/`err`); `convID` is a map key only (validation is the caller's job upstream of `Enqueue` — a hostile convID at worst makes an isolated FIFO that never drains, never reaches another session); **the inbound bound/backpressure landed in #869**: `Config.MaxQueuedPerConversation` (`<= 0` ⇒ `defaultMaxQueuedPerConversation`, 100) caps each conversation's backlog (`len(c.items)`, in-flight head included) at the `Enqueue` chokepoint — past the cap `Enqueue` returns `0` (never a valid id; `uint64` arity unchanged, zero call-site cascade across ~45 sites) without appending, notifying, or spawning a drain (**reject, never drop** — the inbound mirror of ADR 025's "control never drops"), and `send_message` maps `id == 0` to a retryable `protocol.CodeServerBinaryBusy` reply with no ack. **#719 added the introspection/remove/notify API** (additive, still unwired): `Snapshot(convID) []QueuedMessage` (ordered copy under the lock, unknown conv ⇒ `nil` not an error, mirrors `eventring.After`'s copy-out; includes the in-flight head, doesn't flag it); `Remove(convID, id) bool` (drops a not-in-flight queued msg, `true` iff removed — a **merely-waiting** head is droppable by design (#487), refused only once `committing` (narrowed from `draining` in #1085); non-head removal always safe since the drain holds a value-copy of index 0); and an injected `OnChange ChangeFunc` (`Config` field, nil ⇒ disabled, mirrors the `DeliverFunc` seam) fired **after unlock** on enqueue/delivery-advance/successful-remove (no-ops fire nothing) carrying only `convID` (edge-triggered, consumer re-reads `Snapshot`) — `q.mu` now never held across `onChange` *or* `deliver`, so a re-entrant callback can't deadlock; the `advanceLocked` backing-array hygiene `switch` was extracted into a shared `shrinkLocked` (head-drop + mid-FIFO-drop). 2 new exported types (`QueuedMessage{ID,Text,TS}`, `ChangeFunc`); `Enqueue`/`Run`/stable-id untouched. **Wired live by #721**: the daemon constructs one `Queue` in `cmd/pyry/runSupervisor`, runs `Queue.Run(ctx)` under the daemon lifecycle (joined via a buffered `qDone` channel), and `send_message` now **enqueues-and-acks** instead of delivering synchronously — delivery seam `newInboundDeliver(router.resolve)` re-resolves per attempt over a stamp-free `sessionRouter.resolve` core (so the drain never moves the #687 active-conversation cursor) and writes with the raw lifecycle ctx (the #594 deliver timeout removed). **Both #719 consumers now live**: #722 wired the `queue_state` producer (`OnChange`+`Snapshot`) and **#723 wired the `dequeue_message` handler** — `internal/relay`'s `handleDequeueMessage` reaches `Remove` via the consumer-declared `QueueRemover` seam on an interactive phone's inbound control frame, and a successful removal's `notify` drives the #722 producer to refresh `queue_state` (the **first live consumer of `Remove`**; the in-flight-head/unknown-id `false` no-op is what makes a hostile/stale id a safe no-op). `SnapshotAll() map[string][]QueuedMessage` (#878) enumerates every conversation's backlog in one lock hold, keyed by conversation id, omitting any conversation whose backlog is empty (a drained-but-retained convQueue's items == nil); a pure read (mints no id, dequeues nothing), consumed by cmd/pyry's outstandingQueues adapter feeding internal/relay's connect-time queue reconcile ([codebase/878.md](codebase/878.md)). **#1000 bounds the drain's persistent-failure retry**: a `GiveUpFunc`/`OnGiveUp` seam mirroring `ChangeFunc`/`OnChange` plus `Config.GiveUpAfter` (`<= 0` ⇒ `defaultGiveUpAfter`, 2 minutes) — a per-head elapsed deadline, latched in a `drain`-local `firstFailedAt` and reset on every successful delivery, chosen to exceed `internal/supervisor`'s max backoff window (`BackoffMax=30s`/`BackoffReset=60s`) so an ordinary respawn still clears without tripping it. Errors are indistinguishable by type between a transient respawn and a persistent startup wedge, so the bound separates them by elapsed time instead. On the bound, `giveUp` drops the abandoned head, clears `draining`, fires `notify(OnChange)` then `notifyGiveUp(OnGiveUp)` off-lock (daemon-generated `reason` = elapsed + `err`, never `head.text`), and the drain **exits** (not continues to the next head, which would burn one bound-window per head for one wedge) — `draining == false` + non-empty backlog respawns the drain on the next `Enqueue`. Shipped **unwired**: `OnGiveUp` was `nil` in production at #1000's landing (split from #991, not-security-sensitive engine-side half); the wire vocabulary (#1007 — `TypeSessionError`/`CodeSessionBlocked`/`SessionErrorPayload`, also unwired) and **#1008 wired it live**: a `sessionErrorEmitterV2` producer (`cmd/pyry/session_error_v2.go`, structural near-clone of the `queue_state` producer minus the queue reference, so queued phone text is structurally unreachable) sets `OnGiveUp` and fans a typed, terminal `session_error` to interactive phones on every give-up. Nothing remains deferred on this engine now. **[#1199](codebase/1199.md) closed a caller-side gap** (the engine itself unchanged): the stream-json delivery seam previously returned on write instead of blocking for the `DeliverFunc` contract's turn-end pacing, so `newInboundDeliver` now waits on `cmd/pyry`'s per-conversation turn-busy tracker and marks it busy before writing, restoring the queued-backlog UI and drop-before-drain control on that runner to parity with `pty`. **[#1484](codebase/1484.md) closed a TOCTOU that could drop a message or panic the daemon**: `advanceLocked` had dropped the FIFO head unconditionally, but since #1085 a merely-waiting head is droppable, so a `Remove` landing during an in-flight delivery either spliced away the message queued behind the head (no trace, violating "No silent drop") or panicked on the nil slice a drained FIFO leaves (`items[1:]` on `nil`, killing the daemon). `advanceLocked(id)` now drops the front only when the FIFO is non-empty **and** its front still carries that id — the same check-and-splice-atomically discipline `commitGate` already used, now asked in the lock hold that actually splices; both advance sites (the success path and `giveUp`) carry it, and `giveUp` reports whether it abandoned anything so a dequeued head is treated as cancelled (no drop, no report, drain continues) rather than abandoned. See [codebase/704.md](codebase/704.md), [codebase/719.md](codebase/719.md), [codebase/721.md](codebase/721.md), [codebase/722.md](codebase/722.md), [codebase/723.md](codebase/723.md), [codebase/869.md](codebase/869.md), [codebase/878.md](codebase/878.md), [codebase/1000.md](codebase/1000.md), [codebase/1007.md](codebase/1007.md), [codebase/1008.md](codebase/1008.md), [codebase/1199.md](codebase/1199.md), [codebase/1484.md](codebase/1484.md). |
| [modalbridge-package.md](features/modalbridge-package.md) | `internal/modalbridge` + `cmd/pyry/interactive_modal_v2.go` (#716, split from #703, extended #706/#876/#877/#1065; EPIC #597 Phase 3, ADR 025) — the **outbound half** of the daemon-side modal bridge: turn a detected tui-driver permission/trust modal into a typed `modal_shown` event and push it to interactive phones — **never raw PTY bytes**. Establishes the **outstanding-modal registry** keyed by a one-time `modal_id` nonce that #717's inbound resolution half consumes. **`internal/modalbridge` is relay-free by construction** — #717 imports it from inside `internal/relay` (intercepts `modal_answer` at `dispatchAppFrame`), so this package must NOT import `internal/relay` (cycle); it imports only `internal/protocol`, `internal/turnevent`, and the **typed** `pkg/tuidriver` `ModalClass` API — no raw-screen substrate literal, `cmd/substrate-guard` stays green. The `relay.ActiveConn` fan-out stays in `cmd/pyry` (mirrors `interactiveTurnEmitterV2`). Surface: `Registry` (`sync.Mutex`+`map[string]Outstanding`; `New`/`Record`/`Lookup`/`Resolve`/`Snapshot`), `Outstanding`, `PermissionRequestForClass`. **Class mapping** (minimal fixed-option-set, design option (a) — *not* screen-scraping; tui-driver v1.3.0 has no permission/trust option extractor, `EventKindPtyModalShown` carries only the class): `ModalClassPermission`→wire `"permission"` + 4 `turnevent.PermissionOptionKind` options (`allow_once`/`allow_always`/`reject_once`/`reject_always`); `ModalClassTrustFolder`→wire `"trust"` + `proceed`/`exit`; every other class → `ok=false` ⇒ no `modal_shown` (AC1). **Fail-safe deny default (security-review MUST-FIX):** `default_option_id` is the **deny** option (`reject_once`/`exit`), NOT `options[0]`, so a careless confirm on a *remote* surface denies — `options` stays in claude's display order (allow-first); display order ⊥ highlighted default, keeping `default_option_id ∈ options[].id` by construction. UI pre-selection only (answering gated #702, deny-on-timeout #717). **`modal_id`** = `crypto/rand` UUIDv4 (122 bits, *not* `math/rand`, mirrors `conversations.NewID`), minted **once** per modal in `Record` (single-writer-nonce; rising-edge ⇒ one event = one mint) — the primitive #717 relies on to reject stale/replayed answers; `Record` mints+records atomically under the leaf mutex. The **surfacer** (`interactiveModalEmitterV2`) is a **passive state machine** (no goroutine/queue, mirrors `interactiveTurnEmitterV2`): `Handle(ctx, ev, convID, screenText)` → gate → `Record` → marshal once → capability-gated fan-out (`Interactive` conns only #607, per-conn monotonic `env.ID`, **`EventID` nil** = control event, never dropped by `forwardEnvelope`). **Outbound `conversation_id` scoping (#1065):** `ModalShownPayload` carries a daemon-asserted `conversation_id` (declared first, no `omitempty`, mirrors `TurnStatePayload`/`QueueStatePayload`) so a per-conversation modal never displays on a conn viewing a different conversation — the `#1062`-shaped stamp-and-filter, not new daemon-side per-conn gating. `convID` is threaded into `Handle`/`Record`/`Outstanding`/`Snapshot` from the **same single** `active.CurrentConversation()` read that selects `screenText` (`boundScreenText` in `interactive_modal_stream_v2.go` now returns the paired `(convID, screen)`), so content and scope label cannot diverge; `Record` stamps + stores atomically in one critical section, and `Snapshot` restamping `ConversationID` scopes `reconcileModals`'s reconnect replay identically to initial delivery at zero cost to the replay path. Inbound is unchanged: `ModalAnswerPayload`/`ModalCancelPayload` still carry no `conversation_id`. `Prompt` carries a defensive `maxPromptBytes`=4096 rune-boundary bound (a `modal_shown` is an **un-droppable control frame**). **Security (`security-sensitive`):** outbound-only (mints+records; inbound `modal_id` boundary is #717's); modal body (`title`/`prompt`/`screenText`) **never logged** (logs carry only `event`/`class`/`conn_id`/`env_id` + transport-sentinel `err`); `screenText` arrives pre-rendered plain text (no raw bytes reach the phone, AC3). **Registry mutex = deterministic safety net** (two real goroutines: surfacer `Record` + #717 dispatch `Lookup`/`Resolve`), **not** goroutine-confinement — single-writer-nonce alone doesn't make the map read-safe. **Local resolution arm (#706):** the surfacer also handles `EventKindPtyModalHidden` — when the operator answers a modal at the local `pyry attach` TTY, it correlates the just-hidden class to the `modal_id` it surfaced (emitter-tracked `outstandingID`/`outstandingClass`, single-goroutine), `Resolve`s it through the shared registry, and — **only on the first-answer-wins winner path** — audits `{dismissed_local, local}` (no answering device) and broadcasts `modal_dismissed{source: local}` via a shared `broadcastInteractive` helper. Resolution is **single-shot across both heads** (local TTY + paired phone): all four resolution paths (remote answer #717, cancel #727, deny-on-timeout #725, local #706) route through the one mutex-guarded one-shot `Resolve`, so a `modal_id` is resolved/broadcast/audited at most once across the producer-Run vs relay-dispatch goroutines; the remote arm's consume-before-keystroke ordering means a local resolution that wins makes a later remote answer miss **before any keystroke** (ADR 025 §4). The local arm routes **no** keystroke (operator already pressed the key) — `dismissed_local` is a producer sentinel since the daemon can't observe which option was picked. **Live daemon wiring (#798):** `cmd/pyry/interactive_modal_stream_v2.go` (`startInteractiveModalStreamV2`) gives the surfacer its first non-test caller — constructs it over the daemon-singleton `modalReg` with the `V2SessionManager` as both broadcaster and deny-on-timeout armer, feeds it live `Shown`/`Hidden` from a **second, independent** follow-active `Session.Events()` subscription (reusing `resolveTarget` + `NewTargetSubscriber`, which yield **raw** `tuidriver.Event`s — a mapper-free drain, NOT a second `turnbridge.Producer`, NOT an `OnModal` seam), pairs each `Shown` with the bound host's `ScreenSnapshot` (reached via a `cmd/pyry`-local `screenSnapshotter` type-assert, not by widening `turnbridge.SessionHost`), gated + torn down like the turn stream; the two-phone e2e capstones are #791/#793. **Current-truth enumeration (#876):** `Registry.Snapshot() []protocol.ModalShownPayload` answers "what is outstanding right now" for the reconnect-reliability direction (#829) — a direct field-copy from each stored `Outstanding` (not a re-derivation via `buildPayload`), restamping its original `modal_id`, `Options` cloned per payload so a mutating consumer can't corrupt registry state, map-walk order unspecified (callers reconcile by `modal_id`). Pure read: mints no id, retires nothing, not security-sensitive. Registry-only — the connect-time producer that calls it and re-sends over the relay **landed in #877** (`security-sensitive`; see [v2-session-manager.md](features/v2-session-manager.md)'s `OutstandingModals` seam + `reconcileModals`). See [codebase/716.md](codebase/716.md), [codebase/706.md](codebase/706.md), [codebase/798.md](codebase/798.md), [codebase/702.md](codebase/702.md), [codebase/876.md](codebase/876.md), [codebase/877.md](codebase/877.md), [codebase/1065.md](codebase/1065.md), [features/turnevent-package.md](features/turnevent-package.md). |
| [acp-package.md](features/acp-package.md) | `internal/acp` transport (#755 inbound + #757 outbound) + `pyry acp` subcommand (#756) + `session/new` over an embedded pool (#761) + `session/load`/`session/cancel` lifecycle (#762) + `initialize`/`authenticate` handshake (#747) + outbound streaming adapter (#750) + `session/cancel` actuation & mode/config pin (#753) + permission proxy `session/request_permission` (#752) + `session/prompt` held turn call (#749) + outbound turn-event stream wiring (#796) + permission-proxy live wiring (#801) + held-`session/prompt` `stopReason` resolution on `TurnEnd` (#751) + generic-ACP conformance capstone (#754), EPIC #600 — the **bidirectional transport** for the Agent Client Protocol (ACP, Zed-stewarded): line-delimited JSON-RPC 2.0 over one `io.Reader`+`io.Writer`, a method dispatch table for host→agent requests, and an outbound-request primitive for agent→client calls. Greenfield, **stdlib-only** (does NOT import the mobile-wire `internal/protocol` envelope types — ACP's JSON-RPC 2.0 shape ≠ the mobile custom envelope). 3 exported types (`Transport`, `Handler`, `Error`) + `New`/`NewError` + `Code*` constants (`-32700/-32600/-32601/-32602/-32603`), held under the sizing line by a **single decode-by-shape `rpcMessage`** classified by field nil-ness (absent-vs-present-`null` is the whole classifier's input: a null-id *request* dispatches with response id `null`, a no-id *notification* gets none). `handleLine` classifies each line — parse-error/batch-reject/invalid-request/**request**→dispatch+one echoed-id response/**notification**→dispatch+no response/**response-frame**→**routed** to the `Call` awaiting its id (#757; unknown/reclaimed id logged+dropped). Handler contract `(result, err)`: `*Error` controls the wire code, any other error → `-32603` generic (detail logged to stderr, never leaked). One read-loop goroutine dispatching serially-inline (+ a separate caller goroutine per `Call`); `writeMu` leaf lock now **load-bearing** (a `Call` and an inbound reply race on `w`); `atomic.Bool` `started` gate mirrors [`internal/dispatch`](features/dispatch-package.md). `bufio.Scanner` framing (16 MiB line cap, reuses `internal/agentrun/jsonl` sizing); EOF→nil, ctx-checked between frames; **never panics on malformed input**. Diagnostics go only to an injected `*slog.Logger`, never onto the writer, never carrying line bytes or `params` content (user prompt material). Package doc records the **hard cost invariant** (later tickets drive a real *interactive* `claude`, never `claude -p` or the metered Agent SDK) and the batch-rejection decision. **Subcommand (#756):** `cmd/pyry/acp.go` `runACP` is the composition root — registers the `pyry acp` verb (no flags/args), serves the transport over real stdio with a plain-stderr logger (no `SlogTee`/ring — the host owns the stderr ring), exits **0** on EOF and on SIGINT/SIGTERM. **AC#4 closer:** rejects the ticket's `os.Stdin.Close()` (deadlocks per lesson #78 — a `Read` parked in-kernel on the non-pollable stdin fd can't be woken by `Close`), instead bridges real stdin through an in-memory `io.Pipe` so `pr.CloseWithError` unblocks the blocked read; clean-exit guard is `ctx.Err() != nil` (absorbs the wrapped `os.ErrClosed` the forced close surfaces), not `errors.Is(context.Canceled)`. As shipped in #756 it registered **no** handlers and drove **no** claude; #761 extends it (below). **Outbound primitive (#757):** `Transport.Call(ctx, method, params)` issues an agent→client request with a freshly-generated id and blocks for the id-correlated response (raw result or mapped `*Error`) — **0 new exported types** (reuses `*Error`), one new method. First cross-goroutine transport state: `nextID atomic.Uint64` + `pendingMu`-guarded `pending map[uint64]chan callResult` (cap-1 waiter channel keeps `routeResponse` non-blocking on the read loop). Load-bearing ordering: marshal params first (no id burned on failure), register the waiter before writing, ctx-cancel reclaims the slot so a late response is dropped not delivered; idempotent reclaim resolves every cancel-vs-deliver race. MUST be called off the Serve goroutine (handler re-entrancy = T8 concern). Unused until T7/T8 — the unit tests are the contract. **`session/new` + embedded pool (#761):** the first ACP method that does real work + its composition root — `runACP` stands up an embedded `internal/sessions` pool trimmed to the ACP subset (no relay/control/conversations/queue), `serveACPWithPool` backgrounds `pool.Run` + gates on `Pool.Ready()`, and `newSessionHandler` calls `pool.Create(ctx,"")` → one supervised **interactive** `claude --session-id <uuid>` (no `-p`/SDK — the hard cost invariant), returning `{"sessionId":"<uuid>"}`. Exactly one claude per ACP session (divergence 6): `Config.BootstrapEvicted` parks the eager bootstrap so `Create` is the sole spawn; service-mode `Bridge` keeps claude's PTY output off the JSON-RPC stdout. `cwd`/`mcpServers` params deliberately ignored (spawn reuses the daemon workdir + shared `trustMark` → NOT security-sensitive; flip if a later ticket honours caller `cwd`). `serveACP` gained a nil-defaulted `register func(*acp.Transport)` seam. See [ADR 026](decisions/026-embedded-acp-pool-exact-one-claude.md). **`session/load` + `session/cancel` (#762):** the two lifecycle verbs that address an already-opened session — both resolve an ACP `sessionId` onto its pool session via `Pool.Lookup` through one shared `decodeSessionID`, registered in #761's existing closure (one file, 0 new exported types). The decode rejects empty/missing `sessionId` as `CodeInvalidParams` *before* `Lookup` — load-bearing, because `Pool.Lookup("")` resolves the parked **bootstrap** session (not an error), so an empty id would otherwise let `session/load` activate the bootstrap. `session/load` = `Lookup` + `Pool.Activate` (idempotent on the already-active session → **no second claude**, divergence 6; unknown id → `CodeInvalidParams`, no spawn — deliberately NOT `GetOrCreate`, which creates-on-miss). `session/cancel` is an ACP notification whose no-response is **structural** (`dispatchNotification` discards every notification handler's return; registering the handler is the whole mechanism); `resolveCancelTarget` → `sess.Supervisor()` is the pointer-equality-unit-tested seam the abort keystroke lands on (actuated by **T9 #753**, below). Within-process boundary only (ACP pool in-memory, no `RegistryPath`/reconcile → cross-process reload deferred). NOT security-sensitive (existing ids only, no caller path). Still deferred: Serve-exit pending-drain. **`initialize` + `authenticate` handshake (#747):** the two **stateless** methods every connection opens with, in a new `cmd/pyry/acp_handshake.go` (**package `main`**, keeping `internal/acp` method-agnostic), registered by `registerHandshake` in #761's `register` closure with no pool (the structural proof they hold no session state). `initialize` returns `SupportedProtocolVersion = 1` unconditionally (one version ⇒ nothing to negotiate; `min(client,agent)` clamp deferred) + a **minimal-safe** `agentCapabilities` (all flags false — `loadSession`, `promptCapabilities.{image,audio,embeddedContext}`; `mcpCapabilities`/`set_mode`/`set_config_option` omitted) + `authMethods:[]` (non-nil, marshals `[]` not `null`). **Divergence 5** (pyry needs no host `fs/*`/`terminal/*`) is **structural, not a wire field** — the result has nowhere to request a client capability and pyry issues no `fs/*`/`terminal/*` `Call`s; unmodelled `clientCapabilities` are dropped on decode ("accept and ignore"). `initializeParams` decodes only `protocolVersion` as a **shape gate** (non-object params → `-32602`). `loadSession` stays **false despite `session/load` being registered (#762)** — a deliberate honest under-advertisement, since ACP's `loadSession` promises `session/update` replay that's unwired (Phase 2 / #596); **coordination: #748 flips it `true` when replay lands**. `authenticate` is a no-op stub returning `{}` (local trusted-host stdio, no credential in play). NOT security-sensitive. **Outbound streaming half (#750):** `Transport.Notify(method, params)` — `Call`'s write-only sibling (marshal-first: nil params omit the key, a marshal failure writes nothing; then one `writeMessage` under `writeMu`; never awaits a response) in a NEW `internal/acp/notify.go` with a distinct unexported `notification` wire struct (`request.ID` is non-omitempty `uint64` → can't be reused), 0 new exported types. Plus the stateless `cmd/pyry/acp_turn_stream.go` `acpTurnStream` sink — the ACP analogue of `interactiveTurnEmitterV2` but far thinner (no lifecycle/coalescing/fan-out; ACP's host owns turn pacing), whose `Handle` **is** `turnbridge.Config.OnEvent`. It maps the four emit-able variants via [`acpbridge.MapUpdate`](features/acpbridge-package.md) (#769) → `Notify(MethodSessionUpdate, {sessionId, update})`, type-switching `TurnEnd` (→ out-of-band `onTurnEnd(string(Reason))` signal to the T7/#751 held-`session/prompt` owner, divergence 1) and `Stall` (→ stderr `Warn`, divergence 3) BEFORE `MapUpdate` (which collapses both to `ok==false`); `msgID` is discarded (ACP has no per-message delimiter → chunks stream as separate `agent_message_chunk` in arrival order, no coalescing). NOT security-sensitive (outbound-only local stdio). **Producer wiring (#796):** `acpTurnStreams` (`cmd/pyry/acp_turn_streams.go`, unexported per-process manager) gives the #750 sink its **first non-test caller** — one `turnbridge.Producer` goroutine per addressable session tailing `<id>.jsonl` and feeding the sink, so `session/update` flows during a turn. Fixed-target resolver (`resolveBoundSessionJSONL(dir, id)` reused verbatim from #679, `Switch: nil` — the degenerate one-host case of follow-active), fresh `tuidriver.NewTracker` per session; `serveACPWithPool` gains `claudeSessionsDir` (= `DefaultClaudeSessionsDir(trustedWorkdir)`), `attach`ed in `register`, `start(id)`ed after `Create`/`Activate` (idempotent), joined at shutdown (`cancel()`→`wait()`); producer runs the session **lifecycle** (re-subscribes per turn); the `onTurnEnd` seam shipped **nil** here and is wired by #751 (below). The dir is **deliberately NOT set on `Config.ClaudeSessionsDir`** — that would enable the `/clear` rotation watcher and break host-held id addressing; the pool stays byte-unchanged. Content-free logging preserved. NOT security-sensitive. **`session/cancel` actuation + mode/config pin (#753, ADR 027 Open Item #1 resolved):** the inbound **control** surface (T9). `session/cancel` now actuates the interrupt onto #762's seam — a consumer-declared `interrupter interface{ SendEsc() error }` (package `main`, `*supervisor.Supervisor` satisfies it, #726); `cancelSessionHandler` takes an **injected resolver** and calls `_ = intr.SendEsc()` **best-effort** (a `SendEsc` failure is swallowed — nothing to roll back, a notification owes no reply; returning it would double-log via `dispatchNotification` and break the `count==1` assertion — silent-swallow vs `relay.handleInterrupt`'s `Warn`-then-tolerate); the composition root returns a **true `nil` interface** on resolve error (the typed `*supervisor.Supervisor` would wrap a non-nil interface). Not routed through the neutral `turnevent.Cancel` (#707, declared-not-constructed) — calls the concrete lever directly. **Single-mode pin:** no tui-driver lever toggles claude's plan/edit mode, so pyry pins one mode advertised in the **session responses, not the handshake** (ACP `initialize`/`agentCapabilities` has no mode flag) — `newSessionResult` gains a `Modes sessionModeState` populated at `session/new` + `session/load` with one self-referential `{currentModeId:"default", availableModes:[{id:"default"}]}` ("no switch that does nothing"). `setModeHandler` (stateless, no pool) accepts `"default"` (empty `{}` success) and rejects any other/non-object → `CodeInvalidParams`; `setConfigOptionHandler` rejects **wholesale** (no config surface — the deliberate mode≠config asymmetry). Both are requests through `dispatchRequest` (exactly one response). Sourceless `current_mode_update`/`config_option_update` never emitted (nothing changes; parked ADR 027 Open Item #2). End-to-end cancel→`stopReason:cancelled` is #751's (now landed — a `cancel`-driven `TurnEnd{cancelled}` returns `stopReason:"cancelled"`, a result not an error). NOT security-sensitive (local stdio inbound control). **Permission proxy (#752, T8, ADR 027 divergence 2 — the highest-risk seam, security-sensitive):** `acpPermissionProxy` (`cmd/pyry/acp_permission.go`, two new files; shipped unwired, **live-wired by #801**) answers claude's on-screen permission modal by asking the ACP host — on a permission-class modal it issues a blocking `session/request_permission` `Call` and routes the host's choice back into claude's live prompt as the correct keystroke (the interactive path — **no** `claude -p`/SDK, hard cost invariant). A permission modal is a **tui-driver PTY-state event** (`EventKindPtyModalShown`/`ModalClassPermission`), NOT a `turnevent.Event`, so the adapter takes its own `Session.Events()` modal subscription (fan-out, no contention with T6/T7). **Concurrency:** a drain goroutine (serial `Handle`) owns `inflight`; `handleShown` builds the four-option set via `modalbridge.PermissionRequestForClass` and spawns **one round-trip goroutine** to block in `Call` off the Serve read loop (AC-5 — a `Call` on the read loop deadlocks); an `atomic.Bool` one-shot (`CompareAndSwap`) is the sole cross-goroutine arbiter — retirement on **any** `ModalHidden` cancels the in-flight `Call` so a reply for a retired modal routes nothing (AC-4), no mutex, `-race` clean. **Default-safe (ADR 025):** the only allow path is a decoded `outcome:"selected"` whose `optionId` is a **member of the daemon-built set** → `Answer(digit)` (`digit = idx+1`, the `classifyAnswer` index→digit discipline over neutral `[]turnevent.PermissionOption`); every other outcome — call error/timeout/teardown, undecodable body, forged `optionId`, `cancelled`, unknown outcome — routes deny (`SendEsc`). Deny is the failure mode, not a branch; a forged id is unlocatable so can never allow. Routes via the supervisor **keystroke seam** (`modalKeystroker`, #726), NOT `turnevent.PermissionResponse` (declared-but-unwired — refines divergence 2's prose). Correlation doubly guarded: transport-owned id `pending` map + the `atomic.Bool` one-shot. `toolCall` omitted (`omitempty` — modal path has no tool-call id); response decodes the nested `{"outcome":{outcome,optionId}}` form default-safe (mismatch ⇒ deny, fails closed). Trust modal out of scope (workdir pre-trusted via `trustMark`; gates on `ModalClassPermission` only, `AcceptTrust` unused). Architect security pass + code-review security goggles both PASS. **`session/prompt` — the main call (#749, T5, security-sensitive):** `promptHandler` (`cmd/pyry/acp_prompt.go`, one new file + ~15-line `serveACPWithPool` edit, 0 new exported types) resolves the target session via `pool.Lookup`, maps the host's ordered content blocks to a user-turn payload (`mapPromptContent`), delivers it into the supervised **interactive** claude via `Session.WriteUserTurn` → tui-driver `DeliverPrompt` (hard cost invariant, no `-p`/SDK) on a **spawned goroutine** (bounded `promptDeliverTimeout=15s`), and **holds its own JSON-RPC call open** for the whole turn by returning #765's `acp.ErrDeferred` — deliver-in and hold-open are one handler because an ACP turn IS one held `session/prompt`. Cheap, race-free work stays inline on the read loop (decode/map/resolve/guard) so the loop keeps classifying while a call is held; only the seconds-long `WriteUserTurn` moves off it (the reason #765 exists). **`promptHolds`** = per-session in-flight registry (`sync.Mutex`+`map[string]*acp.Responder`, ≤1 held call per session): `begin` (atomic check-and-insert inline; a **concurrent second prompt** → `CodeInvalidRequest`, no hold/spawn), `end(id,reason)` (the **T7 #751 seam** — resolve `{stopReason}`), `fail(id,*Error)` (delivery error → resolve + free slot). `end`/`fail` **capture-then-release `mu` before** `Reply`/`ReplyError` (which take `writeMu`) so `mu` stays a leaf; missing entry = idempotent no-op; exactly-one-frame rides #765's `Responder.done` CAS. **`mapPromptContent` fails closed** (all `CodeInvalidParams`): non-text block (names the kind; `promptCapabilities` all-false so any non-text is a host protocol violation), **disallowed control byte**, empty/absent prompt, malformed JSON; text blocks joined with `\n` (bracketed-paste-safe), passed **verbatim** (no escaping, 16 MiB transport frame is the only cap). **Security MUST-FIX (folded in):** because delivery frames the payload as a **bracketed paste**, a raw `ESC` (e.g. paste terminator `ESC[201~`) could break out of paste framing and reach claude's TUI as keystrokes — `hasDisallowedControl` rejects any C0/DEL byte except `\t\n\r`, scanning **bytes not runes** (UTF-8-safe: disallowed bytes are single-byte ASCII, never a continuation byte). Host content is delivered **strictly as a user turn** — single narrow sink (`WriteUserTurn`), no shell (fixed argv at spawn), never a path, never logged; empty/missing `sessionId` rejected via shared `decodeSessionID` **before** `Lookup` (bootstrap trap), `ErrSessionNotFound`→`CodeInvalidParams "unknown session"`. Architect security pass PASS. Resolving the held call on real `TurnEnd` is #751 (now landed — below). **Held-`session/prompt` resolution (#751, T7, divergence 1 — the event-stream-to-RPC-return join):** flips `acpTurnStreams`' `onTurnEnd` seam from **nil** to `promptHolds.end` (a `func(sessionID,reason string)` field/param added to the manager, bound to the fixed `sessionID` at `start` and passed to the sink), wired at the composition root where `holds` is declared before `streams`. On `TurnEnd` the outbound stream resolves the held call with the ACP `stopReason` — **identity `string(Reason)`, no new mapping function** (the sink's existing cast; `TurnEndReason` values ARE the ACP strings). Rides the producer's **single Run goroutine**, synchronously in the sink's `TurnEnd` branch **after** the last `session/update` (streaming strictly precedes the return); no new goroutine/lock, no `writeMu` re-entrancy (terminal branch emits no frame); exactly-once via `end`'s delete-under-mu + #765 `Responder.done` CAS. `cancelled` resolves as a **result, not an error** (AC-2). AC-1 pins the five `stopReason` strings as **literals** (`end_turn`/`max_tokens`/`max_turn_requests`/`refusal`/`cancelled`) so a future taxonomy divergence fails there. ~12 production lines across `acp.go`+`acp_turn_streams.go`, 0 new exported types. NOT security-sensitive (daemon-originated closed-taxonomy reason; content-free logging unchanged). **Conformance capstone (#754, epic #600 proof-of-correctness):** one new test file (`cmd/pyry/acp_conformance_test.go`, zero production files) — `TestACPConformance_FullSessionDrive` drives one scripted ACP host through the whole surface (`initialize`→`session/new`→`session/prompt`+scripted turn→permission round-trip→`session/cancel`) over the real transport against a deterministic fake claude, asserting the emitted wire shape is the generic ADR 027 dialect with all six divergences **composing in one session**; a scripted-host harness mirrors `serveACPWithPool`'s register-closure (same constructors — a compile-time-checked mirror) with `dir==""` (real outbound off) + `newRecordingDeliverer(false)` (hold stays held for the scripted `TurnEnd`) and a **continuous frame classifier** that auto-answers the inbound `session/request_permission` and records `session/update` notifications. `TestACPConformance_DialectLock` asserts the discriminant/`stopReason`/permission-kind constants equal the **literal** ADR 027 strings — the drift guard the per-ticket tests (which compare against the same constants) cannot be. See [codebase/755.md](codebase/755.md), [codebase/756.md](codebase/756.md), [codebase/757.md](codebase/757.md), [codebase/761.md](codebase/761.md), [codebase/762.md](codebase/762.md), [codebase/747.md](codebase/747.md), [codebase/750.md](codebase/750.md), [codebase/753.md](codebase/753.md), [codebase/752.md](codebase/752.md), [codebase/749.md](codebase/749.md), [codebase/796.md](codebase/796.md), [codebase/751.md](codebase/751.md), [codebase/801.md](codebase/801.md), [codebase/754.md](codebase/754.md). |
| [acpbridge-package.md](features/acpbridge-package.md) | `internal/acpbridge` (#769, EPIC #600, [ADR 027](decisions/027-acp-mapping.md)) — the **outbound ACP adapter**: `MapUpdate(ev turnevent.Event) (update any, msgID string, ok bool)`, a pure, exhaustive, table-testable function mapping each neutral [`turnevent.Event`](features/turnevent-package.md) to its ACP `session/update` payload (or "no notification"). The exact ACP mirror of [`turnbridge`](features/turnbridge-package.md)'s outbound `MapEvent` (#627): same neutral source, ACP `sessionUpdate`-discriminant framing instead of the mobile v2 envelope — and `ThoughtChunk` **is** mapped here (`agent_thought_chunk`) where the mobile adapter drops it. Seven mapped variants: `TextChunk`→`agent_message_chunk`, `ThoughtChunk`→`agent_thought_chunk`, `ToolStart`→`tool_call` (no status field ⇒ ACP default `status:"pending"`, no content — a new invocation has no result yet), `ToolUpdate`→`tool_call_update`, plus **`BackgroundTaskStarted`/`Updated`/`Roster`→`pyry/background_task_*`** (#1402, extension discriminants declared by #1401, ADR 027 divergence 7 — emitted unconditionally, no gate; `mapBackgroundTasks` forwards a nil roster as nil so `BackgroundTaskRoster.MarshalJSON`'s nil→`[]` normalisation stays the type's alone; no ACP-lane producer until #1400). `TurnEnd` (→ `session/prompt` `stopReason` **return**, divergence 1) and `Stall` (internal-only → stderr) have **no `session/update`** — `MapUpdate` reports `(nil, "", false)`; both are the consumer's job. The `default:` arm now names five existing sealed variants (`ThinkingProgress`/`ApiRetry`/`Compacting`/`Unrecognized`/`PermissionRequest`) out of `turnevent.Event`'s 14-member sealed set, not "impossible future" ones. **Kind/status are identity, not a translation table** — `turnevent.ToolKind`/`ToolStatus` values already ARE the ACP strings (`other` the neutral fallback). **Message id is returned out-of-band** (`msgID`, the third value) — ACP has **no `messageId` wire field**, so the neutral `MessageID` is preserved for the consumer's chunk grouping without emitting a non-spec field (a golden-JSON test locks that `agent_message_chunk` marshals without `messageId`). 7 exported wire types = ACP domain-variant count (flat `omitempty` structs, camelCase tags verbatim; the sanctioned data-model-taxonomy sizing exception — held S, precedent `turnevent` #606). Pure value-to-value: **no I/O, goroutine, clock, or shared state** — race-free by construction, `context.Context` correctly absent; `default` arm kept on both `MapUpdate` and the exhaustive `mapToolContent` (text/diff/terminal/nil) so a future producer variant can't silently vanish. **Import discipline:** imports only `internal/turnevent` + `encoding/json` — NOT `internal/acp` (the sealed stdlib-only transport floor — a `session/update` method payload there would break the seal) nor `internal/protocol` (mobile wire); a sibling `*bridge` package to `turnbridge`/`modalbridge`. NOT security-sensitive (outbound, daemon-internal, no untrusted input/dispatch). The `{sessionId, update}` params wrapper + `TurnEnd`-signal + `Stall`-to-stderr live in the **streaming consumer [#750](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode/issues/750)** (now built — see the [acp-package.md](features/acp-package.md) outbound streaming adapter section + [codebase/750.md](codebase/750.md)). See [codebase/769.md](codebase/769.md). |
| [contextwindow-package.md](features/contextwindow-package.md) | `internal/contextwindow` (#856) — `Read(path) (Usage, error)` scans a resolved claude transcript and reports current context-window occupancy (`UsedTokens`/`WindowTokens`); reuses [`jsonl`](features/jsonl-reader.md), last-usage-wins (surfaces auto-compaction as a reset for free), single documented 200K default window. Not `security-sensitive`. Shipped unwired; **wired by #857** onto `screen_snapshot` via the optional `SnapshotUsage` seam on `internal/relay`'s `V2SessionConfig` — using a dedicated `resolveOwnBootstrapJSONL` instance (`cmd/pyry`-local probe-preferred resolver), not the `ResolveTranscript`/`newProbePreferredTranscriptResolver` seam this package's own spec anticipated (see [codebase/857.md](codebase/857.md) for the predicted-vs-actual correction). See [`codebase/857.md`](codebase/857.md). |
| [debugbundle-package.md](features/debugbundle-package.md) | `internal/debugbundle` (#811, `security-sensitive`) — the **producer** of the debug-bundle feature (design: the desktop "Diagnostics and Debug Bundle" vault note): a zero-dependency stdlib-only leaf that packages a session's evidence — the most-recent `.cast` recording (when #802 debug capture was on) plus a caller-supplied daemon log-ring snapshot — into **one in-memory `tar`+`gzip` archive** with a content-free `Manifest`. `Assemble(recordingsDir string, logs []string) ([]byte, Manifest, error)` reads the newest `.cast` **by mtime** (not lexical stamp — same-UTC-second sessions share a stamp prefix and need a tiebreak; mtime doesn't) via a non-recursive `filepath.Glob("*.cast")` (matches all three #802/#552 name forms: in-flight `<stamp>-<sid>.cast` + finalized `-ok`/`-err`), joins the caller's `[]string` log snapshot (the [`internal/control`](features/control-plane.md) `RingBuffer.Snapshot()` `pyry logs` returns — passed as a param so the package never imports `internal/control`; whole-ring only, the flat 200-line ring has no per-session key), and streams both plus `manifest.json` into a `gzip.Writer`→`tar.Writer` over a `bytes.Buffer`. `DefaultRecordingsDir()` **duplicates** `~/.local/share/pyry-recordings` rather than importing `cmd/pyry`'s unexported `resolveRecordingsDir`. **Ships unwired** (0 non-test callers) — the serving path is #812 (chunked transport under the 64 KB frame cap) then #813 (request verb + recipient authorization, which owns *who* may receive a bundle). **Security (the first read path over "the highest-value secret surface" — every PTY byte):** invariants hold **structurally** — **no disk write** (read-only `Glob`/`Stat`/`OpenFile O_RDONLY` + an in-memory `bytes.Buffer`, so the bundle is never written to any synced/backed-up path); **fixed hard-coded tar member names** (`manifest.json`/`logs.txt`/`recording.cast`, never derived from the on-disk basename → a crafted filename can't inject a traversal path into the archive structure; the real name travels only as a JSON *value* in `RecordingName`); **`O_NOFOLLOW`** on the recording open defeats the stat→open TOCTOU (a same-user symlink swapped in fails the open, honest error, never exfiltrates the target); **zero `slog` calls** ⇒ no log emission exists to leak recording bytes or log-line values (AC5 by construction); content-free manifest (name/size/count only). **`io.CopyN` not `io.Copy`** (the one deliberate deviation from the spec): the header `Size` comes from the selection `stat`, but tui-driver may append to the in-flight recording between stat and read, so a plain `io.Copy` would overrun the declared size and trip `tar.ErrWriteTooLong` — `io.CopyN(tw, f, size)` writes exactly the declared prefix. A **selected** recording that can't be read returns a wrapped error, never a false `RecordingPresent:false` (a manifest must not lie — "absent" means no recording exists). No goroutines/locks; synchronous, reentrant-safe; reading the in-flight file is safe (`O_EXCL` constrains only the create, a read-only open sees the flushed valid asciinema-v2 prefix). See [ptyrunner-package.md § Session flight recorder](features/ptyrunner-package.md#session-flight-recorder-pyry_record_dir) (recording source, #552/#802), [codebase/811.md](codebase/811.md). |
| [permbridge-package.md](features/permbridge-package.md) | `internal/permbridge` (#1103, split from #1079, `security-sensitive`) — in-memory registry of pending tool-approval requests for the Streamrunner Interactive permission bridge (T1 spike #1075: a non-YOLO headless `claude --permission-prompt-tool mcp__<server>__<tool>` synchronously blocks on the registered MCP tool's allow/deny JSON, so a pending request is real owed-silence of unbounded length). `Register(id, Request, timeout) (*Pending, error)` parks a request and arms a registry-owned `time.AfterFunc` fail-closed deadline; `Resolve(id, Verdict) bool` satisfies it; `Pending.Await() Verdict` blocks the caller. Mirrors `internal/modalbridge`'s registry shape (leaf mutex, opaque-id-keyed map) plus a per-entry buffered(1) completer channel + timer. **Security core:** `resolve` (unexported) funnels both `Resolve` and the timer through one `delete(pending, id)`-under-`mu` one-shot arbiter — exactly one wins and becomes the sole channel writer, so allow can never land after a deadline-deny; proven by a 300-iteration `-race` test. Self-contained (stdlib only, zero `internal/` imports — no relay-import cycle to document, unlike modalbridge); log-free. **#1104 (landed)** is the first production consumer — the `mcp.approve` control-socket verb (`internal/control`) calls `Register`/`Await` against one shared instance created in `cmd/pyry`'s `runSupervisor`. **#1105 (landed)** is the second consumer — see [pyry-mcp-approve-command.md](features/pyry-mcp-approve-command.md). **#1106 (landed)** completes the enforce-vs-skip switch (spawn-arg injection + mcp-config generation pointing claude at #1105) without calling the registry directly — see [codebase/1106.md](codebase/1106.md). **#1080 (landed)** is the resolver: `cmd/pyry`'s `streamApprovalBridge` calls `Lookup`/`Resolve` against the same #1104 registry instance, keyed by a `modal_id ⇄ tool_use_id` correlation it owns — see [v2-session-manager.md](features/v2-session-manager.md) § Stream-json approval bridge. Known NIT (not fixed): the timer closure captures `id` not `*pending`, so a stale timer could in a tight window resolve a **reused** id's new entry — fail-closed-only, unreachable with claude's unique `tool_use_id`s; #1104 does not recycle ids, so the contract holds. See [codebase/1103.md](codebase/1103.md), [codebase/1104.md](codebase/1104.md), [codebase/1105.md](codebase/1105.md), [codebase/1080.md](codebase/1080.md). |
| [pyry-mcp-approve-command.md](features/pyry-mcp-approve-command.md) | `pyry mcp-approve` (#1105, split from #1079, `security-sensitive`) — MCP stdio tool host claude calls via `--permission-prompt-tool mcp__pyry_approve__approve` for every non-allowlisted tool use; forwards each `tools/call approve` to the daemon over the `mcp.approve` control-socket verb ([control-plane.md](features/control-plane.md) § Approve, #1104) and returns the verdict as the MCP tool result. Pure forwarder + fail-closed adapter: self-originates **only** deny (`denyResult`, pinned to `permbridge.BehaviorDeny`); allow reaches claude solely by passing through the daemon's trusted `resp.Approve` (#1080's future resolver) — no code path constructs an allow. `input` stays `json.RawMessage` end to end, never parsed/dispatched/logged. Reuses `internal/acp.Transport`/`serveACP` verbatim for a second, non-ACP JSON-RPC dialect (proving the transport is genuinely protocol-neutral). New `internal/control.Approve` client helper mirrors `Rekey` (patient-read deadline ≥ daemon approval window, fast-fail dial ≤`dialRetryBudget`). `mcpApproveClientMargin=30s` added atop `mcpApprovalTimeout` so the daemon's own timeout-deny message wins over the client's generic one. Code review PASS, one NIT (dead defensive-code message fallback). **[#1106](codebase/1106.md) (landed)** generates the mcp-config that registers this server and the argv (`--permission-prompt-tool`) that points claude at it (`cmd/pyry/mcp_config.go`'s `permissionArgs`/`renderMCPApproveConfig`, deriving `mcp__pyry_approve__approve` from this subcommand's own `mcpServerName`/`approveToolName` constants). **[#1168](codebase/1168.md) (landed)** wires both primitives onto the live interactive `streamsup` spawn — `newStreamRunnerFactory`'s closure injects `permissionArgs(false, mcpApprovePath)` per-spawn unless `--dangerously-skip-permissions` is already present, so a non-yolo interactive stream session now surfaces an answerable approval modal instead of silently skipping the gate. See [codebase/1105.md](codebase/1105.md), [codebase/1106.md](codebase/1106.md), [codebase/1168.md](codebase/1168.md). |
| [pyry-mcp-files-command.md](features/pyry-mcp-files-command.md) | `pyry mcp-files` (#2168, split from #2165, `security-sensitive`) — the caller half of `attachment.file` (#2164): a second MCP stdio server (not a second tool on `pyry_approve`), `pyry_files`, exposing exactly one tool `send_file`, forwarding `tools/call` to the daemon via `control.AttachFile`. Session id is a constructor parameter read once from `PYRY_SESSION_ID` by `runMCPFiles` (never by the constructor, so tests stay `t.Parallel`); model-controlled arguments unmarshal into `sendFileArgs`, which has no field a caller-supplied session id can land in — a type-level leak barrier, mutation-tested against the payload-type swap. Daemon refusals pass through byte-verbatim; the tool description states the workspace-confinement rule and the `maxAttachFileBytes`-derived bound as its whole contract; the decision log never carries the session id, `call.Name`, the path, or the socket path. Registered on the interactive spawn by **#2169**, which also puts `PYRY_SESSION_ID` on each non-bypass child's environment — composed per-spawn from `streamsup`'s live id (`spawnEnv`/`beginSpawn`), not fixed at runner construction, so it tracks a `RestartFresh` rotation; a `/clear` rotation never respawns, so a live child keeps its stale id until its own next spawn (fail-closed refusal, never a misfile). A bypass child gets no `--mcp-config` at all, `pyry_files` included. See [control-plane-attachment-file-confine-and-store-a-claude-named-path.md](features/control-plane-attachment-file-confine-and-store-a-claude-named-path.md). |
| [transcript-package.md](features/transcript-package.md) | `internal/transcript` (#1148, split from #973) — leaf package, the single authoritative home two near-twin transcript resolver families (`internal/sessions/reconcile.go`, `cmd/pyry/interactive_turn_stream_v2.go`) and the rotation watcher's duplicate UUID-stem regexp migrate onto. Composable, context-free primitives (`ValidStem`, `CanonicalDir`, `GuardProbedPath`, `StatByID`, `Newest`, `Probed`) returning a neutral `Result{Path, Size}` — the divergent not-found conventions and cold/warm offset semantics deliberately stay in the call-site adapters, not the core. `Probe` is a locally-redeclared one-method interface (not imported from `rotation`) so the package stays a leaf with no import cycle. All three consumers now migrated: Family B in #1150 (PR #1158), rotation watcher in #1151 (PR #1159, sheds only `regexp`), Family A in #1149 (PR #1157) — the original triplicated UUID-stem regexp is fully retired — see [codebase/1148.md](codebase/1148.md), [codebase/1149.md](codebase/1149.md), [codebase/1150.md](codebase/1150.md), [codebase/1151.md](codebase/1151.md). |
| [attachments-package.md](features/attachments-package.md) | `internal/attachments` (#1769, #1770, #1772, #1776, #1777, #1787, #1781, #1788, #1782, #1795, split from #1741/#1766) — accumulates one inbound attachment upload's chunks in memory keyed by index, admits/refuses a first-chunk declaration (`CheckDeclaration`, `CheckDeclaredSize`), checks assembled bytes against declared length/sha256, sanitises filenames, bounds one upload's retained bytes to `maxUploadBytes` (16 MiB) at two rungs behind one sentinel, holds in-flight uploads in a conn-keyed `Registry` whose `Admit` decides admission under one lock acquisition, and writes verified bytes to disk (`EnsureDir`/`Store`); no wire codes, no logger. Blocked: #1796 (in-flight-upload entry-count cap), #1742 (expiry/abandonment), #1744 (dispatch, first production caller). |
| [cli-verb-dispatch.md](features/cli-verb-dispatch.md) | `cmd/pyry`'s `run`/`runArgs` verb switch (no `default` — an unrecognised first argument forwards to claude by design) and `printHelp`'s `const helpText` seam; the loud-sentinel convention for a deleted verb (`errAttachRemoved`/`errACPRemoved`, mirroring `interactive_runner`'s `case "pty"`) over a pre-switch guard or a `default`, since a duplicate `case` constant is a compile error rather than a silently shadowed route. |
| [questionbridge-package.md](features/questionbridge-package.md) | `internal/questionbridge` (#1965, split from #1926/#1906) — discriminant (`ToolName = "AskUserQuestion"`) + fail-closed `Parse` turning claude's clarifying-question tool input into `protocol.QuestionShownPayload`; consumed by `streamApprovalBridge.Surface` (#1973), which surfaces a well-formed batch as `question_shown` and falls through to the permission modal otherwise. |
| [history-package.md](features/history-package.md) | `internal/history` (#2112) — durable, versioned, segmented per-conversation message log read newest-first by walking backwards on demand; the interactive emit chokepoint and session transitions append to it (`#2114`), the operator's own typed message appends via `msgqueue`'s `OnDelivered` seam (`#2115`), `#2116` serves it. Rejects reading claude's own transcripts as the source; cursor validation applies `eventring`'s #2022 "no arithmetic on an untrusted boundary" lesson a second time; a failed write to either a fresh or an active segment needed both a write-side undo and a read-side tolerance — cleanup alone left a conversation permanently unreadable. |
